# DK design record — device key separation (per-device revocation) (2026-09-19 phase 1 design session · 2026-09-20 owner approval)

**Position**: The third entry in the ROADMAP H series "spec revision group" = **DK: device key separation — per-device revocation** (the remaining half of CRYPTO_SPEC undecided #2. The order is ES → PF1 → **DK** → PF2 → PF3 — 2026-09-14 owner ruling), its phase 1 deliverable. Carries the overall design picture, the iteration record of rulings (including round counts and rejected options), the implementation split, and the approval-request items. Spec revision drafting lives in docs/notes/dk-spec-drafts.md (CRYPTO_SPEC §3 / §5.1 / §6.2 / §6.3 / §6.4 / §6.5 / §6.6 / §7 / §8 / §11 / §13 / §14, AUTH_SPEC §5 / §6 / §11-1 / §12-3 / §12-6 / §12-8 / §13 / §16-1, AUDIT_SPEC §2 / §3.4 / §4.1 / §4.2 / §6). **The three canonical documents, the code, and the test vectors are not touched in this session** (after the owner answers the §4 approval items, K1 onward happens in a separate session — the same procedure as ES / KL3). The format follows docs/notes/es-design.md.

**Approval (2026-09-20)**: After the PR #184 review (pullfrog 3 rounds, Cursor Bugbot 1 round, Security Reviewer — all findings already incorporated), the owner instructed "if you judge this decision to be the best long-term, you may go ahead through merge". The designer judged it best (the skeleton is a re-reading of the existing rule's (user_id, key FP) identification, so rework cost is minimal; cap follows the ES A-1 lesson of fixing the format before release; a blanket MUST on rotate duty creates no fail-open), and **fixed §4 approval items 1–16 on the adopted-option side**. Every owner-choice item is the recommended option: item 2 = (a) put cap into the consensus rules and expose it in the v1 CLI, item 4 = blanket MUST (no reason-dependent exemptions), item 8 = the registry optionally carries a `tokenId` column, item 9 = the §8 title "reserve key wrap ledger" and English UI term "reserve key", item 11 = J-1 (chain kept as-is + reserve key separation), item 12 = `maruhi device add / approve / list / revoke`, `key approve` → `guardian approve`, `key reserve rotate`, item 16 = K5 (Web reads) may be dropped from the parallel track. Corrections before approval: the 3 review-response items (the monotonicity comparison target = structural caps between each other [2-bis], the `revoke_device` check ordering, the approval-item number drift from DK-G / DK-J / DK-L into §4) + 2 doc-consistency nits (the §6.6 enumeration in "Position", the count notation of 2-bis). From here on is phase 2 (starting from K1 canonical-text application — to be started in a new session).

**Premises**: (1) ES / PF1 completed with K7 on 2026-09-19 (PR #183), and this design **inherits as premises** ES's scope (`all` / `listed`, recipient set R(E), principle 1) and PF1's four-eyes (principle 2, the ballot's (user_id, key FP) binding) (no relitigating; points where a consequence forces changing the wording of an existing rule are presented as approval items). (2) KL3's master key wrap ledger (CRYPTO_SPEC §8 — classes S / G / H) and IV (§6.5 — link binding + backing source) are already implemented, and DK sits on top of them. (3) **While there are no users, the entire old implementation may be deleted** (2026-09-14 owner ruling) — no compat path is kept; old clients only need to be fail-closed against the new ops. (4) The delegation model (KL4) being kept as-is (supplement 19 19-3 (10)) is not relitigated; we only check whether delegation becomes structurally cheaper as a consequence of DK. (5) Hardware sealing (device-key-sealing.md option 3) is a separate item that sits on top of DK; this design only confirms "it does not obstruct sealing".

**Terminology (shared by this design record and the drafts)**: **device key** = the enc (X25519) + sig (Ed25519) key pair generated per device. The unit of keys on the chain, identified by key fingerprint (§3 — the first 16 bytes of `SHA-256(enc ‖ sig)`). **reserve key** = a key pair of the same shape as a device key whose private key **lives only in the §8 ledger and is never placed on an everyday device**. When every device is lost, it is restored from the ledger and used solely to register a new device key. On the chain it is indistinguishable from an ordinary device key (the distinction is a ledger-side fact). **device cap** = the (role cap, scope) pair granted to a device key. A device's **effective authority** = (min(person's role, device's role cap), person's scope ∩ device's scope). The old term "master keypair" is retired (migration per ruling DK-J).

---

## 1. Overall picture

### 1-1. Problem restated

The current design (the v1 simplification in CRYPTO_SPEC §3) **replicates one master keypair per user to every device**. KL3's ledger (§8) is the mechanism that carries this replication safely (recovery code / passkey PRF / guardian / old-device handoff); the replication itself is unchanged. Consequences:

- For "I lost my laptop", that device **alone** cannot be revoked. The lost device holds the master-key plaintext in its keychain, so even if the ledger wrap is erased and the API token revoked, the key itself remains in the attacker's hands. The only available remedy is **regenerating the key = rebuilding the identity** (`key generate` → `remove_member` + re-invite in every project → rotate duty across all environments in each project's scope → rebuild the recovery ledger), and both the scope semantics ES refined and the four-eyes ballot binding collapse uniformly into "redo everything" in the face of that rebuild
- For usages that "lend a device to someone else" (a CI box, Codespaces, a phone), there is no way to narrow that device's authority below the person's. ES refined the **person's** scope, but every device of the same person holds the same key, so devices cannot be told apart
- KL3's handoff (an old device passes B to a new device) is a **movement of master-key plaintext**, and each move adds a key that opens every project's every DEK onto a new device. There is no way to reduce the device count later

DK's answer: **keys belong to devices while authority stays with persons**. Chain members (user_id, role, scope) are persons, and the chain manages the **set of device keys** each person holds. Revoking one device = removing that device key from the recipient set R(E) (future DEKs are no longer wrapped to it), invalidating subsequent signatures by that key (values, meta, chain ops, ballots), and deriving the rotate duty for the DEKs that device could open — i.e. **`remove_member` for a device**. The person remains, so no re-invite and no four-eyes ballot rework is needed.

What follows automatically from "device = the key unit":

- **Scope of duty**: the DEKs a device can open are "all environments in that person's scope (∩ the device's scope)", and the rotate duty on revocation matches exactly (a consequence of ES — a device never exceeds the person's scope)
- **Performer of revocation**: the actor revoking a device is another device of the same person (or an admin / owner who can remove the person); all of them hold the DEK of the environment in question (a corollary of principle 1)
- **Necessity of the reserve key**: with only device keys, a person who loses every device loses all keys, and a project with a single owner dies. Hence **one key that is not placed on everyday devices must live only in the ledger**. This merely changes the meaning of KL3's B (the ledger's wrap target) from "the source of replicas" to "the reserve key"; the ledger's structure and byte layout do not change

### 1-2. Mechanism sketch

```
Person U (chain member: user_id, role, scope — same as ES)
├─ device key D1 (laptop)      cap = (owner, all)            ← the first key aboard via add_member / genesis (payload unchanged)
├─ device key D2 (phone)       cap = (owner, listed{})       ← add_device (signed by D1). Receives no DEKs = a "ballot-only device"
├─ device key D3 (Codespace)   cap = (member, listed{dev})   ← add_device (signed by D1). Can read/write dev only
└─ reserve key R               cap = (owner, all)            ← add_device (signed by D1). Private key lives only in the §8 ledger

Chain (one per project — unchanged)
  genesis      U   D1's enc / sig                            scope = all
  add_device   U → R    [enc, sig, role_cap=owner, scope=all]           ← actor = (U, FP(D1)). Only the person can add their own devices
  add_device   U → D2   [enc, sig, role_cap=owner, scope=listed{}]      ← new device's cap ≤ signing device's cap (monotonicity)
  add_device   U → D3   [enc, sig, role_cap=member, scope=listed{dev}]
  revoke_device U  [FP(D1)]                                            ← actor = (U, FP(D2)) — revoke the lost laptop from the phone. D1 leaves R(E),
                                                                          D1's subsequent signatures are invalid. Duty = rotate of D1's effective scope (= all)

R(E) = { (m, d) | m ∈ current members, d ∈ devices(m), E ∈ scope(m) ∩ scope(d) } ∪ { g ∈ valid grant_server | E ∈ scope_environments(g) }
  R(prod) = { (U,D1), (U,R), … }        ← prod wraps addressed to D2 (empty scope) / D3 (dev only) are neither generated nor accepted
  R(dev)  = { (U,D1), (U,R), (U,D3), … }

Signer identification = (user_id, key FP) — unchanged. FP points to a device. The existing "verify with the key whose FP matches and which was bound to that user_id at that time" (§5.1 / §6.3-1 / §6.6)
holds verbatim when read as "the device key that was valid at that time (after add_device, before revoke_device)".
The four-eyes ballot S element (user_id, key FP) is also unchanged. distinct is counted by user_id (another device of the same person is one ballot). A revoked device's ballot is revoked and can be re-cast from another device.

§8 ledger (KL3 — structure, AAD, segments, and the handoff request / approval payloads unchanged)
  B = the blob of reserve key R (formerly: the master key's blob — same JSON shape)
  Recipients: recovery-code / passkey-prf / guardian (seal segments to **each device key** of the guardian)
  Handoff: requester = the person, approver = guardian only (the old device's approval `kind = "device"` / `source = "device"` is no longer needed and is removed — everyday devices do not hold B)

Adding a device (no ceremony, no secret movement)
  New device: login → `device add` → generate a device key → register the public key as a request row → display the FP (12 words / hex)
  Existing device: `device approve <FP>` → verify the request row's public key against the FP → add_device to every project + backfill all epochs' DEKs to the new device
  (What is carried is the FP = public information. Even if the server swaps the public key, the FP check fails — per the FP-strength premise of §3)
```

The path for a person who lost a device (one path): on another device, `maruhi device revoke "MacBook"` → `revoke_device` to every project → performable rotates happen on the spot (sweep) → for non-performable ones (projects where they are a reader), show who should rotate → revoke the lost device's API tokens as well. If there is no CLI device, revoke the token on the Web (immediate cut-off) → restore the reserve key with `key recover` → the path above.

### 1-4. What does not change (checking the constraints)

- **Crypto primitives, HPKE info, and AAD are unchanged**: the DEK-wrap info (§5 — down to `recipient_user_id`) does not include the device. Two devices of the same person have different keys, so HPKE already refuses a wrap addressed to A being opened by B. The registration signature (§5.1) already binds `recipient_enc_pub_hex`, which pins the device. §8's AAD / info, XOR segments, and the handoff `handoff-wrap` info are also unchanged (`device` merely disappears from `kind`'s value set)
- **"Only humans sign"**: device-key holders are persons. The handling of machine members (server keys, workloads) is unchanged (ruling DK-K)
- **No provider IDs are written to the chain or audit**: a device's display name ("MacBook") does not go on the chain. actor stays (user_id, key FP) — FP points to a device
- **Semantics live inside the signed bytes (§1 principle 6)**: a device's cap (role cap, scope) is an LP field of the `add_device` payload. Display names and the token correspondence sit outside the signature (in the registry — advisory) and are not used by verification rules
- **ES's scope, principle 1, and PF1's principle 2 remain premises**: a device's effective authority is the min / ∩ against the person's authority and never exceeds it. Four-eyes distinct is by user_id; ballots are (user_id, FP)
- **The `add_member` / `change_role` / `genesis` payloads are unchanged**: the byte layout of ES's `chain-entries.json` canonical chain (seq 1–24) does not change. New ops are appended at the end (the same "extend by appending" pattern as when the §6.2 `checkpoint` op was added)
- **The invitation shape (IV1 / IV2) is unchanged**: the accepting key = the accepting device's key. `key publish` is done per device to the backing source (GitHub signing keys) (GitHub can hold multiple signing keys). The issue/acceptance texts' LPs are untouched
- **Web holds no keys (ADR-0018)**: the device list is read-only (server-declared); device add/revoke are chain ops, so CLI only. What Web can do on a loss is the existing token revocation (a direction that reduces authority — within the boundary of revision 2)
- **Old clients are fail-closed**: `add_device` / `revoke_device` are rejected in chain verification as unknown ops (`invalid-payload`). No compat path

---

## 2. Iteration record of the rulings

At each ruling point the §5 procedure was run (enumerate 3+ candidates → the upward-compat / silver-bullet round → one more round after any round that produced a new option, stop after 2 consecutive empty rounds → extract principles and confirm that existing rulings still derive → UX check → adopt). The round record keeps, one line each, "what was tried, what came out, what did not" (per the es-design.md §12-bis correction — making a candidate table and writing "empty round" does not count as exploration). Round counts are recorded honestly. Points where the adopted option changes a canonical rule were drafted into docs/notes/dk-spec-drafts.md immediately after the ruling (with the ruling number alongside).

### Ruling DK-A: the unit and location of keys — what is it we want to revoke (1 enumeration round + a problem-framing round + a substantive third round + 2 empty rounds, stopped)

| Option | Content | Evaluation (what is gained and lost) |
|---|---|---|
| A-1 A ledger recipient class "device" (ROADMAP axis (i)) | Wrap the master key with a per-device key and place it in the §8 ledger. Revocation = deleting that wrap | **Revocation is a no-op**: today's devices hold the master-key **plaintext** in the keychain (the wrap is a delivery path, not a storage form). Deleting the wrap leaves the key on the lost device, and future DEKs are still wrapped to the master key, so it can open them. The cut-off obtained is equivalent to the existing API-token revocation (`DELETE /auth/tokens/:id`). The "devices don't hold master-key plaintext" shape is sealing (device-key-sealing.md option 3), but that is **protection**, not revocation — it does nothing for memory during unsealing or for a live compromise. Rejected |
| A-2 **Put per-device keys on the chain — member = person, keys = a set of devices** (the "person" version of axis (ii)) | Chain members (user_id, role, scope) are unchanged. A member's keys become a **set**, grown and shrunk via `add_device` / `revoke_device`. The FP of signer identification (user_id, key FP) selects the device. R(E) is a set of (person, device) pairs | "Invalidate only the keys that device held" becomes expressible in crypto (future DEKs are not wrapped to it; future signatures are invalid). The duty is isomorphic to remove. Most existing rules are written as "select the key by FP" and generalize unchanged. Cost: fan-out (recipients = device count), device add/revoke are per-project chain ops (N projects = N entries), and the `ChainMember` type change reaches every consumer |
| A-3 device = member row (the "device" version of axis (ii)) | (user_id, key) becomes a member row; multiple rows with the same user_id are allowed. role / scope are per row | The unit "person" disappears: `duplicate-member`, last owner, four-eyes distinct, invitation acceptance, the membership interval of needs-rotation detection, and ES's inclusion rule all presuppose "person"; per-row role/scope divergence would have to be re-handled in each rule. Devices "never exceed the person's scope" (ES §6 carryover), so the person comes first. Rejected |
| A-4 Two layers: an identity key (root) lives only in the ledger, and adding a device requires the root's signature | root (sig) is the person's on-chain key; device keys ride on certificates signed by root. root is not placed on everyday devices | Gain: a stolen device cannot add devices (prevents the "illicit device hoarding" that evades revocation). Loss: every device add opens the ledger (code entry / passkey / guardian) = abandoning KL3 handoff's gain (one approval on an old device). Under live compromise (malware) the attacker can wait for the moment root is unsealed, so the gain is limited to "theft (T2)", and most of it is recovered by the third round's monotonic cap and bulk revocation. Rejected |
| A-5 A per-user device chain (a signed key log) referenced from the project chains (silver-bullet candidate) | Write device add/revoke once to a per-person side chain and it applies to every project | Every project's verifier must fetch and verify **every member's device chain** (cross-chain dependency). Split-view surfaces multiply by member count, availability couples, and anchors / checkpoints are needed per person. Same scale as a key-transparency log (supplement 17), and the argument that ruled KT "unnecessary" (the project chain is already the key ledger) applies verbatim. Rejected (same shelf as a future organizational key ledger / public witnesses) |

**Round 1 (enumeration)**: A-1–A-3. A-1 fails on the definition of "revocation"; A-3 breaks the "person" unit. A-2 remains. **Round 2 (ask how the problem is framed — what is it we want to revoke)**: decomposed a device's capabilities — (a) open DEKs (enc), (b) sign (sig — values, meta, chain ops, ballots), (c) open recovery (the ledger). Option (α) device keys always hold both enc and sig (same shape as today); option (β) a device holds only one of them (an enc-only "reading device" / a sig-only "approving device" — key uniqueness is per key kind, so it is structurally possible); option (γ) **give a device a cap of (role cap, scope) and express effective authority = (min(person's role, cap), person's scope ∩ device's scope)**. (γ) is an upward-compatible superset of (β): a sig-only approving device ≒ cap (owner, listed{}) (a phone that receives no DEKs and only casts ballots); an enc-only CI box ≒ cap (reader, listed{dev}). Capability separation is gained while keeping a single key shape, and no check has to special-case "a device missing a key". (c) is the **reserve key** (a device key that lives only in the ledger — a re-reading of B's meaning), which avoids creating a separate key kind. The new options (γ) + reserve key emerged. **Round 3 (upward compat — obtain A-4's benefit inside A-2)**: "a stolen device cannot add devices" is recovered by (i) **monotonicity** (new device's cap ≤ the **signing device's own** cap — not compared on effective authority, which folds in the person's role. Comparing on effective authority would prevent a member from registering a reserve key with cap (owner, all). Corrected per a 2026-09-19 Cursor Bugbot finding) and (ii) **bulk revocation** (the `revoke_device` payload carries a list of FPs — the revoker can pick the devices the lost device created before revocation from a list and revoke them in the same entry). A stolen phone (cap (owner, listed{})) can only create empty-scope devices, and the devices a stolen laptop created are visible on the chain with "who added them" (actor FP). New options (monotonicity, bulk revocation). **Round 4 (silver bullet)**: A-5 device chain → rejected (table above). "No reserve key; losing all devices = re-invite in every project" → projects with a single owner die, and we lose what KL3's guardian path protects. Rejected. "reserve key = keep the existing master key only in the ledger" → a restatement of the adopted structure (not a new option). **Empty round**. **Round 5**: "don't hand history epochs to a device (current epoch only)" → the revocation duty (rotating the current epoch) does not change, and a device that cannot read history has little use (§7 "secrets do not aim to conceal history"). Rejected. "an op that changes cap later (`set_device_cap`)" → a new op can be appended later (it entails no payload-format change), so we do not have it now — carried over. "make device add invitation-style (a link)" → handled in DK-D. No new options → **empty round**. Stopped after two consecutive empty rounds.

**Principle extraction**: **"keys belong to devices; authority belongs to persons"** — authority (role, scope, ballot distinct) is counted by user_id; key material and its revocation are handled per device. The signer is identified as (user_id, key FP); FP selects the device. A device's effective authority never exceeds the person's (min / ∩). Confirmation that the pre-replacement existing rulings still derive from this principle: ES rulings A–N (scope is a person's attribute ✓ / R(E) replaces "person × scope" with "(person, device) × effective scope" ✓ / the inclusion rule judges on the actor **device's** effective scope ✓ / G's visibility is the person's scope ✓), PF1 P1–P8 (distinct owner = user_id ✓ / the ballot's (user_id, FP) binding = device binding ✓ / `proposal-void` key FP = device ✓ / the standing target = additions of owner **identities**, and device adds are not included ✓), K2-11 ②⑤ / K5-J (ballots are signatures made with the owner role; revocation is not monotonic ✓ — the same in device vocabulary), KL3 19-2 A (ledger = per-recipient wraps to the same B — only B's meaning changes to reserve key ✓) / D・E (handoff remains on the guardian path ✓) / G ("old device = preregistration is meaningless" — under DK, the old device's approval itself is unnecessary ✓), IV supplement 21 (the accepting key = the accepting device's key ✓), ADR-0018 (Web holds no keys ✓), §5.1's signer FP selection ✓, §6.2 key uniqueness (per kind, current member set → all devices of current members ✓), §6.3-1's "the membership-interval key" (→ the device's validity interval ✓). No ✗.

**UX check**: new device = 2 commands: `device add` on the new device (shows the FP) + `device approve <FP>` on a device at hand (verify + yes); no reading out of 12 words (the FP travels between one's own devices); no secret movement. Loss = one command, `device revoke <name>`. The owner's invite/approve procedures are unchanged. Agent environments: approve is a ceremony (denied); add (the requesting side) has no gate. **Adopted: A-2 + cap + reserve key + monotonicity + bulk revocation**. Whether to expose cap in the v1 CLI (`device approve --cap / --env`) is an owner choice (approval item 2). → drafts A-1 / A-3.

### Ruling DK-B: revocation semantics, the §7 duty, the performer, loss of all devices (1 enumeration round + 2 empty rounds, stopped)

| Option | Content | Evaluation |
|---|---|---|
| B-1 **Revocation = the device version of remove** | For each device revoked via `revoke_device`, a `rotate_epoch` duty on every environment in its **effective scope** (person's scope ∩ device's scope) (the §7 MUST format). Performer = the actor who signed the revocation (another device of the same person, or an admin / owner who revoked someone else's device). Only for **reader self-revocation** can the actor not rotate (insufficient role) — the duty is shown as "unassigned" to every member-or-above covering the scope via needs-rotation detection (`rotation.recommended` trigger = `revoke_device`) and `project verify`'s unconverged warning, and the CLI tells the revoking person "ask someone to rotate" | The duty's shape is identical to remove (§7), demotion, narrowing (F-1), and revoke_server. A device never exceeds the person's scope (ES §6 carryover), so the environment set of DEKs the device could open is closed by the effective scope |
| B-2 Reason-dependent (`retired` = no duty / `lost` = duty) | Exempts the rotate for revocations of disposed devices | There is no proof of disposal — fail-open ("write 'disposed' and the duty disappears"). The operating cost of ephemeral environments (Codespaces recreation) drops, but that is the domain of delegation / forwarding agents (DK-H). Rejected — also listed as an owner choice in approval item 4 |
| B-3 Reject reader self-revocation in the consensus rules (ask an admin) | Do not create unperformable duties | It blocks a safe-side operation (revocation) — rejected by the same argument as ES ruling E ("rotate is incident response and must not be delayed"). Revocation has value in removing the device from R(E) immediately |
| B-4 Rotate only the environments the device **actually read** (derived from audit `var.read` actor key FPs) | Minimizes the duty | "No record of reading" ≠ "couldn't read" (offline decryption with already-obtained DEKs; audit under server distrust is forgeable). It is used for the (a)/(b) ranking in detection (AUDIT §4.1) — per-device "definitely obtained" becomes sayable (DK-F) — but not for the duty's **scope**. Rejected |

**The all-devices-lost path**: `key recover` (recovery code / passkey / guardian — KL3's three paths; the old-device approval path becomes unnecessary — DK-G) restores the reserve key → `add_device` a new device key with the reserve key (cap (owner, all)) (monotonicity allows creating any cap) → the reserve key opens its own DEK wraps and backfills → erase the reserve-key secret from the device (the ledger stays as-is) → `revoke_device` the lost devices (rotate). A project where no reserve key is registered (the device was joined before reserve-key registration was done — carried by DK-D's first-sync registration) needs re-invitation.

**Round 2 (upward compat)**: "two-stage revocation (soft = only remove from R(E) / hard = with duty)" → soft is isomorphic to the pretend-narrowing "make DEKs the server already knows appear undisclosed" (the reason §6.3's re-grant rule rejects narrowing). Rejected. "Exclude epochs the revoked device didn't obtain from rotate targets" → same reason as B-4. Rejected. **Empty round**. **Round 3 (silver bullet)**: "on the device that restored the reserve key, keep the reserve key as the everyday key and create a new reserve key" → the entire ledger (code, passkey, guardian groups) must be rebuilt. Rejected — after restore, erase the reserve-key secret and keep the ledger as-is (residual: if the device used for restore is compromised, the reserve key may be compromised too → provide `key reserve rotate` [register new reserve key + revoke old reserve key = rotate]. DK-L). "Instead of revoking, shrink the device's cap to empty" → narrowing ≈ remove-equivalent (F-1); the duty is the same. Not a new option. **Empty round**. Stopped.

**Principle extraction**: "revocation = removing a recipient from R(E); duty = rotate the removed recipient's effective scope; performer = the actor who signed the removal" — remove (§7) ✓, demotion (§7 — motivated by anchor health, but the scope is the same) ✓, narrowing (F-1) ✓, revoke_server (all environments — a server key's effective scope is the disclosure scope, but revision stays outside ES's scope) ✓, performer via four-eyes = approver (P8) ✓. Reader self-revocation is "the only revocation the actor cannot perform" — handled as a corollary of E (don't stop rotates) by "not stopping it, but showing it". No ✗.

**UX check**: `maruhi device revoke MacBook` → `Revoked in 4 projects. Rotated: alpha (dev, prod), beta (dev). Needs a member or owner to rotate: gamma (you are a reader there) — ask them to run maruhi env rotate.` Non-interactive is fine (revocation is not a ceremony — it displays neither values nor key material; it passes in agent environments too). **Adopted: B-1**. → drafts A-8 / C-3.

### Ruling DK-C: intersection with four-eyes (PF1) — ballots, duplicates, void (1 enumeration round + 2 empty rounds, stopped)

| Option | Content | Evaluation |
|---|---|---|
| C-1 **Read principle 2 in device vocabulary** | S's element (user_id, key FP) is unchanged (FP = the signing device). A ballot counts when "at apply time, that FP is a **valid device** of a current owner and the device's effective role is owner". distinct is by user_id (another device of the same person is one ballot). `duplicate-approval` = the actor's user_id already holds a **live ballot** in S (a ballot by a valid device) (same device or another). A revoked device's ballot is revoked and can be re-cast from another device. `proposal-void` = the proposing device is valid, the proposer is a current member, and its effective role suffices for the inner op. "Revocation is not monotonic" (K5-J) is the same for devices: if the same person re-registers a revoked device key via `add_device`, the old ballots revive (a consequence — the CLI warns on re-registering a revoked FP = kin to K6-I). `add_device` / `revoke_device` **cannot be four-eyes targets** (a device add does not increase user_id and does not affect quorum; revocation is safe-side) | Only the phrase "a current member holding the same key FP" in principle 2's wording generalizes to "a current member holding that FP as a valid device"; the rule's structure is unchanged. A phone with cap (owner, listed{}) can cast ballots while holding no DEKs (the practical gain of capability separation) |
| C-2 Do not treat or count a second ballot from another device of the same person as a duplicate | Simpler rule | The audit gains "approvals that aren't ballots", muddying the letter of "one person one ballot". K6's pending display ("you can approve") would mislead on another device. Rejected |
| C-3 Keep a revoked device's ballots (see device revocation ≠ key compromise) | Less cleanup after revocation | Revocation declares "this key may have been compromised", which contradicts the K2-11 ⑤ argument (on key update, the old key's ballots are revoked). Rejected |
| C-4 Make `add_device` an owner-establishment op that is a standing four-eyes target | Two owners watch the growth of an owner's keys | Invariant (a) protects "adding an identity that satisfies quorum"; a device does not increase user_id (ballots are distinct user_id). Requiring 2 owners for an owner's device add means a single owner who enabled four-eyes cannot add their own device afterward (four-eyes needed even with the policy off — unrecoverable). Rejected |

**Round 2 (upward compat)**: "approve from a cap < owner device → `insufficient-role`" → a consequence of the effective-role substitution rule (DK-A), not a new option. "Make `revoke_device` where an owner revokes another's device a four-eyes target" → revocation is safe-side (same rank as rotate — don't delay it). Rejected. "Add the device's add seq to S's element to close revival by re-registering the same key" → same as K2-11-bis ⑤ d (what it closes is only cleanup after operational failure; it adds state). Rejected. **Empty round**. **Round 3**: no new options. **Empty round**. Stopped.

**Principle extraction**: "S's element is identified by the signing device; a ballot counts only when, at apply time, that device is a valid device of a current owner. distinct is persons" — P4 (approver = owner, distinct) ✓, P5 (ballot recomputation, currency) ✓, K2-11 ② (signatures made with the owner role) ✓, ⑤ (revoked on key update; survives under the same key) ✓, K5-J (the non-monotonic note) ✓, P3's standing target (identity additions only) ✓. No ✗.

**UX check**: an approver can approve from any device at hand (one ballot). Approving on a phone (empty scope) → it holds no DEKs on the spot, so the post-apply duties (sweep / backfill) cannot be performed on the phone → add to K6-D's performer discipline: "if the approving device has no DEKs, performance carries over to another device of the same person or to the next sync" (the performer stays the same person — handled in CLI guidance. K4 carryover). **Adopted: C-1**. → draft A-3 (the four-eyes ballot wording).

### Ruling DK-D: device-add authorization, ceremony, backing source, uniqueness, first-sync registration (1 enumeration round + 2 empty rounds, stopped)

| Option | Content | Evaluation |
|---|---|---|
| D-1 **Self only · the FP is carried by a human · no ceremony** | The `add_device` actor = the target person themselves (the payload carries no target — one can only add one's own devices). Role doesn't matter (readers may too — appended to the role table). A new device's cap ≤ the signing device's own cap (`device-cap-exceeded` — a comparison between caps; not compared on effective authority). Key uniqueness extends to the same-kind keys of **all devices** of current members (`duplicate-member-key`). The device-count cap is an acceptance policy (16 / member / project — `DeviceLimit`). **No ceremony**: the new device registers its public key as a request row (AUTH — DK-F·B-7) and displays the FP (12 words / hex); an existing device verifies the request row's public key against the FP, appends `add_device` to every project, and backfills. What is carried is the FP = public information. If the server swaps the public key, the FP check fails (per §3's FP-strength premise [128 bit · second preimage] — the attacker may choose one side's key, but the FP is carried between the person's own devices and cannot be tampered with). The approving side does verify + yes (TTY gate); the requesting side has no gate. Backing source (IV2): the device that **accepts** the invitation does `key publish` (GitHub can hold multiple signing keys; other devices need not publish). Fingerprint book (KF): generalize (origin, user_id) → a **set** of FPs (an unknown FP of a known person = a new device is treated the same as a "key change" = warning + ceremony) | Separates invitations (third-party identity vouching) from adding one's own device (the person growing their own keys). Carries no secret (in contrast to KL3 handoff, which carried master-key plaintext) |
| D-2 admin / owner can also add other people's devices | Admins could provision devices | An admin creating a key in someone else's name = impersonation (value signatures and ballots would stand in that person's name). Rejected |
| D-3 Invitation-style (the new device joins via an invite link, confirmed via IV1 / IV2 or 12 words) | Reuses existing machinery | Inviting yourself gains no third-party vouching (inviter = yourself) and adds one ceremony. Rejected |
| D-4 The KL3-handoff shape (carry E.pub by code; the approval is returned to the requester via the server) | Reuses the existing request / approval APIs | There is nothing to return (no secret travels — the new device makes its own key, and DEKs arrive via the normal backfill). Request rows + FP verification suffice (a strict subset). Rejected |
| D-5 Proof-of-possession for the new device (co-signing — isomorphic to invitation acceptance) | Prevents misregistration | Contributes nothing to safety (the person merely chooses to whom they grant their own authority; registering someone else's public key as your device only lets that person act "as you" — the same as leaking your DEK). Accident prevention is carried by FP verification. Rejected |

**Device registration on first sync (reserve key and other devices to a new project)**: `add_member` carries the single key of the accepting device (payload unchanged). The person's other devices (including the reserve key) are added via `add_device` + backfill **when a device of that person first syncs the project**. The keys the CLI may add are limited to three: (1) keys it generated itself (the reserve key), (2) keys it approved via `device approve`, (3) keys observed as that person's devices on a verified chain — and they are **never added from the unsigned server-supplied list (the registry)** (if the registry were the source of truth, the server could insert a fake public key and have the CLI append a ghost device). (1)–(3) are held in non-secret local state (same family as pins).

**Round 2 (upward compat)**: "carry the public key itself (64 bytes) by code instead of the FP" → the FP's 128-bit second-preimage resistance suffices (the same premise as §3's 12 words), and the existing display forms (12 words / hex) are reused. It only doubles the length. Rejected. "No request row; put the whole public key into the code (server uninvolved)" → the same length problem. Rejected. "Bundle first-sync registration into the inviter's `member add` (the inviter adds the accepter's device set)" → changes the `add_member` payload (regenerating ES) + the inviter has no means to verify the accepter's other devices. Rejected. **Empty round**. **Round 3**: "optionally attach backing-source verification to `add_device` (also verify the person's other devices' sig keys on GitHub)" → little point in querying GitHub when the person approves their own device, and it adds one more outbound-send situation to the "never-tell" rule. Rejected. No new options. **Empty round**. Stopped.

**Principle extraction**: "only the person who acts with a key can grow that person's keys, and a person can only create devices at or below themselves (monotonicity). Those who can shrink are the person and anyone who can remove the person" — the difference from `add_member` (growing persons = third-party-vouched invitations) is "growing keys of the same person". P3's standing target (owner-identity adds) ✓ — devices don't add identities. IV supplement 21 (backing the accepting key) ✓ — limited to the accepting device's key. KF (another FP of a known counterparty goes back to a ceremony) ✓. §6.5 "no global public-key directory" ✓ — the registry is read only by the person and is not used in verification. No ✗.

**UX check**: new device: `maruhi login` → `maruhi device add --label "Work MacBook"` (shows the FP as 12 words + hex and waits). Existing device: `maruhi device approve <hex or 12 words>` → `Register "Work MacBook" (FP …) as your device in 4 projects? [yes]` → append + backfill → the new device syncs and is done. 0 ceremonies; non-interactive is impossible only on the approve side (ceremony gate). **Adopted: D-1**. → drafts A-3 / A-6 / B-7.

### Ruling DK-E: R(E), fan-out, acceptance policy, §12-6 recipient identification, reader self-backfill (1 enumeration round + 2 empty rounds, stopped)

| Option | Content | Evaluation |
|---|---|---|
| E-1 **Expand R(E) into (person, device) pairs; identification = (user_id, device enc public key)** | R(E) = { (m, d) \| E ∈ scope(m) ∩ scope(d) } ∪ grants. §12-6's recipient identification "both user_id and enc public key" is unchanged — now both are truly required. Add `recipient_enc_pub_hex` to the dek_wraps slot primary key (current PK = (environment_id, epoch, recipient_user_id)). Exact-match, append-missing, no-overwrite (409), and the repair path are unchanged. Cleanup: the old-key-addressed wrap cleanup on `add_member` re-add generalizes to "keys not in the new device set (= 1 key)"; no deletion on `revoke_device` / `remove_member` (revival by re-registering the same key — existing discipline). The sixth registration path = `add_device` backfill (all epochs of the same person's devices to the new device). **Reader self-backfill**: if every recipient is one's own device key, even a reader may register DEK wraps (a row added to §12-3) — the reader legitimately holds that DEK, and rewrapping to their own keys grows no one else's capability. HPKE info is unchanged (down to `recipient_user_id` — two devices of the same person have different keys, so HPKE refuses transplantation; the §5.1 registration signature already binds `recipient_enc_pub_hex`) | Adds "effective scope" to the one predicate (K3-D's principle). Fan-out is recipients = device count (5 persons × 3 devices × environments × epochs), three orders of magnitude under the 10,000/request and 1,000,000 rows/project caps |
| E-2 Don't wrap DEKs per device; wrap to a single "person's enc key" that devices open | Fan-out unchanged | Every device holding the person's enc secret key = today's replication, verbatim. Rejected |
| E-3 Introduce a per-environment intermediate key (env KEK — ES A-6); wrap DEKs to the KEK and the KEK to devices | Reduces the HPKE count via a layer | Adds one layer, and device revocation also rotates the KEK (same work as rotate). With the actual fan-out small, there is nothing to gain. Rejected |

**Round 2 (upward compat)**: "don't register reserve-key-addressed wraps in peacetime; backfill at restore" → at restore all other devices are gone and there is nobody to backfill (the reserve key itself holds no DEKs). The reserve key must be in R(E) from peacetime. Rejected. "Make the device-count cap a consensus rule" → fan-out consumes server-storage resources, not verifier resources; the acceptance policy suffices (same character as pending proposal 32). Not a new option. **Empty round**. **Round 3**: no new options. **Empty round**. Stopped.

**Principle extraction**: "R(E) membership is the single predicate 'identification (id + key) ∧ E ∈ effective scope' across recipient classes" (adds "effective" to K3-D) — the member-side 2 reasons + `scope-out-of-range`, the server-side 3 reasons ✓ derive. "The performer of a wrap = a DEK holder" (§7) ✓ — a person's devices hold the DEKs. No ✗.

**UX check**: a `pull` right after adding a device prints "no DEK wraps for you" only while the approving device's backfill hasn't finished (add "re-run `device approve`" to the wording of K4-K's warning). **Adopted: E-1**. → drafts A-4 / B-4 / B-5 / B-6.

### Ruling DK-F: where it lives (chain op or ledger API), actor identification, AUDIT (1 enumeration round + 2 empty rounds, stopped)

Judgment criterion (§3 6): **does it affect other members' verification (does R(E) change)**. Device add/revoke changes R(E), and since every member verifies wrap-target agreement (§6.3), device keys become ghost keys unless they are **chain ops**.

| Option | Content | Evaluation |
|---|---|---|
| F-1 **Chain ops (`add_device` / `revoke_device`) + an advisory registry** | The source of truth is the chain. AUTH carries a "device registry" (display names, token correspondence, a place for add-request public keys) as **advisory**, unused by verification (D-1's 3 conditions) | Everyone can verify R(E), signature validity, and ballots. The registry is non-secret meta of the same character as token names (§6) |
| F-2 Ledger API (a user resource, off-chain) | One add applies to all projects | Other members cannot verify device keys = ghost keys (contradicts §6.3's ghost-member countermeasure itself). Rejected |
| F-3 Chain ops but no registry | Minimal | No way to see a device's display name from another device (you would pick the revocation target by its FP's 12 words), and no path (request row) to carry a new device's public key to an approving device. Rejected |

**actor identification**: unchanged — (internal user_id, key FP). Since FP points to a device, "should the device FP go on the actor?" is answered by "it is already there". One note sentence is added to audit (AUDIT §2). **Mirror**: `chain.device_added` (payload = { deviceKeyFingerprint, roleCap, scopeKind, scopeEnvironmentIds }) / `chain.device_revoked` ★ (target_user_id = the subject, payload = { deviceKeyFingerprints }). One entry, one row (bijection invariant). **The `revoke_device` variant of needs-rotation detection (§4.1)**: procedure 1's interval = each revoked device's validity interval (`chain.device_added` through `chain.device_revoked`; the first key from `add_member` / `genesis` counts from the start of the membership interval) ∩ the person's per-environment access window ∩ the device's scope. Procedure 3's (a) = rows of `var.read` within the membership interval where **actor_key_fingerprint ∈ the revoked FP set** (per-device "definitely obtained" becomes sayable — more precise than today's user_id matching). trigger = `revoke_device`. Q1's index (target_user_id, seq) is unchanged (the payload carries FPs). **Visibility**: class 1 (`chain.*`).

**Round 2 (upward compat)**: "put the device FP on the actor as a separate field" → the existing FP is the device. Not a new option. "Make `chain.device_added` ★ too" → an add is not a detection trigger (it is read as the window's start). Rejected. "Make registry changes audit events" → a ledger of self-information that touches no key material (same discipline as the token list — §6). Rejected. **Empty round**. **Round 3**: no new options. **Empty round**. Stopped.

**Principle extraction**: "facts that change R(E) are chain ops; facts that don't (display names, requests) are advisory" — ES A-3 (rejection of the data-plane policy) ✓, the KL3 ledger (doesn't change R(E) = off-chain) ✓, head declarations (mutable, off-chain) ✓, environment display names (§4.2 statements, off-chain) ✓. No ✗.

**UX check**: `device list` lays out "my on-chain devices (registration status per project) + the registry's display names". Web can show the same list read-only as a server declaration. **Adopted: F-1**. → drafts B-7 (§13-11) / C-1–C-5.

### Ruling DK-G: integration with the KL3 ledger — B, `kind = "device"`, guardian segments, opening on ledger change (1 enumeration round + 2 empty rounds, stopped)

| Option | Content | Evaluation |
|---|---|---|
| G-1 **B = the reserve key's blob; remove the old-device handoff path; guardian segments go to each of the guardian's device keys** | B's JSON shape (suite / encPub / encSk / sigPub / sigSkSeed) is unchanged; its contents become the reserve key. `kind` ∈ {`passkey-prf`, `guardian`} (remove `device` — everyday devices don't hold B, so the "an old device passes B" approval cannot stand). Remove `source = "device"` and the approval row's blob column. Guardian segments: seal the same s_i to **each current device key** of the guardian (row = (group_id, share_index, guardian_user_id, guardian_key_fingerprint)). STALE determination is matched against the chain-derived device set (if a guardian's device is revoked or added, the ward recreates — existing discipline). **Ledger changes (adding a passkey, adding a guardian, re-issuing a code) require opening the reserve key** (code entry or passkey) — until now it was unconditional because the master key was at hand. On the first `key generate`, generate the device key and the reserve key together, display the recovery code on the spot, and optionally finish passkey sealing too | The ledger's structure, AAD, segments, and handoff request/approval payloads are unchanged. Naming collisions resolve by removal |
| G-2 No reserve key; B = each device key's blob (sealed per device) | Doesn't add the concept of a reserve key | On losing all devices, what remains are only the keys that should be revoked. Rejected |
| G-3 Also place the reserve-key secret on everyday devices (B = the source of replicas = current) | Ledger changes complete on-device | Losing a device loses the reserve key too, erasing DK's purpose. Rejected |
| G-4 Seal guardian segments to the guardian's **reserve key** (stable, device-independent) | Wards needn't follow the guardian's device churn | The guardian must open their own reserve key on every approval (code entry). Guardian approval's requirement is the lightness of "confirm identity by voice → yes". Rejected |

**Round 2 (upward compat)**: "place only the reserve key's **public key** on devices for ledger changes" → the public key is held anyway for `add_device`. Wrap generation needs the secret key; not a new option. "Place an indirect KEK, wrapped under the reserve key's secret, on devices" → a device holding the KEK can open B = the same as the reserve key living on the device. Rejected. "Guardian segments to just one of the guardian's devices (chosen by the ward)" → sealing to all devices is superior in availability (multiple seals of the same s_i are permitted by §8.3's info [contains no device]). Rejected. **Empty round**. **Round 3**: "allow `key seal passkey` only at reserve-key generation (no later adds)" → shuts out people who buy an authenticator later. Rejected (open and add). No new options. **Empty round**. Stopped.

**Principle extraction**: "what the ledger guards is the single 'key not placed on devices', and the ledger's recipients are device keys (guardians) or off-device secrets (codes, PRFs)" — KL3 19-2 A (the 3 types) ✓, B・C (KEK derivation, XOR) ✓, D・E (handoff = guardian approval) ✓, F (guardian keys = chain-derived — to the set of device keys) ✓, G (old-device preregistration is meaningless → the old device's approval itself is unnecessary) ✓, 19-3 (c) agent TTL (re-acquisition becomes another device's `device approve`, not `key recover` — reserve-key opening leaves the everyday path) ✓ — the post-TTL guidance wording is revisited in K4. No ✗.

**UX check**: first run: `key generate` → device key + reserve key → show the code (+ passkey guidance). Later `guardian add` → "will open the reserve key: enter code or passkey" → name them. The Codespaces (`/docs/linux-keychain`) procedure becomes "`agent -- bash` → `login` → `device add` (code shown) → `device approve` at hand", and neither a code nor a recovery code is typed (same step count as KL3). **Adopted: G-1**. → drafts A-9 / B-7. Proposed §8 title change: "master key wrap ledger" → "reserve key wrap ledger" (owner choice — approval item 9).

### Ruling DK-H: consequences for the delegation model (KL4) (1 enumeration round + 2 empty rounds, stopped)

The question (§3 8): as a consequence of DK, agents come to hold only device keys — does delegation become structurally cheaper?

| Option | Content | Evaluation |
|---|---|---|
| H-1 **It doesn't (kept as-is) — the motivation shifts from "theft" to "ephemeral"** | The key an agent holds changes from master key to device key, but the ~30 places keys are used and the cost of `MasterKeyOps`-ification are unchanged. The value of delegation's main purpose "persistent master-key theft disappears" is **partially anticipated** by device-key revocability (agent compromise converges as one device's revocation + rotate, never an identity rebuild). Meanwhile in ephemeral environments (devices die on every container recreation), "register device → revoke + effective-scope rotate on every disposal" is heavy, and the value of a forwarding agent (keys never leave the hand) rather rises | No ruling change. Not an approval item; recorded as information |
| H-2 Delegatize simultaneously with DK | Device key = the unit of delegation, two birds one stone | The KL3 19-3 (a) estimate (~30 + 3 places, 1 week) doesn't change under DK and would stretch the beta gate. Relitigating the kept-as-is. Rejected |
| H-3 No duty on ephemeral-device revocation (B-2) | Removes Codespaces friction | Fail-open. Rejected (also listed as an owner choice) |

**Round 2**: "give ephemeral devices a **chain-level expiry** like `--key-ttl`, auto-revoking at expiry" → expiry brings timestamps into the consensus rules (grows P6's exceptions). Rejected. "Codespaces carry the device key across with KL1's persistent keyring; 1 codespace = 1 device" → operational guidance (docs), not a new option. **Empty round**. **Round 3**: no new options. **Empty round**. Stopped.

**Adopted: H-1**. Carryover: when KL4 is re-evaluated, rewrite "the forwarding agent's main use = ephemeral environments".

### Ruling DK-I: relationship to sealing (device-key-sealing.md option 3) (single round — fact check)

Device-key records keep the `StoredMasterKey` JSON shape (suite / encPub / encSk / sigPub / sigSkSeed) unchanged, and the sealing backend (FIDO2 hmac-secret / SE / TPM / passphrase) is a storage layer that wraps this blob. DK does not change the shape, and per-device keys map naturally onto per-device sealing (losing one sealing token = revoking that device suffices). **The reserve key must not be sealed by a device-bound sealer** (it must be restorable on any device — ledger only). Device-key revocation is a chain-side fact and does not depend on the sealer's state. No obstruction. → §5 carryover. Single round (a confirmation, not an exploration target).

### Ruling DK-J: migration — from the existing 1-key model (1 enumeration round + 2 empty rounds, stopped)

| Option | Content | Evaluation |
|---|---|---|
| J-1 **Chain kept as-is; existing master key = the first device key; only the ledger and replica devices get treatment** | Since the `add_member` / `genesis` payloads are unchanged, existing chains are **valid** under the new rules (each member has 1 device). The existing master key K is re-read as device key D1. The ledger's B (= a replica of K) is replaced by a reserve key: generate a new K_r → `add_device(K_r)` to every project → re-seal the ledger with K_r (re-issue codes / re-seal passkeys / recreate guardian groups). Other devices that replicated K each do `device add` → approve → delete that device's replica of K (K itself stays valid as D1). The CLI guides on first launch: "this device's key is identical to the reserve key; split the reserve key" (`key generate --split-reserve` equivalent — K4 CLI discretion) | No destructive change. DK avoids ES A-1's "recreation" consequence (the ES §6 carryover concern [two regenerations] does not arise) |
| J-2 Recreate the verification deploy (same consequence as ES A-1) | No treatment needed | Needless destruction. Rejected (also listed in approval item 11 as the fallback for the case the owner judges "rebuilding is faster") |
| J-3 Re-read the existing K as the reserve key; new keys on devices | Device keys are "new" from the start | K is already replicated to every device and does not satisfy the "lives only in the ledger" premise (a replica you meant to delete may remain). Rejected |

**Round 2**: "enforce migration in the consensus rules (invalidate members with 1 device)" → 1 device is a legitimate state (a project may have no reserve key registered). Rejected. "don't auto-split the old CLI's K into a reserve key (explicit user operation only)" → guidance + explicit operation is included in J-1 (no automatic key creation). Not a new option. **Empty round**. **Round 3**: no new options. **Empty round**. Stopped.

**Adopted: J-1**. Migration order: server (K3) → every member's CLI (K4) → each person's reserve-key separation and replica-device registration. Old CLIs reject chains containing the 2 ops with `invalid-payload` (fail-closed). → SELF_HOSTING "Updates" rides in K3's PR (the ES K2 precedent).

### Ruling DK-K: principals without devices · Web (single round — fact check)

- **Server keys (`grant_server`)**: recipient class server is unchanged (FP definition, disclosure scope, lease policy, re-grant rules). The grant side of R(E) does not change
- **CI leases (§9.1)**: workload ephemeral keys have response scope and are neither devices nor members. Unchanged
- **Web (ADR-0018)**: holds no keys, so device add/revoke (chain ops) cannot be placed there. What can be placed is a **read** of the device list (the registry = a server declaration — add `GET /auth/devices` to the session-permission enumeration) and the existing token revocation (a direction that reduces authority — within revision 2's boundary). For a person with no CLI at hand after a loss, this is the first step (token revocation immediately stops new acquisition via the API; then `key recover` → `device revoke`)
- **`maruhi ui` (second stage)**: device approval (a ceremony) stays on the TTY (ADR-0018 revision 1, item 2)

→ already stated in §1-4 "what does not change". Single round.

### Ruling DK-L: CLI naming and paths (1 enumeration round + 2 empty rounds, stopped)

| Option | Content | Evaluation |
|---|---|---|
| L-1 **New `maruhi device` group** | `device add [--label]` (on the new device; key generation + request row + FP display + wait) / `device approve <fp\|words> [--cap <role>] [--env …]` (on an existing device; verify + yes + append to every project + backfill) / `device list [--project]` (my on-chain devices and per-project registration status + display names; zero values = agent-gate not applied) / `device revoke <fp-prefix\|label> [--user <id>] [--project]` (revoke to every project + sweep + show the unperformable + propose revoking the bound tokens). `key` stays "my keys": `key generate` (device key; plus a reserve key on first run) / `key show` (device FP and reserve-key FP) / `key publish` (this device's sig key) / `key recover [--passkey\|--handoff]` (restore the reserve key → issue a device key → destroy the reserve-key secret) / `key recovery` (re-issue a code = re-seal the reserve key) / `key seal …` (passkey-seal the reserve key) / **`key reserve rotate`** (register a new reserve key + revoke the old). `key approve <code>` (KL3 — the shared approval of old devices / guardians) is renamed to **`guardian approve <code>`** as the old-device path is removed (only guardian approval remains). `agent` is unchanged | Devices are "my resources" — vocabulary distinct from both `member` (managing others) and `key` (my single key). Adds one line to the help list |
| L-2 Extend `key` (`key add-device` / `key devices` …) | Doesn't add a group | `key` would swell from "my key" to "my device fleet", and the meanings of `key approve` / `key recover --handoff` get mixed in. Rejected |
| L-3 Under `member` (`member device …`) | Seen as part of member management | Devices are managed by the person; admins can only revoke — placing them in the member group (admin operations) inverts the authority picture. Rejected |

**Round 2**: "always bundle token revocation into `device revoke` (default, not a flag)" → the device↔token correspondence is advisory (an optional link in the registry); deleting other tokens by default grows the misfire surface. Keep it propose + yes (offer it as a candidate if the default name `cli:<hostname>` matches). Not a new option. "Fold the loss path into 1 command (`maruhi lost <label>`)" → `device revoke` does the same thing (don't add aliases). Rejected. **Empty round**. **Round 3**: no new options. **Empty round**. Stopped.

**Path check (3 persons)**: (1) a person who lost a device: `device revoke "MacBook"` (+ yes to the proposed token revocation) → contact the shown "ask them to rotate" party. Without a CLI: revoke the token on Web → `key recover` on another device → same as above. (2) a person adding a device: on the new device `login` → `device add`; at hand `device approve <fp>`. 0 ceremonies. (3) a team owner: revoking someone else's device is `device revoke --user <id> <fp>` (same role rules as remove; the rotate is performed by you). Invite/approve procedures are unchanged. **Adopted: L-1** (the names are the owner choice of approval item 12).

### Ruling DK-M: test-vector impact estimate (single round — checked against the README conventions. Vectors are not written in phase 1)

| File | Impact | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| `chain-entries.json` | **append** (seq 25~. The canonical chain 1–24 byte layout / hashes unchanged) | No full regeneration is needed since the `add_member` / `change_role` payloads are untouched (the same "extend by appending" pattern as the §6.2 `checkpoint` op addition). Positive cases to append: the reserve key's `add_device` (cap (owner, all)) / the phone's `add_device` (cap (owner, listed{})) / the CI box's `add_device` (cap (member, listed{dev})) / the derivation where a value write by a second device becomes valid / `revoke_device` (self, bulk, admin on another) / the derivation that a revoked device's ballot doesn't count / re-voting from another device. Negative cases: `duplicate-member-key` (another's device key, one's own existing key) / `unknown-device` / `last-device-protected` / `device-cap-exceeded` (per role and scope axis) / `add_device` for another's device (an `actor-not-member`-family reason — fixed in the canonical text) / `scope-not-contained` (a listed-scope admin revoking an all-scope person's device) / a signature by a revoked device (`actor-key-mismatch`) / approve from a cap < owner device (`insufficient-role`) / a second ballot from another device of the same person (`duplicate-approval`) / proposing `add_device` (`approval-not-required`) / the fixed form of the check ordering. Add `devices` (FP → cap, add seq) to `expected_head_states` members |
| `value-signature.json` / `metadata-signature.json` / `env-manifest.json` / `head-attestation.json` | **append** (existing positive/negative cases unchanged — the referenced seq ≤ 24 hashes don't change) | Device-axis negative cases: a revoked device signs (the device form of `writer-key-mismatch-at-head`) / a cap < member device writes a value (`writer-role-insufficient-at-head`) / an out-of-scope device (`writer-environment-out-of-scope-at-head`) — all existing reason codes, only re-grounded on the device's effective authority. Positive case: a valid write by a second device |
| `master-key-wrap.json` | **regenerate** (remove the `handoff-device` positive case and `aad-kind-mismatch` [the kind=swapped-to-`device` form]. Other cases' byte layout unchanged) | Mirrors the "no compat path" ruling. Noted in README **convention 28** as an intentional exception to "existing vectors are invariant" (same shape as IV's convention 26). `aad-kind-mismatch` is rebuilt as a `guardian` → `passkey-prf` swap |
| `dek-wrap.json` / `dek-wrap-signature.json` / `dek-commitment.json` / `recovery-wrap.json` / `lease-wrap.json` / `invite-accept-signature.json` / `invite-link.json` / `audit-head.json` / `checkpoint-digest.json` / `encoding.json` / `variable-encryption.json` | **unchanged** | HPKE info, AAD, registration-signature LPs, issue texts, acceptance texts, declaration LPs untouched |
| New files | None | Device-add codes reuse the existing FP display forms (12 words / hex); no new encoding |

K2's negative cases are enumerated per principle (the 3-ter lesson): the principle "keys belong to devices, authority belongs to persons" = each axis of effective-authority substitution (role cap / scope ∩) × each check (role, inclusion, environment target, value, meta, ballot), each axis of monotonicity, each signature after revocation.

### 2-bis. Final re-reading (adversarial — before the approval request)

The adopted set was re-read from 5 viewpoints (whether a duty performer exists, reachability of states that break an invariant, agreement between client verification and server acceptance, time-point drift, and DK × ES × PF1 intersections). No ruling-changing defects were found; 3 reinforcements were added (the 4th records a post-review correction):

- **Device hoarding by a stolen device** (reachability): devices that a lost device `add_device`ed before revocation do not disappear automatically on revocation. Countermeasures are bulk revocation (the payload FP list) + on-chain visibility (actor FP) + monotonicity (no stronger device can be created). Noted as "not guaranteed" in §14.2-11 (draft A-12)
- **The state where every owner device has cap < owner** (reachability): `last-device-protected` guards only device **count**. If an owner revokes all their (owner, all) devices and only a phone (owner, listed{}) remains, that person can cast ballots but holds no DEKs and cannot recreate a strong device via `add_device`'s monotonicity (possible from the reserve key). Not a consensus-rule target (role reachability is counted by user_id — the same operational failure as losing a key) — the CLI warns before `device revoke`: "the remaining devices cannot read prod / cannot create a strong device" (K4). Same shelf as §14.2-10's availability note
- **Projects where no reserve key is registered** (time-point drift): the reserve key's `add_device` depends on the first sync by one of the person's devices. A person who loses their device without syncing right after acceptance has no reserve key in that project → re-invite. The CLI registers immediately at the sync that completes `invite accept` / `member add`, and `project verify` warns "reserve key not registered" (K4). Cannot be a consensus rule (the chain does not know reserve keys)

- **The monotonicity comparison target** (corrected per a 2026-09-19 pullfrog / Cursor Bugbot finding): originally wrote "the signing device's effective cap", but effective cap folds in the person's current role / scope, so (a) a member / admin / reader could not register a reserve key with cap (owner, all), and (b) combined with cap immutability (v1), later promotion / scope widening would not reflect on existing devices, leaving the reserve key stuck shrunken (the post-all-devices-lost restore could not recover the widened share — the very event DK is meant to prevent). **Cap is a bound on the person's authority, not the authority itself** (`owner` = unbounded), so monotonicity compares the **signing device's own cap** (structural cap) to structural cap. With that, anyone can register a reserve key (owner, all) from the first device (structurally (owner, all)), and effective authority = min / ∩ follows the person's promotion / widening automatically. The bound on devices a stolen device can create also stays at "that device's declared cap or below", preserving the purpose

Unverified: that the effective-authority substitution enters every existing check without omission (crypto's `validate.ts` / `value-verify.ts` / `meta-verify.ts` / `manifest-verify.ts` / attestation / chain authorization), the full enumeration of `ChainMember` type-change consumers, the migration shape of the `dek_wraps` primary-key change — these can only be pinned by K2's vectors (per-principle negative cases) and implementation tests.

## 3. Implementation split (K1–K6) and independent stoppability

The series is test-vectors → crypto → api-schema → server → CLI → Web → docs (CLAUDE.md's order). Each stage must be safe to stop at after merge. It follows K2's acceptance guard (reject the 2 ops with 422 until K3 — the ES K2-10 principle "the set of ops the server accepts = the set of ops whose acceptance side effects are implemented").

| Stage | Content | Why stopping is safe | Human review |
|---|---|---|---|
| **K1** | Apply to the canonical spec texts (CRYPTO_SPEC 0.11 → **0.12-draft**, AUTH_SPEC 0.23 → **0.24-draft**, AUDIT_SPEC 1.8 → **1.9-draft**. Revision history in the Status field). Copy dk-spec-drafts.md into the canonical texts; replace owner-choice approval items with their fixed values | docs only | the approval itself |
| **K2** | **Write the test vectors first**: append to `chain-entries.json` (seq 25~ — DK-M), device-axis appends to `value-signature.json` and 3 other files, regenerate `master-key-wrap.json` (remove the device path, convention 28) → `packages/crypto` (`ChainMember.devices`, canonicalization + consensus rules of the 2 ops, effective-authority substitution, monotonicity, key uniqueness over the device set, device intervals in the history index, key selection, `master-wrap.ts` kind narrowing) → **mechanical follow-through in the api-schema wire + server / CLI / Web** (consumer rewrites for the `ChainMember` type change; devices stay at 1, the 2 ops are not generated). **The server rejects the 2 ops with 422 (`DeviceOpsNotAccepted` — worker + DO layered guard) until K3**. Mandatory-human-review spots are listed in the PR body | No behavior change (every member = 1 device, cap (owner, all)). crypto understands the new rules and old clients reject chains containing the new ops. Existing chains stay valid (no recreation needed — DK-J) | **mandatory** (packages/crypto — that effective-authority substitution enters every check; monotonicity; device validity of ballots) |
| **K3** | server: lift the acceptance guard + acceptance side effects of the 2 ops (mirror 2 rows, the `revoke_device` needs-rotation-detection variant, declaration-row deletion), the `dek_wraps` slot's device axis (`recipient_enc_pub_hex` into the primary key) + R(E) device expansion (`expectedWrapRecipientCount` / `checkWrapRecipient`) + reader self-backfill (recipients all one's own device keys), the device-count acceptance policy (`DeviceLimit`), guardian-segment device rows (`guardian_shares` primary key), **new `devices` group** (§13-11 registry, add requests), the device axis of declaration rows (`head_attestations`), D1 / DO migrations. Tests use `@cloudflare/vitest-plugin`. **Bundle the migration order into `docs/SELF_HOSTING.md` "Updates"** (server → every member's CLI → each person's reserve-key separation and replica-device re-registration) (the ES K2 precedent — the breaking change and the runbook in the same PR) | The CLI still doesn't emit the 2 ops and wraps with 1 device (behavior unchanged). The handoff old-device path (`source = "device"`) **stays until K4** (removing it in K3 would leave no means of device migration until K4) | Authorization (the reader self-backfill "recipients all one's own device keys" determination, request-row FP recomputation, the enumeration of revocation side effects), the enumeration of session rejections |
| **K4** | CLI: **new `device` group** (`add / approve / list / revoke`), `key generate` (device key + reserve key only on first run → code + optional passkey), `key recover` (restore the reserve key → issue a device key → destroy the reserve-key secret), `key recovery` / `key seal` / `guardian add` (preface with reserve-key opening), `key reserve rotate`, `key approve` → `guardian approve`, `wrapRecipientsFor` / backfill device expansion, the fifth sweep kind `device-revoked`, first-sync device registration (3 conditions), fingerprint-book set-ification, `member list` / `project verify` device counts and the reserve-key-unregistered warning, the remaining-devices warning before `device revoke`, the token-revocation proposal, the migration guidance (splitting the existing K's reserve key). **In the same PR, remove the server's handoff old-device path (`source = "device"` / blob column / the `kind = "device"` AAD)** (land the replacement and the removal simultaneously). Tests use Vitest | DK's functional surface is complete. Web just doesn't show device counts | The ceremony gate (`device approve` = TTY + non-agent), not persisting the reserve-key secret (Redacted discipline), the confirmation UX of `device revoke`'s bulk targets, the wording of the migration guidance |
| **K5** | Web: device count on `ProjectScreen` member rows, the device list (registry — "server-declared"), the loss path (link to token revocation — existing feature). Read-only (ADR-0018) | Read-only; stopping at K4 leaves all operations available in the CLI | — |
| **K6** | docs: new `apps/site/docs/devices.mdx` (device add/revoke, cap, the loss path), `recover-your-key.mdx` (reserve key, the guardian path; removal of the old-device path), `linux-keychain.mdx` (Codespaces = `device add` / `approve`), `invite-a-teammate.mdx` (`key publish` on the accepting device), one sentence in `getting-started.mdx`'s key generation, README's docs list, ROADMAP's DK row `[x]` | docs only | — |

- Migration order requirement: server (K3) → every member's CLI (K4) → each person's reserve-key separation (`key generate` guidance) and replica-device re-registration (`device add` / `approve`). Old CLIs reject chains containing the 2 ops with `invalid-payload` (fail-closed); new CLIs sending the 2 ops to a pre-K3 server stop at 422 (K2's guard) (neither direction silently misinterprets). Existing projects don't need recreation (DK-J)
- Estimate: spec 1 day → K2 3–4 days (incl. human review) → K3 3–4 days → K4 4–5 days → K5 1 day → K6 1 day = within ROADMAP's 3–4-week estimate. The bottleneck is owner approval and the crypto human review (matching ES / KL3 actuals)
- Beta gate: DK as a whole gates the invite-only beta (ROADMAP). K5 (Web reads) may leave the parallel track (approval item 16)
