## 4. Approval-request items (those requiring an owner ruling)

Each item carries a summary of "adopted option / rejected options / reasons". Stacked are those concerning the spec, crypto, chain rules, wire format, or audit-event additions. Implementation internals and test shapes are decided by me as I go. Items marked **owner choice** are points where doubt remains and are fixed by the answer (unanswered → proceed with the adopted option). **On 2026-09-20, items 1–16 were fixed on the adopted-option side** (see the approval record at the top — owner-choice items are also all the "adopted" column's values). K1 copies this table's "adopted" column into the canonical texts.

| # | Item | Adopted | Rejected | Reason (summary) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Key unit (DK-A) | **Members (persons) are unchanged, each holding a set of device keys**. Add the 2 ops `add_device` / `revoke_device`. The `add_member` / `change_role` / `genesis` payloads are unchanged (existing chains valid, no ES regeneration). Signer identification (user_id, key FP) is unchanged; FP points to a device | (i) a device class in the ledger (revocation a no-op) / device = member row / root-key two layers / a per-user device chain | "Keys belong to devices; authority belongs to persons". The existing rules' FP selection becomes device selection verbatim |
| 2 | Device cap (DK-A γ)**〔owner choice〕** | **(a) recommended**: the `add_device` payload carries `role_cap` + scope (2 fields); effective authority = (min, ∩); monotonicity (a new device's cap ≤ the signing device's own cap — cap-to-cap, not effective authority; `owner` = unbounded). The v1 CLI exposes `device approve --cap <role> --env …` (default = (owner, all) = the person's authority itself) | (b) in the consensus rules but not exposed in the CLI / (c) no cap (payload = 2 keys — adding it later = a format change = full regeneration) | Expresses "a ballot-only phone" and "a dev-only CI box" in one mechanism, and a stolen device cannot create a strong device. Fix the format before release (the ES A-1 lesson). The cost is that every check reads effective authority |
| 3 | Reserve key (DK-A / DK-G) | A device key that lives only in the ledger (cap (owner, all)). An ordinary device on the chain. After restore, `add_device` a new device and erase the reserve-key secret. A project with no registered reserve key needs re-invite (the CLI warns) | No reserve key (all-devices-lost = re-invite — single-owner projects die) / every device key into the ledger | One "key not placed on devices" is needed. A mere re-reading of KL3's B's meaning suffices |
| 4 | Revocation duty (DK-B)**〔owner choice〕** | Rotate duty on every environment in the revoked device's effective scope (MUST — same shape as remove). Performer = the actor. Reader self-revocation is not rejected; the duty is shown to member-or-above via the detection flag (`trigger = revoke_device`) and a `project verify` warning | Reason-dependent exemption (`retired` = no duty — fail-open) / rejecting reader self-revocation (blocks a safe-side operation) / rotate only the environments read | "Revocation = removal from R(E) + effective-scope rotate". **Whether to resolve ephemeral-device friction via exemption (B-2) is an owner choice — the recommendation is not to take it (the forwarding agent's domain)** |
| 5 | Four-eyes intersection (DK-C) | S's element (user_id, FP) unchanged; distinct by person; `duplicate-approval` = the person holds a live ballot (even from another device); a revoked device's ballot is revoked; a cap < owner device cannot cast a ballot; `proposal-void` is judged on the proposing device's validity; the 2 ops are not four-eyes targets | Don't silently count a second ballot from another device / keep a revoked device's ballots / make `add_device` a standing target | Principle 2 in device vocabulary. Consistent with K2-11 ⑤ (ballots revoked on key update) |
| 6 | Device-add authorization and ceremony (DK-D) | actor = self only (role doesn't matter; readers may). A new device's cap ≤ the signing device. Key uniqueness across all devices of current members. **No ceremony**: the person carries the FP (12 words / hex); the approving device verifies the request row's public key against the FP + yes (TTY gate). The requesting side has no gate. `key publish` is the accepting device's key. The fingerprint book is a set of FPs. First-sync registration is limited to keys meeting the 3 conditions (self-generated, self-approved, observed on a verified chain) | admin adds others' devices / invitation-style / handoff-style / co-signed proof of possession / backing-source verification | "Only the person can grow keys, and only devices at or below themselves". Carries no secret |
| 7 | R(E) · acceptance (DK-E) | R(E) = (person, device) pairs × effective scope. §12-6's identification (user_id, enc public key) unchanged; the enc public key goes into the slot PK. Reader self-backfill (all recipients are one's own device keys). Devices ≤ 16 / member / project (acceptance policy `DeviceLimit`). HPKE info / registration signature unchanged | A person's single enc key (= replication) / an env KEK layer / device count into the consensus rules | Just adds "effective" to K3-D's one predicate |
| 8 | Location · registry · audit (DK-F)**〔owner choice〕** | Chain ops + **an advisory device registry** (AUTH §13-11: display names, public keys, an optional `tokenId`, add-request rows. Not used in verification). Mirrors `chain.device_added` / `chain.device_revoked` ★. The detection variant is the device's window + actor key FP. Class 1. The registry carries no audit events | Ledger API (ghost keys) / no registry (pick revocation targets by FP alone) | "Facts that change R(E) are chain ops; facts that don't are advisory". **Whether the registry carries a `tokenId` binding column is an owner choice — the recommendation is to carry it (optional column)** |
| 9 | KL3 ledger (DK-G)**〔owner choice〕** | B = the reserve key; `kind` ∈ {passkey-prf, guardian} (remove `device`); remove `source = "device"` and the blob column; guardian segments go to each of the guardian's device keys (a row per device); ledger changes are prefaced by opening the reserve key (code / passkey). Naming collisions resolve by removal | The reserve-key secret on devices / segments to the guardian's reserve key | "What the ledger guards is the single key not on devices". **The §8 title "reserve key wrap ledger" and the English UI term "reserve key" are an owner choice** |
| 10 | Delegation model (DK-H) | Keep KL4 as-is. Not structurally cheaper under DK. Rewrite the forwarding agent's main use as "ephemeral environments" (carryover) | Delegatize alongside DK | No relitigating the kept-as-is. Presented as information |
| 11 | Migration (DK-J)**〔owner choice〕** | **J-1 recommended**: chain kept as-is (existing master key = the first device key). The ledger is replaced by a reserve key (re-issue codes / re-seal passkeys / recreate guardians). Replica devices re-register via `device add` / `approve` and delete the replicas. The CLI guides on first launch | J-2 recreate the verification deploy / re-read the existing key as the reserve key | Doable without destructive change. Choosing recreation = the same consequence as ES A-1 |
| 12 | CLI naming (DK-L)**〔owner choice〕** | `maruhi device add / approve / list / revoke` (new group). `key generate / show / publish / recover / recovery / seal` stay "my keys"; `key approve` → **`guardian approve`**; new `key reserve rotate` | Extend `key` / `member device …` | Devices are one's own resources. **The names are an owner choice** (`device` / `guardian approve` / `reserve`) |
| 13 | Test vectors (DK-M) | Append to `chain-entries.json` (canonical chain 1–24 unchanged); append device-axis negative cases to 4 files; regenerate `master-key-wrap.json` (device-path removal — an intentional exception per convention 28). The rest unchanged. No new files | Full regeneration | The existing ops' payloads are untouched |
| 14 | Per-device declaration rows (DK-A / drafts A-7 / B-8) | Store and distribute head declarations per (user_id, device FP). `revoke_device` deletes the row | Keep one row per member (declarations from lagging devices hit 409) | Devices sync independently |
| 15 | Implementation split K1–K6 (§3) | Ordering, the acceptance guard (K2 → K3), the old-device-path removal in K4 (simultaneous with the CLI), SELF_HOSTING bundled into K3, the human-review spots | — | Follows the ES / KL3 procedure |

Approved on 2026-09-20. Phase 2 starts in a separate session from K1 (canonical-text application).

## 5. Carryovers · out of scope

- **Sealing (device-key-sealing.md option 3)**: device-key records (`StoredMasterKey`'s JSON shape) are unchanged; the sealing backend is a storage layer that wraps this blob. Per-device keys correspond to per-device sealing, and losing one sealing token = revoking that device suffices. **The reserve key is not sealed by a device-bound sealer** (ledger only — draft A-9's prohibition). That memo's §6 sketch (2026-08-10) does not reflect KL3 / ES / PF1, so treat this design record's §1-3 as the source of truth for impact scope (a cross-reference is added to the top of device-key-sealing.md)
- **Delegation (KL4)**: kept as-is. On re-evaluation, rewrite the consideration from "theft preparedness" to "ephemeral environments (devices die on every container recreation; revoke + rotate is heavy)". Since post-DK agent compromise converges as one device's revocation, delegation's value purifies to "key material never leaves the socket"
- **Intersection with VH (value history)**: `var history`'s writer FP points to a device. A device's display name is readable only by the person (the registry), so VH's display stays FP (12 words) + user (the registry is not shown to others). Rollback writers are also devices. No VH-side spec change is needed
- **Intersection with PF2 (mirror)**: recipient class server is unchanged. Even if the design becomes "mirrors as members", it is outside the device axis (server keys have no devices)
- **`set_device_cap` (changing a device's cap)**: in v1, cap is immutable (change = revoke + re-add). If demand arises it can be appended as a new op (no payload-format change) — widening is the actor's backfill; narrowing is a rotate duty (same shape as `change_role`)
- **Four-eyes performer (K6-D)**: if the approving device holds no DEKs (a phone with an empty cap scope), the post-apply sweep / backfill carries over to another device of the same person or to the next sync. The performer stays the same person. Handled in CLI guidance (K4)
- **K4-K's pull warning**: add "re-run `device approve`" as the next step after "no DEK wraps for you" (device-add backfill incomplete)
- **Codespaces docs (K6)**: 1 codespace = 1 device (`device add` / `approve`) + carry the device key across with KL1's persistent keyring. Operations that discard the container every time repeat device revoke + rotate; in that case, write that they should wait for the forwarding agent (KL4) or manage with an `agent --key-ttl`-equivalent operation
- **ES §6's carryover "option (ii) would touch `add_member`'s payload again"**: does not arise (`add_member` unchanged — resolved)
- **Residuals (to the threat-model document)**: (1) devices created by a lost device before revocation do not disappear automatically (handled by bulk revocation, visibility, monotonicity). (2) If the device used for restore is compromised, the reserve key may be compromised too (`key reserve rotate`). (3) The device registry is a server declaration — a forged display name can induce picking the wrong revocation target, but revocation is confirmed by FP and the CLI shows the FP's 12 words alongside
- **Out of scope (not handled by this design)**: the design/implementation of hardware sealing (option 3), the PQ suite, the delegation-model implementation (KL4), per-user device chains / an organizational key ledger, per-device history-epoch limits, and the correspondence between devices and non-token credentials (sessions)
- **Unperformed checks (to be pinned in K2)**: that effective-authority substitution enters every existing check's inputs, the full enumeration of `ChainMember` type-change consumers (measured with fallow), the migration shape of the `dek_wraps` / `guardian_shares` / `head_attestations` primary-key changes, and the measured wrap count for 16 devices × members × environments × epochs

---

## 6. K1 addendum (2026-09-20 — rulings at canonical-text application)

K1 is application work; the approved items 1–16 (§4) are unchanged. The rulings that arose at the point of writing into the canonical texts are the following 9 (corresponding to this session's request §3's enumeration). Each ruling was run through the §5 procedure (enumerate 3+ candidates → the upward-compat / silver-bullet round → one more round after any round that produced a new option, stop after 2 empty rounds → extract principles and confirm derivation of existing rulings → reader check → adopt). Round counts are recorded honestly.

### K1-1. Where principles D1 / D2 live — promotion to CRYPTO_SPEC §1 or inside §6.2

Draft A-3 places the 2 principles inside §6.2's "device key" block. Ruled in light of ES K1-1's principle "what goes in §1 is only principles that cross-cut multiple mechanisms of the crypto design" and the notice at that ruling's end: "the moment a DK principle is found to apply beyond §6.2, a §1 promotion will be proposed".

| Option | Content | Evaluation (cross-cuttingness · double-bookkeeping · reader) |
|---|---|---|
| K1-1a Stay inside §6.2 (per the draft) | Declare D1 / D2 at the head of §6.2 "device key"; what follows are derivations | Minimal revision scope. But D1 is the side that **generates rules** for §3 (the key hierarchy — device key / reserve key definitions), §5.1 (registration-signature key selection), §6.3 (the inputs of 1 / 3 / 3′), §6.6 (the attester's key), §7 (revocation duty, backfill), §8 (the key the ledger guards = the reserve key), and AUTH §12-6 (recipient identification) — a different cross-cuttingness from ES principles 1 / 2 (closed inside §6.2). A reader who opens only §6.2 cannot trace the reasons for the §5.1 / §8 changes |
| K1-1b **Promote D1 to §1 principle 7; keep the definition and derivations in §6.2. D2 stays inside §6.2** | §1 = 1 paragraph (person/device separation, signer identification, effective authority, that every check reads the signing device's effective authority, the ledger's target). §6.2 = the effective-authority formula, payload, authorization, monotonicity (D2), check ordering | The same shape as principle 6 (a principle bearing on every signature structure placed in §1 — the precedent paired with AUTH §12-10). The double-bookkeeping concern is controlled by the split "§1 = principle text only, §6.2 = definition + derivations" (K1-1b's drawback in ES was "the same sentence in 2 places"; here the sentences differ). D2 (monotonicity) bears only on `add_device`'s consensus rule, so it stays in §6.2 |
| K1-1c Only a one-line reference in §1 | "7. Keys belong to devices; authority belongs to persons (§6.2)" | A reference line with no normative content (the same rejection reason as ES K1-1c) |
| K1-1d Make §3 (key hierarchy) the principles' home | §3 is the section that defines device key / reserve key / cap; every section references it | §3 is the "key shapes" section, not the place for a "what the checks take as input" principle. If it does not stand in §1's line of principles, it is not readable as a design principle on par with principle 6 (signed bytes) |

**Round 1 (enumeration)**: K1-1a–c (the 3 options of request §3-1). **Round 2 (upward compat · silver bullet)**: "can the cross-cuttingness be made explicit while avoiding §1/§6.2 double-bookkeeping" → K1-1b's split (§1 = principle text, §6.2 = definition + derivations) erases only the drawback = fixed as K1-1b's upward-compat form. As a silver bullet, "make §3 the home" (K1-1d) emerged → rejected for the reasons in the table. A new option emerged, so one more round. **Round 3**: "raise D2 to §1 too?" → D2 bears on only one `add_device` rule and is derived to stay inside §6.2 by ES K1-1's principle. No new option — **empty round**. **Round 4**: no new option — **empty round**. Stopped after 2 consecutive empty rounds (1 enumeration round + 1 new-option round + 2 empty rounds).

**Principle extraction**: use ES K1-1's principle "what goes in §1 is only principles that cross-cut multiple mechanisms of the crypto design" **as-is** (no correction needed). Derivation check: principles 1–5 = all-mechanism cross-cutting ✓, principle 6 = all-signature-structure cross-cutting ✓, ES principles 1 / 2 = §6.2 only → inside §6.2 ✓, **D1 = crosses §3 / §5.1 / §6.2 / §6.3 / §6.6 / §7 / §8 / AUTH §12-6 → §1 ✓**, D2 = §6.2 only → inside §6.2 ✓. No ✗ — ES K1-1's principle derives D1's promotion; the principle side needs no fix.

**Reader check**: a reader who opens only §1 learns that "every check reads the signing device's effective authority" and "what the ledger guards is the reserve key", and traces the formula and check ordering in §6.2. A reader who opens only §6.2 reads down to the check ordering in one place as before (§6.2's text is the same as K1-1a).

**Adopted: K1-1b**. Reason: D1 already generates rules outside §6.2 (§5.1 / §8 / AUTH §12-6), and both ES K1-1's principle and its notice point to promotion. No approved item changes (item 1's content is unchanged — only the location ruling). Canonical text: CRYPTO_SPEC §1 principle 7 + a reference "principle D1 (§1 principle 7)" at the head of §6.2 "device key".

### K1-2. §6.2's description structure — one device-key block or dispersed across spots

| Option | Content | Evaluation |
|---|---|---|
| K1-2a One block + short references at consuming spots (per the draft) | A "device key" block right after the "environment scope" block (payload, authorization, validity interval, R(E), relation to four-eyes, check ordering). The role table / op table / four-eyes `approve` / key uniqueness get only row substitutions and references | The same shape as the precedent where ES wrote an "environment scope" block + spot appends. A reader enumerating negative cases in K2 can read the check ordering in one place |
| K1-2b Disperse across spots | Write the authorization rules split across the op table, four-eyes, and key-uniqueness blocks | The derivation of the principle "run every check on the device's effective authority" scatters across 4 spots, and the check ordering cannot be read in one place |
| K1-2c Only one block (don't touch the tables, four-eyes, key uniqueness) | No references | The op table lacks the new ops and `approve`'s wording stays in the old vocabulary — a reader looking only at the tables misreads |
| K1-2d (round 2) For `approve`, don't rewrite the existing paragraph; place only a device-vocabulary **appended paragraph** | Preserves the existing giant `approve` paragraph; writes DK's reading in a separate paragraph | The existing paragraph's "the actor's (user_id, current key FP) is not an element of S" and "the current member holding the same key FP as at signing time is an owner" remain as-is, and **both** it and the device-vocabulary paragraph look normative (unreadable which is correct) |

**Round 1 (enumeration)**: K1-2a–c. **Round 2 (upward compat)**: "can `approve`'s wording be substituted without destroying the existing paragraph" → K1-2d (append only) emerged but is rejected for the table's reasons. Instead, **K1-2a′ = of K1-2a, for `approve`, "replace the existing paragraph's 3 phrases (the role check, the duplicate condition, the ballot-count condition, the `proposal-void` reading) with device vocabulary + append a device-vocabulary supplement as the immediately following 1 paragraph"** (substitutions at phrase granularity, the supplement in one place). A new option, so one more round. **Round 3 (silver bullet)**: "consolidate the check ordering of all ops into one table" → would mean rewriting the existing ops' check-ordering descriptions, exceeding the scope of DK's application (a copy). Rejected. No new option — **empty round**. **Round 4**: no new option — **empty round**. Stopped.

**Principle extraction**: "a rule is written once, in the block of the principle that generates it; consuming spots (tables, other blocks) get only substitution rows and references. Phrases whose meaning changes in an existing paragraph are replaced at phrase granularity, and the old wording is not left as normative" — ES's "environment scope" block ✓ (table-row substitution + check-ordering reference), PF1's "four-eyes" block ✓, key uniqueness ✓ (its own block + a check-ordering note), KL3 §8 ✓. No ✗.

**Reader check**: the K2 implementer opens §6.2 "device key" and reads the reason codes and ordering of `add_device` / `revoke_device` in one place, and reads the `approve` paragraph consistently in device vocabulary (no old wording remains).

**Adopted: K1-2a′**.

No divergence between the draft and §4's "adopted" column was found (the 2 fields of device cap, monotonicity = structural cap to structural cap, `revoke_device` check ordering, the treatment of reader self-revocation, device count 16, the `kind` set, the `tokenId` column, the title "reserve key wrap ledger", the device axis of declarations, the 2 mirror events, class 1 — all match). **Draft vs canonical-text divergences** are the following 2 (the canonical text wins):

### K1-10. Copying carryovers from the review (PR #184), and carryovers to K2

- **PR #184's 3 findings were copied into the canonical texts**: monotonicity = structural cap to structural cap (§6.2 "`add_device` authorization" — kept the Cursor Bugbot finding's history) / `revoke_device`'s check ordering = `unknown-target` → `unknown-device` → target-dependent role rules → `last-device-protected` → `scope-not-contained` (§6.2 "check ordering") / the parallelism of the 3 specs' Status lines (K1-8)
- **Carryovers to K2**: (1) the vector append/regenerate list stays as §4 item 13 / DK-M (copied into CRYPTO §11's 0.12-draft item). (2) README convention 28 (`master-key-wrap.json`'s intentional exception) is written in K2. (3) Normative-text ambiguities found during application: (a) `revoke_device`'s "target's scope ⊆ actor's effective scope" is the **person's** scope (not the revoked device's effective scope) — DK-B's duty scope is the device's effective scope, but the authorization inclusion is judged on the target's whole scope (judging on the device's effective scope would make the duty scope and the inclusion scope coincide, but on a ballot-only device [empty scope] the inclusion holds vacuously and no `scope-not-contained` negative case can be made — judge on the person's scope as the draft says. K2's negative case [a listed-scope admin revoking an all-scope person's device] is pinned under this reading). (b) `add_device`'s `unknown-environment` requires each `listed` id to be preceded by `create_environment`, but deleted environments may be listed, same as ES (no harm). (c) The reserve key's "cap = (owner, all)" is CLI discipline, not a consensus rule (the chain doesn't distinguish reserve keys — §3). (4) Identifier renames are kept as-is (K1-3's carryover)
- **Points to put to the owner**: new §13-11's creation HTTP status was aligned to the precedent 200 (K1-6). If K3 takes 201, the spec side gets a one-word fix. K1-11–K1-13 (below), which arose in the PR #185 review, are put to the owner alongside

### K1-11. Token level of `add_device` / one's own `revoke_device` (PR #185 pullfrog finding 1 — correcting draft B-3)

Draft B-3 wrote "the token level is the same admin as `add_member` etc.", but CRYPTO §6.2 (approval item 6) makes `add_device` / one's own `revoke_device` role-agnostic (readers allowed). Since AUTH §6 / §12-3's tables read as "token-scope requirement + role floor", flat admin would mean readers / members cannot add devices or revoke a stolen device (DK's central path blocked at the acceptance layer).

| Option | Content | Evaluation |
|---|---|---|
| (a) Flat admin (the draft) | Passes with a default login token (`*` × admin) | A narrowed-scope token cannot revoke one's own device. The table reads as role floor admin — misread. Rejected |
| (b) **write × reader-or-above (self) / admin (others)** | The same level as §12-3's reader self-backfill row. A device add entails backfill [write] and a revocation entails rotate [write], so write is natural | Expressible on 2 axes with no ambiguity. A read-token sync-only client cannot add devices, but that is a sound line: a client that cannot write values does not grow devices |
| (c) read × reader-or-above (self) | The same level as head declarations (§16-1) | Revocation is safe-side, but allowing adds (R(E) growth + backfill) at read makes "a read token grows DEK recipients". Rejected |

Round 1: the table. Round 2: "lower only one's-own revocation to read (adds stay write)" → the rotate right after revocation requires write, and read cannot perform the duty (the CLI does both in one command). Not a new option — empty round. Round 3: empty round. Stopped. **Principle**: "a chain op's token level matches the level of the data operations it necessarily entails (backfill / rotate)" — `create_environment` / `rotate_epoch` = write ✓, `add_member` etc. = admin (member management) ✓, the four-eyes 4 ops = the inner op ✓. No ✗. **Adopted: (b)**. §13-11's registry and add-requests stay `*` × admin (user-wide self-information — the same shelf as the token list §6 and the ledger §13-7. A new device holds the default token from §4's login) — one phrase of reason added to the canonical text. Approval item 6's content is unchanged (the token level was not in the item — recorded as a correction of the draft's error).

### K1-12. The (a) of AUDIT §4.1's `revoke_device` variant — `var.read` carries no actor key FP (PR #185 pullfrog finding 2 — correcting draft C-3 and ruling DK-F)

Ruling DK-F and draft C-3 wrote "(a) is the `var.read` rows where `actor_key_fingerprint` ∈ the revoked FP set", but `var.read` is an unsigned read and carries no FP column (§3.3's typology, §5.2's partial index, the remove variant's user_id matching — the canonical text says all consistently). The latter half of approval item 8's "detection variant = the device's window + actor key FP" does not stand.

| Option | Content | Evaluation |
|---|---|---|
| (1) **user_id matching + the interval narrowed by the device's validity interval ∩ the window ∩ the device scope** | Lowers the precision claim. Reads by another device of the same person may mix into (a) (over-declaration = safe side) | Closed inside AUDIT; §3.3 / §5.2 not unwound. The interval narrowing alone is already narrower than the remove variant |
| (2) Match the registry's `tokenId` (advisory) against `actor_api_token_id` to attribute to a device | Per-device (a) becomes sayable | Requires a ruling that uses the advisory — which §13-11 declares "is never an input to any verification or authorization" — as a basis rank (rank is a display emphasis, not authorization, but redrawing the line is the owner's) |
| (3) Carry an FP on `var.read` | Highest precision | Fixing §3.3's typology (FP = evidence of signing) and §5.2's partial index in the same revision = a change to audit's design principles. Beyond K1's scope |

Round 1: the table (the review's 3 options). Round 2 (upward compat): "put (1) in the canonical text and note (2) as a future refinement" → fixed as (1)'s upward-compat form. Round 3: empty round. Round 4: empty round. Stopped. **Principle**: "the inputs to an audit basis rank are only the columns audit rows carry — don't demand an FP of a read that isn't signing evidence" — §3.3's typology ✓, the remove / change_role variants (user_id) ✓, the revoke_server variant (the server-key FP is `server.lease_issued`'s actor) ✓. No ✗. **Adopted: (1)** (correct canonical AUDIT §4.1). **Put to the owner**: item 8's "+ actor key FP" is revised to "+ interval narrowing by the device window" (item 8's other content unchanged). If (2) is chosen, it is presented separately as a ruling widening advisory's use. §2's ruling DK-F and §4's table are not rewritten in this session (the discipline that K1 doesn't change §2 / §4) — this addendum is the authoritative correction.

### K1-13. Existing guardian segments when a guardian's device is revoked (PR #185 pullfrog finding 3 — answering a question)

§8.3 only wrote "segments don't follow when a guardian adds a device"; the revocation side was missing. Facts: a sealed `s_i` remains on the revoked device (§7's duty is DEK rotate only). One segment cannot open B (only the ward themselves can fetch the group's wrap, and approval requires the ward's request + account authentication), so the residual folds into the same line as §14.3-8 (collusion).

| Option | Content | Evaluation |
|---|---|---|
| (a) Noted as an intentional residual in §8.3 / §14.3-8 + a warning (SHOULD) to the ward and a re-create proposal (advisory) | Without threshold sharing, segment whereabouts are not recovered on device revocation. The warning is "if even one sealing target is a revoked device" (don't wait for all devices to be revoked). Re-creation requires a ceremony (opening the reserve key), so it is not made a duty | No added guarantee; the negative-case enumeration and `project verify`'s warning design get fixed |
| (b) Impose a re-create duty on the ward | Recovers segment whereabouts | Performing the duty needs reserve-key opening = a ceremony, leaving an unperformable duty (the same shape as reader self-revocation rotate, but this one adds no guarantee). Rejected |
| (c) Write nothing | — | K2's negative cases and the warning design wouldn't be fixed. Rejected |

Round 1: the table. Round 2: "delete the revoked device's segment row from the ledger (a server side effect)" → the server doesn't see the chain (the ledger is per-user, off-chain), so it has no deletion trigger. Rejected. Empty round. Round 3: empty round. Stopped. **Principle**: "ledger residuals fold at the line 'B cannot be opened without the ward's account authentication'" — §14.3-8 ✓, §8.1's double defense ✓. **Adopted: (a)** (append to canonical §8.3 / §14.3-8; the warning and proposal are K4's CLI). The one normative sentence not in the draft is the SHOULD warning — listed alongside the points to put to the owner.

### K1-14. Handling the PR #185 review nits

- The relation of §12-8's 16 and §13-11's 32: added to CRYPTO §3 the phrase "one device-key pair per device, the same key across all projects", and noted on §13-11 "32 is headroom for revoked/unapproved rows"
- `revoke_device`'s inclusion check: supplemented CRYPTO §6.2 with "the target **person's** scope", and kept in parentheses the reason inclusion holds vacuously on a ballot-only device (copied K1-10 (3)(a)'s carryover into the canonical text)
- The 2 HTTP-status points (200 / 204) remain pending owner ruling
- The 2 round-2 nits: AUTH §6 "only `checkpoint` is determined including its payload" → also include `revoke_device` (whether the target is self or other) / re-grounded §13-11's 32 as "operational headroom", and noted that CRYPTO §3's "the same device key across all projects" is CLI discipline that the chain does not enforce (`add_device` is a per-project op)

### K1-15. Separating `revoke_device` from API-token revocation (PR #185 Cursor Security Reviewer — MEDIUM)

The finding: `revoke_device` is a chain fact, while token revocation remains a client "proposal", so a stolen device can authenticate with its token until the default TTL (90 days) (it can read ciphertext, plaintext meta, and self-information; DEKs of post-revocation epochs don't reach it). §14.3 has no non-guarantee for this.

| Option | Content | Evaluation |
|---|---|---|
| (a) Server-side forced revocation (on `revoke_device` acceptance, revoke the token of the registry's `tokenId`) | Reliable cut-off | The registry is advisory — "never an input to any verification or authorization" (approval item 8). Making the revocation target's identification depend on an advisory row lets the server (or any party that can write the registry) swap the token-revocation target = an availability-attack surface (the same argument as §12-6's overwrite prohibition). Requires inverting approval item 8 |
| (b) **Note the non-guarantee in §14.2-11 + raise the CLI's proposal to SHOULD and normalize the display when declined** | Keeps the design (DK-L round 2: "deleting tokens by default grows the misfire surface — propose + yes") and corrects user expectations in the spec | No added guarantee, but implementers and users can read "`revoke_device` alone doesn't cut off". Web's immediate cut-off (token revocation) path is as in design record §1-2 |
| (c) A CLI MUST (always bundle the revocation) | Forces the cut-off via the CLI | Since the correspondence is advisory (`tokenId` optional), the MUST's target cannot be determined, and matching by the default name produces misfires that delete other tokens. Rejected |

Round 1: the table. Round 2: "carry the token id in `revoke_device`'s payload" → brings a server-issued id into the signed object — the shape of the chain referencing AUTH's credentials (doesn't contradict §6.1's identity-rule spirit, but breaks the chain↔token independence). Rejected. Empty round. Round 3: empty round. Stopped. **Principle**: "revoking off-chain credentials (tokens, sessions) is an AUTH operation; don't place it as a chain op's side effect via an advisory correspondence" — §16-1's declaration-row deletion (a chain-derived fact — the correspondence is signed) ✓, §12-6's old-key cleanup (chain-derived) ✓, `auth.token_revoked` (an explicit operation by the performing principal) ✓. **Adopted: (b)** (canonical CRYPTO §14.2-11 / AUTH §6). **Put to the owner**: if (a) is chosen, it is presented as an inversion of approval item 8 (making the registry an authorization input).

Round-4 (pullfrog) correction: the material for candidates and expiries is `GET /auth/tokens`' `expires_at` (`GET /auth/me` is the presented token's expiry and is not material for other devices). Since the list requires `*` × admin and one's own `revoke_device` is write, on a write-capped token the candidate display and expiry display are omitted, relaxed to a shape that conveys only the fact of remainder and the means of revocation (a `*` × admin device or the Web) (the revocation path is not premised on `*` × admin — it normally works on the default login token). Carryover: the `§16-2` misreference in AUTH §6's default-TTL item (ruling CI), which predates it (correctly the same item in this section), is kept as-is as outside DK's scope, to be fixed in K3's AUTH revision.

### Owner ruling (2026-09-20 — K1 merge delegation)

After the PR #185 review (pullfrog 6 rounds, Cursor Bugbot, Security Reviewer 1 item — all findings incorporated; round 7 was 2 nits to this record), the owner instructed "if your decision is truly sound, you may go through merge". The designer re-checked the 4 "points to put to the owner", judged each the best long-term, and **merged as-is** (the same shape as PR #184's approval): (1) K1-12 — a correction grounded in the fact that `var.read` has no FP column, and the only option that doesn't break audit's typology (§3.3) and partial index (§5.2). Approval item 8's "+ actor key FP" becomes "+ interval narrowing by the device window" (the §4 table is not rewritten; this item is authoritative). (2) K1-15 — consistent with the line that an advisory registry is not an authorization input (approval item 8), and server-side forced revocation can be added additively if it becomes necessary (no spec-format change). (3) K1-13 — an advisory warning that adds no guarantee; the negative-case enumeration and `project verify` design get fixed. (4) HTTP statuses (§13-11 creation 200, §13-7 approval 204) — per the canonical texts' precedents; if K3 takes a different value, it's a one-word fix. With the above, the spec wording of CRYPTO_SPEC 0.12-draft / AUTH_SPEC 0.24-draft / AUDIT_SPEC 1.9-draft is approved, and phase 2 proceeds to K2 (test vectors first + crypto — a separate session).


## 7. K2 addendum (2026-09-20 — test vectors first + crypto + wire / acceptance guard / mechanical follow-through)

Rulings made in K2's implementation PR. Like K1, recorded as: candidate table → rounds (stopped after 2 consecutive rounds producing no upward-compat / silver bullet) → principle extraction → reader check → adopt. **The canonical texts' normative sentences are unchanged** (divergences and ambiguities are implemented fail-closed and enumerated in the closing "carryovers to the canonical texts"). Numbers and names were copied from the canonical texts (CRYPTO_SPEC 0.12-draft §6.2 "device key" / §6.3 / §8.1, AUDIT_SPEC 1.9-draft §3.4) and README convention 28.

### K2-1. Where the device-key positive cases live — extending the canonical chain or a derived chain (candidate 1)

| Option | Content | Judgment |
|---|---|---|
| a-1 Extend the canonical chain `entries` to seq 25–37 (the letter of DK-M's "seq 25~") | The meaning of `head-beyond-local-seq` (seq 25 = a future head), `tenure_extension` (seq 25's re-add), `basic` (head 24) among the 4 existing files' positive/negative cases changes; it needs regenerating the 4 files + recomputing convention 27's "canonical-chain dependent" | Rejected |
| a-2 **Place the positive cases on a derived chain `device-ops` (base 24, seq 25–37); intermediate heads (29 / 33 / 34 / 36) are given via prefix-derived chains (`device-added` / `device-dead-vote` / `device-revote-applied` / `device-recovered`)** | The canonical chain seq 1–24 byte layout and `expected_head_states` are unchanged (take DK-M's "existing positive/negative cases unchanged" side). The 4 files' device-axis positive/negative cases reference the derived chain via `chain: "device-ops"` (convention 28) | **Adopted** |
| a-3 An independent chain of device ops only (a new genesis) | Cannot exercise intersections with existing members / environments / policies (cap × scope ∩, four-eyes ballots) | Rejected |

Round 2: "express a derived chain's prefix by `base_seq` alone, without making prefix-derived chains" → for the 4 files' positive cases to declare intermediate heads (33 = after the dead ballot, 36 = the return), they need something to pull the entry_hash at that point (`extendedVectorChainHistory(name)` resolves by chain name). Expressing a prefix with new reference semantics is costlier than laying 4 chains with the existing derived-chain mechanism (the ES K2-2 precedent). Empty round. Round 3: empty round. Stopped. **Principle**: "a vector's reference is always 'chain name + seq'; no new reference semantics is brought in" — ES K2-2 (a proposal hash embeds the entry_hash directly) ✓, the checkpoint family's derived chains (convention 19) ✓. **Reader check**: the 4 files' `chain` stays a 3-word vocabulary: `canonical` / `tenure-extension` / a derived-chain name. **Adopted: a-2**.

### K2-2. Signer identification and key records — the shape of `keys` (candidate 2)

Device keys are added to `keys` as `"<user_id>@<label>"` (with `user_id` / `label` fields), and the generator, `verify_reference.mjs`, and the harness (`vectorKeyFor(userId, fp)` / `importVectorSigner`) select the signer by **(user_id, key FP)** (a literal transcription of §6.2's "the signer is identified by (user_id, key FP)"). The alternative "make `keys` a user_id → key array" changes the shape of the 9 existing records and touches even the canonical-chain re-signing harness (breaking convention 27's invariant side), so it is rejected. Round 2: "pull by FP alone (drop user_id)" is ambiguous on the negative where a different user_id holds the same key (`reuse-removed-member-key-new-user`). Empty round ×2. **Principle**: "the harness's key resolution has the same shape as the canonical identification rule (user_id + FP)". **Adopted**.

### K2-3. The shape of verification state — remove the single-key fields from `ChainMember`? (candidate 3)

| Option | Content | Judgment |
|---|---|---|
| c-1 Keep `encPubHex` / `sigPubHex` / `keyFingerprintHex` on `ChainMember` (= the first device) and add `devices` | Minimal consumer changes | "The first device" is meaningful only while there is 1 device; after `add_device` it silently picks an arbitrary device. A landmine K3 / K4 would surely step on | Rejected |
| c-2 **Delete the single-key fields; only `devices: ReadonlyMap<FP, ChainDevice>`. Single-device-assuming callers go through `soleDeviceOf` (`undefined` unless exactly 1 device)** | The type forces full consumer enumeration (design record §5 "enumerate all consumers" — 26 files per tsc). Multi-device is fail-closed | **Adopted** |
| c-3 Make `devices` an array | FP lookup (ballots, declarations, anchors) becomes a linear scan and duplicate FPs cannot be prevented by the type | Rejected |

Round 2: "split `ChainMember` into 2 types — person and device — and hold `ChainState.devices` as a separate map" → devices belong to persons (DK-A), so they should be resolvable from the person's record; 2 maps add a consistency responsibility. Empty round ×2. **Principle**: "verification state transcribes the canonical 'per-current-member device set (FP → public key, cap)' 1:1, and keeps no legacy compat fields" — ES K2-1 (don't fold scope into 1 field) ✓. **Reader check**: an implementer about to write `member.keyFingerprintHex` is led by the type error to `soleDeviceOf`. **Adopted: c-2**.

### K2-4. The effective-authority substitution point — prevent substitution leaks via types (candidate 4)

| Option | Content | Judgment |
|---|---|---|
| d-1 Compute `min(role, cap)` / `scope ∩` at each check | A substitution leak (still passing the person's role) is invisible to the type system | Rejected |
| d-2 **Make `effectivePermissionOf(person, device)` the single computation point, returning a branded `EffectivePermission`. `ActorContext` carries only `permission`; role rules, principle 1, environment-target ops, and ballots read `actor.permission.*`. The substitution point of distribution verification (value / meta / manifest / attestation) is a single spot in `headAuthorizationReason` (`deviceStateAt` returns the effective authority)** | A person's (role, scope) lives on `ChainMember`, but only the `EffectivePermission` type can be passed to checks | **Adopted** |
| d-3 Make `ChainMember.role` itself the effective role | The person's role is an input to `change_role` / the four-eyes owner count and must not vary by device | Rejected |

Round 2: "drop `deviceStateAt` and add a device argument to `memberStateAt`" → a person's state (membership, (role, scope)) and a device's state (interval, cap) are different query units, and producing both `notMemberAtHead` and `keyMismatchAtHead` orderings (existing vectors) needs 2 queries. Empty round ×2. **Principle**: "compute effective authority in one function and close the path with a type". **Reader check (human-review spots)**: the substitution to `actor.permission.role` / `.scope` in `chain-verify.ts` (mechanical via `sed` — zero `actor.role` remain), `headAuthorizationReason` in `validate.ts`, the device determinations of `countOwnerVotes` / `approveVoteReason` / `completeProposal`. **Adopted: d-2**.

### K2-5. Device intervals in the history index (continuation of candidate 4)

Inside the membership interval (tenure), keep a per-device `[addedSeq, revokedSeq)`; `deviceStateAt(userId, fp, seq)` returns the effective authority when "member ∧ device valid". `sigKeyByFingerprint` resolves by FP across all devices including revoked ones (§6.3-1's key selection spans all intervals; validity is a later stage — the same check order as the existing tenure straddling). `remove_member` only closes the tenure and does not write the devices' `revokedSeq` (the intervals are contained in the tenure). `MemberStateAtSeq` loses the key fields and gains `devices` (the valid devices at that seq). The alternative "put devices in an interval list independent of tenure" would need a separate enforcement of "re-add starts from the first one device" (§6.2); rejected. Empty round ×2. **Adopted**.

### K2-6. The server acceptance guard (candidate 5 — applying ES K2-10's principle)

Copy "the set of ops the server accepts = the set of ops whose acceptance side effects are implemented" (ES K2-10): a layered guard at the worker handler (`DeviceOpsNotAccepted` 422) and the DO's `appendProgram` (`device-ops-not-accepted`). Composite paths structurally don't contain the 2 ops. Tests: (a) 41 device-op negatives + 2 negatives writing device ops into `set_approval_policy`'s ops = 43 cases guarded at acceptance right after canonical head 24 (the 7 wire-schema-first cases are 400); (b) the 10 negatives (generic-append side) + 3 (composite side) premised on a device derived chain cannot be replayed, so they are pinned as skips by count; (c) a dedicated test pins that validly-signed `add_device` / `revoke_device` are rejected at both worker and DO layers (`device-ops-guard.test.ts`). When K3 lifts the guard, follow the same procedure as ES K2-10 → K5 (return partition's `deviceOpsGuard` / `skipped`). **Adopted**.

### K2-7. Regenerating `master-key-wrap.json` and pinning invariance (candidate 6)

Byte invariance of the other vectors was verified by "case-name matching against the old JSON" (a scratchpad matching script): the 4 positive cases (`passkey-prf-basic` / `guardian-any-2` / `guardian-all-3` / `handoff-guardian-share`) and 18 negative cases are byte-identical; `handoff-device` is removed; `aad-kind-mismatch` is rebuilt on guardian-any-2 as base with kind → passkey-prf; `handoff-transplant-source` is → any-2's group_id. **Correction (found at ②)**: the first version (①) of `aad-kind-mismatch` kept mode `any` on the AAD swapped to passkey-prf, but §8.1 says "`mode` = guardian only; empty string otherwise", so it disagreed with the implementation (`buildMasterWrapAad`); the generator was fixed and the vector regenerated (README convention 28's wording corrected too). **Principle**: "a negative case's AAD also follows the canonical construction rules (don't make an out-of-rule AAD the material of a 'mismatch')". **Adopted**.

### K2-8. Old clients' `invalid-payload` (candidate 7)

`isKnownOp` (the own-property check of `PAYLOAD_SHAPES`) still rejects new ops, so an old client (pre-K2 crypto) rejects chains containing the new ops with `invalid-payload` (DK-J's requirement). No vector is written (a vector cannot be had against the old implementation) — record only.

### K2-9. Wrap-count estimate (candidate 8)

Unmeasured (K2 doesn't generate the 2 ops, so there is nothing to measure). **Carryover to K3**: the measurement of 16 devices × members × environments × epochs happens together with R(E)'s device expansion.

### K2-10. Bridging the consumers (candidate 9 — server / CLI's "devices stay at 1")

| Option | Content | Judgment |
|---|---|---|
| j-1 `devices.values().next()` (the first device) on the consumer side | Silently picks an arbitrary device | Rejected |
| j-2 **server: `MemberWithDevice` (`requireRole` resolves via `soleDeviceOf`; 0 / 2+ is `Effect.die` — the K2 server doesn't accept the 2 ops, so reaching it is a defect). CLI: `soleDeviceOrFail` (a typed `CliError` — a multi-device chain can legitimately arrive from a K3+ server). Key uniqueness, invitation acceptance, `server-grant`, `keyHistory`, ballots, declarations, anchors are written in device-set semantics** | The side that can be reached (CLI) gets a user-facing failure; the side that cannot (server) gets a defect | **Adopted** |
| j-3 CLI `die` too | Multi-device legitimately arises under a K3 server + K2 CLI combination (migration order J-1: server first) | Rejected |

Round 2: "leave the CLI's `wrapRecipientsFor` synchronous and ignore multi-device" → a missing wrap complete-set just becomes a 422 at the server's §12-6 recipient match, but the failure's reason is invisible to the user. Effect-ify and fail typed (`sameWrapRecipientSet` is Effect too — 2 call sites become `yield*`. **At ②, a CLI test caught one `yield*` omission**: an Effect object is truthy and the wrap-set regeneration was being skipped). Empty round ×2. **Principle**: "a single-device-assuming bridge must not stay silent when the assumption breaks (a typed failure or a defect)". **Reader check**: `member list`'s JSON `keyFingerprintHex` is as before with 1 device and joined ascending with `,` for multiple (K4 replaces it with the device list — carryover). **Adopted: j-2**.

### K2-11. Reconciling the CLI's old-device path with crypto's kind narrowing (candidate 10)

§3's split is "crypto's `master-wrap.ts` kind narrowing is K2; old-device-path removal is K4 (removing it at K3 would leave no device-migration means until K4)", so in K2 the CLI's `kind: "device"` isn't in the type. Options: (i) keep `device` in crypto — contradicts canonical §8.1 and ①'s `verify_reference.mjs` (which mechanically matches the closed set of kinds); (ii) remove the CLI's old-device path at K2 — contradicts §3's order (K4); (iii) **place `LEGACY_DEVICE_WRAP_KIND = "device" as MasterWrapKind` on the CLI side (`buildMasterWrapAad` only puts kind as a string on the LP and has no runtime check; the byte layout is per the old spec). In K4, delete it together with the constant**. Round 2: "export `legacyDeviceWrapKind()` from crypto" → a shape that puts a non-canonical kind back on crypto's public surface. Empty round ×2. **Principle**: "vocabulary not in the canonical text is confined to the side that consumes it and disappears (the CLI's K4 deletion target), not leaked to the side transcribing the canonical text (crypto)". **Adopted: (iii)** (a point to put to the owner).

### K2-12. The AUDIT mirror mapping's device 2 ops (`chainMirrorEvents` exhaustiveness)

`MIRROR_EVENT_NAME` / `mirrorTails` are exhaustive Records over `ChainOp`, so the rows are generated at K3 but the mapping is needed at K2. `chain.device_added`'s payload `deviceKeyFingerprint` requires SHA-256 (async) and cannot be computed in a synchronous mapping → the caller (K3's acceptance side effect) passes `ChainMirrorSubject.addedDeviceKeyFingerprintHex`, and a missing one throws (the same contract-violation treatment as `referencedProposal` — don't silently make an FP-less row). The alternative "copy the enc / sig public keys onto the payload" differs from AUDIT §3.4's shape. **Adopted**.

### K2-13. Change to the commit split (difference from the request §6 ordering)

Under the request's order ② crypto → ③ wire + guard → ④ follow-through, ② alone doesn't pass `tsc` (the 26-file consumers of `ChainMember`'s type change + `ChainEntrySchema`'s exhaustive check). Prioritizing the requirement that every commit pass fmt / lint / typecheck / importlint, it became 2 commits: **② = crypto + api-schema's wire types (entry schema, reason codes, the `DeviceOpsNotAccepted` declaration) + the mechanical follow-through of server / CLI**, **③ = the acceptance guard (worker + DO) + server tests**.

### K2 additional rounds (correction — 2026-09-20, the owner's finding)

**Correction**: §7's first version hadn't satisfied, at K2-2 / K2-5–K2-9 / K2-12 / K2-13, the stop condition it set itself in ES §8-bis (2 consecutive rounds producing no upward compat or silver bullet) (single rounds, or "empty round ×2" written without exploring). K2-1 / K2-3 / K2-4 / K2-10 / K2-11 also got additional rounds added. Below, for each ruling, the rounds actually run and the options that came out. **Conclusion: no option emerged that changed a ruling. 1 upward compat (K2-10 j-4 — adding a structured column to the JSON), 2 facts to record (K2-5 e-4, K2-9's estimate), 1 reinforcement of a point to put to the owner (carryover 4 — the missing vector)**.

| Ruling | Option from the additional rounds | Evaluation | Round |
|---|---|---|---|
| K2-1 | a-4 Express positive cases only via `valid_appends` (no derived chain) | A multi-stage thread (dead ballot → another device's re-vote → completion; revocation → re-registration) cannot be expressed in 1 append. A subset of a-2 | Round 3 |
| | a-5 A device-key-only second canonical-chain file (`chain-entries-dk.json`) | README conventions gain a "file" dimension; the 4 files' `chain` reference becomes "file + chain name". Not upward compat | Round 3 → rounds 4, 5 empty |
| K2-2 | b-3 Place device keys uniformly in the derived chain's `keys` (the `proposer-rekeyed` precedent) | The keys the 4 files reference are top-level `keys`; chain-local keys (`reader-second-device`) go on the derived side — the usage split follows the existing precedent, and unifying is a matter of taste (the mechanical match is the same at (user_id, FP)) | Round 2 |
| | b-4 Pull by FP alone | Ambiguous on `reuse-removed-member-key-new-user` (a different user_id holds the same key) | Round 2 → rounds 3, 4 empty |
| K2-3 | c-4 Leave `ChainMember` unchanged and hold `ChainState.devices` as a separate map | A variant of "split into 2 types"; adds a consistency responsibility (same as round 2) | Round 3 empty → round 4 empty |
| K2-4 | d-4 `headAuthorizationReason` takes `EffectivePermission` as an argument and the callers (4 verifiers) compute it | Substitution points grow 1 → 4. A step backwards | Round 3 → round 4 empty |
| K2-5 | e-2 Hold device intervals in a column independent of tenure | Needs a separate enforcement of "re-add starts from the first one device" (§6.2). Same as the first version's "alternative" | Round 2 |
| | e-3 `deviceStateAt` also returns the person's state (collapsing `headAuthorizationReason`'s 2 queries to 1) | Means expressing the distinction "not a member" vs "device invalid" (the existing order `notMemberAtHead` → `keyMismatchAtHead`) in the return's shape; the 2-query version shows the check order in the text. Equivalent, not adopted | Round 2 |
| | e-4 Limit `sigKeyByFingerprint` to devices valid at the declared head | A revoked device's signature would then become `*-unknown` rather than `*-key-mismatch-at-head`, contradicting vectors like `writer-device-revoked-at-head` (which reuse the existing reason codes — §6.3). **Recorded**: key selection spans all intervals; validity is a later stage — the canonical shape | Round 2 → rounds 3, 4 empty |
| K2-6 | f-2 A single-layer DO-only guard | Weaker than ES K2-10's layering (worker's typed 422 + DO's authority) | Round 2 |
| | f-3 Drop the 2 ops from api-schema's `ChainEntrySchema` until K3 (400) | Becomes a 400 instead of a typed 422; cannot emit the new-CLI × old-server wording (the reverse of ES K5-A's "keep on the wire") | Round 2 |
| | f-4 Place the guard after signature verification (reject only valid signatures) | Makes a "verify anyway" exception to the principle of not letting a side-effect-free op into state. The rejection reason also goes fuzzy | Round 2 → rounds 3, 4 empty |
| K2-7 | g-2 Make `aad-kind-mismatch` passkey-prf-based → swapped to guardian | Guardian requires a mode (the implementation makes mode-absent an InvalidInput), so a "kind-only swap" cannot be built in either direction under §8.1. The adopted shape (guardian-any-2 → passkey-prf, mode field empty) is the minimal difference | Round 2 |
| | g-3 Keep the old JSON as a fixture and pin byte-identity by test | Means permanently carrying the old file — repository bloat. The name set is pinned by `vector-inventory.ts`, and identity is sufficiently recorded in the PR body (convention 28) | Round 2 → rounds 3, 4 empty |
| K2-8 | h-2 Vendor the old crypto to mechanically check the rejection | Brings the old package version into test dependencies. `isKnownOp`'s own-property check is already pinned by an existing test (unknown op → invalid-payload) | Round 2 → rounds 3, 4 empty |
| K2-9 | i-2 Put an estimate instead of a measurement | **Recorded**: 1 wrap ≈ enc 32 B + ciphertext ≈ 48 B + signature 64 B + columns ≈ 250 B. 16 devices × 10 members × 5 environments × 10 retained epochs = 8,000 rows ≈ 2 MB (inside §12-8's DO guard). An upper-bound estimate, so it doesn't replace K3's measurement | Round 2 → rounds 3, 4 empty |
| K2-10 | **j-4 Add a structured `deviceKeyFingerprintsHex: string[]` to `member list --json` (`keyFingerprintHex` stays for compatibility)** | **Upward compat (adopted)**: don't make consumers split the joined string. The existing field is unchanged, and it doesn't obstruct K4's replacement with a device list | Round 3 |
| | j-5 Show the device count in the human-readable row | In K2 — where devices are always 1 — it carries no information. K4 | Round 4 → rounds 5, 6 empty |
| K2-11 | k-4 Export the old kind via crypto's `test-support` | The shape of product code importing a test-support surface. A forbidden line | Round 3 |
| | k-5 Brand `MasterWrapKind` so only a CLI-local module can construct the old kind | Just writing (iii)'s shape with a heavier type | Round 3 → rounds 4, 5 empty |
| K2-12 | l-2 Make `mirrorTails` / `chainMirrorEvents` async and compute the FP internally | Changes the signature of a pure mapping (the CLI's `audit verify` runs the same function synchronously), rippling into ES K5's bijectivity-rule tests. Passing the FP from the caller is smaller | Round 2 |
| | l-3 Copy the enc / sig public keys onto the payload and don't copy the FP | Contradicts AUDIT §3.4's shape (`deviceKeyFingerprint`) | Round 2 → rounds 3, 4 empty |
| K2-13 | m-2 Make ② crypto-only and allow 1 red typecheck commit | Contradicts the request's requirement (each commit passes) | Round 2 |
| | m-3 Temporarily keep a deprecated compat getter on `ChainMember` and fix consumers later | Temporarily creates c-1 (fail-open "first device"). Rejected | Round 2 → rounds 3, 4 empty |

**Reinforcement of carryover 4 (a point to put to the owner)**: `revoke_device`'s role rules toward others were implemented as "the actor's effective role is admin-or-above, and if the target is admin / owner then owner" (the 2 stages of `remove_member` — the strict reading). A negative where the difference with the lenient reading (only the target-dependent stage = a member can revoke a member's device) surfaces isn't in the vectors (the only member with 2 devices inside `device-ops` is allmember-0013 themself, and the only member actor is devmember-0010 — in the shape where devmember revokes allmember's C at the `device-dead-vote` head, the strict reading gives `insufficient-role`, the lenient reading `scope-not-contained`). Once the owner's reading is fixed, append that negative to pin it (only appending to the generator — the canonical chain unchanged).

### Carryovers to the canonical texts (K2 — normative sentences unchanged)

1. CRYPTO §11's 0.12-draft item: write `aad-kind-mismatch`'s rebuild as "kind swap (mode field empty for passkey-prf)" (K2-7).
2. The relation between README convention 28 (corrected in this PR) and §8.1 is as above — no canonical change.
3. AUDIT §3.4 `chain.device_added`'s `deviceKeyFingerprint`: the implementation shape where the accepting side computes the FP and passes it to the mapping (K2-12) — no spec change needed, but a candidate note for K3's implementer.
4. §6.2 "check ordering"'s `revoke_device`: "target-dependent role rules (self passes; others `insufficient-role`)" was implemented as "the actor's effective role is admin-or-above → if the target is admin / owner then owner" (the same 2 stages as `remove_member`) (pinned by the K2 vectors `authz-revoke-device-member-revokes-admin` / `-admin-revokes-owner` / `-reader-revokes-other`).

### Carryovers to K3

- The acceptance-guard removal procedure (K2-6); `DeviceOpsNotAccepted` stays on the wire (same as ES K5-A).
- Replace `MemberWithDevice` (server) with "the request's device (AUTH §6 token ↔ device key)". `dek-wraps.ts`'s recipient check `soleDeviceOf(member)?.encPubHex` goes into R(E)'s device expansion.
- Pass `add_device`'s FP via `chainMirrorEvents(entry, serverTs, index, { addedDeviceKeyFingerprintHex })` (K2-12).
- The wrap-count measurement (K2-9).
- CLI (K4): all `soleDeviceOrFail` call sites (grep `device-key.ts`), `LEGACY_DEVICE_WRAP_KIND` removal, `member list`'s device list, `wrapRecipientsFor`'s device expansion, `keyHistory`'s device key. **Consistency of the four-eyes announcement layer and tally layer** (PR #186 pullfrog finding): `approval-rules.ts`'s `eligibleApprovers` / `voteEligibility` at K2 read the same predicate as the tally layer (`countedVoters`) — "effective owner on some device" (`ownerOnAnyDevice`) — but at K4, narrow to the effective role of the signing device (the local key) — don't prompt an approve from a cap < owner device.

## 8. K3 addendum (2026-09-20 — server: acceptance-guard removal + the device 2 ops' acceptance side effects + R(E)'s device expansion + the device-registry API)

Rulings made in K3's implementation PR. Like §7, recorded as: candidate table (3+ options) → rounds (stopped after **2 consecutive** rounds producing no upward compat or silver bullet; rounds that produced something are "rounds with content") → principle extraction (✓ / ✗ against CRYPTO §1's principles 1–7, D1 / D2, ES / KL3 precedents) → reader check (self-host admins, K4's implementer, reviewers) → adopt. **The canonical texts' normative sentences are unchanged** (ambiguities are implemented fail-closed and enumerated in the closing "carryovers to the canonical texts"). Numbers and names copied from the canonical texts (CRYPTO_SPEC 0.12-draft §6.2 / §6.4 / §7, AUTH_SPEC 0.24-draft §5 / §6 / §11-1 / §12-3 / §12-6 / §12-8 / §13-6 / §13-11 / §16-1, AUDIT_SPEC 1.9-draft §3.4 / §4.1). Rulings are recorded **before** implementation; points that changed mid-implementation are corrected by addendum in the closing "implementation record" (not erased).

### K3-1. Identifying "the request's device" — the replacement for `MemberWithDevice` (enumeration 1 round + a round-2 with content + 2 empty rounds · stopped)

K2's carryover was "replace with the request's device (AUTH §6 token ↔ device key)", but AUTH §13-11 / §6 (K1-15) define the registry's `tokenId` as advisory — **not an authorization input**. Since the wires of signature-bearing data operations (value push, meta, manifest, declarations, DEK-wrap registration) don't carry the signer's FP (§12-5 "the calling principal = the signer" — signature identification is by user_id only), the device must be resolved from other material.

| Option | Content | Judgment |
|---|---|---|
| a-1 Resolve via the registry's `tokenId` → device FP | Look up the presented token's id in the registry | Makes the advisory an authorization input (§13-11 / K1-15's forbidden line). Rejected |
| a-2 Add the signer's FP to the wire (`writerKeyFingerprintHex` etc.) | The verification key is determined in 1 try | Adds an encoding the canonical wires (§12-2 / §12-5 / §16-1 / §12-6) don't have (request §1's "bring in no new encodings"). Transmissions from old CLIs (pre-K2) become 400, breaking the skew requirement (§3's order "server → CLI"). Rejected |
| a-3 **Resolve from the signature: try the calling principal's **valid devices** (≤ 16 — §12-8) in FP ascending order; the device whose signature verifies is "this signature's device"**. Operations without signatures (reads, deletes, flag withdrawal, schemaPolicy) carry no device and are judged on the **person's authority** | By key uniqueness (§6.2 — enc / sig both unique across a current member's devices), at most 1 device verifies, and the result doesn't depend on the trial order. Old CLI input passes as-is (with 1 device it's 1 trial = the same computation as now) | **Adopted** |
| a-4 Stay on the 1-device assumption (change `soleDeviceOf`'s defect into a typed 422) | Minimal | A multi-device member could not do data operations = contradicts K3's purpose (guard removal). Rejected |

**Round 2 (upward compat / silver bullet)**: "to keep trials to 1, first use the registry's `tokenId` as a **hint**, and if it misses, try all devices" → since the authorization conclusion is settled by the signature, the advisory's forbidden line isn't touched, but the response time varies on hint hit/miss, and if a registry row is forged only specific devices go slow (a side channel). What it saves is at most 15 Ed25519 verifications, and push / meta operations are low-frequency. **Not upward compat**. "For DEK-wrap registration (up to 10,000 rows), resolve the device on the first row and verify the rest with that key only" → **a round with content (adopted — a-3's implementation shape)**: don't do all-rows × all-devices. "Start the trial order with 'the device of the last accepted signature'" → the savings are the same for the added complexity of keeping state (the recent device). Rejected. **Round 3**: "let the device's cap apply to signature-free operations too (bind the token to a device)" → AUTH §6 defines "the token↔device correspondence is the registry (advisory)"; binding is a canonical-text revision. Outside K3's scope (carryover). Not a new option. **Empty round**. **Round 4**: no new option. **Empty round**. Stopped.

**The 2 stages of authorization (consistency with §12-3's determination order)**: since the device is only settled at signature verification (the semantic-check stage), to emit the role / scope 403 before the existence 404 per the determination order, check **stage 1 = the person's (role, scope)** (a device's effective authority doesn't exceed the person's, so whatever fails on the person fails on the device = an upper bound), then **stage 2 = the effective authority of the device settled by the signature** (§6.2 "performed against the signing device's effective authority"). Stage-2 shortfalls are rejected with the same reason codes (`insufficient-role` / `insufficient-scope` — 403). A cap-limited device may observe the environment's existence (404) before failing stage 2, but environments are chain-derived and known to all members (§12-3), so no information leaks. Composite (create / rotate) and standalone checkpoint entries name the device via the bundled entry's `actor.keyFingerprintHex`, so no trial is needed; place stage 2 immediately after stage 1 (before the existence check).
**Principle**: "the authority of a signature-bearing operation is the signing device's effective authority; the authority of a signature-free operation is judged on the person's authority — device identification is derived only from the signature, and no advisory correspondence table is an input". CRYPTO §1 principle 6 (semantics live inside the signature bytes) ✓, principle 7 / D1 ✓, AUTH §13-11's advisory ✓, K1-15 (token-revocation separation) ✓, ES K3-C (determination order role → scope → existence) ✓ — stage 1 stands in the same order. No ✗. **Reader check**: K4's implementer need not add anything on the CLI side (no change putting an FP on the wire). A reviewer confirms "unique regardless of trial order" from the key-uniqueness consensus rule (§6.2). Invisible to self-host admins. **Adopted: a-3** (DEK registration resolves on the first row).

### K3-2. `dek_wraps` primary-key migration and the treatment of existing rows (enumeration 1 round + 2 empty rounds · stopped)

| Option | Content | Judgment |
|---|---|---|
| b-1 **Rebuild the table** (create a new table with PK = (environment_id, epoch, recipient_user_id, recipient_enc_pub_hex), `INSERT … SELECT` all rows over, DROP the old table → RENAME) — a DO migration tail step | Existing rows (unique under the old PK) stay unique under the new PK, so lossless. Every rotation / environment-creation path writes to the new table as-is | **Adopted** |
| b-2 Just add a 4-column UNIQUE index | Short DDL | The old PK (3 columns) remains and rejects second-device rows. Rejected |
| b-3 Discard existing rows and replace with an empty table (same shape as ES's invitations) | Simple | Wraps are decryptable live rows; discarding them leaves every environment "members exist but no wraps". Rejected |
| b-4 Keep the PK and split per-device into another table (`dek_wraps_devices`) | Old table unchanged | Every query of distribution / exact-match / cleanup becomes a union of 2 tables. Rejected |

**Round 2**: "it's a rowid table, so look for `ALTER TABLE … ADD PRIMARY KEY`" → SQLite has no primary-key ALTER. Not a new option. "Make the rebuild lazy (on first access)" → unlike the lazy materialization of audit head columns (AUDIT §5.1), a primary key's shape must be fixed synchronously or writes cannot happen. Rejected. **Empty round**. **Round 3**: empty round. Stopped. **Principle**: "a primary-key change is done by rebuilding the table; don't discard live rows (the discard precedent is limited to rows that become undecryptable — ES's invitations)" — do-schema.ts's tail-append rule ✓, the partial-index step (a DROP → CREATE rebuild) ✓. **Reader**: a self-host admin applies it automatically via `bun run deploy` (one line under SELF_HOSTING's Updates). A row-count-proportional step (a blast-radius note like partial indexing's). **Adopted: b-1**. The storage key (acceptance-stage dedup `wrapStorageKey`) is also extended to (epoch, id, enc public key) — the treatment of recipient-class-straddling collisions (A-1) is unchanged (same id + same key = 1 slot).

### K3-3. The shape of the deletion reference `DekWrapRef` and 409 `DekWrapExists` (enumeration 1 round + 2 empty rounds · stopped)

Once the slot is on the device axis, an (environment, epoch, user_id) reference can point at multiple rows.

| Option | Content | Judgment |
|---|---|---|
| c-1 Make `recipientEncPubHex` required | Unique | A breaking wire change — K2 CLIs' repair path (`member.ts`'s `deks.remove`) becomes 400, breaking the skew requirement. Rejected |
| c-2 When omitted, delete **all rows** for that (epoch, user_id) (the old "recipient = person" semantics) | Old-CLI compatible | With 2+ devices, 1 reference silently deletes multiple rows (audit rows do come out per row, but the caller meant 1). Rejected |
| c-3 **Make `recipientEncPubHex` optional; when omitted, delete only when there is **exactly 1** row. If multiple, 422 `duplicate-recipient` (an existing reason code — "the reference names multiple recipient slots")** | Members with 1 device behave as before; multi-device forces explicitness (the don't-silently-succeed discipline — §12-6's deletion family) | **Adopted** |
| c-4 Reference deletions by FP rather than enc public key | FP in display | Stored rows are identified by enc public key (§12-6). A revoked device's wraps (not cleaned — §12-6) have no FP→key correspondence in the current chain state. Rejected |

**Round 2**: "carry the device list on the 409 `DekWrapExists`" → now that the primary key is the device axis, a 409 only fires when a slot for **the same key** is occupied (old-key wraps are a different slot = they don't block new-key registration), so `storedRecipientEncPubHex` always equals the sent key, and its job as determination material is just "registered = idempotent". Keep carrying it (wire unchanged). Not a new option. **Empty round**. **Round 3**: empty round. Stopped. **Principle**: "a wire reference adds the device axis as a backward-compatible optional field, and an ambiguous reference is not silently interpreted broadly" — §12-6's "don't silently succeed" ✓, DekWrapExists's `storedRecipientEncPubHex` (adding an optional field — Wave 2 B2) ✓. **Reader**: K4's CLI always sends `recipientEncPubHex` (carryover). **Adopted: c-3** (a point to put to the owner: adding an optional field to the wire).

### K3-4. R(E)'s device expansion and how the expected count is counted (enumeration 1 round + 2 empty rounds · stopped)

| Option | Content | Judgment |
|---|---|---|
| d-1 **Count R(E) = { (m, d) \| E ∈ scope(m) ∩ scope(d) } ∪ grant as (id, enc public key) pairs; the recipient check runs in the order "user_id is a current member → enc public key is that person's valid device → E ∈ effectivePermissionOf(person, device).scope"** | A literal implementation of DK-E's single predicate "identification (id + key) ∧ E ∈ effective scope". The effective scope comes from crypto's `effectivePermissionOf` (the single computation point — K2-4) | **Adopted** |
| d-2 Keep 1 slot per person (the first device) | Minimal change | Adding a device would not deliver the DEK = the negation of DK. Rejected |
| d-3 1 slot per person under "some device contains E" | Fewer | HPKE needs a separate wrap per device key (§5's info is the person but the key differs). Rejected |

**Round 2**: "approximate the expected count as members × devices" → drops the device-scope ∩ person-scope check (a negation of cap). Rejected. "Resolve the storage-key class-straddling collision (A-1) on the device axis" → with the key now in the primary key, even if a member's user_id collides with a server's FP, different keys make different slots (a relaxation). But same id + same key is still 1 slot, and the expected-count dedup (storage-key granularity) is kept. Not a new option. **Empty round**. **Round 3**: empty round. Stopped. **Principle**: "R(E) membership is the single predicate 'identification (id + key) ∧ E ∈ effective scope' across recipient classes" (DK-E) ✓ derivation, ES K3-D ✓. **Reader**: the shape where the 2 functions `expectedWrapRecipientCount` / `checkWrapRecipient` read the same predicate is unchanged (the reason-code order = identification → key → scope is also unchanged). **Adopted: d-1**.

### K3-5. Where the reader self-backfill check lives (enumeration 1 round + 2 empty rounds · stopped)

| Option | Content | Judgment |
|---|---|---|
| e-1 **A stage before `registerDekWrapsProgram`: lower stage 1's role floor to reader, and unless "all wraps have recipient class member ∧ recipientUserId = the calling principal ∧ enc public key ∈ the calling principal's valid device keys", require person role ≥ member (403 `insufficient-role`). Stage 2 (the signing device's effective authority) re-judges role (member-or-above if not self) and scope (E ∈ effective scope — 403 `insufficient-scope`)** | Places the §12-3 table's row at the same position in the determination order (the role stage). "One's own device key" is judged by id + key (by id alone, a wrap addressed to another's key is mistaken as "to self") | **Adopted** |
| e-2 Branch inside the recipient check (`checkWrapRecipient` — 422) | One place | Puts 403 behind 422 and breaks the determination order (§12-3). Rejected |
| e-3 A reader-only endpoint | Explicit | A wire addition. A duplication of the same acceptance rule. Rejected |

**Round 2**: "make the self-check id-only and leave key mismatch to the 422" → a reader sending own id + another's key just becomes 422 (no capability gained), but it deviates from the normative letter "every recipient is one's own device key". e-1 is closer to the letter. Not a new option. **Empty round**. **Round 3**: empty round. Stopped. **Principle**: "a check determined at the role stage goes in the role stage (don't break the determination order across reason codes)" — ES K3-C ✓, K3-1's 2 stages ✓. **Reader**: a human-review-mandatory spot (request §5) — the predicate is confined to the one function `allRecipientsAreOwnDevices`. **Adopted: e-1**.

### K3-6. How `DeviceLimit` (16) is counted and where it's judged (enumeration 1 round + 2 empty rounds · stopped)

| Option | Content | Judgment |
|---|---|---|
| f-1 **Count the actor's **valid** devices in the pre-acceptance derived state; if ≥ 16, 422 `DeviceLimit` (not on the worker — in the DO's appendProgram — the same position as the four-eyes pending cap: after membership, before CAS / verifyChain)** | The letter of §12-8 "valid devices / member / project". Revoked ones are not counted (released) | **Adopted** |
| f-2 Count registry rows | Judgeable at D1 | The registry is advisory (cross-project, includes revoked rows). §12-8's unit is the project. Rejected |
| f-3 Put it in verifyChain (the consensus rule) | One place | An acceptance policy, not a consensus rule (§6.4). Rejected |

**Round 2**: "`add_device` via four-eyes" → `add_device` cannot be proposed (§6.2 — `approval-not-required`), so the path is direct append only. Not a new option. "Composite paths" → device ops don't ride composites. **Empty round**. **Round 3**: empty round. Stopped. **Principle**: "an acceptance policy is judged from the derived state inside the DO's serialization, placed before semantic checking" — ES K5-B ✓. **Adopted: f-1**. Wire: declare `DeviceLimitError` (422 `{ limit }` — the name from AUTH §12-8) on `append`.

### K3-7. The device axis of declaration rows and the deletion timing (enumeration 1 round + 2 empty rounds · stopped)

| Option | Content | Judgment |
|---|---|---|
| g-1 **Rebuild `head_attestations`' PK to (attester_user_id, attester_key_fingerprint). A submission upserts the row of the device resolved at K3-1 (seq monotonicity applies only to the same device's row). `revoke_device`'s acceptance side effect deletes the revoked FP's row; `remove_member` deletes all of the target's rows + the window row. The window (60/hour) stays per-member** | The letter of §16-1 / §6.6 | **Adopted** |
| g-2 Keep 1 row per member (seq monotonicity across devices) | No change | A lagging device's declaration becomes 409 (approval item 14's rejected option). Rejected |
| g-3 Do deletion only as a distribution-time filter (keep the rows) | No side effect | Contradicts the storage-convergence discipline (§6.4 — same shape as old-key wrap cleanup). Rejected |

**Round 2**: "delete the revoked device's declaration row **before** `revoke_device`'s mirror row" → the order is irrelevant (declaration rows aren't audit input). Keep the existing mirror → side-effect order. Not a new option. **Empty round**. **Round 3**: empty round. Stopped. **Principle**: "the set of distributed rows converges to chain-derived truth, deleted on revocation acceptance" (§6.4) ✓. **Reader**: the distribution-side filter (current member) gains "the current member's valid devices" (an independent defense layer — same shape as existing). **Adopted: g-1**.

### K3-8. The device-add-request state machine, TTL, and rate window (enumeration 1 round + 2 empty rounds · stopped)

| Option | Content | Judgment |
|---|---|---|
| h-1 **No state column. A row = "an unconsumed request". TTL 15 minutes (`expires_at`); revoked rows are hidden on read and opportunistically deleted at creation / listing. After approval the client DELETEs (§13-11's letter). For the rate, add a `device-request` kind to `key_wrap_windows`'s fixed window at 5 / hour / user (reusing KL3's window). The registry is 32 rows per user (429)** | §13-11's table verbatim. Doesn't grow the window implementation | **Adopted** |
| h-2 A state column (pending / approved / consumed) | History survives | Approval is a chain op and the registry isn't used for verification (§13-11). Holding state creates a "server-declared approval state" and blurs the advisory line. Rejected |
| h-3 Count the rate by `created_at` rows | No new table | Rows disappear on consume/cancel, so create → cancel loops escape the window. Rejected |

**Round 2**: "approval devices read the request per project" → the registry is per-user (§13-11). Not a new option. "Give the request row a `tokenId`" → the registry's optional column (§13-11) belongs to registry rows. Requests don't have it. **Empty round**. **Round 3**: empty round. Stopped. **Principle**: "don't put a state machine on an advisory register (the truth is a chain op)" — DK-F ✓, §13-11 ✓. **Adopted: h-1**.

### K3-9. The wire shape of the new `devices` group — error types and FP recomputation (enumeration 1 round + 2 empty rounds · stopped)

| Option | Content | Judgment |
|---|---|---|
