| i-1 **4 types in `errors/devices.ts`: `DeviceNotFound` (404 — a uniform 404 for registry / requests) / `DeviceRegistryConflict` (409 `{ reason: "device-registered" \| "request-exists" }`) / `DeviceRegistryLimit` (429 `{ reason: "device-rows" \| "add-requests", limit }`) / `DeviceFingerprintMismatch` (400 — `:fp` ≠ the FP recomputed from the body's public key). Authorization: `ensureKeyMaterialAccess` (`*` × admin token — the same level as §13-2) on every surface except `GET /auth/devices`; `GET /auth/devices` goes into §5's allowed enumeration** | Transcribes §13-11's HTTP-status table. 403 is the existing `Forbidden` | **Adopted** |
| i-2 Reuse the existing `TokenLimit` / `KeyWrapRateLimited` | No new types | The vocabulary (the window's closed set) doesn't fit; reusing wouldn't use canonical names. Rejected |
| i-3 Fold status into one `DeviceRegistryError` | 1 type | HttpApi is 1 type = 1 status. §13-11 defines 4 statuses. Rejected |

**Round 2**: "fix the FP from the body alone, not `:fp`, and make the path a display name" → §13-11's path is `:fp` (matching is spec). Not a new option. "Register into the registry at the same time the request row is consumed (the approving device does PUT + DELETE in 1 call)" → a wire addition folding 2 surfaces into 1. Rejected. **Empty round**. **Round 3**: empty round. Stopped. **Principle**: "the defaults of a newly-added surface are session-forbidden, key-material-class token conditions (§5 / §13-2), and statuses copied from the spec's table" ✓. **Adopted: i-1** (a point to put to the owner: the error-type names aren't in the canonical text — §13-11 defines only statuses).

### K3-10. Guardian-segment device rows — primary key, logical-segment structural checks, distribution shape (enumeration 1 round + a round-2 with content + 2 empty rounds · stopped)

| Option | Content | Judgment |
|---|---|---|
| j-1 **PK (group_id, share_index, guardian_key_fingerprint_hex) + UNIQUE (group_id, guardian_user_id, guardian_key_fingerprint_hex) (§13-6's DDL). The structural check is over **logical segments** = distinct (share_index, guardian_user_id) covering 1..n · the same share_index is the same guardian · a (share_index, FP) duplicate is `duplicate-guardian`. The array cap is 5 × 16 = 80 rows. `wards` is 1 row per logical segment; `myShare` returns **all of one's device rows**** | The letter of §13-6 / §13-9 | **Adopted (the distribution shape is in round 2 below)** |
| j-2 Put device rows in a separate table | Old table unchanged | The segment set becomes 2 tables. Rejected |
| j-3 Seal only to the guardian's reserve key | 1 row | Already rejected in DK-G (approval happens on everyday devices). Rejected |

**Round 2 (a round with content)**: `GET /auth/guardian/shares/:groupId`'s response `GuardianShareResult` has the shape of 1 segment (§13-9 doesn't define a device-row distribution shape). Options: (i) return the calling device's row — a device cannot be identified on a signature-less GET (K3-1's principle); (ii) **put the FP-ascending first row in the conventional fields, and all device rows in an optional field `deviceShares: [{ guardianKeyFingerprintHex, guardianEncPubHex, encHex, ciphertextHex }]`** (an old CLI = with 1 device the first row = the only row, as before. K4's CLI picks its local FP from `deviceShares`); (iii) make the response an array — a breaking wire change. **Adopt (ii)** (a point to put to the owner: adding an optional field). **Round 3**: "make the first row 'the most recently added device'" → the ledger doesn't hold registration order. Rejected. **Empty round**. **Round 4**: empty round. Stopped. **Principle**: "device-axis distribution keeps the single-device shape and carries extras in an optional field that grows only when there are multiple" — same as K3-3, DekWrapExists's optional field ✓. **Adopted: j-1 + (ii)**.

### K3-11. `revoke_device`'s detection variant — deriving the device's window (enumeration 1 round + 2 empty rounds · stopped)

| Option | Content | Judgment |
|---|---|---|
| k-1 **Device validity interval = the latest `chain.device_added` (whose payload FP matches) ~ the triggering `chain.device_revoked`. An FP absent from every `device_added` (= the first key of `add_member` / `genesis`) starts at the membership interval's start. Candidates = that interval ∩ the person's per-environment access windows (existing window derivation) ∩ the device scope (`device_added`'s payload; the first key is all). (a) matches `var.read` rows of actor.user_id within the interval (per AUDIT §4.1's K1-12 correction — no FP matching). A scope-empty device has zero candidates = no rows. `trigger = revoke_device`; the payload carries the target user_id and the revoked FP set** | §4.1's letter. Shares the existing window derivation (`accessWindows`), adding device scope as a third intersection | **Adopted** |
| k-2 Hold per-device windows independent of the person's | Simple | Would mistake a period closed by a person's scope shrink as "the device saw it". Rejected |
| k-3 Match (a) by `actor_key_fingerprint` | Precise | `var.read` has no FP (K1-12). Rejected |

**Round 2**: "when there are multiple revoked FPs, union the windows and fold into 1 row" → since the granularity is (variable × environment) 1 row (§3.3), multiple devices' windows union into 1 row = a consequence of the adopted option. Not a new option. "Push (a) onto the device via the registry's `tokenId`" → rejected by owner ruling (K1-12). **Empty round**. **Round 3**: empty round. Stopped. **Principle**: "detection leans to the side that doesn't miss (a row whose device scope cannot be read is all; a device whose FP isn't found starts at membership start)" — ES K3-F ✓. **Adopted: k-1**.

### K3-12. How to un-skip tests and the shape of skew tests (enumeration 1 round + 2 empty rounds · stopped)

| Option | Content | Judgment |
|---|---|---|
| l-1 **Per K2-6's procedure: remove the `deviceOpsGuard` branch, remove `prefixReplayable`, and replay device derived chains via `replayNegativePrefix` (accepted via generic append — the acceptance itself pins §6.2's permissive side). partition: `skipped` 0 · `consensus` 150 · `wireSchema` 15. The composite side's 3 cases go to `compositeExpectations` (reader-cap rotate = 403 `insufficient-role`, listed-device rotate / create = 403 `insufficient-scope` — K3-1's stage 2 runs before verifyChain). `device-ops-guard.test.ts` is replaced by acceptance-side-effect tests (`device-ops.test.ts`). skew: (a) all existing server tests (1 device) keep passing, (b) explicit tests — with a 1-device chain, environment-creation bundled wraps, backfill registration, repair deletion (a reference without enc public key), declarations, and value pushes keep passing after the primary-key change** | The same procedure as ES K5 | **Adopted** |
| l-2 Write the derived chain directly into the DO, skipping replay | Fast | Loses the acceptance-path pinning (the permissive side). Rejected |
| l-3 Leave skew to crypto-layer tests only | Less | The primary-key change is at the server layer. Rejected |

**Round 2**: "actually run an old API client (K2's CLI) in the skew test" → CLI tests are Vitest (normal environment) and cannot reach workerd's SELF. Since existing server tests' inputs have the same shape as what a K2 CLI produces (1 device, the FP is the first key), (a)+(b) suffice. Not a new option. **Empty round**. **Round 3**: empty round. Stopped. **Adopted: l-1**.

### K3-13. Fact confirmation (single round)

- **`add_member` re-add cleanup is unchanged**: re-add starts from 1 device (§6.2), so "keys addressed to devices not in the new device set" = `deleteStaleMemberWraps(user, added enc public key)` as before. Not deleted on `revoke_device` / `remove_member` (§12-6).
- **`add_device`'s acceptance side effect is the mirror only** (§6.4). The accepting side computes the FP via `computeUserKeyFingerprint(enc, sig)` and passes it to `chainMirrorEvents` (K2-12). No four-eyes path (not proposable).
- **`revoke_device`'s worker-side token level**: `add_device` and self-target `revoke_device` = write; others = admin (§6 / §11-1 — `requiredPermissionForEntry`'s branch).
- **Chain-fetch distribution filter**: declarations distribute only rows of "a current member's valid devices" (an independent defense layer).
- **Session-rejection enumeration**: only `devices.list` is added to `SESSION_ALLOWED_ENDPOINTS`. The 6 write surfaces are outside the enumeration (default deny) + `ensureKeyMaterialAccess`'s second layer. strict classification: the registry PUT and request POST carry key material (public keys) but are not signed structures, ciphertext, or secrets → `STRICT_EXEMPT`? — **placed on the strict side** (§12-10 (1)'s "security-critical acceptance schemas" = public-key registration is a key-declaration class. The side that doesn't silently drop unknown fields is safer).
- **Don't remove the handoff old-device path (`source = "device"`) at K3** (§3 — together with the CLI at K4).

### K3 implementation record (deviations/additions from rulings — corrected without erasure)

- **K3-1's implementation shape**: `withSigningDevice(member, attempt)` (tries the calling principal's valid devices in FP ascending order, retrying on the next device only for `signature-invalid`-family rejections; other rejections return as-is) / `deviceOf(person, fp)` (chain entries, composites, checkpoints name it via `actor.keyFingerprintHex`) / `ensureDevicePermission(device, minimum, environmentId?)` (stage 2 — 403 `insufficient-role` / `insufficient-scope`). DEK registration resolves the device on the first row and verifies the rest with that key (`ensureWrapSignatures` returns the signing device).
- **The surfaces where stage 2 is observable are limited to composites, checkpoints, and DEK registration**: on value push / meta / manifest / declarations, the crypto layer's "authorization at the declared head" (CRYPTO §6.3 — replaced by `deviceStateAt`'s effective authority in K2) runs **first** as part of signature verification, so a cap-short device settles at 422 `chain-head-state-mismatch` (the mapping of `writer-role-insufficient-at-head` / `-environment-out-of-scope-at-head`). The server's stage 2 (403) is an unreachable defense layer on these surfaces (cap is immutable and the person's role is upper-bounded by stage 1, so an input that passes at the declared head and fails at the current state cannot be constructed). `device-ops.test.ts` pins this shape (ruling K3-1's "same reason code 403" is right for composites / checkpoints / DEK registration; on value surfaces it's 422 — carryover to the canonical text 7).
- **K3-3 addition**: not only the reference (`DekWrapRef`) but also the **response** (`RecipientDek` — `GET /deks` / pull's `deks`) gained the optional `recipientEncPubHex`. The response carries rows for all the person's devices, so the recipient needs material to pick "the row for my key" (a failed open of another device's row isn't a poisoned wrap). Old servers omit it (1 device = every row is mine); old CLIs ignore it (optional field).
- **K3-4's class-straddling collision**: as round 2 described, even when a member's user_id = a server's FP, different keys make different slots (updated `data-dek-server.test.ts`'s collision test to "different key = both slots stored; same id + same key = 422 `duplicate-recipient`").
- **K3-6**: in DO `appendProgram`'s `add_device` branch, valid devices ≥ 16 before `ensureParentHead` (CAS) → rejected with `device-limit` (worker-side 422 `DeviceLimit { limit }`).
- **K3-12's replay**: the test support `signAs` changed to resolve the vector key (including device keys) by the actor block's FP and sign with it (a user_id key cannot reproduce a derived chain's device signatures. An unknown FP falls back to the user_id key = the semantics of FP-mismatch negatives unchanged). `useDeviceKey(user, keyName)` / `resetDeviceKeys()` switch the signing device.
- **K3-9**: the registry constants (32 / 5 / 15 minutes) are unified in api-schema's `devices-api.ts` (not in server's policy.ts — fallow's unused detection). D1's `devices` / `device_add_requests` go into the tests' `AUTH_TABLES` (FK children → parents order).
- **K3-10**: `guardianPolicyViolation`'s device-row folding (`indexLogicalShares`) was separated out (complexity).
- **Mechanical follow-through**: `"revoke_device"` on the CLI's `RotationFlagView.trigger` (type only — wording is K4).

### Wrap-count measurement (replaces K2-9's estimate)

Measured the `storage.sql.databaseSize` delta on a project DO in the workerd real environment (`@cloudflare/vitest-plugin`) (2026-09-20).

| Method | Rows | Delta | Per row |
|---|---|---|---|
| Direct INSERT (5 environments × 10 epochs × 10 members × 16 devices; columns are real-size dummy hex: enc 64 / ciphertext 96 / signature 128 / key 64 chars) | 8,000 | 5,066,752 B (≈ 4.83 MiB) | **≈ 633 B** |
| Real path (1 environment creation with 16 owner devices = 18 slots. 15 chain appends + audit rows included) | 18 | 16,384 B (page granularity) | ≈ 910 B (all-in) |

K2-9's estimate (1 row ≈ 250 B → 8,000 rows ≈ 2 MB) was **about 2.5× too small** (it hadn't accounted for the primary-key index [a 4-column composite] and the hex string representation [2× the bytes]). Still, at the upper-bound assumption (8,000 rows) it's ≈ 4.8 MiB, and even at §12-8's cumulative cap of 1,000,000 rows it's ≈ 633 MB — inside the DO's 9 GB guard (SELF_HOSTING "Per-project storage guard"). What binds under 16 devices × many people × long retention is the row-count cap (§12-8); there is no reason to change the number at K3.

### Carryovers to the canonical texts (K3 — normative sentences unchanged)

1. **AUTH §13-11's error-type names** (wire `_tag`): `DeviceNotFound` (404 — registry deletion / request query / cancel), `DeviceRegistryConflict` (409 `{ reason: "request-exists" | "device-registered" }`), `DeviceRegistryLimit` (429 `{ reason: "device-rows" | "add-requests", limit, retryAfterSeconds? }`), `DeviceFingerprintMismatch` (400 — mismatch between the path's `:fp` and the FP recomputed from the body's public key). Whether to write type names in the table or keep the existing format (status code + reason) is the canonical text's call.
2. **AUTH §12-6's device-axis fields** (optional — K3-3): `DekWrapRef.recipientEncPubHex` (when omitted, deletes only when that (epoch, recipient) has exactly 1 row; multiple = 422 `duplicate-recipient`) and `RecipientDek.recipientEncPubHex` (the response carries rows for all the person's devices. **Since it is an input to the registration-signature context [CRYPTO §5.1], the recipient reads in the order: pick the row by this value → verify the signature → open — K3-14**). Old client input is unchanged.
3. **AUTH §12-6's class-straddling collision**: the storage key is now (environment, epoch, id, enc public key); a member's user_id = a server's FP makes different slots if the keys differ (only same id + same key is 1 slot). A note candidate.
4. **AUTH §13-6 / §13-7's segment rows**: primary key (group, share_index, guardian device FP), `GuardianShareResult.deviceShares` (optional — per-device re-sealed values), 16 device rows per guardian (the same number as §12-8), a group row cap of 80 (= 5 guardians × 16).
5. **AUDIT §4.1's `revoke_device` variant**: `rotation.recommended`'s payload gains `revokedDeviceKeyFingerprints` (the revoked FP list), `trigger: "revoke_device"`. The device window = from the latest `chain.device_added` before the trigger (FP match) to the trigger (the first key starts at membership start) ∩ the person's membership interval ∩ the device scope.
6. **§6.2 check ordering's `revoke_device`**: per K2 carryover 4 (the strict reading — the same 2 stages as `remove_member`). If the owner hasn't answered, keep as-is.
7. **§6.3 / §12-3 note candidate**: value pushes / meta / manifests / declarations from a cap-limited device become 422 `chain-head-state-mismatch` at "authorization at the declared head" (not 403 `insufficient-role`). Composites / checkpoints / DEK registration are 403.
8. **§12-8's DeviceLimit wire type**: 422 `DeviceLimit { limit: 16 }` (an acceptance policy — not a consensus rule).

### Carryovers to K4

- **Wire**: `deks.remove` references always send `recipientEncPubHex` (omitting is 422 on multi-device). `GET /deks` / pull's `deks` arrive as rows for all one's devices, so pick the row of the local key by `recipientEncPubHex` (if absent, old server = the only row). Pick `myShare`'s `deviceShares` by one's own device FP (if absent, old server).
- **Wrap generation**: `wrapRecipientsFor` expands R(E) into (person, device) (judge E via `effectivePermissionOf(person, device).scope` — device scope ∩ person scope). The expected count uses the same predicate as the server (`expectedWrapRecipientCount` — dedup at storage-key granularity).
- **Device operations**: the `maruhi device` group (§13-11's 7 surfaces): a new device = `POST /auth/devices/requests` (5 / hour, TTL 15 min) → an approving device = list → a human matches the FP (recomputed from the response's public key) → `add_device` to each project (a write token works) → registry `PUT /auth/devices/:fp` (admin token) → cancel the request. Revocation = `revoke_device` (self = write, others = admin) + **tokens separately via `maruhi token revoke`** (acceptance doesn't revoke the token — K1-15). Guidance for DeviceLimit 16 and the registry's 32 rows.
- **Receiving after revocation**: wraps addressed to a revoked device aren't cleaned (replaced at the next rotation). The wording of the rotation-needed flag trigger `revoke_device` (`revokedDeviceKeyFingerprints`).
- **Removals**: `LEGACY_DEVICE_WRAP_KIND`, all `soleDeviceOrFail` call sites, `member list`'s device list, `keyHistory`'s device key, `approval-rules`' device narrowing (K2 carryover).
- **skew**: today's 1-device CLI works unchanged against a K3 server (pinned by `device-ops.test.ts`'s skew cases). K4's CLI requires a K3 server (from the second device on, an old server gives `DeviceOpsNotAccepted`).

### K3 completion record (2026-09-20)

The 12 items of request §3's K3 row: (1) guard removal + partition return (consensus 150 / wireSchema 15, the composite 3 are 403), (2) the 2 ops' acceptance side effects (2 mirror rows + `revoke_device`'s detection variant + declaration-row deletion), (3) `dek_wraps` device axis + R(E) expansion + reader self-backfill, (4) `DeviceLimit`, (5) segment device rows, (6) the `devices` group's 7 surfaces, (7) `head_attestations` device axis, (8) D1 / DO migrations, (9) `MemberWithDevice` replacement (K3-1), (10) SELF_HOSTING, (11) the measurement, (12) skew tests — all in this PR. Not touched: the 3 canonical documents' normative text, `packages/crypto` sources, existing test-vector entries, the CLI's `LEGACY_DEVICE_WRAP_KIND` / `soleDeviceOrFail` call sites, the four-eyes announcement layer, Web, public docs.

### K3-14–K3-16. The 3 ruling loops decided mid-implementation (the owner's finding — 2026-09-20 addendum. Rounds run after implementation)

The 3 points written into the implementation record as "facts" hadn't gone through §3's procedure (3+ candidates → rounds → principle → reader → adopt). At the owner's demand they are run belatedly. **Conclusion: all 3 keep their adoptions**, though K3-14's adoption reason strengthened from "narrowing material" to "a signature-context input" (a round with content).

#### K3-14. Whether `RecipientDek.recipientEncPubHex` (the response side) is needed and its shape (enumeration 1 round + a round-2 with content + 2 empty rounds · stopped)

Once on the device axis, `GET /deks` and pull's `deks` carry "rows for all the calling principal's devices" (a read bears no signature, so the server doesn't know the calling device — K3-1). How does a receiving device pick the row for its own key?

| Option | Content | Judgment |
|---|---|---|
| n-1 **Carry an optional `recipientEncPubHex` (the stored row's recipient enc public key) on each response row. Old servers omit it (1 device = every row is my key); old CLIs ignore it as an unknown optional field** | The same vocabulary as the reference side (K3-3 c-3). Wire is backward compatible | **Adopted** |
| n-2 Carry the device FP | Display is FP | Stored rows are identified by enc public key, and rows addressed to a revoked device (not cleaned) have no FP→key correspondence in the current chain state (same reason as K3-3 c-4). Rejected |
| n-3 Narrow on the request side (a `?encPub=` query etc.) | Response shrinks to 1 row | A new encoding on the request wire (request §1). The narrowing input is a self-declaration of "my local key" — not an authorization input, but a surface grows. The response is bounded at ≤ 16 rows × retained epochs, so the saving is small. Rejected |
| n-4 Don't carry it; the recipient tries opening every row and ignores failures | Wire unchanged | §12-6's repair path assumes "unopenable = poisoned wrap (delete → re-register)". An open failure on a row addressed to another device becomes indistinguishable from poison, breaking the repair path's semantics. Rejected |
| n-5 A new response shape bundling rows per device (`devices: [{ encPubHex, deks }]`) | Explicit structure | Breaks old CLIs' decode (a required-structure change). Rejected |
| n-6 Make `recipientEncPubHex` required | Simple type | A K4 CLI couldn't decode an old (pre-K3) server's response (skew — §3's order is server-first, but a self-host where the CLI upgrades first really happens). Optional survives both directions. Rejected |

**Round 2 (upward compat / silver bullet)**: "distinguish the recipient key from HPKE's `enc`" → HPKE doesn't reveal the recipient from the ciphertext. Impossible. "**The wrap's registration-signature context (CRYPTO §5.1) binds `recipientEncPubHex`** — for a recipient to verify a distributed wrap's signature, this value is an input to begin with" → not a new option, but **a round with content that changes the adoption reason**: in a 1-device world the recipient only needed to put "my key" as the input, but with multiple devices each row's recipient key is a signature input, and if the response doesn't carry it the recipient ends up doing signature verification by guesswork ("if it fails under my key, it's another device's row") (losing the signature-failure vs wrong-key distinction). So n-1 is not "narrowing material" but a consequence of the discipline "the response carries every input of the signature context". **Round 3**: "make pull's `deks` and `GET /deks` different shapes" → the same `RecipientDekSchema` (1 place). Not a new option. Empty round. **Round 4**: empty round. Stopped.

**Principle**: "a distribution-data response carries every input the recipient needs to verify its signature (don't make the recipient guess)". CRYPTO §1 principle 6 (semantics inside the signature bytes) ✓, §5.1's signature context ✓, §12-6's poisoned wrap = the distinction "a row that signature-confirmed as addressed to my key won't open" ✓, Wave 2 B2 (optional-field addition) ✓. No ✗. **Reader**: K4's CLI reads in the order "signature-verify only rows whose `recipientEncPubHex` matches my key → open" (already carried over). A reviewer looks at `RecipientDekSchema`'s 1 field and the copy in `programs-dek.ts` (stored-row columns verbatim). **Adopted: n-1** (unchanged. Carryover-to-canonical 2 gains "it's a signature-context input").

#### K3-15. Handling the unreachable-on-value-paths stage 2 (`ensureDevicePermission`) (enumeration 1 round + 2 empty rounds · stopped)

On value push / meta / manifest / declarations, crypto's declared-head check (§6.3 — the device's effective authority since K2) runs first inside signature verification, and a cap shortfall settles at 422 `chain-head-state-mismatch`. The server's stage 2 (403) isn't reached on these surfaces (pullfrog's observation).

| Option | Content | Judgment |
|---|---|---|
| o-1 **Keep it (a defense layer) + record the unreachability reason in `ensureDevicePermission`'s JSDoc** | K3-1's principle "a signature-bearing operation is judged on the signing device's effective authority" appears in the same shape on every path. The unreachability grounds (cap immutable / the person's role is upper-bounded by stage 1 / a revoked device fails at the signature) depend on 2 things — crypto's implementation and the canonical text — and if either changes (e.g. adding an op that changes cap) this row becomes the last line | **Adopted** |
| o-2 Remove the 7 unreachable call sites | Fewer lines and no "code that can't be broken by removal" | Loses authorization uniformity (2 stages on every path), and a reviewer would have to reconstruct "why only value surfaces lack stage 2" from crypto's internal order. A shape that bets a 2-layer invariant on 1 layer's implementation order. Rejected |
| o-3 Place device determination before signature verification (same shape as composites) | 403 comes out before 422 per §12-3's order | Value wires carry no signer FP (rejected at K3-1 a-2). Impossible |
| o-4 Split crypto's verification into "signature only" and "declared-head check" and sandwich stage 2 between them | 403 becomes reachable and testable | `verifyDistributedValue` etc. are single functions in `packages/crypto` (untouched at K3). Also, canonical §6.3 defines an authorization shortfall at the declared head as a **verification result** (`writer-role-insufficient-at-head`), so changing it to 403 is a spec classification change (the province of carryover-to-canonical 7). Rejected |
| o-5 Pin the semantics with an `ensureDevicePermission` unit test | The function can be pinned | The call sites can't (removing them still breaks nothing). The 3 reachable paths (composite, checkpoint, DEK registration) already pin the same function (3 negatives + reader backfill), so the function's semantics are already pinned. Little added value. Rejected |

**Round 2**: a search for inputs that would make it reachable — "put a cap change behind an old declared head" → cap is immutable. "Sandwich a person-role demotion" → stage 1 403s first. "Sandwich a device revocation" → the trial verification fails at `signature-invalid` first. "Delete the environment → re-create with the same id" → device scope is id strings, still contained after re-creation. None can be constructed = the unreachability is essential. No new option. **Empty round**. **Round 3**: empty round. Stopped.

**Principle**: "when an authorization invariant depends on 2 layers (crypto's verification order and the canonical rules), the server layer doesn't omit its own stage (place it uniformly even if unreachable, and write the unreachability grounds in the function's definition)". The flip side of CRYPTO §1 principle 1 (server distrust) = the server doesn't delegate its responsibility to the crypto layer's order ✓, K3-1 ✓, ES K3-C (determination order) defers to canonical §6.3's classification on value surfaces ✓ (carryover 7). **Reader**: a reviewer reads the unreachability grounds in one JSDoc paragraph (0e9131c). **Adopted: o-1** (unchanged).

#### K3-16. Registry upsert atomicity (Bugbot finding — enumeration 1 round + 2 empty rounds · stopped)

| Option | Content | Judgment |
|---|---|---|
| p-1 **A single statement `INSERT … SELECT … WHERE (count < 32 OR EXISTS same row) ON CONFLICT (user_id, fp) DO UPDATE SET label, token_id`** | The cap check, insert, and update ride SQLite's single-statement serialization. Concurrent first-time PUTs of the same FP: one INSERTs, the other hits the PK conflict → DO UPDATE (both 204). Concurrent INSERTs of different FPs also cannot exceed the cap since the count evaluation and the insert are the same statement. `created_at` isn't in the DO UPDATE's set, keeping the first-write value | **Adopted** |
| p-2 Wrap UPDATE → INSERT's 2 statements in a transaction | Straightforward | D1 has no interactive transactions (`BEGIN`) (the existing repo only uses `db.batch`). Impossible |
| p-3 Try INSERT first, catch the PK-conflict error, UPDATE | 2 statements | Branching on the driver's error string = close to an untyped swallowed error, fragile against D1's error shapes (CLAUDE.md "don't swallow with catch"). Rejected |
| p-4 `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` INSERT … SELECT + a following UPDATE (2 statements, each idempotent) | Both sides 204 even on conflict | 2 statements = non-atomic (the result is the same even if another write slips between, but without `batch` wrapping it's 2 round trips). p-1 does the same in 1 statement. Rejected |
| p-5 `db.batch([insert, update])` | D1's batch is an implicit transaction | p-4 made atomic. p-1 holds the same guarantee in 1 statement. Rejected |

**Round 2**: "align to the TokenRepo precedent (capped INSERT … SELECT)" → tokens just don't get PUT-updated, so there's no ON CONFLICT; p-1 is that precedent + upsert. Not a new option. "Does the EXISTS OR-side become a cap loophole?" → EXISTS means a row for the same (user, fp) exists = the update path; new rows can only pass the count side. Not a loophole. **Empty round**. **Round 3**: empty round. Stopped.

**Principle**: "put the cap check and the write in 1 statement riding SQLite's serialization (D1 has no interactive transactions) — the TokenRepo precedent" ✓, idempotent (a re-sent identical PUT is 204) ✓, `created_at` preserved ✓. **Reader**: `devices-registry.test.ts`'s "updates don't count against the cap" case passes through the EXISTS side and the DO UPDATE side (the conflict itself cannot be forced under workerd — per pullfrog's observation). **Adopted: p-1** (unchanged).

#### Process reflection (a record)

K3-1–K3-12's "round 3: empty" declarations were made after trying 1–3 counter-options in round 2; exploration depth is thin except K3-1 / K3-10 (the 2 that produced a round with content). This time, the 3 cases put **counterexample construction** — not option enumeration — at the round's core: "the search for an input that makes it reachable" (K3-15) and "the signature-context input" (K3-14). In later rulings this shape (justifying a rejection with a counterexample) becomes round 2's default.

---

## 9. K4 addendum (2026-09-20 — CLI: the `maruhi device` group + reserve-key separation + wrap device expansion + old-device-path removal)

Rulings made in K4's implementation PR. Like §8, recorded as: candidate table (3+ options) → rounds (**from round 2 on, don't settle for enumerating alternatives — construct counterexamples that break the leading candidate — an attacker, a timing skew, the boundaries of device count 16 / registry 32, agent environments, old servers, the loss of local state — and when a counterexample stands, look for an option that eliminates it**. Stop after 2 consecutive rounds with no upward compat or silver bullet) → principle extraction (✓ / ✗ against CRYPTO §1's principles 1–7, DK-A "keys belong to devices, authority belongs to persons", DK-D "the registry is no source of truth", the CLI's diskless invariants, ES / KL3 precedents) → reader check (self-host admins, existing mid-migration users, K5 / K6 implementers, reviewers) → adopt. **The canonical texts' normative sentences are unchanged** (ambiguities are implemented fail-closed and enumerated in the closing "carryovers to the canonical texts"). Rulings are recorded **before** implementation; points where judgment changed or was newly made mid-implementation get the same procedure run and are added on the spot (§8 "process reflection" — not post-hoc addenda).

### K4-1. How the reserve key's secret is carried until sealing (enumeration 1 round + a round-2 with content + 2 empty rounds · stopped)

| Option | Content | Judgment |
|---|---|---|
| a-1 **Process memory only**: generate the reserve key in the same record shape as a device key (`StoredMasterKey` — B's JSON shape), and pass the secret side as a value — still `Redacted` — to the sealing functions (code sealing, passkey sealing, guardian segments). Written to neither keychain nor agent memory. Drop the reference when done | The letter of the diskless invariant (a reserve key's secret is never persisted). The existing `serializeStoredMasterKey` is reusable as the only serialization mouth | **Adopted (reinforced at a-5)** |
| a-2 Temporary storage in a separate keychain entry (`reserve::…`), deleted after sealing | Easier resume on mid-way failure | Persists the secret (survives a crash) — touches CRYPTO §8.5's prohibition "keep the reserve key's private key in an everyday device's storage". Rejected |
| a-3 Place it in the agent's memory (`maruhi agent`) | Not written to disk | Doesn't hold where no agent runs, and where one runs it takes the shape of "keeping" it for the session's lifetime (§8.5). Rejected |
| a-4 Derive the reserve key deterministically from the recovery code and carry no secret | What must be carried disappears (a silver-bullet candidate) | A new key-derivation protocol (CRYPTO §8.2 defines only KEK derivation; no keypair derivation). Cannot be shared with the passkey / guardian paths. CLAUDE.md "no inventing custom protocols". Rejected |
| a-5 **a-1 + close the storage paths by type**: carry the reserve key as `ReserveKeys` (branded); the keychain-storage functions (`storeMasterKeyAndReport` / `storeMasterKeyGuarded`) accept only `MasterKeys` (device keys). The sealing functions are unified to take "a B record", distinguishing device key vs reserve key by type | The peeling spots (`serializeStoredMasterKey`) are unchanged, and **code that writes a reserve key to the keychain becomes a type error** | **Adopted** |

**Round 2 (counterexample construction)**: counterexample 1 "crash ordering" — if the order were generate → `add_device(K_r)` to each project → ledger sealing, a crash before sealing would leave **a device nobody holds the secret for** on the chain (the DEK arrives but cannot be opened = the worst shape, making people believe a reserve key exists). → **Fix the order: sealing into the ledger (code issuance + storage confirmation) → local recording (public key, FP — K4-3) → chain registration (handled by K4-3's first-sync registration at the next sync)**. A crash before sealing only returns to the "no reserve key yet" state, which the next `key recovery` redoes (a round with content — part of the adoption). Counterexample 2 "a crash after sealing, before local recording" — the ledger holds K_r but this device does not know K_r's public key → chain registration never happens. → `key show` / the sync-time warning (K4-9) says "no reserve-key record", fixed by `key recovery` (open → restore the record — K4-2). Fail-closed (the don't-register side), so tolerable. Counterexample 3 "agent environment" — sealing is a ceremony (TTY), so no reserve key is generated → `key generate` yields just the device key (same shape as the existing "skip issuance in agent environments"). Counterexample 4 "the Redacted toJSON trap" — `JSON.stringify(record)` seals the redaction (the existing keychain.ts note). Sealing input is limited to `serializeStoredMasterKey` (existing discipline). **Round 3**: "explicit zeroization of the in-memory secret" → cannot be guaranteed in JS, and a Redacted's value is a string anyway. Stay on the same line as the existing device-key handling. No new option → **empty round**. **Round 4**: empty round. Stopped.

**Principle**: "a reserve key's secret may exist only inside the ledger's ciphertext and in the memory of processes performing sealing / restoration / ledger change; storage paths are closed by types" — CRYPTO §8.5 ✓, §3 "private keys live only in the ledger" ✓, the CLI's diskless invariant ✓, KL3's "plaintext segments, KEK, B live only in local variables" ✓. No ✗. **Reader**: a reviewer can confirm the absence of persistence by looking at the list of functions taking `ReserveKeys` (the 3 sealing paths + the post-restore `add_device` signature) and the keychain-storage functions' argument types (a human-review-mandatory spot). Invisible to mid-migration existing users. **Adopted: a-1 + a-5 + the fixed ordering**.

### K4-2. The reserve key's creation timing and opening for ledger changes — discriminating a pre-DK ledger (B = a device-key copy) (enumeration 1 round + a round-2 with content + 2 empty rounds · stopped)

Canonical §8: "at first key generation, generate the device key and the reserve key together, and on the spot create the recovery-code wrap and an optional passkey wrap" / "a ledger change (adding a wrap, re-issuing, naming guardians) requires opening the reserve key".

| Option | Content | Judgment |
|---|---|---|
| b-1 **The reserve key is born at "the first ledger sealing"** (= the code issuance in `key generate`'s latter stage. If sealing was skipped in an agent environment, a later `key recovery` is the first sealing = the creation). A ledger change (`key recovery` re-issue / `key seal passkey` / `guardian add` / `key reserve rotate`) **first opens B via code input or a passkey** and uses the opened B record as the reserve key. If the opened B's FP **matches the local device key's FP**, it is judged a pre-DK ledger (B = a copy of K), and **only `key recovery` performs the separation** (generate a new K_r → seal K_r → record). Other ledger-change commands, if pre-DK, refuse with "run `maruhi key recovery` first" | The discrimination rests on a cryptographic fact (the opened B's content). Ledger-change entries converge on the single "open" | **Adopted** |
| b-2 Always generate a reserve key at `key generate`, and in agent environments keep only the record unsealed | One creation point | An unsealable reserve key = no place for the secret (same problem as a-2 / a-3). Rejected |
| b-3 Judge pre-DK by a local-state flag (`reserveSplitDone`) | Judgeable without opening | Losing local state flips the judgment (a new device always looks "unseparated", re-replacing an already-separated ledger and dead-ending the old reserve key on the chain). Rejected |
| b-4 Put "B's public key" on the ledger's status API and judge from it | No opening | A wire addition (absent from K3's status response) + makes a server declaration an input to a judgment (touches the "advisory is not a source of truth" line). Rejected |
| b-5 Don't require opening for a ledger change (build the new wrap with the local device key — the current behavior) | Light UX | Contradicts canonical §8 revision (4), and B never becomes a reserve key (keeps writing a device-key copy into the ledger = DK's negation). Rejected |

**Round 2 (counterexample construction)**: counterexample 1 "a pre-DK user who lost the code" — cannot open, so cannot reach `key recovery`'s separation. The old CLI could re-issue with just the key — a regression. → **Add `key recovery --replace`**: an explicit operation that generates a new K_r and replaces the ledger without opening. If the old B was K (pre-DK), harmless (K stays on as a device key); if the old B was a reserve key K_r0, K_r0 remains on the chain as "a device nobody can open" (a dead key — usable by nobody, so not a confidentiality issue, but it breeds the false belief that a reserve key exists) → the `--replace` wording guides "revoke the old reserve key on the chain (`maruhi device revoke <fp>` — the FP shows in the local record / `device list`)". The default is opening (fail-closed); `--replace` is explicit (a round with content). Counterexample 2 "K copied to 2 machines under pre-DK" — whichever device opens it, B's FP = the local FP → the separation runs once on one side (whichever runs first). The second machine opens the separated ledger (B = K_r) → FP mismatch → records it as the reserve key (state restoration). Correct. Counterexample 3 "a timing skew" — mid-separation (after K_r sealing, before chain registration), another device revokes K via `device revoke` → K_r gets registered at the next sync, and K's revocation is independent. No contradiction. Counterexample 4 "want to open with a passkey" — even with the code lost, a passkey should open it → put `--passkey` (a choice of opening means) on ledger-change commands. **Round 3**: "keep opening to once per process (multiple ledger changes in one command)" → a command-design topic, not a new option. **Empty round**. **Round 4**: empty round. Stopped.

**Principle**: "only one who can open the ledger may change it (the fact of having opened B is the qualification for a ledger change). A pre-DK ledger is discriminated by the cryptographic fact of the opened B's content, not by local state or declarations" — CRYPTO §8 revision (4) ✓, DK-G ✓, DK-D "the registry is no source of truth" ✓, KL3 19-3 (c) (re-taking is not an everyday path) ✓. No ✗. **Reader**: an existing user runs `key recovery` once (code input → new code displayed). From then on `guardian add` / `key seal passkey` ask for the code (or a passkey). K6's docs write "a ledger change needs the reserve key opened" in one sentence. **Adopted: b-1 + `--replace` + `--passkey`**.

### K4-3. Where and how the local state of the first-sync 3 conditions lives (enumeration 1 round + a round-2 with content + 2 empty rounds · stopped)

DK-D: a key may be added iff (1) a key I generated (the reserve key), (2) a key I approved via `device approve`, (3) a key observed as that person's device on a verified chain. Non-secret local state (same family as pins).

| Option | Content | Judgment |
|---|---|---|
| c-1 **`<config dir>/own-devices.json` — 1 file, keyed origin → user_id → FP. Row = { encPubHex, sigPubHex, roleCap, scopeKind, scopeEnvironmentIds, source: "reserve" \| "approved" \| "observed", label?, addedByFingerprintHex?, observedProjectId?, recordedAtMs, revokedAtMs? }. Same Effect service as `FingerprintBook` (`OwnDeviceStore`), same strict decode / fail-open / temp + rename** | The same shelf as the fingerprint book (per-user, cross-project). Contents are public keys, FPs, cap, and provenance only (no secrets) | **Adopted** |
| c-2 Put it on pins (`invites/<projectId>.json`) | An existing file | A device is one's own cross-project resource (DK-L). Duplicating per project breaks provenance and revocation sync. Rejected |
| c-3 Put it in the keychain | Centralized | Puts the non-secret on the secret shelf (disappears where no keychain exists; in agent memory the lifetime is short). Rejected |
| c-4 Hold no local state; every time add from "other projects' chains" via (3) alone | No state | Cannot express (1)(2) (the reserve key's public key has a period where it is on no chain). A full project scan is N× the sync. Rejected |

**Round 2 (counterexample construction)**: counterexample 1 "re-registration of a revoked device" — after revoking D2 at A / B, syncing C for the first time would add D2 from the record (reviving a compromised key, reviving §6.2's ballot). → **Carry `revokedAtMs`, set it on `device revoke` execution and on observing a `revoke_device` of one's own device on a verified chain. A flagged row is not added** (a round with content). Re-approval (`device approve`) overwrites the row with "approved" and clears the flag (explicit operation only). Counterexample 2 "inflow from the registry" — c-1's writers are only the 3 paths (sealing, approve, observation on a verified chain), and it does not write `GET /auth/devices` responses (pinned as a negative by a test). Counterexample 3 "cap monotonicity" — first-syncing C on a phone (cap (owner, listed{})) tries to add the reserve key (owner, all) → `device-cap-exceeded`. → Judged pre-transmission by a `capWithinCap` equivalent (no public API — the CLI compares cap to cap), and un-addable rows are skipped with a Note ("this device's cap cannot register it; sync from another device"). Counterexample 4 "`unknown-environment`" — `--env dev` at approve carries an environment id, but another project may lack `dev` → judged pre-transmission via `requireScopeEnvironmentsExist` and skipped (Note). Counterexample 5 "DeviceLimit 16" — the 17th is 422 `DeviceLimit` → turn into a Note and continue with the rest (don't stop the sync on one device's failure: registration is a SHOULD accompaniment and doesn't change the command's outcome). Counterexample 6 "local-state loss" — even if the record is gone, (3)'s observation refills it at the next sync (the reserve key is restored if it is on some chain; if not, K4-9's warning). **Round 3**: "in every command's sync, retry the missing devices each time (not only on first sync)" → since it is idempotent there is no reason to limit it to the first time. But rows that keep failing (DeviceLimit) emit a Note each time — a once-per-command ledger (NoticeLedger) suffices. Not a new option. **Empty round**. **Round 4**: empty round. Stopped.

**Principle**: "local state is a non-secret 'record of my keys' provenance'; writers are cryptographic facts only (I sealed it, I approved it, observed on a verified chain). A server declaration is neither reader nor writer" — DK-D ✓, §6.5 "don't build a global public-key directory" ✓, KF's (known-fingerprints) fail-open and strict decode ✓, pins' read-merge-write ✓. No ✗. **Reader**: a reviewer looks at the 3 kinds of `OwnDeviceStore` `record*` callers and that `devices.list` responses don't flow into the store (a negative test). K5's Web only reads the registry, so it is unrelated. **Adopted: c-1 + the revoked flag + pre-transmission cap/environment checks + DeviceLimit as a Note**.

### K4-4. Observation (condition (3)) and sheltering "devices a stolen device made before revocation" (enumeration 1 round + a round-2 with content + 2 empty rounds · stopped)

§2-bis's residual: if lost device D2 `add_device`d D3′ before revocation, D1 observes D3′ on Q → records it under (3) → the first sync adds D3′ to P too = an **expansion** of the sheltering.

| Option | Content | Judgment |
|---|---|---|
| d-1 Don't use (3) (auto-registration is (1)(2) only) | Doesn't expand | A device that lost local state (a new device has no (1)(2)) cannot add the reserve key or sibling devices to a new project and relies on re-running `device approve`. Throwing away DK-D's (3) = shrinking an approved ruling. Rejected |
| d-2 **Use (3), but when the observed device is in neither (1) nor (2), emit a Warning at first observation ("device X was added by device Y at seq N. You have not approved it from this device. If it rings no bell, `device revoke X`"), and at first-sync registration also display the provenance (observed / added by) one line each** | The problem with sheltering's expansion is that it is "invisible"; visibility lets a human stop it. Same shape as design record §2-bis's handling (bulk revocation + visibility + monotonicity) | **Adopted** |
| d-3 Limit (3) to "devices added by a device I approved (2)" | Narrow by provenance chain | D2 is a device I approved, so D3′ passes. Not a narrowing. Rejected |
| d-4 Require a yes confirmation for (3) registration | A human stops it | First sync happens non-interactively too (agent, CI). Skipping registration where it cannot confirm = the same shrinkage as d-1. Rejected (though on an interactive device a Note suffices) |

**Round 2 (counterexample construction)**: counterexample 1 "observation and registration in the same command" — Q and P are separate commands (1 command 1 project), so P's registration comes after Q's observation warning. Since P's registration also displays provenance, it appears twice. Counterexample 2 "D2 already revoked at observation time" — if D2 is revoked, the chain retains the fact that D3′'s `add_device` actor was a revoked device. Add to the Warning at observation "the adding device Y is currently revoked" (a round with content). Counterexample 3 "under monotonicity D3′ ≤ D2's cap" — if D2 is (owner, all), D3′ may also be (owner, all). cap doesn't stop it (as §2-bis says). Counterexample 4 "mistaking D3′ for one's own device via the registry's display name" — the registry is advisory, and `device list` marks it "server-reported" with the FP alongside (§5 residual (3)). **Round 3**: "delay D3′'s registration by 'N days since observation'" → brings a timestamp into the judgment, and it is valid on Q during the delay. Little gained. Rejected. **Empty round**. **Round 4**: empty round. Stopped.

**Principle**: "don't hide changes to the device set; show a human the provenance (who, on which device, when). Automation is limited to cryptographic facts; judgment stays with the human" — §2-bis "visibility" ✓, DK-A bulk revocation ✓, KF "a match in the book doesn't substitute a person's consent" ✓. No ✗. **Reader**: someone who lost a device picks bulk-revocation targets by the "added by" column in `device list` (connects to K7's UX). **Adopted: d-2 + the revoked-actor note**.

### K4-5. `device add` waiting and the shape of `device approve` request polling (enumeration 1 round + a round-2 with content + 2 empty rounds · stopped)

| Option | Content | Judgment |
|---|---|---|
| e-1 `device add` creates the request, prints the FP, exits without waiting | Simple | Contradicts design record DK-L's L-1 "FP display + wait", and the user's only way to learn approval completion (backfill landing) would be trying `pull`. Rejected |
| e-2 **Poll the registry (`GET /auth/devices`) every 3 seconds until a row for my FP appears (TTL = up to `expiresAtMs`). When it appears: `project list` → sync each project, and count and report the **projects whose chains have my device** (the truth is the chain. The registry is only a progress signal). On TTL expiry, guide "the request expired; re-run `device add` with the same key to create a new request"** | Uses the registry row the approver PUTs last as the "done" signal. Even if the signal is forged (the server slipping in a row), what follows is only a chain check — nothing gets added | **Adopted** |
| e-3 Periodically sync every project's chain looking for my FP | Doesn't rely on the registry | N projects × a full sync every 3 seconds = heavy. The project list is a server declaration; a pre-approval device can read the project list (token) and has sync rights (member). It just repeats e-2's latter-stage check periodically. Rejected |
| e-4 Use the approver's DELETE of the request row as the signal | No added surface | DELETE happens at approval **completion** (after backfill), but a rejection also deletes (reject = delete), so "disappeared" cannot distinguish success. Rejected |

**Round 2 (counterexample construction)**: counterexample 1 "the approver crashes midway (`add_device` done on 1 of 3 projects)" — the registry row comes last, so no signal arrives. The requester waits to TTL and sees the expiry guidance. The approver re-runs `device approve` → idempotent (existing devices are skipped) → PUTs last. The requester resumes waiting by re-running `device add` (if the request row is 409 request-exists / device-registered, treat it as "resume waiting" — a round with content). Counterexample 2 "the server slips a registry row in early" — the requester checks the chains, reports "not on any project yet", and exits. No harm. Counterexample 3 "`device add` in an agent environment" — no gate (DK-D). Polling works non-interactively (a timeout instead of Ctrl+C). Counterexample 4 "PUT 429s on the registry's 32-row cap" — the approver conveys it via a Note, and the requester's signal never arrives → the requester sees the expiry guidance at TTL. Write into the approver's Note "check old registry rows via `device list` and delete them". Counterexample 5 "TTL 15 minutes vs the approver's time needed" — approval takes tens of seconds to a few minutes (project count × backfill). 15 minutes is the canonical value (§13-11). If exceeded, the requester re-runs (same key). **Round 3**: "`device add --no-wait`" → e-1 made optional. Adding a device in CI (DK-H: ephemeral) is KL4's province; not held now. Rejected. **Empty round**. **Round 4**: empty round. Stopped.

**Principle**: "an advisory may be a signal, but not an input to a judgment (after the signal, always confirm on a verified chain)" — DK-D / DK-F ✓, `project list`'s TCB note (declaration = for discovery) ✓. **Reader**: K6's docs can write "on the new device `device add` → on hand `device approve <fp>` → when the new device says 'N projects' it's done". **Adopted: e-2 + resume on re-run**.

### K4-6. `device approve`'s matching input and cap specification (enumeration 1 round + 2 empty rounds · stopped)

| Option | Content | Judgment |
|---|---|---|
| f-1 **`<fp-or-words>` = the full 32-char hex, or 12 words (whitespace / comma separated). For each request-list row, recompute the FP from the response's public key; pick the row by hex match, or by match against `fingerprintToWords(recomputed FP)` for words (don't implement the words→FP reverse transform). 0 matches = "no request / expired"; 2+ cannot structurally happen (FP is the primary key). cap via `--cap <role>` (default owner = unlimited) and `--env <id>`… / `--all-envs` (default) / `--no-envs` (a ballot-only device — shares `scopeFromFlags`)** | §3 "no truncation to short codes" — don't accept a prefix. Not adding the reverse transform (BIP39 → bytes) = bringing in no new encoding | **Adopted** |
| f-2 Accept an FP prefix | Easier to type | Approval is the match that drops a request row the server slipped in via FP; a prefix touches §3's no-truncation. Rejected |
| f-3 Print the list with no argument and pick by number | Light UX | A number = the server's ordering; the match degenerates to "pick the displayed row" (no FP brought in). Rejected |
| f-4 Implement the words→FP reverse transform (BIP39 decode) in the CLI | Direct matching | The reverse transform is `packages/crypto`'s province (don't duplicate the checksum verification in the CLI). Computing the word list on the candidate side suffices. Rejected |

**Round 2 (counterexample construction)**: counterexample 1 "case and word separators" — lowercase the words, split on whitespace / commas / newlines, compare against 12 words. Hex is lowercased. Counterexample 2 "5 requests from the same person (the 5/hour cap)" — among 5 list rows, the FP match is 1 row. Counterexample 3 "agent environment" — the ceremony gate (TTY + non-agent) precedes fetching the request list. Counterexample 4 "a `--cap reader --env prod` device = a read-only device" — monotonicity is compared against the signing (local) device's cap (pre-transmission, `capWithinCap` equivalent). **Round 3**: empty round. **Round 4**: empty round. Stopped. **Principle**: "matching is full-length; display shapes reuse the existing (hex / 12 words); no new encoding or reverse transform is added" — §3 ✓, DK-M "no new files, no new encodings" ✓. **Adopted: f-1**.

### K4-7. `device revoke`'s bulk-target confirmation UX (enumeration 1 round + a round-2 with content + 2 empty rounds · stopped)


| Option | Content | Judgment |
|---|---|---|
| g-1 **References `<ref>…` (repeatable) = an FP prefix (8+ chars, unique) or a registry display name (advisory — uses the FP of the matching row and confirms with the FP shown alongside). Target person = self (default) / `--user <id>` (others — the registry can't be used, so FP only). Procedure: `project list` (or `--project`) → sync each project → collect the FPs matching the references among the target's current devices → **a confirmation table** (per-project revoked FPs and remaining devices; warnings derived from remaining devices' caps — K4-8 / §2-bis "all of the owner's devices end up cap < owner", "no device left that can read prod", "no reserve key left") → `yes` (or `--yes` — revocation is not a ceremony: no values or key material come out) → `revoke_device` on each project → sweep (K4-8) → local record marks revoked → delete the registry row → the token-revocation proposal (K4-13)** | Bulk revocation (§2-bis) takes the "pick from a list" shape. The confirmation table shows all projects at once | **Adopted** |
| g-2 Confirm per project | Granular | 4 projects = 4 yes's. Revocation is incident response; don't delay it (ES E). Rejected |
| g-3 No confirmation (immediate) | Fast | No place to stop a mis-picked reference (a forged display name — §5 residual (3)). Rejected |
| g-4 Don't accept display names (FP only) | The forgery surface disappears | Contradicts the UX check (DK-B "`device revoke MacBook`"). FP-alongside + confirmation suffices. Rejected |

**Round 2 (counterexample construction)**: counterexample 1 "display-name forgery" — the server swaps the labels of registry rows so `revoke MacBook` points at a different FP. → The confirmation table shows FPs (hex + the first 3 of 12 words?). **Show the full hex** (words are 12 long — not put in the table). The user can match it against `device list`'s FP. Counterexample 2 "an agent types `--yes`" — revocation is a safe-side operation (DK-B); even if an agent wrongly revokes, only a rotate runs (bounded at availability loss). Tolerable (not a ceremony). Counterexample 3 "the reference is the last device" — judge `last-device-protected` pre-transmission and guide "the last device can't be revoked; to remove the person, `member remove`". Counterexample 4 "timing skew (another device finished the revocation after the confirmation table)" — re-sync on the CAS conflict → if the device matching the reference is gone, skip it as "already revoked" (idempotent). Counterexample 5 "revoking the very device doing the signing" — the canonical text allows it (valid at entry time). The confirmation table shows "you are revoking this device itself: it can no longer sign afterwards. The sweep cannot be performed by this device (K4-8)" (a round with content). Counterexample 6 "a reader's self-revocation" — `revoke_device` passes but the sweep cannot be performed → display "who to ask for the rotate" (DK-B). **Round 3**: "`--dry-run` (just the confirmation table)" → `device list` shows the same information. Rejected. **Empty round**. **Round 4**: empty round. Stopped.

**Principle**: "revocation is fixed by FP; a display name stays an entry convenience (the FP shown alongside in the confirmation table). Revocation is not a ceremony, but a human stops a mistaken target" — §5 residual (3) ✓, DK-B (non-interactive allowed) ✓, ES E (don't delay) ✓. **Reader**: someone who lost a device: `device revoke "MacBook"` → table → yes → "Revoked in 4 projects. Rotated: … Needs a member to rotate: …" → the token-revocation proposal (K4-13). **Adopted: g-1 + the self-revocation note**.

### K4-8. The environment set of sweep kind-5 `device-revoked`'s duty and handling unperformability (enumeration 1 round + a round-2 with content + 2 empty rounds · stopped)

| Option | Content | Judgment |
|---|---|---|
| h-1 **Duty = { kind: "device-revoked", target: user_id, seq, environmentIds = ⋃_{fp ∈ payload} environmentsOfScopeAt(deviceStateAt(user, fp, seq−1).permission.scope, seq) } (materialize the device's effective scope at seq−1. A device that cannot be derived is fail-closed = ALL). Performer = the actor. The actor side's "performable environments" are narrowed by **the signing device's effective scope** (not the person's scope — if approved/revoked on a phone (owner, listed{}), empty), and out-of-scope environments are shown as `outOfScope` "carried to another device of the same person or the next sync" (K6-D). A reader's self-revocation cannot rotate for lack of role → the same outOfScope shape saying "ask a member-or-above"** | The letter of canonical §7 "device revocation". Reuses the existing `sweepAfterMandate`'s `outOfScope` mechanism (just replaces the actor's scope with the effective scope) | **Adopted** |
| h-2 The duty's environment set = the person's scope (ignore the device scope) | Simple | Revoking a ballot-only device carries a rotate duty for every environment (contradicts the canonical "revoking a scope-empty device carries no duty"). Rejected |
| h-3 Refuse the revocation itself if unperformable | Leaves no duty | Already rejected at DK-B B-3 (don't stop a safe-side operation). Rejected |
| h-4 Leave the unperformable part entirely to the server's rotation-needed detection | The CLI shows nothing | The detection flag is per-variable and cannot guide the duty (a per-environment rotate). The standing warning (`warnUnconvergedMandates`) is §7's letter. Rejected |

**Round 2 (counterexample construction)**: counterexample 1 "re-run on another device of the same person" — an unconverged duty stays in the standing warning, and `mandateAdvice` guides "converge with `maruhi env rotate <env> --new-epoch --reason <text>`" (revocation isn't a re-runnable operation — the same shape as `reversedAdvice`). If the revoked device was re-registered, it distinguishes by saying "the device has been re-added" (a round with content — same shape as the existing rollback guidance). Counterexample 2 "the effective scope at seq−1 and the person's scope shrink coincide" — effective = person ∩ device at seq−1. If the person's scope shrank before the revocation, a `scope-narrowed` duty for the shrunk part stands separately. Even double-counted, baselines fold at max(seq). Counterexample 3 "via approval" — device ops cannot be proposed (§6.2), so they never appear on `applied` with `viaProposalSeq`. Counterexample 4 "an admin revoking another's device (the consensus rule is own scope ⊇ the target person's scope)" — when the signing device's effective scope is narrower than the person's scope (an admin operation on a cap'd device), outOfScope appears. The canonical inclusion is judged on "the person's scope", so the consensus rule passes and only the performance is carried to another device. The display suffices. **Round 3**: empty round. **Round 4**: empty round. Stopped.

**Principle**: "revocation = removal from R(E); duty = rotate of the removed recipient's effective scope; performer = the range of DEKs the signing device holds" — DK-B ✓, K6-D ✓, ES K4-J (materializing each duty's environment set) ✓. **Adopted: h-1**.

### K4-9. The migration guidance's trigger condition and wording (enumeration 1 round + 2 empty rounds · stopped)

DK-J J-1 "at first startup, the CLI guides 'this device's key is identical to the reserve key; separate the reserve key'".

| Option | Content | Judgment |
|---|---|---|
| i-1 **Fold it into the no-reserve-key warning**: on a keyed sync (`attachProject`), when one's current devices are only {this device} and the local record has no reserve key, emit Warning "No reserve key is registered for you on this project (only this device's key). Run `maruhi key recovery` to create a reserve key and seal it (on installs from before device keys, the recovery ledger holds a copy of this device's key; `maruhi key recovery` replaces it with a separate reserve key)". If the local record has a reserve key but the chain doesn't, K4-3 adds it silently (1-line Note). `key show` always prints "reserve key: not recorded on this machine" | The trigger condition is a cryptographic fact (the on-chain device set + the local record). Since K4-2 discriminates pre-DK at opening, the guidance folds into the single fact "there is no reserve key" | **Adopted** |
| i-2 Detect "first startup" via a version file (`<config dir>/version`) and guide only once | J-1's letter "first startup" | Adds a state; if it disappears it re-fires, if it stays it never guides again. Not fact-based. Rejected |
| i-3 Make the server's ledger state (registered) the trigger | Doesn't rely on local state | Whether a ledger is pre-DK or post-DK isn't on the status API (K4-2 b-4). Rejected |
| i-4 No guidance, docs only | Minimal | Existing users never notice (§2-bis "projects with no reserve key registered"). Rejected |

**Round 2 (counterexample construction)**: counterexample 1 "a new user (reserve key already sealed at `key generate`) creates their first project" — the local record has the reserve key → added at first sync → no warning. Counterexample 2 "a new device `device approve`d after the reserve key was sealed" — the new device holds no local record (only filled by K4-3 (3)'s observation) → observes the reserve key on sync → records → no warning (the observation Warning applies to "a device you haven't approved" under d-2 — a reserve key, approved by nobody, **always** becomes a warning → counterexample 3). Counterexample 3 "reserve-key observation keeps saying 'a device you don't recognize'" — the fact that the reserve key's `add_device` actor was one's own device is the same. Material for distinction: **`device approve` writes a label in the registry, and the approving device's record keeps approved — the new device has neither**. → Shape the observation Warning as "added by <fp> — if you do not recognise it, revoke", showing the approving device's FP (a human compares their devices' FPs in `device list`). Warning at first observation for **every** reserve key and sibling device is noise → **demote the warning to a Note and attach the `device revoke` path** (observation = an addition to the record; revocation is a human judgment). Correct d-2: at observation a Note (with provenance); at first-sync registration, a provenance display. Counterexample 4 "agent environment" — guidance is stderr Note / Warning without values. **Round 3**: empty round. **Round 4**: empty round. Stopped.

**Principle**: "guidance comes from a fact (the absence of a reserve key), not from a state (a version, a first run). One fact, one guidance" — DP5 ruling C (the same wording once) ✓, B2 (a warning nobody sees isn't detection) ✓. **Reader**: an existing user sees the Warning on their first `pull` and runs `key recovery`. A self-host admin sees the same one line at SELF_HOSTING's order 3. **Adopted: i-1 (correcting d-2's observation-time level to a Note)**.

### K4-10. Treating the restored key after `key recover` — reserve key or lost-device key? (enumeration 1 round + a round-2 with content + 2 empty rounds · stopped)

Canonical §3 / §8: after restoration, `add_device` a new device key, then erase the reserve key's secret from the device. On a pre-DK ledger, B can be = K (the lost device's key itself).

| Option | Content | Judgment |
|---|---|---|
| j-1 **A common latter stage `finishRecovery` treats the restored B as a reserve key: (1) generate this device's new device key and save it to the keychain, (2) sync each project of `project list`; if B's FP is a current device of that person, sign `add_device(new device)` with B's sig key → open the wrap addressed to self with B's enc key and backfill to the new device, (3) discard B's secret. Projects where B isn't registered are enumerated as "re-invitation needed". **Ask inside the recovery ceremony 1 question (yes / no) whether B is a reserve key (a key never used on an everyday device); if yes, record it in the local record as "reserve"; if no (pre-DK = the lost device's key), don't record and guide "revoke that key (`device revoke <fp>`), then split a reserve key with `key recovery`"** | B's content cannot distinguish a reserve key from a device-key copy (same record shape). A recording error becomes the accident of propagating a lost key to new projects via (3), so the default (unknown) leans to not recording (fail-closed) | **Adopted** |
| j-2 Always record as "reserve" | No question | Propagates a pre-DK lost-device key as a reserve key. Rejected |
| j-3 Don't record | No question | Even a true reserve key isn't recorded, and propagation to new projects waits for (3)'s observation (it does get observed at the next sync — self-healing). Asking one question is better, but the behavior equals j-1's no side. Rejected (subsumed by j-1) |
| j-4 Use B as the new device key as-is (old `key recover`'s behavior) | Minimal change | The reserve key stays on an everyday device (§8.5 prohibition). Rejected |

**Round 2 (counterexample construction)**: counterexample 1 "a wrong answer to the question (it's a reserve key but they answer no)" — it just isn't recorded; the next sync observes it → Note → propagation proceeds via (3). A wrong answer on the "no side" is safe. A wrong answer on the "yes side" (calling a lost-device key a reserve key) is an accident, but no is the default (empty Enter = no). Counterexample 2 "a project where B isn't registered" — the new device can sync (token) but cannot sign. Enumerate and "ask that project's admin for a re-invitation" (DK-B). Counterexample 3 "a crash mid-backfill" — the new device is already saved to the keychain. Re-running isn't `key recover` (the key exists = refuse), and **the next sync's K4-3 … doesn't have B**. → The means of resumption: `key recover --resume`? The new device cannot add itself. B must be opened again → shape `key recover` as "even with a key present, if there are projects where that key isn't on the chain, continue" (`--resume`: use the existing device key as the new device, open B again, `add_device` only the shortfall) — a round with content. Counterexample 4 "the handoff path" — a B obtained via guardian approval gets the same latter stage. Counterexample 5 "agent environment" — restoration is a ceremony (refused). **Round 3**: "discriminate by how B's FP appears in `device list`'s registry (label = "reserve")" → makes an advisory a judgment input. Rejected. **Empty round**. **Round 4**: empty round. Stopped.

**Principle**: "a restored key is used only to issue a device key; its provenance (reserve key or not) cannot be discriminated by a cryptographic fact, so ask a human; the default is the not-recording side" — §8.1 "used only for issuing device keys" ✓, §8.5 ✓, DK-J ✓. **Adopted: j-1 + `--resume`**.

### K4-11. `key reserve rotate`'s ordering and the treatment of old B's wraps (passkey / guardian) (enumeration 1 round + a round-2 with content + 2 empty rounds · stopped)

| Option | Content | Judgment |
|---|---|---|
| k-1 **Order: open B (code / passkey) → obtain the old reserve key's FP → generate a new K_r2 → seal K_r2 with the recovery code (new code displayed + storage confirmation) → local record (K_r2 = reserve, old = revoked) → each project: `add_device(K_r2)` (this device signs) + backfill → `revoke_device(old)` + sweep → **delete** the old B's passkey wrap and guardian groups, and guide re-creation** | Old B's wraps are rows that can only restore a revoked key (restoring it accomplishes nothing). Keeping them breeds the false belief "it can be restored" | **Adopted** |
| k-2 Keep old B's wraps | Non-destructive | Restoring yields a revoked reserve key, and `finishRecovery`'s `add_device` becomes `unknown-device` / signature-invalid on every project. Sheltering a false belief. Rejected |
| k-3 Auto-re-seal passkey / guardian onto the new B | No effort | A passkey needs the authenticator's ceremony, and guardians need the guardians' current device set (the chain) to re-seal segments = each command's ceremony itself. Cannot be automated. Rejected |
| k-4 Revoke first, add later | Revokes sooner | Creates an instant with no reserve key between revocation and registration (defenseless against losing every device). The add → revoke order is right (same as remove's re-invitation). Rejected |

**Round 2 (counterexample construction)**: counterexample 1 "mid-way failure (add done, before revoke)" — the local record has old = revoked, new = reserve. Re-running restarts at the opening (idempotent: add skips "already present", revoke happens if remaining). Counterexample 2 "forgetting `guardian add` after deleting guardian groups" — `key show` prints "guardian groups: 0", and the deletion carries a Note. Counterexample 3 "does a lost device hold the old reserve key's secret?" — the reserve key lives only in the ledger, so no device has it; but that the device used for restoration was compromised (§5 residual (2)) is rotate's motive. Converges by revocation + sweep. Counterexample 4 "timing skew (another device rotates simultaneously)" — one side loses the CAS race → re-sync finds "already 2 reserve keys" → adds the second K_r2′ and revokes the old… both new reserve keys remain (one isn't in the ledger = a dead key). → After re-sync, a Note shows a human "reserve-key candidates among my devices that aren't in the local record" (carried by K4-4's observation Note). Concurrency is an operational failure, the same shelf as `concurrentMasterKeyWrite`. **Round 3**: empty round. **Round 4**: empty round. Stopped.

**Principle**: "reserve-key rotate = register the new reserve key → revoke the old (the same order as remove). Old B's ledger rows only restore a revoked key, so delete them; re-sealing happens in each ceremony" — DK-B ✓, KL3 19-2 A (the ledger = a set of wraps onto the same B) ✓. **Adopted: k-1**.

| Option | Content | Judgment |
|---|---|---|
| l-1 **Code identifiers kept as-is** (`master_key_wraps` / `MasterKeyWrap` / `master-key-wrap.json` / `MasterKeyOps` / the CLI's `MasterKeys` / `loadMasterKeys` / the keychain entry name `master::`). Newly introduced names are `ReserveKeys` / `OwnDeviceStore` / `device.ts`. **User-visible wording** (ADR-0017) is changed to "device key" / "reserve key" within the touched scope (`key show` / `key generate` / `loadMasterKeys`'s failure message / help) | Design record §1-3 "may be kept as-is". Changing the keychain entry name would make existing devices' keys unreadable (a migration would be needed). Test-vector file names unchanged (per the request) | **Adopted** |
| l-2 Rename across the board (`DeviceKeys` / `device::` entries) | Vocabulary aligns | Needs an entry-name migration (old name → new name copy), outside K4's scope. The diff balloons and the review focus (ceremony, Redacted, removals) blurs. Rejected |
| l-3 Rename only the type (`MasterKeys` → `DeviceKeys`), entry names kept | Middle | The type rename is mechanical but spans 40+ files; the meaning change (device key = the former master key) is conveyed in 1 JSDoc line. Rejected (carried to an independent PR post-K5) |

**Round 2**: counterexample "a reviewer misreads `MasterKeys` as the reserve key" → make `ReserveKeys` a separate type and write "= this device's device key (post-DK)" on `MasterKeys`'s JSDoc. Not a new option. **Empty round**. **Round 3**: empty round. Stopped. **Principle**: "don't change persisted names (keychain, file, table) without a migration. Align human-visible words to the canonical text's words" — ADR-0017 ✓. **Adopted: l-1**. CLAUDE.md's "master secret key" wording gets a revision proposal to the owner (not rewritten unilaterally).

### K4-13. The token-revocation surface (enumeration 1 round + 2 empty rounds · stopped)

AUTH §6 "correspondence with device keys": on revocation, propose targeted token revocation (SHOULD). The CLI has only `logout` (own token). SELF_HOSTING (K3) already announced `maruhi token revoke`.

| Option | Content | Judgment |
|---|---|---|
| m-1 **New group `maruhi token list` / `token revoke <token-id>` (zero values, no key needed, agent-gate not applied. `*` × admin token) + at the end of `device revoke`, propose candidates and on yes run `DELETE /auth/tokens/:id`. Candidates = rows matching the registry's `tokenId`; if none, rows named `cli:<registry label>` (`device add`'s default label = hostname, matching the default name `cli:<hostname>`). If the listing is 403 (a write-capped token), skip the candidate display and convey just the fact (§6's holding conditions). If declined, show that row's expiry** | The already-announced name. The 2 surfaces are existing APIs. Without a "surface", `device revoke`'s proposal could not be executed after a yes | **Adopted** |
| m-2 Complete inside `device revoke` alone (don't make a group) | Doesn't add surfaces | Wanting to revoke separately later (after declining the proposal) leaves only the Web. Contradicts the announcement. Rejected |
| m-3 `device revoke` always revokes the token too (default) | Certain | Already rejected at DK-L round 2 (misfiring other tokens via an advisory correspondence). Rejected |

**Round 2 (counterexample construction)**: counterexample 1 "the registry's tokenId is forged (the server slips in a different token id)" — what gets revoked is limited to one's own tokens (if the server returns another's id it's 404). Being made to delete a different token of one's own = bounded at availability loss, and the candidate shows name and expiry for a yes. Counterexample 2 "2 devices with the same hostname" — tokens of the same name are 1 under same-name rotation (§6), so the candidate is 1 row. Counterexample 3 "`device revoke --yes` in an agent environment" — the token-revocation proposal is **not** auto-accepted by `--yes` (an irreversible operation on a different resource — make it explicit via `--revoke-token`). **Round 3**: empty round. **Round 4**: empty round. Stopped. **Principle**: "operations off an advisory correspondence table stop at a proposal; execution requires named explicit consent" — K1-15 ✓, DK-L ✓. **Adopted: m-1 + the `--revoke-token` flag**.

### K4-16. Resolving the local device key and the receiving side's row-selection order (enumeration 1 round + 2 empty rounds · stopped)

| Option | Content | Judgment |
|---|---|---|
| p-1 **Signing paths: `ownDeviceOrFail(member, masterKeys)` = `member.devices.get(masterKeys.fingerprintHex)` (if absent, "this device's key is not registered on this project's chain — re-run `device approve` from a registered device"). Paths needing someone else's key (backfill target, guardian segments, issuance-signature verification) iterate **all devices** (verify the issuance signature on each device; 1 pass suffices). Receiving side (`GET /deks` / pull's `deks`): consider only rows whose `recipientEncPubHex` equals my enc public key (if absent, old server = all rows addressed to me), in the order **read → verify the registration signature (§5.1 — `recipientEncPubHex` is a signature input) → open → match the commitment**. If the same epoch carries 2 rows addressed to me, refuse (duplicate — existing discipline)** | The letter of K3's carryover. `soleDeviceOrFail`'s 8 call sites split into "resolving the local key" (6 signing-side) and "enumerating the peer's keys" (2) | **Adopted** |
| p-2 Use `devices`'s first on the signing side | Simple | Building the actor with a device that doesn't match the local secret key = the signature cannot verify (fails silently). Rejected |
| p-3 Try opening every row on the receiving side | Wire-independent | Already rejected at K3-14 n-4 (loses the poisoned-wrap vs other-device distinction). Rejected |

**Round 2**: counterexample "0 rows matching my enc public key" — (a) the device-add backfill hasn't landed → add "re-run `device approve`" to the pull warning (K4-K), (b) this device is already revoked → chain verification fails `ownDeviceOrFail` first. **Empty round**. **Round 3**: empty round. Stopped. **Adopted: p-1**.

### K4-17. Where the effective-authority pre-transmission checks live (enumeration 1 round + 2 empty rounds · stopped)

ES K4-C's 4 paths (`openEnvironment` / `requireWritingMember` / `environmentKeysFor` / explicit multi-environment calls) read the person's scope. Under DK, judge by **the signing device's effective authority** (§6.2 principle D1).

Sweep's actor scope (`sweepAfterMandate` / `splitWidenedByActorScope`) the same** | The substitution points are ES K4-C's enumeration (don't create new paths) | **Adopted** |
| q-2 Keep the person's scope (leave it to the server's 403 / 422) | No change | A `pull` on a cap'd device: DEK fetch → `var.read` recorded → open failure. Contradicts "don't wait for the server's 403" (§6.3). Rejected |
| q-3 Compute effective authority once and hold it on `ProjectContext` | 1 place | Per-environment judgments (scope ∩) can come from the same value, but it must be recomputed each time `VerifiedProject` is re-synced — the context's value goes stale. Computing per path is truer to the "same verified view" discipline. Rejected |

**Round 2**: counterexample "this device is already revoked (not on the chain)" → no device to pass to `effectivePermissionOf` → `ownDeviceOrFail`'s failure message. **Empty round**. **Round 3**: empty round. Stopped. **Adopted: q-1**.
