If it's a pre-DK copy, `--replace` generates a new key and erases the copy from this device"). `--replace` is explicit consent (works non-interactively too): it deletes the old key from the keychain, generates a new one, and issues the request. The old key remains on the original device (the pre-DK copy premise), so it isn't a loss** | The implementation shape of J-1 "deleting the copy". A non-interactive flag = consent | **Adopted** |

| r-3 If a key exists, issue the request with the existing key (don't generate) | Simple | The existing key K is already on the chain as D1 → the approving side sees "already registered" → nothing changes and the copy stays. Rejected |

**Round 2**: counterexample "`--replace` typed on what is actually the only device (not a copy)" → the key is lost. But it can be restored from the ledger (pre-DK: B = K / post-DK: the reserve key) (`key recover`). Write into the wording "don't type this if this device is the only one; restoration is `key recover`". **Empty round**. **Round 3**: empty round. Stopped. **Adopted: r-1**.

### K4 ruling list (adoptions and principles — pinned before implementation)

| # | Topic | Adopted | Principle |
|---|---|---|---|
| K4-1 | How the reserve key's secret is carried | Memory only · storage paths closed by the `ReserveKeys` type · order: seal → record → chain | The secret lives only in the ledger's ciphertext and the ceremony process's memory |
| K4-2 | Creation timing and opening for ledger changes | Generated at the first sealing · ledger changes open first · pre-DK discriminated by opened-B FP = local FP · separation via `key recovery` (`--replace` / `--passkey`) | Only one who can open the ledger may change it |
| K4-3 | The 3 conditions' local state | `own-devices.json` (reserve / approved / observed + revoked) · never written from the registry · cap / environment / DeviceLimit = pre-transmission checks and Notes | Writers are cryptographic facts only |
| K4-4 | Observation and sheltering's expansion | Use (3); provenance visualized via a Note (K4-9 corrects Warning → Note) | Don't hide changes to the device set |
| K4-5 | add's wait / approve's shape | Registry row as the signal → confirm on the chain · TTL-expiry guidance · re-run resumes | An advisory goes as far as a signal |
| K4-6 | approve's matching input | Full-length hex / 12 words (word list computed on the candidate side) · cap via `--cap` / `--env` / `--all-envs` / `--no-envs` | Matching is full-length; encodings are existing |
| K4-7 | revoke's confirmation UX | References = FP prefix / display name (FP alongside) · confirmation table → yes / `--yes` · the self-revocation note | Revocation is fixed by FP |
| K4-8 | device-revoked sweep | Duty = the device's effective scope at seq−1 · performance by the signing device's effective scope · carry-over is outOfScope | Revocation = removal from R(E) |
| K4-9 | Migration guidance | Folded into the no-reserve-key Warning · `key recovery` separates | Guidance comes from a fact |
| K4-10 | The post-recover key | `finishRecovery` (new device key → add_device by B → discard) · 1 question whether a reserve key (default no) · `--resume` | A restored key is used only to issue a device key |
| K4-11 | reserve rotate | Open → seal new → add → revoke + sweep → delete the old B's passkey / guardian rows | Register new → revoke old |
| K4-12 | Identifiers | In-code kept as-is · user wording = device / reserve | Don't change persisted names without a migration |
| K4-13 | Token revocation | `token list` / `token revoke` + `device revoke`'s proposal (`--revoke-token`) | Off an advisory, at most a proposal |
| K4-14 | DeviceOpsNotAccepted | A 1-sentence mapping · a post-separation 422 is carried as a Note | — |
| K4-15 | guardian approve | `key approve` deleted | No compat while no users exist |
| K4-16 | Device-key resolution / receiving order | `ownDeviceOrFail` · all-device enumeration · read → verify → open | The signer = the device matching the local key |
| K4-17 | Pre-transmission checks | Pass a device into ES K4-C's 4 paths | The signing device's effective authority |
| K4-18 | device add's existing key | Refuse / `--replace` | Explicit consent |
| K4-19 | key generate and the ledger | Refuse if a ledger exists / `--new-identity` | An identity is 1 |
| K4-20 | member list | `devices=n` + cap display · JSON `devices[]` · the joined column removed | Table + JSON |

### Mid-implementation additional rulings (K4-21–K4-30 — enumerated, counterexampled, principled on the spot)

Points that needed decisions mid-implementation. Each enumerated 3+ options, with counterexamples constructed in round 2 (the record is a summary).

| # | Topic | Options and judgment | Adoption / principle |
|---|---|---|---|
| K4-21 | `device add` re-run (the collision of K4-5 "re-run resumes waiting" and K4-18 "refuse if a key exists") | (a) Always refuse if a key exists — cannot resume interrupted waiting (contradicts K4-5 counterexample 1). (b) Always resume if a key exists — would issue a request even for a pre-DK copy (a return of K4-18 r-3). (c) **With a key present, resume if that key's FP has a request row (`GET /auth/devices/requests/:fp`) or a registry row; otherwise the K4-18 refusal (`--replace` guidance)**. Counterexample "re-running after the request expired at TTL" — no request row → refusal → the wording needs "`--replace` is only for a copy. To re-create a request with the same key …" → keep the refusal wording; write into the TTL-expiry wording (K4-5) "re-running creates a new request with the same key". But a device whose request expired and isn't in the registry is refused under (c) → **re-running after expiry isn't `device add --replace` but issuing a request with the same key without looking at the request-creation 409/404**: the implementation refused on "key present + no request row + no registry row" (safe side). Re-creating an expired request is not `--replace` but a procedure where a human re-carries the `key show` FP to the approving side (carryover 1) | (c). Principle "an advisory goes as far as a signal (K4-5) — resumption judgment also uses the registry / request rows only as 'signals of existence'" |
| K4-22 | Making approve's eligibility check device-aware (`eligibleApprovers` / `voteEligibility`) | (a) Judge both on the signing device — which device another person votes from cannot be known. (b) Both on "a person holding at least one effective-owner device" — approving from one's own cap'd device would eat a 422. (c) **One's own eligibility (`voteEligibility`) is judged on the device of the local key's FP; others' announcement (`eligibleApprovers`) stays "a person holding an effective-owner device"**. A keyless run (`approval show` via MARUHI_TOKEN) says "the device cannot be determined". Counterexample "the local key is unregistered" — add the `device-not-registered` reason and guide `device approve` | (c). Principle "one's own check is the signing device; others' announcement is the person" |
| K4-23 | Whether `member remove` / `change-role`'s sweep picks up `device-revoked` duties | (a) Pick them up (all duties of the same person) — a no-op re-run would pick up another operation's duty and start a rotate (the same problem as ES's S2). (b) **Don't pick them up (`memberMandatesFor` excludes `device-revoked`)** — performance is `device revoke`'s sweep and the standing warning. (c) A flag to choose — adds a surface | (b). Principle "each command converges the duties of its own operation" (ES existing) |
| K4-24 | How to recognize `DeviceOpsNotAccepted` (K4-14) at first sync | (a) Add a kind to `CliError` — a type change on every path. (b) `catchTag` on the append path and return a different type — breaks `appendWithCas`'s shared scaffolding. (c) **Recognize by the prefix of failure.ts's mapping sentence (1 place) and fold into a Note** | (c). Principle "the wording's truth is 1 place (K4-14 n-1) — the check leans on that same 1 place". When changing the wording, change device-sync.ts's prefix at the same time (carryover 2) |
| K4-25 | The position of `key recover`'s "key present" refusal | (a) Refuse after the ceremony (code input / passkey / request creation) — wastes key-material input and the request creation (the old test pinned "refuses before touching the server"). (b) **Refuse before the ceremony, before touching the server (`--resume` the only exception)** | (b). Principle "fail-closed happens at the foremost point" |
| K4-26 | The shape of the fingerprint book's set-ification | (a) Keep 1-person-1-FP and overwrite — adding a device erases the previous device's confirmation. (b) **A v2 of (origin, user_id) → { FP → confirmed-at }, reading v1 as a 1-element set and promoting to v2 on the next write**. (c) Record each device as a different person — breaks the person↔key relation. Counterexample "a mismatch warning" — an FP not in the set returns to the ceremony with 3 choices "a different device, a rebuilt key, or not the person" (no automatic failure) | (b). Principle "keys belong to devices, authority belongs to persons (§1 principle 7) — the book is also person → device set" |
| K4-27 | `guardian add`'s ordering (the ceremony and the ledger opening) | (a) Open the ledger first, then the ceremony — even a ceremony-failed run exposes B in memory. (b) **The per-guardian-device ceremony → open the ledger → seal → send** (the position to insert opening into the existing order is right before sealing) | (b). Principle "open a secret right before using it (K4-1)" |
| K4-28 | How the server's `rolesFor` treats the ward themself (formerly `["device"]`) | (a) null (a uniform 404) — a requester could not query their own request (used by future displays). (b) **An empty array (queryable, but no approval role)** — `approvalPermitted` is false when empty = cannot approve one's own request | (b). Principle "removal removes the role, not the surface" |
| K4-29 | K4-9's warning trigger condition | (a) Ledger unregistered (a server declaration) — cannot tell pre-DK (K4-9 i-3). (b) **One's on-chain devices are only {this device} and the local record has no reserve key**. (c) Only "no reserve key in the local record" — would fire every time on an approved second device (before observation fills it) | (b). Principle "one fact, one guidance (K4-9)" |
| K4-30 | The grounds for recording/registering the reserve key's cap (owner / all) without a ceremony (a review finding) | (a) Put the reserve key through `device approve`'s ceremony (TTY + non-agent) — a reserve key's only use is restoration after losing devices, at which point no device exists to run the ceremony (K4-1). (b) Let a human choose the reserve key's cap — the restore-target device can be added with a different cap at restore time (the reserve key's own cap = "the restorable range" = the person's full authority is fine). (c) **The reserve key is fixed at (owner, all), and the first-sync registration (`syncOwnDevices`) adds it only to projects "whose syncing device's cap wraps it (`capWithinSignerCap`)". The ceremony is carried by the ledger's opening (code input / passkey = presenting the person's secret)** — the reserve key's secret lives only in the ledger (not on disk or in a record), and one who can open the ledger = the person themself. cap exceeding the person's authority cannot happen under `effectivePermissionOf`'s ∩ | (c). Principle "a ceremony attaches to an operation that emits a secret (K4-1 · K4-6) — registering the public side emits no secret". When tightening `device approve`'s gate, treat this path separately since it relies on the ledger-side ceremony (carryover 7). **Round 2 (counterexamples)**: counterexample 1 "an attacker who opened the ledger gains full authority via the reserve key" — the material for opening the ledger is the code / passkey = the person's secret, and the reserve key's secret lives nowhere else (K4-1). One who opened it cannot be distinguished from the person = equivalent to a ceremony. Counterexample 2 "first-syncing on a capped device (listed {}) adds the reserve key" — skipped pre-transmission by `capWithinSignerCap` + Note (K4-3 counterexample 3). Counterexample 3 "registering the reserve key exceeds the person's authority" — `effectivePermissionOf` is person ∩ device; cap = (owner, all) is only an upper bound. **Rounds 3 & 4**: empty. Stopped |

### Additional rulings during review handling (K4-31–K4-38 — responses to PR #188's review findings)

Fixes in response to review findings (Cursor Bugbot / pullfrog) were initially implemented as the finding's indicated option with the record omitted (a §3 procedure violation — surfaced by the owner's finding on 2026-09-21). Here each judgment's option enumeration and counterexample rounds are re-run, confirmed to match the implementation, and recorded. No ruling changed the implementation (except K4-32's carryover).

| Ruling | Topic | Candidates and counterexamples | Adopted / principle |
|---|---|---|---|
| K4-31 | When `device approve`'s latter stage (local record, registry PUT, request cancel) happens | (a) Always (the original) — even when every project is failed / skipped it records · PUTs · cancels → the requester proceeds on the registry row's signal to the chain check, ends at "0 projects", and the approver's re-run cannot match since the request is gone. (b) Gate only the cancel; record and PUT always — the registry row is a fake signal (the requester exits waiting), and record "approved" makes first sync repeat the same failure every time. (c) **Gate all 3 on "≥1 registered / already"; otherwise warn and exit code 1 (1 even when all skipped)**. **Round 2 (counterexamples)**: counterexample 1 "part success, part failure" — the latter stage runs. The failed parts converge to already / registered on re-run (idempotent). Counterexample 2 "all already (a re-run of an already-registered)" — the latter stage runs but the record overwrites, the PUT is same-value, the cancel swallows a 404 (existing). Counterexample 3 "exit code 0 when all skipped" — looks like success though neither record nor cancel ran → make it 1 (pullfrog finding 2). **Rounds 3 & 4**: empty | (c). Principle "writing to an advisory comes after the truth (the chain) (K4-5 / DK-D) — don't write a fact not yet on the chain into the registry / record first" |
| K4-32 | `key reserve rotate`'s revocation set (re-run after interruption) | (a) Only the opened B (the original) — if the last run was interrupted at the ledger swap, B is the last run's new key and the original reserve key stays on the chain (Bugbot). (b) All (owner, all) devices of one's on-chain set other than B — would revoke even an approved regular device (a second PC). (c) **B + the local record's source-"reserve" rows (regardless of the revoked flag) − the new key**. `appendRevokeDevice` targets only on-chain-valid devices, so idempotent. **Round 2 (counterexamples)**: counterexample 1 "re-run on a device that lost its record" — without the record it degrades to (a), unable to detect the left-behind original reserve key. Another device's record enters via observation (observed), not source "reserve", so it cannot be picked up → a structural limit. A procedure comparing the reserve key's provenance (reserve / observed) and cap in `device list` goes to carryover 8. Counterexample 2 "the Sealed rows growing with each rotate" — the wording names B and counts the rest (pullfrog nit). Counterexample 3 "double-revoking by re-including a revoked-flagged row" — if invalid on the chain, `signRevokeDevice` excludes it, and `markRevoked` updates only null rows (pullfrog follow-up). **Rounds 3 & 4**: empty | (c) + carryover 8. Principle "the revocation target is the set of 'keys this device knows as reserve keys', not a single one (K4-11's re-run safety)" |
| K4-33 | When the request query fails in `device add`'s 409 resumption | (a) Use `Date.now()` as the deadline (the original) — misguides "it expired" on the first wait round, hiding the query failure. (b) **Convey the failure as a typed error**. (c) Wait with a deadline TTL ahead — waits up to 15 minutes past the real deadline and hides the failure too. `device-registered` skips the query (no request row) and picks up the registry row as the signal on the first round (the deadline is nominally TTL ahead). **Round 2 (counterexamples)**: counterexample 1 "emitting a generic 'request deadline / get it approved' on device-registered" — guidance that cannot be followed → branched by the discriminated type `RequestState`, not emitted (K4-35). Counterexample 2 "the query drops on a transient 5xx" — (b) can resume with the same key on re-run (K4-21). **Rounds 3 & 4**: empty | (b). Principle "don't hide a failure precisely on a recovery path (the narrow, misleading path is the worst — pullfrog)". CLAUDE.md "no swallowing" |
| K4-34 | When multiple requests for the same key match | (a) Take the first (the original). With the same public key, `add_device`'s payload is identical and the approval result the same = no real harm. (b) **Show the labels and stop** — the server should make FPs unique; multiple is a sign of a server-invariant violation. (c) Let the user pick by label (interactive) — the result is the same either way; only adds a ceremony stage. **Round 2 (counterexamples)**: counterexample 1 "stopping is excessive when there's no real harm" — the grounds for stopping is not a difference in result but that "the server's response is breaking this CLI's premise". Don't answer a premise-broken input with a grant of authority (fail-closed — §3). Counterexample 2 "an old server returning 1 request duplicated across 2 pages" — the request list is 1 page (cap 5/h), so duplication cannot happen. **Rounds 3 & 4**: empty | (b). Principle "don't pass a broken premise silently (fail-closed)" |
| K4-35 | Expressing `device add`'s resumption result | (a) Return only the deadline number and branch the wording (part of pullfrog's original suggestion) — the caller re-derives the "no request" fact. (b) **A discriminated type `{ pending, expiresAtMs } \| { already-registered }`** — the caller cannot emit request-shape guidance. (c) If a registry row exists, skip the wait and go straight to the chain check — when the registry GET transiently fails (null), the wait's retry is lost. Also split `deviceKeyForRequest`'s resumption Note into "request exists" vs "registry only" (Bugbot finding 3 — saying the same fact consistently in 2 places). **Round 2 (counterexample)**: counterexample "already-registered yet a request row exists too" — the request-creation 409 reasons are exclusive (the server looks at the registry first). **Rounds 3 & 4**: empty | (b). Principle "facts travel in types (no re-deriving from strings)" |
| K4-36 | `device revoke`'s sweep source and handling post-acceptance failure | (a) Sweep on the pre-append view (the original — a bug: `appendWithCas` returns the pre-append view, so the `device-revoked` duty is absent and the sweep is always empty). (b) Re-sync via `resyncExtended` after acceptance, folding failure into the revocation's failure — an accepted revocation gets reported as "failed", drops out of `revokedAll`, and the local record's revocation and registry deletion are skipped (K4-3 counterexample 1's re-registration prevention leaks — Bugbot / pullfrog). (c) **Re-sync, and report a post-acceptance failure as `sweepFailed`, keeping the revocation (exit code 1)**. (d) On re-sync failure, fall back to the pre-append view and sweep null (pullfrog's suggestion) — the failure gets demoted to a Note and unperformed rotates go silent at exit code 0. **Round 2 (counterexamples)**: counterexample 1 "no means to re-run only the sweep after sweepFailed" — re-running `device revoke` ends at "already revoked". The standing warning names the environment and `env rotate` converges (pullfrog residual). Adding a means grows a surface (carryover 9). Counterexample 2 "the append was accepted yet revoked is empty" — `revoked.length === 0` means "nothing appended (already revoked)" and skips the re-sync. **Rounds 3 & 4**: empty | (c). Principle "an accepted fact isn't erased by a later failure (the same discipline as member remove's `resyncExtended`)" |
| K4-37 | First-sync auto-registration (`syncOwnDevices` (c)) performs `add_device` + DEK wraps on the grounds of the unsigned `own-devices.json` (Cursor Security Reviewer HIGH): an agent environment could plant rows and bypass `device approve`'s ceremony gate (K4-6) | (a) As-is — the record's 3 provenances derive from a ceremony or a verified chain, but the file itself is writable by anyone. An agent denied `device approve` could write a row and run `maruhi pull` to add a signer. (b) Sign / HMAC each row with the device key and verify — closes file tampering, but a crypto operation absent from the spec (CRYPTO_SPEC) = needs a spec revision and owner approval (CLAUDE.md). Impossible in this PR. (c) **Perform only the registration step inside the same ceremony gate as `device approve` (known agent → stdin / stdout must be a terminal), and if it doesn't pass, show the candidates in a Note and skip** (observation / revocation recording involves no signature, so as before). (d) Ask the human yes / no per candidate — a question fires every time the reserve key is added to a new project (changing K4-3's "add silently" UX). Owner's province. (e) Stop auto-registration and make it an explicit operation like `device approve --self` — makes reserve-key registration manual, losing K4-3's purpose (automatic maintenance of restorability). **Round 2 (counterexamples)**: counterexample 1 "a file planted in a person's terminal session (a malicious same-user process)" — under (c) it registers (a Note does appear). A same-user-privilege attacker can rewrite the config's `server` and the binary too, so it is outside the ceremony gate's threat model (agent environments — ADR-0016 decision 7). Closing it needs (b) → proposed to the owner as carryover 10. Counterexample 2 "want to register the reserve key to a new project in CI / an agent" — carried over until a human syncs on a device (fail-closed). Distinguished by a "recorded but unregistered" Note rather than the no-reserve-key warning (K4-9). Counterexample 3 "a human behind a pipe (`maruhi pull \| tee`)" — a non-terminal is skipped, and the Note guides a re-run. Counterexample 4 "too many Notes" — only 1 line, only when candidates exist. **Rounds 3 & 4**: empty | (c) + carryover 10. Principle "a signature-bearing operation lives inside the ceremony gate (K4-6) — if the grounding state is unsigned, gate on the operation's side" |
| K4-38 | `key recovery --replace`'s flag description says "revoke the old reserve key on every project" while the implementation only seals a new key (Cursor Security Reviewer MEDIUM). On code leak the old reserve key (owner / all) stays alive | (a) Align the description to the implementation (revocation is manual) — the period in which someone who opened the ledger with the leaked code can keep `add_device`ing with the old reserve key depends on manual action. (b) **Align the implementation to the description: seal the new key without opening the ledger, revoke on every project the reserve keys in this device's record (including revoked-flagged ones) (signed by this device's key — the same path as rotate) + delete the ledger rows that sealed the old B**. If absent from the record, warn "couldn't revoke" and guide `device list` / `device revoke`. (c) Abolish `--replace` and lean on `key reserve rotate` — rotate needs B opened, and the escape for a lost code disappears. **Round 2 (counterexamples)**: counterexample 1 "`--replace` on a device that lost its record" — (b) cannot revoke and warns (the same limit as K4-32 — carryover 8). Counterexample 2 "deleting the ledger's passkey / guardian rows without opening" — they only restore the old B (K4-11), so deleting is fine. Counterexample 3 "agent environment" — code issuance is a ceremony and refused on TTY (existing). **Rounds 3 & 4**: empty | (b). Principle "the promise shown to the user (help) and the implementation must match — on the safe side, align the implementation to the promise" |

### K4 completion record (2026-09-21)

- **Deliverables**: new under `apps/cli/src/`: `device.ts` (add / approve / list / revoke), `device-ops.ts` (2 ops' appends + backfill + sweep kind 5), `device-sync.ts` (first-sync registration and observation), `device-key.ts` (device-key resolution), `own-devices.ts` (`<config dir>/own-devices.json`), `reserve.ts` / `ledger-open.ts` / `key-recover.ts` / `key-record.ts` (reserve key, ledger opening, restoration, rotate), `token.ts`. Modified: `dek-wrap.ts` (R(E)'s device expansion, `requireWritingMember`'s effective authority), `deks.ts` (own-device row selection, effective scope), `member.ts` (all-device backfill, `deks.remove`'s device key, `member list`'s device column), `rotation-sweep.ts` (`device-revoked` + `partitionSweepBaselines`), `scope.ts` / `context.ts` (device-aware pre-transmission checks + the sync registration hook), `approval-rules.ts` (the signing device's eligibility), `known-fingerprints.ts` (the v2 set), `guardian.ts` / `handoff.ts` / `passkey.ts` / `recovery.ts` / `keygen.ts` (reserve key), `failure.ts` (K4-14 / DeviceLimit), `agent-gate.ts` (the ceremony gate's common shape). Server: removed the `device` branch from `handlers-key-wraps.ts`'s `approvalPermitted` (the ward's role is empty), D1 migration `20260921003334_dk_k4_drop_handoff_blob` dropping `key_handoff_approvals`'s 3 blob columns. api-schema: `HandoffSourceSchema` is ledger-id only · `blob` removed · the old device's 1 excluded from `MAX_HANDOFF_APPROVALS_PER_REQUEST`.
- **Tests**: `device.test.ts` (the ceremony gate, full-length FP / 12-word matching, ignoring declared-FP mismatches, first-sync's 3 provenances + revocation / registry negatives, observation provenance and revocation recording, expected-count agreement, revoke's confirmation table / last-device-protected, add's `--replace` / TTL, wire-type `source: "device"` rejection, non-persistence of the reserve-key secret). Existing tests = recovery / passkey / guardian / handoff / agent / member-add / member-list / invite / known-fingerprints / approval-rules / keygen / help golden revised to the new behavior. All 7 quality-gate stages green (fallow's new complexity / duplication / unused-exports resolved by splitting).
- **Carryovers** (to the canonical texts, K5 / K6): (1) Re-running after the request's TTL expiry is "re-create the request with the same key", but reconciling with the keyed-device default refusal (K4-18) was cut at "resume when a request row or a registry row exists" — after expiry (neither exists) the path isn't `--replace` but guides the approving side's re-run; write it into K6. (2) Recognizing `DeviceOpsNotAccepted` is by failure.ts's wording prefix (K4-24). (3) `eligibleApprovers` stays a per-person announcement (K4-22) — a note candidate on canonical §6.2's "approve ballot device vocabulary" saying "announcement is by person, judgment is by device". (4) `revoke_device` acceptance doesn't revoke the token — `device revoke` stops at a proposal (K4-13); `DELETE /auth/tokens/:tokenId` sufficed on an existing surface, so no new surface. (5) `known-fingerprints.json` is v2 (a set) — K5's Web doesn't read it. (6) CLAUDE.md's "master secret key" wording: propose to the owner a revision to "this device's key secret (post-DK)" (code identifiers stay — K4-12). (7) Registering the reserve key's (owner, all) is ceremony-free (K4-30) — when changing `device approve`'s gate, check this path (`device-sync.ts`'s `registrationCandidates` source "reserve") separately. (8) `key reserve rotate`'s revocation set relies on the local record (K4-32) — re-running on a device that lost the record cannot detect the left-behind original reserve key. Write into K6 a procedure comparing (owner, all) devices with provenance reserve / observed in `device list`, and `device revoke`ing unrecognized ones. (9) No surface exists to re-run only the sweep after `sweepFailed` (K4-36) — it converges via the standing warning + `env rotate`. (10) `own-devices.json` is unsigned (K4-37) — tampering by a same-user-privilege process isn't closed by the ceremony gate. Closing it needs device-key signatures on rows (a crypto operation not in CRYPTO_SPEC = a spec revision + owner approval). Put to the owner as a proposal.

## 10. K5 addendum (2026-09-21 — Web: device read-out display)

Rulings made in K5's implementation PR. Like §9: candidates (3+) → rounds (construct counterexamples that break the leading candidate — a hostile server's declarations, revoke/re-add ordering, an old server's 404, 16 devices / 32 registry rows, a pre-DK member, a missing tokenId, the same person's other device, the empty state, 401 / 403, screen width — and look for an option that eliminates them. Stop after 2 empty rounds) → principle extraction (✓ / ✗ against CRYPTO §1 principles 1–7, ADR-0018 revision 2's boundary and display discipline, DK-D "the registry is no source of truth", ES K4-D / PF1 K6-J Web precedents). **Pinned before implementation**; additional judgments mid-implementation are added as K5-11+ in the same shape. Premises (not re-litigated): the Web doesn't implement verification and renders "as reported by the server" (ADR-0018 revision 2, item 4); mutations are revocation-family only; no chain writes, device registration / deletion / approval (DK-K).

**Fact confirmation (single round)**: (1) A `ChainSnapshot` `add_device` entry's payload carries **2 public keys and a cap**, and **carries no device FP** (FP = SHA-256(enc ‖ sig)[:16] is a derived value outside the signed object — CRYPTO §3). `revoke_device` carries **an FP list**. So the addition and the revocation identify differently, and tying them needs an FP computation. (2) K6-J's principle "the Web's folding reproduces the chain's semantics only within what is mechanically transcribable from the server declaration's byte layout, and **computes no crypto (hash / signature)**" applies here too (the FP is a plain SHA-256 involving no LP normalization, but K6-J's principle targets hashes in general). (3) `GET /auth/devices` is in the session-allowed enumeration (`session-capability.ts`). PUT / DELETE / the request family are session-rejected. (4) `RotationFlagSchema.trigger` already contains `revoke_device`, and the row's target is `targetUserId` (no device FP carried). (5) The audit mirrors `chain.device_added` / `chain.device_revoked` can be rendered by the existing generic drawing (event name, actor, target, the payload's JSON verbatim). (6) `apps/web/src/ui.package/` doesn't exist, and this PR needs no new visual pattern (the existing Table / Token / HexText / Callout suffice).

| Ruling | Topic | Candidates and counterexamples | Adoption / principle |
|---|---|---|---|
| K5-1 | The folding unit of the device set, and the FP's provenance (the wire carries no FP) | (a) Call WebCrypto's SHA-256 on the Web to compute the FP — the device set becomes accurate, but touches K6-J's principle (compute no hash) and the interpretation of ADR-0018 revision 2 item 4 = an owner judgment. (b) **Identify devices by the public-key pair (enc, sig), and bind FPs only "within what is mechanically transcribable from the server declaration's byte layout"**: the genesis actor's FP is the first key's FP; thereafter, an entry signed by that member binds its actor FP to the device only when "exactly 1 FP-unbound device" exists (with 2+, don't bind; keep it as a member-level "unbound signing FP"). A `revoke_device` FP matching a bound device removes that device; a non-matching FP is removed arithmetically "for as many unbound devices" (if the non-matching FP count = the unbound-device count, remove all. If fewer, count "some of them were revoked (u of them)" as **unresolved** and keep the device count = rows − unresolved, always accurate. If more, the rule cannot be read = ignore that row). A learned public-key → FP correspondence is kept across the whole chain and carried into re-adds / re-memberships after revocation (a generalization of K6-J's "same key ⇒ same FP"). (c) Don't show FPs, only the device count — needs the revocation matching, so it includes (b)'s arithmetic: inferior to (b). (d) Pull the audit mirror `chain.device_added` (its payload carries the FP) from Overview and tie — needs paging through all audit rows and depends on the class's visibility (the same rejection reason as K6-J J-d). (e) Add the FP to the wire (a server annotation) — an api-schema change = not added in this PR (request §1-bis). **Round 2 (counterexamples)**: counterexample 1 "a reserve key never signs" — the reserve key R lives only in the ledger and doesn't normally sign, so its FP stays unbound. Under (b), the cap (owner/all) and the addition seq come out accurately but the FP is "not reported". The display is a "fingerprint not reported" chip with the cap attached (K5-3). Counterexample 2 "the same person adds D2 and R in sequence from D1, and D2 signs later" — 2 unbound means no binding, and D2's signing FP enters the unbound-signing FPs. Later revoking D2 gives 1 non-matching FP against 2 unbound → unresolved 1 (the device count is accurate; which one is unknown — the display is "1 of these was revoked; which one is not reported"). Restoring accuracy needs (a) or (e) → put to the owner. Counterexample 3 "a hostile server declares the same FP twice, an out-of-scope FP, or all devices revoked" — each FP must be a currently-valid device (`unknown-device`), and 0 devices after revocation is invalid (`last-device-protected`), so an unreadable shape is ignored row-and-all (fail-closed — the same treatment as the existing "ignore unknown ops"). Counterexample 4 "the same person re-registers a revoked key via `add_device`" — valid under §6.2 (not a duplicate unless currently valid). (b) folds it as a re-add by the public-key pair and carries over the learned FP. Counterexample 5 "a pre-DK member (1 device = the first key)" — the genesis creator is bound by the actor FP; an `add_member`-ed member stays unbound until it signs (same behavior as K6-J). A device count of 1 is accurate. **Rounds 3 & 4**: empty | **(b)**. Principle "the Web's folding ties only what is mechanically transcribable from the declaration's byte layout; for what cannot be tied, keep counts accurate (arithmetic) and show identification honestly as 'not reported'. Don't compute hashes" — K6-J ✓, ADR-0018 revision 2 item 4 ✓, ES K4-D (scope folding) ✓. **A point to put to the owner 1**: the proper means of tying `add_device` FPs on the Web (permit WebCrypto SHA-256 as "deriving a display identifier", or have the server annotate the entry with the derived FP). Until decided, (b)'s degraded shape (unresolved / not reported) is authoritative |
| K5-2 | The four-eyes ballot's device vocabulary (moving K6-J's "the member's current key FP, 1 of it" to a device set) | (a) Keep as-is (the 1 FP that last signed) — when the same person's other device signs, the previous device's ballot disappears (approve on D2 → `add_device` on D1 → D2's ballot stops being counted). An error post-DK. (b) **Count a ballot if its FP matches a current member's bound device and that device's effective role is owner (the person is owner and the roleCap is owner). An unbindable FP (an unbound signing FP) is counted — without identification, accurately — if **all** of that person's unbound devices have roleCap owner ("whichever device, it's an owner"). Otherwise don't count (fail-closed)**. (c) Count regardless of FP if a current owner — already rejected at K6-J round 2 (fail-open). **Round 2 (counterexamples)**: counterexample 1 "an unbound-signing-FP device later gets ambiguously revoked" — when unresolved grows, drop that person's unbound signing FPs (a revoked device's ballot is revoked — §6.2). It falls to the not-counting side, possibly leaving a shape where an applied remove stays in Members (the side that shows vanished access = the same residual as K6-J; never the side that hides live access). Counterexample 2 "a cap (member, …) phone declares an approve" — a ballot from a non-owner-roleCap device isn't counted (§6.2's effective role). Counterexample 3 "an owner's old ballot after re-adding with a different key" — the new membership's device is a different public-key pair with unbound FP → the old FP doesn't match and isn't counted (as in the existing test). With the same key, the learned FP carries over and it's counted (as in the existing test). **Rounds 3 & 4**: empty | **(b)**. Principle "a ballot's judgment is the device (§6.2's device vocabulary); when it cannot be identified, copy the conclusion only if 'the conclusion is the same on every candidate device'" — K4-22 "judgment is the device" ✓, K6-J's fail-closed ✓ |
| K5-3 | The Members table's information design | (a) Only a device-count column. (b) **A "Devices" column with the device count + a chip per device (bound = the FP's short form `shortId`; cap is attached as `(role/scope)` only when capped, same as the CLI's `describeDevice`. Unbound = "fingerprint not reported" + cap. If unresolved > 0, one sentence "n of these was revoked; which one is not reported"). The chip's `description` carries the full-length FP and cap**. (c) A separate "Devices" section (table) under Members — the row↔person correspondence drifts apart. (d) Row expansion (Collapsible) — adds an interactive element inside the Table. **Round 2 (counterexamples)**: counterexample 1 "16 devices" — chips wrap (HStack wrap — same shape as ScopeChips). Counterexample 2 "cap's letter differs from the CLI" — `describeCap` = `owner/all` / `member/dev, staging` / `owner/no environments` should be the same function-shape on the Web (the Web doesn't import crypto, so the string functions are transcribed — for the existing `describeMemberScope` vocabulary "all environments / no environments / ids", device cap follows the CLI's `all`: CLI `describeCap` is `all`, `describeScope` is `all environments`. The Web's cap display aligns to CLI `describeCap`'s letter). Counterexample 3 "the same person's other device with the same cap" — chips distinguish by FP; unbound-to-unbound have only cap, so identical-looking chips line up (the count stays accurate). **Rounds 3 & 4**: empty | **(b)**. Principle "the same fact has the same letter on Web and CLI (K4-20's `devices=n` + cap)" |
| K5-4 | The range of consensus rules the fold transcribes (the fail-closed line) | (a) Transcribe every consensus rule (monotonicity, role rules, scope inclusion) — needs signing-device identification (sometimes impossible at K5-1), and ignoring rows over an untranscribable rule corrupts the device count. Also approaches a second implementation of verification (ADR-0018). (b) **Only the structural rules needed for the fold's consistency**: `add_device` = the actor is a current member · the public key doesn't duplicate any current member's devices (`duplicate-member-key`) · the payload shape is readable. `revoke_device` = the target is a current member · the FPs are readable (no duplicates, each FP resolvable arithmetically) · doesn't end at 0 devices (`last-device-protected`). If unreadable, ignore the row. Monotonicity, role rules, scope inclusion are not transcribed (the province of the server's acceptance surface — the same line as the existing change_role / remove folds). (c) Check nothing — a hostile declaration drives the device count negative / erases a nonexistent device. **Round 2 (counterexamples)**: counterexample 1 "a declaration placing `add_device` / `revoke_device` inside a propose" — §6.2 forbids proposing them (`approval-not-required`). The fold is mounted only on ENTRY_FOLDERS (direct entries) and isn't applied as an inner op (the summary shows the op name). Counterexample 2 "`add_member`'s duplicate key isn't checked by the existing fold" — asymmetric but the existing isn't changed (outside this PR's scope. The device set's uniqueness is directly needed for the device ops' fold consistency, so only the device side is checked). **Rounds 3 & 4**: empty | **(b)**. Principle "the fold transcribes only the rules needed for its own state's consistency, and doesn't claim to verify" — ADR-0018 revision 2 item 4 ✓, the existing chain-view discipline ✓ |
| K5-5 | The wording of `trigger = revoke_device` | (a) Fold into "member removed" (current — a targetUserId is treated as remove) — would misread a device revocation as an ejection. (b) **"device revoked: <userId>"** (in the remove / change_role / revoke_server row. The flag has no FP, so just the person). (c) "device revoked" + FP — not on the wire. **Round 2 (counterexample)**: counterexample "an old server row with no trigger" — keep the existing fallback (inferring from the target's presence). The `revoke_device` branch enters only on the `trigger` letter. **Rounds 3 & 4**: empty | **(b)**. Principle "a flag's wording comes from the trigger's letter, or the existing inference when absent" (ES K4's Web row) |
| K5-6 | The Audit tab's device 2 events | (a) A dedicated drawing per event (a table in `audit-read.ts`) — the existing has dedicated drawing only for `var.read`'s aggregate shape; other mirror events use the generic drawing. (b) **Keep the generic drawing (event name, actor, target, the payload JSON verbatim) and pin "doesn't break" with a fixture and e2e**. (c) Promote the FP to a detail fragment — it already appears in the payload JSON. **Round 2 (counterexample)**: counterexample "the payload has an array (deviceKeyFingerprints)" — `RecordedPayload` renders via JSON.stringify regardless of shape. The visibility class is the existing one (no new class for device events — AUDIT §6). **Rounds 3 & 4**: empty | **(b)**. Principle "audit is verbatim (display discipline §4)" |
| K5-7 | Devices screen (S11)'s information design / placement | (a) Inside the Members table (project axis) — the registry is user-axis; mixing it into the project's table makes "chain devices" and "registry rows" look like the same truth. (b) **An independent route `/dashboard/devices` (the ruling CP user axis = same placement as S9). Columns = Label (the registry display name — text node only) / Fingerprint (full-length HexText — a reference value) / Registered (ServerTime) / Token (K5-8). Description = "as reported by the server; the chain is the source of truth — `maruhi device list` verifies it". Order = the server declaration's (don't sort). A loss-time guidance Callout (`maruhi device revoke <fingerprint>` → token revocation here)**. (c) A tab on the API tokens screen — tokens and the registry are different resources (the registry has 32 rows, devices 16). **Round 2 (counterexamples)**: counterexample 1 "display-name forgery (swapped by the server / the person)" — always place the full-length FP next to the display name, and call it advisory in the description. Put the label only as a `Text` child (a text node); don't embed it in href / attributes. Counterexample 2 "32 rows" — 1 screen (no paging — the API has none either). Counterexample 3 "empty state" — EmptyNotice "No devices registered". Counterexample 4 "the same person's other device with the same label" — distinguishable by FP (full-length alongside). Counterexample 5 "width 390px" — the Table scrolls horizontally (same as existing tables). **Rounds 3 & 4**: empty | **(b)**. Principle "the registry is advisory self-information (user axis), not put in the same box as the chain (project axis) (DK-D)" |
| K5-8 | Matching `tokenId` against the token list and the path to revocation | (a) Show the raw tokenId and link only — the API tokens screen has no id column, so even arriving there you can't find it. (b) **The Devices screen also reads `GET /auth/tokens`, matches on tokenId, and shows the name + prefix (if not found, "not among your tokens, as reported" + id). The row gets "Revoke token" (the existing `useRevocation` + `RevokeDialog` — `DELETE /auth/tokens/:tokenId`) and a link to the API tokens screen**. (c) Add an id column to the tokens screen — widens the screen for a far path. (d) Highlight the row via `?token=` — grows route state. **Round 2 (counterexamples)**: counterexample 1 "tokenId missing" — the Token column is "—" (no token linked). Counterexample 2 "no match (revoked / expired and gone from the list)" — "not among your tokens" + id, no Revoke (nothing to revoke). Counterexample 3 "only the token-list fetch fails" — render the registry; the Token column shows just the id instead of a FailureNotice (don't drop the whole screen on a partial failure). Counterexample 4 "re-fetching after revocation" — re-read both resources (the registry's tokenId field stays advisory, so it lands on counterexample 2's shape). Counterexample 5 "XSS blast radius" — revocation is an ADR-0018 permitted mutation (reduces credentials). **Rounds 3 & 4**: empty | **(b)**. Principle "the path follows a fact (the token's presence), and revocation uses the existing permitted surface as-is (K4-13 'off an advisory, at most a proposal' in Web form)" |
| K5-9 | The degraded shape for old servers (no `devices` group = 404) / 403 | (a) The generic "Not found" wording — readable as "your account has no such project". (b) **Add `device` (the registry) to `FailureSubject`, and map 404 to "The server reports no device registry for your account (older servers do not have one)."**. (c) Fold 404 into the empty state — doesn't distinguish a missing surface from an empty one (fail-open-leaning). **Round 2 (counterexamples)**: counterexample 1 "403 session-not-allowed" — the existing ForbiddenNotice (guides to the CLI). Counterexample 2 "401" — the shell goes to sign-in (existing). **Rounds 3 & 4**: empty | **(b)**. Principle "unify wording (ruling BP) — change only the noun (accompanying ruling CN)" |
| K5-10 | Handling the boundaries (16 devices / 32 registry rows / empty / width) | (a) Add paging / folding. (b) **Don't add (chips wrap, the table scrolls horizontally, empty is EmptyNotice)**. (c) Cut at the cap. **Round 2 (counterexample)**: counterexample "16 chips × 64 hex" — chips are the short form; the full length lives in description (hover / SR). **Rounds 3 & 4**: empty | **(b)**. Principle "the API holds the caps (§12-8 / §13-11). The Web only transcribes" |

**Display-discipline check (ADR-0018 revision 2, item 4)**: every new display carries an "as reported by the server" note (the Members description, the Devices screen description, the empty state's prescribed wording). Nothing claims "verified". An FP is a reference value (not matching material — the Devices screen's guidance steers to the CLI: "`maruhi device list` verifies it"). **CSP / strings**: the registry's label / FP / tokenId are drawn only as text nodes; the only thing embedded in an href is `apiPaths.tokenRevoke(tokenId)` (`encodeURIComponent` — the existing discipline).

### Mid-implementation additional rulings (K5-11+ — enumerated, counterexampled, principled on the spot)

| Ruling | Topic | Candidates and counterexamples | Adopted / principle |
|---|---|---|---|
| K5-11 | The Audit tab's "verbatim payload" drawing breaks under strict CSP (the e2e opened a payload-bearing row for the first time on the device-2-events fixture, and Astryx `CodeBlock`'s row chunks emitted an inline `style` (contain-intrinsic-block-size), rejected by `style-src 'self'` — a latent bug since W2. The aggregate `var.read`'s remaining payloads and lease events take the same path) | (a) Leave it (payloads stay undrawable) — audit's recorded values can't be seen = K5-6's "doesn't break" counterexample. (b) Add `'unsafe-inline'` / `'unsafe-hashes'` to CSP — CLAUDE.md forbidden. (c) **Drop `CodeBlock` and draw with `Text type="code"` + xstyle (`whiteSpace: pre-wrap` / `overflowWrap: anywhere`) inside a muted `Card`** (no syntax highlighting needed — a recorded value's JSON). (d) Build a CSP-safe CodeBlock equivalent in `ui.package` — the ③ / ④ stage, but what's needed is just pre-wrap text, which ② xstyle covers. (e) An upstream issue (move CodeBlock's inline style to xstyle / CSS variables) — can run in parallel; this PR doesn't wait. **Round 2 (counterexamples)**: counterexample 1 "a single long hex line" — wraps via `overflowWrap: anywhere` (the same override as HexText — its 3rd appearance = a candidate for variant-ification / ui.package promotion [carryover]). Counterexample 2 "payload is an array / nested" — `JSON.stringify(_, null, 2)`'s newlines are kept by pre-wrap. Counterexample 3 "why the existing e2e passed" — no test opened a payload-bearing row (the fixtures had no payload). This PR's e2e pins it. **Rounds 3 & 4**: empty | **(c) + carry (e) over**. Principle "don't loosen CSP; make the drawing fit CSP (CLAUDE.md 'the Web is TCB'). Styling follows ① → ② (ADR-0013)" |
| K5-12 | Re-registering the same key via `add_device` after an ambiguous revocation (unresolved) makes the leftover row look like a duplicate key, the re-add is ignored, and the device count falls below the chain's (PR #189 Cursor Bugbot — Medium) | (a) As-is — hides a live device (the opposite error direction from K6-J round 2, but a display showing less than truth isn't the fail-closed side; it's just wrong). (b) **An accepted `add_device` means "that key is not currently valid" (the contrapositive of `duplicate-member-key`), so if a device holding the same key is unbound and its owner has unresolved, that very device is determined revoked and removed (unresolved −1), and the new device is added**. (c) Stop the duplicate check — a hostile declaration puts the same key on 2 rows (K5-4's counterexample). **Round 2 (counterexamples)**: counterexample 1 "same key with no leftover (unresolved 0)" — duplicate = ignored as before. Counterexample 2 "same key as a bound device" — a bound device is alive (a revocation would have matched and removed it), so duplicate = ignored. Counterexample 3 "same key as another person's unbound device" — the same inference holds (resolve it if that person has unresolved). **Rounds 3 & 4**: empty | **(b)**. Principle "read an accepted row as a declaration of fact (same as K5-1's arithmetic — tie only what the declaration's acceptance implies)" |
| K5-13 | A revoked bound device's FP gets re-bound to a different unbound device merely because "no device holds it now", and the same key's re-add (restoring the learned FP) puts the same FP on 2 rows of one person's device set. `planRevocation`'s arithmetic (row-count difference) assumes injectivity and on 1 revocation removes 2 rows, bottoming out at 0 devices. Ballots cast with the revoked device's FP also get counted (PR #189 pullfrog — 2 threads) | (a) Make only the arithmetic injection-independent (non-matching FPs count "FPs held by no device"; post-revocation count = rows − matched − unmatched) — the 2 same-FP rows remain, and the ballot error remains. (b) Remember revoked devices' FPs per member and have `signerNeedingBinding` never re-bind them (preserves injectivity) — the arithmetic works under its premise, but there's no insurance if a different path that breaks the premise appears later. (c) **Both (a) + (b)**: maintain the invariant while the arithmetic doesn't depend on the premise. When restoring a learned FP on the same key's re-add, drop it from the revoked set (revocation isn't monotonic — §6.2). **Round 2 (counterexamples)**: counterexample 1 "a row signed by a revoked device's FP (stale actor)" — not bound under (b), and the ballot is rejected because `ownerVoteByDevice` finds no device (falls to K5-2 counterexample 1's side). Counterexample 2 "re-add → re-revoke" — only the 1 row carrying the restored FP is removed. Counterexample 3 "a revocation listing the same FP twice (duplicate)" — ignored by `readableRevokeDevice` (existing). **Rounds 3 & 4**: empty | **(c)**. Principle "an FP points at a device (§1 principle 7) — a revoked device's FP is nobody's until the same key returns. Don't let the arithmetic depend on the invariant". **→ Structured in K5-16 (the `revokedFingerprints` bookkeeping is removed)** |
| K5-14 | S11's Token column says "token list unavailable" even while the token list is still loading (a false claim for an instant on first render — pullfrog) | (a) As-is. (b) **While loading, treat as `pending`, show only the id, and claim nothing**. (c) Wait for the whole table until the list arrives — the registry's drawing gets dragged down by the token list's failure (contradicts K5-8 counterexample 3). **Round 2 (counterexample)**: counterexample "the list fetch fails" — as before, "token list unavailable" + id. **Rounds 3 & 4**: empty | **(b)**. Principle "don't say what the server hasn't said (display discipline §4) — while loading, zero claims" |
| K5-15 | K5-13's injectivity holds only within a membership: `revokedFingerprints` is new per membership while `knownFingerprints` is global, so an FP revoked in membership 1 gets bound to a different key in membership 2, and then `add_device` of the original key restores the learned FP, making 2 rows of the same FP (pullfrog round 2) | (a) Make `revokedFingerprints` global (per user_id) — adds bookkeeping across memberships and needs separate consistency thinking against the same-key-returns-on-re-membership shape (K6-J's same-key return). (b) **Prevent it at the point duplication is born (`pushDevice`): if a different device already holds the restored FP, add with no FP** — an FP claimed by 2 key pairs justifies nothing (the letter of "bind only what the declaration's byte layout justifies"). Doesn't depend on the membership boundary; injectivity is structurally guaranteed. (c) Drop `knownFingerprints` per membership — breaks the ballot carry-over of an owner returning with the same key (existing test, K6-J). **Round 2 (counterexamples)**: counterexample 1 "revoke → re-add the same key (the normal series)" — at that point no row holds the FP (removed by the revocation), so it's restored (as in the existing test). Counterexample 2 "a row added with no FP signs later" — if unbound is exactly 1, that signature binds it (the normal path). Counterexample 3 "a revocation after (b)" — only 1 row is removed. **Rounds 3 & 4**: empty | **(b)**. Principle "guard the invariant structurally at the point of the operation that breaks it, not in bookkeeping". **→ K5-16 also removes `pushDevice`'s guard (it follows from the table's nature)** |

### Re-running the exploration (2026-09-21 — answering the owner's finding "did you run the option-search loop until no new options appeared?")

An honest inventory: K5-1 / K5-2 / K5-8 / K5-11 moved design via added candidates and counterexamples (they were run). K5-3–K5-7, K5-9, K5-10 stopped at 3 candidates + 1 counterexample round, and "Rounds 3 & 4: empty" was boilerplate. K5-12–K5-15 were responses to review findings that took the reviewer's option nearly as-is without constructing counterexamples to break the leading candidate myself (the K5-13 → K5-15 chain is the evidence: it lists "a hostile server's declaration" as a counterexample family but never constructed stale-actor or cross-membership ones). Here each ruling's **upward-compat search** and **counterexample construction** is actually re-run, and the result is recorded as "changed / unchanged (what was tried and what didn't surface)". An empty round is declared only after writing what was tried.

**K5-16 (new — structuring K5-13 / K5-15)**: topic = whether "within one person's device set, FP is injective" and "a revoked device's FP is nobody's until the same key returns" can be kept by structure rather than bookkeeping (a `revokedFingerprints` set + `pushDevice`'s guard + injection-independent arithmetic). Candidates: (a) The current 3-point bookkeeping. (b) **A learned key ↔ FP correspondence table `FingerprintTable` (injective, set-once — binds only when both sides are unlearned), held once for the whole fold**: "the key's FP" and "the FP's owner" both resolve by just reading the table, and revocation, re-adds, re-membership, and someone else's FP are all eliminated by the same single rule "already some key's". (c) Make the device set a `Map<FP, device>` — an FP-unbound device can't be a key. (d) Device set as `Map<key, device>` + table — makes key uniqueness structural too, but the cross-member duplicate check (`currentKeyHolder`) remains, so the gain is small. **Round 2 (counterexamples)**: counterexample 1 "signing with a revoked device's FP (stale actor)" — it's on the table, so not bound ✓ (the same conclusion without K5-13's bookkeeping). Counterexample 2 "revoked in membership 1 → a different key in membership 2 signs with the same FP → re-add the original key" — the table lives across memberships, so it doesn't bind to membership 2's key, and the re-add restores the FP. K5-15's test expectation changes to `[null, null, FP_X]` (the shape faithful to §1 principle 7 that the FP is the original key's) ✓. Counterexample 3 "signing with another person's device's FP" — the table's owner is someone else, so not bound (a hole the K5-13 / K5-15 bookkeeping **hadn't closed** — closed only by making it a table) ✓. Counterexample 4 "a `revoke_device` listing another person's device FPs or already-revoked FPs" — previously, unbound devices were shaved arithmetically as "non-matching FPs" (could inflate unresolved falsely). With the table, "a non-matching FP already belongs to someone's key" is detected and the row is ignored as unreadable ✓ (a newly closed hole). Counterexample 5 "2 rows of the same key" — repelled by `keyAvailable` (unchanged). Counterexample 6 "a normal revocation → re-adding the same key" — restored (same as K6-J's same-key return) ✓. **Round 3 (upward compat)**: "make the table per-user" — inferior since counterexample 3 can't be closed. "Use the table like a verified chain at bindActor time" — that's verification (ADR-0018). No new options. **Round 4**: empty. **Adopted: (b)**. Principle "let the invariant follow not from bookkeeping but from the data structure's nature (injective, set-once). If the same rule eliminates the 3 counterexamples — revocation, re-membership, someone else's — at once, that is the silver bullet". Implementation: removed `revokedFingerprints` and `pushDevice`'s guard; added the `foreign` check to `planRevocation`. Unit tests added (reuse of someone else's FP · ignoring revocations listing someone else's FP / an already-revoked FP).

**K5-3 (the Members table)**: additional candidates: (e) Hide device chips behind a count hover / popover — the information becomes invisible (hover doesn't reach SR). (f) Put a "Device keys" table (User / FP / cap / since seq / note) under Members, Members carrying only the count — rows don't grow vertically even with 16 devices × multiple people, close to `maruhi device list`'s shape. Counterexample "the usual 1–3 devices" — row chips read the person↔device correspondence at a glance. Counterexample "16 devices" — chips wrap and the row grows tall but stays readable. (f) is the upward compat for the 16-device case but worse in normal operation, and K4-20's CLI `member list` (1 row with `devices=n` + FP enumeration) has the same shape as (b), so **unchanged**. (f) is re-ruled if a project with many devices is measured (carryover).
**K5-4 (the range of rules the fold transcribes)**: additional candidates: (d) Consolidate the rules into a per-op declaration table (a list of `readable(payload, state)`) — a readability improvement; behavior unchanged. (e) Transcribe monotonicity (cap ≤ the signing device's cap) "only when the signing device can be identified" — the asymmetry where a row added by an unidentifiable device passes while an identifiable one fails breaks "as reported" consistency. **Unchanged**. Under K5-16, the `unknown-device` transcription widened to "a non-matching FP that is someone's key" (a structural by-product).
**K5-5 (trigger wording)**: additional candidate: (d) **Attach the trigger's `triggerChainSeq` (carried by the response) at the end** — the `revoke_device` row doesn't carry the device FP, but via the seq it leads to the Audit tab's mirror row (`chain.device_revoked` — FP-bearing). The same shape attaches to remove / change_role / revoke_server (consistent). Counterexample "an old server row with no trigger" — `triggerChainSeq` is a required field, so it's there ✓. Counterexample "the seq being confused with Audit's seq column (admin-only)" — word it as "chain seq" to distinguish from the audit seq ✓. **Adopted (implementation change: "device revoked: <userId> (chain seq N)")**. (d) is (b)'s upward compat — overlooked in the first exploration.
**K5-6 (Audit's device 2 events)**: additional candidate: (d) Surface the payload's `deviceKeyFingerprint(s)` in the summary line's fragment — it would be the first non-`var.read` promotion of a payload into the summary line, and the same demand would continue on the next event (bringing AUDIT's per-event table onto the Web). The FP is readable in the expansion's verbatim JSON. **Unchanged**. Tried and didn't surface: "mechanically fragmenting every payload's string values across all events" — would line up even lease events' claims_digest on the summary line, breaking DP3 ruling P's "a summary is subject · coordinates · time".
**K5-7 (S11's information design)**: additional candidates: (d) Attach chain-derived information to registry rows (which projects it's valid in) — the registry is user-axis, the chain is project-axis, and the Web has no cross index (the same constraint as S4). (e) A "this device" mark — the Web holds no key, so it can't be judged. (f) Sort rows by registered-at descending — prioritize the policy of not reordering the server's declared order (same as S9). **Unchanged**.
**K5-9 (old-server 404)**: additional candidate: (d) Split "route missing" and "resource missing" by the 404 body's `_tag` — a list surface doesn't normally return 404, and an old server's missing route is a bare 404 (no JSON), so there's no discriminating material. **Unchanged**.
**K5-10 (boundaries)**: additional candidate: (d) Show "as reported" verbatim beyond the registry's 32 rows — the API cap (32) is on the accepting side; a response never exceeds 32. **Unchanged** (an empty round can only be declared because it was verified that the candidate loses meaning at the API cap).
**K5-12 (re-registration after unresolved)**: the upward-compat search: confirming the soundness of the "use re-registration to resolve leftovers" inference — an accepted `add_device` implies by §6.2 `duplicate-member-key` that "the same key is not currently valid". If the owner has unresolved and the same-key row is unbound, that very row is the revoked one (if alive it wouldn't have been accepted). Counterexample of mis-resolution "the same-key row is alive and the revoked one is a different unbound device" — then the acceptance side refuses with `duplicate-member-key`, so it never appears in a declaration. **Unchanged** (recorded that the counterexample disappears in the acceptance's implication).
**K5-14 (claim while loading)**: additional candidate: (d) Empty the whole cell while loading — the id is the registry's declared value, true regardless of loading. Showing only the id (b) claims zero while giving maximum information. **Unchanged**.

**K5-17 (pullfrog round 3's ℹ️ — silently dropping unreadable device ops)**: topic = a device-op row the fold judges "unreadable" and ignores leaves no trace on the screen (while unresolved gets a sentence). K5-16's `foreign` check widened the set of unreadable rows. Candidates: (a) Write in docs "the Web folds only readable rows; an unreadable row doesn't change the set = the display can be a superset" (K6). (b) **Hold `ReportedChainView.unreadableDeviceEntries` (the count of device-op rows dropped as unreadable) and show 1 sentence under the Members table** — declaring the very fact "among the rows the server declared, some couldn't be transcribed by this screen" (display discipline §4: don't say what wasn't said — its converse = don't hide what couldn't be said). (c) Count per member — an untargetable revocation can't be attributed to anyone. (d) Enumerate the dropped rows' seqs — could balloon under a hostile declaration. A count + "verify with the CLI" suffices. (e) Drop the whole device display to "unknown" when an unreadable row exists — discards even the set accurately derivable from the readable rows (excessive fail-closed). **Round 2 (counterexamples)**: counterexample 1 "doesn't happen on an honest server (the acceptance surface refuses with `unknown-device` etc.)" — if it doesn't happen, the sentence doesn't render (not drawn when 0). Counterexample 2 "counting even ops the fold doesn't transcribe (unknown ops)" — those are out of scope by design, K5-4's line (not counted). Counterexample 3 "the wording when the count isn't 0 implying 'verified'" — the wording is "could not be read … verify with maruhi project verify", guiding to the CLI. **Round 3**: (b) + (a) (the docs sentence stays as a K6 carryover). No new options. **Adopted: (b) + carry (a) over**. Principle "the fold draws only the facts it could read, and shows the existence of unreadable facts as a count (doesn't silently absorb them)".

**K5-18 (pullfrog round 4 — K5-17's note wording is one-directional)**: topic = `unreadableDeviceEntries` folds a dropped `add_device` (can lose a device = display small) and a `revoke_device` (keeps a device = display large) into one count, yet the note and JSDoc only said "superset" (the large direction). The Members description's "the device count is exact either way" could become false after K5-17. Candidates: (a) Make the wording neutral ("may not match what those entries would have produced") + limit the description's "exact" to FP binding ("an unbound fingerprint does not change the count"). (b) Count add / revoke separately and say each direction — grows `ReportedChainView`'s shape and the reader's burden of interpreting 2 numbers (0 on an honest server anyway). (c) Don't show a count, just say "there were unreadable rows" — the count is information about the hostile declaration's scale; no reason to lose it. **Round 2 (counterexample)**: counterexample "with neutral wording the reader can't grasp 'what happened'" — the sentence goes as far as "unreadable rows exist and the set may be off; verify with the CLI"; beyond that isn't the Web's province (display discipline §4-2). **Adopted: (a)**. Principle "if 2 directions fold into 1 number, the wording claims neither direction (a one-directional assertion can be false)". Also pin the note's rendering in e2e (nit — a fixture containing 1 unreadable row).
