Marked advisory, `tokenId` matched against the token list, the loss path = the existing token-revocation `RevocationOutcome`), `AuditEventList.tsx` (payload drawing moved to CSP-safe `Text type="code"` + xstyle — K5-11), `shared.tsx` (`RevocationOutcome` promoted, `RevokeButton`'s label, `FailureSubject` gains the registry), `DashboardShell.tsx` / `routes.ts` / `endpoints.ts` / `types.ts` / `icons.tsx` / `App.tsx` (route `/dashboard/devices`, nav, catalog, types, icon).
- **Tests**: `test/unit/chain-view.test.ts` (adds / binding / bound revocations / unbound arithmetic revocations and unresolved / ignoring unreadable revocations / ignoring duplicate keys and non-member adds / re-add after revocation and FP carry-over / `add_member`'s first key / remove and re-membership / non-application of device ops inside proposals / 3 device-vocabulary ballots), `test/e2e.test.ts` (Members' device chips and counts, Rotation's `device revoked`, Audit's device 2 events and payload, S11's list and matching, the token-revocation path [DELETE + CSRF + re-fetch of both lists], empty / old-server 404 / 401, fixture real-Schema decode), `test/screenshots.ts` (s11-devices / s11-devices-empty). All 7 quality-gate stages + web e2e green (fallow's new findings resolved by splitting — duplication was `RevocationOutcome`'s promotion, complexity was `planRevocation` / `signerNeedingBinding` etc.).
- **Post-review structuring (K5-16)**: replaced K5-13 / K5-15's bookkeeping (the revoked-FP set, `pushDevice`'s guard) with `FingerprintTable` (an injective, set-once key ↔ FP correspondence table), eliminating stale actor, cross-membership, another's-FP reuse, and revocations listing already-revoked FPs under the same single rule. Attached `triggerChainSeq` to Rotation's trigger wording (K5-5 re-exploration).
- **Points put to the owner**: (1) The `add_device` wire carries no FP and the Web computes no hash (K6-J), so Members' device display is in the degraded shape ("fingerprint not reported" / unresolved) — the proper means is either "permit WebCrypto SHA-256 as display-identifier derivation (an interpretation of ADR-0018 revision 2 item 4)" or "the server annotates the entry with the derived FP (an api-schema revision)" (K5-1). (2) Astryx `CodeBlock` emits inline styles and can't coexist with strict CSP (K5-11) — an upstream issue candidate. The `overflowWrap: anywhere` xstyle override is now at its 2nd site (HexText / payload), so judge variant-ification or ui.package promotion (ADR-0013's discipline).
- **Carryovers (to K6 / the canonical texts)**: (0) The `GET /auth/devices` response envelope (`{ devices }`) has no named Schema in api-schema; the Web's `DeviceList` type is handwritten (pullfrog's finding — this PR doesn't touch api-schema). A small revision naming and exporting it as `DeviceListSchema` rides a K6-or-later api-schema PR. (0-b) K6's Members / Devices docs get the sentence "the Web folds only readable rows; an unreadable row doesn't change the set (the display can be a superset). If there were unreadable rows a count is shown — verify with `maruhi project verify`" (K5-17). (1) The docs' Devices page carries the sentence "the Web shows the registry and device counts only 'as reported by the server'; verification and revocation are the CLI's" (the same letter as K5-7's description). (2) A device revocation's rotation-needed row is addressed to a person (no FP carried — K5-5) — align it in docs with `maruhi rotation list`'s display. (3) Until the condition for the Web's device display to become accurate (owner ruling (1)) is decided, docs explain "fingerprint not reported" in 1 line (a non-signing device, a reserve key). (4) `ROADMAP`'s DK row already notes K5's completion. K6 (public docs) hasn't started.

## 11. K6 addendum (2026-09-21 — reflecting device-key separation into the public docs [apps/site])

K6 is the docs-only stage (§3's K6 row). Before starting, read in order: CLAUDE.md → §1 / §3 / §5 ("the Codespaces docs (K6)", "residual") → §9's carryovers (1)(6)(8)(10) and the 2 principles (K4-2 "only one who can open the ledger may change it", K4-5 "an advisory may be a signal but isn't a judgment input") → §10's owner points and carryovers (0)(0-b)(1)(2)(3) → es-design.md §13 (K7-A–K7-K) → all 10 pages of `apps/site/docs/` → README's docs list → ROADMAP's DK row → SELF_HOSTING's "DK K3" / "DK K4" supplements → help golden (`key` / `device` / `token` / `guardian` / `agent`) → `ProjectScreen.tsx` / `DevicesScreen.tsx`'s screen wording. What's written is only the user-visible behavior already shipped at K4 / K5; code, canonical texts, vectors, and api-schema are untouched. Vocabulary authority: command names, flags, defaults, and output wording are transcribed from the help golden and `device.ts` / `device-sync.ts` / `key-recover.ts` / `keygen.ts` / `recovery.ts` / `handoff.ts` / `failure.ts` / `rotation.ts`; numbers from `policy.ts` (`MAX_DEVICES_PER_MEMBER = 16`), `packages/api-schema/src/devices-api.ts` (registry 32 rows, requests 5/hour, request TTL 15 minutes), and `db.package/key-wraps.ts` (`KEY_BLOB_FETCH_LIMIT = 5`, `HANDOFF_REQUEST_LIMIT = 5`, `APPROVAL_LIMIT = 20`); Web section names and chips from `ProjectScreen.tsx` (the "Devices" column, "fingerprint not reported", "… device entries in the reported chain could not be read …") and `DevicesScreen.tsx` (the "Devices" screen, "Lost a device?", "as reported by the server"). At each ruling point, §3's procedure runs (3+ candidates → the upward-compat / silver-bullet / counterexample rounds until 2 consecutive rounds produce no new option → extract principles and check ✓ / ✗ against existing principles → adopt), leaving "what was tried and what surfaced" per round. **Rulings are recorded before implementation, and additional judgments mid-implementation are added on the spot as K6-N+ in the same shape.**

**Fact confirmation (single round — what was verified in code before writing docs)**: (1) `device add` with a key present is "request row present = resume waiting / registry row present = chain check / neither = refuse (`--replace` guidance)" (K4-21). After the request's TTL expiry neither row exists, so a re-run is refused — meanwhile the CLI's expiry wording says "re-run `maruhi device add` … it reuses this key and creates a new request", contradicting the implementation (→ carryover 1). (2) `device approve` performs the registry PUT + request cancel only when "≥1 registered / already" (K4-31), so an approval that stops midway leaves the request, and the approving side's re-run completes it. (3) `key recover` is a ceremony (TTY + non-agent), `device add` has no gate, `device approve` is ceremony-gated, `device revoke` is not a ceremony (`--yes` allowed, `--revoke-token` is explicit). (4) The post-restoration "is it a reserve key?" question defaults to no (K4-10). (5) `rotation list`'s device-revocation row is addressed to a person as "member (device revoked)" (no FP carried — K5-5). (6) `maruhi agent`'s help still says "master key" (outside K4-12's kept-as-is scope — untouched). (7) `session.ts`'s "no key" wording describes `--handoff` as "approval from another device or a guardian" (a leftover of the old path → carryover 2).

### K6-A. The devices page layout — 1 page or split (enumeration 1 round + a round-2 with content + 2 empty rounds · stopped)

| Option | Content | Advantages | Drawbacks |
|---|---|---|---|
| A-a **1 page `devices.mdx`** (add / list / revoke / reserve key / migration / Web / residual) | A device is 1 work unit of "one's own resources" (DK-L) | Someone who "lost a device" can follow revocation → token → rotate on 1 page. 1:1 with `maruhi device --help`'s 4 subcommands | Long (7 sections) |
| A-b 2 pages (`devices.mdx` = add / list / revoke, `reserve-key.mdx` = reserve key / migration) | The reserve key is a `key`-group topic | Each page is short | The reserve key's rotate and device revocation (erasing leftovers via `device revoke <fp>`) become cross-references. `recover-your-key.mdx` already covers the reserve key's opening, so it scatters across 3 pages |
| A-c Absorb the reserve key into `recover-your-key.mdx`; `devices.mdx` is devices only | "Restoration" and "reserve key" have the same reader | The reserve key's rotate / separation aren't a restoration context (normal-time operations) | |
| A-d Add the Web's device display to `environment-scopes.mdx`'s Members paragraph; drop it from `devices.mdx` | The existing Web section | Would mean explaining the Members table's device column and the Devices screen on separate pages | |

**Round 2**: (a) Upward compat — "A-a + put the reserve key's **3 opening shapes** (code / passkey / guardian) on `recover-your-key.mdx`, with `devices.mdx` **referencing** the reserve key as 'a device key that lives only in the ledger' (rotate and separation on the devices side)" → the reader split lands cleanly on "a person with devices (normal times)" and "a person who lost devices (incident)". Adopted as A-a's concretization. (b) Silver bullet — "no new page; scatter into the existing 4 pages" → the `device revoke` path (revocation → token → rotate) would have no single home. Rejected. (c) Counterexample — the reader "an existing user who lost a device (migration incomplete)": what happens if the device to revoke is a pre-DK copy key? → A copy key has the same FP as the first device key, so `device revoke` would revoke one's only device and stop at `last-device-protected`. This person's path has the "all devices lost" shape (`key recover` → new device → then revoke), and devices.mdx's revocation section needs a sentence "if you haven't deleted the copy" (a round with content). **Round 3**: "the Web section on a separate page (`dashboard.mdx`)" → existing docs have no Web-dedicated page, and K5's Members / Devices are device topics, so devices.mdx is the home. No new options (empty round). **Round 4**: empty. Stopped.

**Principle**: "1 page = 1 task (K7-D). Devices are a normal-time resource, restoration is an incident-time task, and the reserve key appears in both, so split by 'where it lives' — opening on the restoration side, registration / rotate / separation on the devices side" — DK-A ✓, K7-D ✓, ADR-0017 (English) ✓. **Adopted: A-a (round 2's (a) split + (c)'s sentence)**.

### K6-B. Removing the old "Handoff from another device" section (enumeration 1 round + a round-2 with content + 2 empty rounds · stopped)

| Option | Content | Advantages | Drawbacks |
|---|---|---|---|
| B-a **Delete the section outright, and replace it with a 1-sentence pointer to 'adding a device (devices.mdx)' as the answer to "how do I put the key on a new device"** | The old path disappeared from the spec at K1 and from the CLI at K4 | Don't document a nonexistent feature (§1-bis). The docs side of premise (3) "while no users exist, delete the old implementations" | Someone who remembers the old docs can't read why it "disappeared" |
| B-b Keep a "Previously …" note | An explanation for migrants | Don't write announcements or eulogies for features that never shipped / were removed (the flip side of K7-F). No readers exist while there are no users | |
| B-c Keep the section name but make the content the guardian path (preserve the anchor) | No broken links | The only references to the `#handoff-from-another-device` anchor are the same page's table and linux-keychain (both rewritten at K6). The name would lie | |

**Round 2**: (a) Upward compat — "B-a + also remove linux-keychain's old anchor reference in the same PR + mechanically check broken links with `bun run validate --strict`" → B-a's concretization (pinned by structure). (b) Silver bullet — "erase the word `--handoff` from docs" → `key recover --handoff` is the guardian path's real flag (help golden). Keep the word, re-map the meaning to "a guardian's approval". Rejected. (c) Counterexample — the reader "a guardian on an old CLI": a requester on a new CLI runs `key recover --handoff` and a guardian on an old CLI types `key approve` → a K4 server rejects the old shape with 400 (K4-15). The docs write the guardian-side command only as `maruhi guardian approve <code>` and leave it to SELF_HOSTING's order 2 (update every CLI). Mixed-version guidance isn't written in docs (the self-host admin's province). No new options. **Round 3**: empty ("an anchor redirect" adds a Blume construct — unnecessary with no referrers). **Round 4**: empty. Stopped.

**Principle**: "a removed path is removed from docs too; only the path to its replacement remains. Broken links are pinned by a check (validate --strict)" — premise (3) ✓, K7-A "docs don't invent names" ✓. **Adopted: B-a + validate**.

### K6-C. Recommended Codespaces / dev-container operation (enumeration 1 round + a round-2 with content + 2 empty rounds · stopped)

| Option | Content | Advantages | Drawbacks |
|---|---|---|---|
| C-a **Primary: carry the device key on a persistent volume (1 codespace = 1 device: `device add` → `device approve` on the laptop); secondary: short-lived operation inside `maruhi agent` (`--key-ttl`); the "throw away every time" pattern is explicitly labeled "repeats revocation + rotate" and not recommended** | The letter of §5 "the Codespaces docs (K6)" | The device key's meaning (1 device = 1 key; discarding = a revocation duty) maps directly onto operations | The volume-operation procedure depends on Codespaces-side features (the existing page is the same) |
| C-b Recommend `device add` / `approve` every time | Carries no secret | Per rebuild: add (the approval ceremony) + revoking the old device + rotate. Hits the 16-device cap (if discarded devices aren't revoked they accumulate) | |
| C-c Add every time as a narrowed-cap device (`--cap member --env dev`) | The revocation rotate duty is dev-only | Fewer rotate duties, but the revocation chore remains. However, "don't give the CI box prod" is a correct use → add 1 cap sentence as a C-a supplement | |
| C-d Write to wait for the forwarding agent (KL4) | §5's letter "or wait for KL4" | Don't document unshipped features (§1-bis). Rejected | |

**Round 2**: (a) Upward compat — "C-a + 2 sentences: 'for a short-lived container, make it a narrow device with `--cap`, and when discarding run `device revoke <label>`'" → takes C-c's advantage into C-a (a round with content). (b) Silver bullet — "hold no key in the container and drive with `MARUHI_TOKEN` alone" → value operations need a device key (the existing page's "Value operations do not"). CI is an OIDC lease (github-actions.mdx). Rejected. (c) Counterexample — the reader "a codespace in an agent environment (Copilot agent etc.)": `device add` runs ungated, but `device approve` is typed by a person on a registered device. Post-approval first-sync registration (K4-37) is inside the ceremony gate — if the codespace is non-interactive, only the candidates Note appears. Docs write "approval always happens on a person's device" and nothing more. Counterexample 2 "the rate limit": 5 requests/hour (`MAX_DEVICE_ADD_REQUESTS_PER_HOUR`) — the discard-every-time pattern stalls at 5 rebuilds per hour. Add to C-a's "not recommended" reason. **Round 3**: empty ("carry `own-devices.json` in dotfiles too" → a non-secret record; even without it, observation fills it [K4-3 counterexample 6]. Not written). **Round 4**: empty. Stopped.

**Principle**: "a device is the unit of a key, so a device you discard is a device you revoke. Primary = a non-discarding operation (volume); if discarding, the 2 sentences of narrow cap + revocation" — DK-A ✓, DK-B (revocation = rotate duty) ✓, §5's carryover ✓. **Adopted: C-a + (a)'s 2 sentences + the rate-limit reason**.

No new options (empty round). **Round 3**: empty. Stopped. **Principle**: "vocabulary is transcribed (K7-A) — if input shapes differ, write them differently" ✓. **Adopted: E-a**.

### K6-F. How the threat model's residual is presented (enumeration 1 round + a round-2 with content + 2 empty rounds · stopped)

| Option | Content | Advantages | Drawbacks |
|---|---|---|---|
| F-a **A "What revoking a device does not do" section at `devices.mdx`'s end: (1) devices the lost device added before its revocation don't disappear automatically — see `device list`'s "added by <fp>" and bulk-revoke, (2) a registry label can be forged but revocation is fixed by FP (the confirmation table shows FPs), (3) if the device used for restoration was compromised, the reserve key is too — `key reserve rotate`, (4) revocation doesn't revoke the token (AUTH §6)** | §5 "residual" (1)–(3) + K4 (4) verbatim | Doesn't weaken or grow guarantees | Enumerating "can't"s feeds anxiety |
| F-b Scatter across sections | Readable in context | Someone reading after a revocation can't see it in 1 place | |
| F-c Don't write it | — | §5 designates "into a threat-model document". No public document exists besides docs | |

**Round 2**: (a) Upward compat — "F-a + attach 'how you notice' to each item (`device list`'s added by / the confirmation table's FP / `key show`'s reserve key)" → turns "can't" into "visible" (a round with content). (b) Silver bullet — none (the residual is a design consequence; docs can't erase it). (c) Counterexample — the temptation "write it with grown guarantees": writing (1) as "cap monotonicity protects" is a lie (§2-bis: an (owner, all) device can create a device of the same cap). Keep monotonicity to "a stolen device can't make a device stronger than itself". Counterexample 2 "K5 (0-b)'s 'the Web can be a superset'" → goes in K6-D (the Web section). **Round 3**: empty. **Round 4**: empty. Stopped.

**Principle**: "the residual doesn't depart from the canonical texts' non-guarantees (§14.3) and design record §5's letter, and each item carries its detection means" — CRYPTO §1 principles ✓ (don't grow guarantees), K4-4 "don't hide changes to the device set" ✓. **Adopted: F-a + (a)**.

- `index.mdx`'s "How maruhi works" gains 1 line "**Keys belong to devices, access belongs to people.**" (ES K7-C's C-a). No Card added (this isn't adding an entry point).

### K6-H. The scope of rewording `master key` (enumeration 1 round + a round-2 with content + 2 empty rounds · stopped)

17 places across 4 pages (getting-started 1 · invite 1 · linux-keychain 9 · recover 6 — of which recover's 6 and linux-keychain's 2 are rewritten section-and-all).

| Option | Content | Judgment |
|---|---|---|
| H-a **Judge per sentence: a sentence where "master key" refers to 'the key living on this device' becomes "device key"; one referring to 'the key the ledger guards' becomes "reserve key"; a sentence premised on "carrying the 1 key to every device" (the _same_ key arrives / bring the same key to a second machine) is rewritten because the fact changed. Other sentences untouched** | K7-A "fix only sentences that differ from fact" | **Adopted** |
| H-b Mechanical global replace | Uniform | Creates lying sentences (ledger talk) via "master key" → "device key" |
| H-c Don't touch | Minimal | `maruhi key show` prints "device key fingerprint". Docs alone on the old word = K7-A's vocabulary mismatch |

**Round 2**: (a) Upward compat — "H-a + fix `linux-keychain.mdx`'s opening 'persists exactly two secrets' to 'the token and this device's key', and write `maruhi agent`'s description as "this device's key"" → an application of H-a. (b) Counterexample — "`maruhi agent --help` and `agent status` still print "master key" (help golden). If docs say "device key" it mismatches the screen" → vocabulary authority is the help golden, but the `key` / `device` groups' new help and `agent`'s old help already mismatch, and docs align to the newer one (the canonical §3's wording). The `agent` help is a revision proposal to the owner (carryover 4 — wording only · golden update). Docs carry the note "(the agent's own messages still call it the master key)" in **exactly 1 place**, so a reader doesn't stumble on `agent status`'s output (a round with content — a mismatch with the screen isn't silenced by docs). **Round 3**: empty. **Round 4**: empty. Stopped.

**Principle**: "vocabulary follows the canonical texts and the new help; a gap with old help isn't hidden but said once, and the revision goes to the owner (K4-12)" — ADR-0017 ✓, K7-A ✓. **Adopted: H-a + the note in 1 place + carryover**.

### K6-J. The path after a request's TTL expiry (enumeration 1 round + a round-2 with content + 2 empty rounds · stopped)

Fact check (1): re-running `device add` after expiry is refused (no request row, no registry row → K4-21's (c)). K4 carryover (1) says "guide the approving side's re-run", but the approving side can't match without a request row (K4-6: 0 matches = "no request / expired").

| Option | Content | Judgment |
|---|---|---|
| J-a **Docs write what the implementation does: if approval didn't finish within 15 minutes, on the new device run `maruhi device add --replace` (the key isn't registered anywhere yet so nothing is lost) → approve the new FP. If the approval **stopped midway** (the request remains), the approving side re-runs `device approve`, and the new device looks at the missing projects after the "N projects" report and asks for a `device approve` re-run** | The shipped behavior | **Adopted** |
| J-b Transcribe the CLI's expiry wording ("re-run reuses this key") | The transcribe-wording discipline | Would pin a wording that contradicts the implementation into docs (a lie) |
| J-c Write K4 carryover (1)'s "the approving side's re-run" | The design record's letter | With no request, the approving side can do nothing |

**Round 2**: (a) Counterexample — "help says `--replace` is 'only for copies' (`device add --replace` = "only for installs from before device keys")" → for the use of discarding a never-registered key there's no harm, but help's letter and docs' guidance would diverge. Docs attach the reason "the key was never registered anywhere, so replacing it loses nothing". (b) Upward compat — "on the CLI side, 'key present + no request + no registry + on no chain' could re-create a request with the same key" → a code change (outside this PR's scope). Put to the owner as carryover 1 (whether to fix the expiry wording or permit re-requesting with the same key). **Round 3**: empty. **Round 4**: empty. Stopped.

**Principle**: "docs write the shipped behavior; when the CLI's wording diverges from the behavior, write the behavior and carry the wording mismatch over (the K7-H shape)" ✓. **Adopted: J-a + carryover 1**.

### Mid-implementation additional rulings (K6-N–K6-P — enumerated, counterexampled, principled on the spot)

| # | Topic | Candidates and counterexamples | Adopted / principle |

| K6-N | `device approve` has no yes confirmation (a divergence between K4-6's ruling "match by FP + yes (the TTY gate)" and the `device.ts` implementation — which is ceremony gate → match → display → register immediately) | (a) Write the design record's letter (ask yes) — differs from the shipped behavior (a lie). (b) **Write what the implementation does — "displays the label, FP, and 12 words, then registers" — without writing whether there's a yes**. (c) Explicitly say "no yes is asked" — unsettles the reader, and docs go stale if the CLI adds a yes later. **Round 2 (counterexample)**: "approving a wrong FP with no yes" — typing the full-length FP / 12 words as the argument is itself the match (K4-6 f-1); a non-match isn't registered. A yes is only a re-confirmation after matching. **Rounds 3 & 4**: the option tried "write 'you can stop with Ctrl+C' in docs" → registration happens right after the request-list match; there's no window to stop. No new options | (b). Principle "docs write the shipped behavior; a divergence from the ruling record is carried over (same shape as K6-J)" → carryover 5 |
| K6-O | `guardian add` doesn't accept `--passkey` (help golden), yet the first draft wrote "every command that changes the ledger accepts `--passkey`" | (a) Keep the first draft — a lie. (b) **Enumerate the commands that do accept it (`key recovery` / `key seal passkey` / `key reserve rotate`), and write that `guardian add` opens with the code**. (c) Hedge with "most do" — the reader tries and fails. **Round 2 (counterexample)**: "a person with only a passkey wants to designate guardians" — re-issue the code via `key recovery --passkey`, then `guardian add`. Derivable from the docs' Passkey section | (b). Principle "flags are transcribed from the help golden (K7-A) — don't generalize". Whether `guardian add --passkey` exists is put to the owner (carryover 6) |
| K6-P | MDX reads bare angle brackets like `<fingerprint>` / `<user>` as JSX (build failure) | (a) Escape to `&lt;` — mixes raw HTML entities into prose. (b) **Put them in code spans** (CLI output fragments were already transcribed in code spans — K7-E). (c) Write "fingerprint" with no brackets — departs from the CLI's letter. **Round 2 (counterexample)**: "in a table (`<fp-or-words>`)" — inside a table it's already a code span. **Rounds 3 & 4**: empty (verified by grep that existing pages follow the same discipline) | (b). Principle "the CLI's letter is transcribed in a code span" |
