### Additional rulings during review handling (K6-Q–K6-S — responses to PR #190 pullfrog round 1)

pullfrog's 2 findings (both ℹ️ · non-blocking but **factual errors**) were verified, and the ruling procedure was re-run. Neither closed at "fixing the wording"; each was taken to **a structure that keeps the same error from recurring** (the K5-16 shape).

| Ruling | Topic | Candidates and counterexamples | Adopted / principle |
|---|---|---|---|
| K6-Q | **Which device** the `DeviceLimit` (16) surfaces on. The first draft wrote "the 17th `add` is refused", but the cap is checked in the DO's `appendProgram` (`add_device`'s acceptance), and the CLI has no pre-check (`MAX_DEVICES_PER_MEMBER` isn't referenced from the CLI — verified). Since the **approving side** performs the append, the 422 lands not on the new device that ran `device add` but on the device that ran `device approve`. A reader would watch the wrong device | (a) Take pullfrog's suggestion as-is ("`device approve` is refused; the request itself is still created") — doesn't cover append paths other than approval (first-sync reserve-key registration · `key recover`'s `finishRecovery` · `key reserve rotate`). (b) Enumerate every append path — accurate, but a 4-path list is far from the reader's interest (what happens at 16). (c) **Write it as a rule: "the cap is checked at chain append time, so it surfaces on the appending command (usually the approving side's `maruhi device approve`). Since it's per project, only the project at the cap fails; the rest register. Revoked devices don't count, so revoking 1 frees a slot"** — stays true as paths grow, and can even state the next move (revoke). (d) Write only the number, not where it surfaces — a retreat from the first draft. **Round 2 (counterexamples)**: counterexample 1 "a reader hits the 16th when first-sync registers a reserve key" — (a) can't explain it ((c) covers it via "the appending command"; `device-sync.ts` turns it into a Note and continues, so the body doesn't fail). Counterexample 2 "approving 4 projects with no `--project`, only 1 at the cap" — `approveOnProject` reports `failed` per project, so (a)'s "approve is refused" overstates → (c)'s per-project clause is needed. Counterexample 3 "do revoked devices occupy a slot" — `policy.ts`'s JSDoc "revoked ones don't count = freed by `revoke_device` / `remove_member`". Written as the next move. **Round 3 (upward compat)**: "re-state it as a symptom in What can go wrong" — the same string twice on 1 page (the duplication harm outweighs K7-E's reverse-lookup gain; the extra section is numeric planning info, not a symptom). Tried, not taken. **Round 4**: empty | **(c)**. Principle "write a constraint by 'which operation takes the check', not 'which device it happens on' — choose a phrasing that stays true as paths grow" — K7-A (vocabulary is transcribed) ✓, K6-J (write the shipped behavior) ✓ |
| K6-R | The "changing the ledger needs the reserve key opened" sentence (K6-K) was **duplicated letter-for-letter** across `devices.mdx` and `recover-your-key.mdx`, and the exception found at K6-O (`guardian add` doesn't accept `--passkey`) was added only on the recover side. As a result only the devices side comprehensively lies with "the code or a passkey" | (a) Add the exception on the devices side too (fix both copies while duplicated) — next time a flag is added or removed, one side goes stale again (reproducing this failure). (b) **One page holds the normative claim: the opening **means** (which commands accept `--passkey`) is stated only by `recover-your-key.mdx`, whose subject is the ledger; `devices.mdx` states only K4-2's **principle** ("only one who can open the ledger can change it") and hands off the means via a link**. (c) Make the devices side authoritative and have the recover side reference — the ledger's 3 opening shapes are recover's subject (split at K6-A). Wrong owner. (d) Keep it on both and add a check that "the 2 sentences match" — a prose-equality check is brittle, and the 2 pages differ in length and context. **Round 2 (counterexamples)**: counterexample 1 "a reader who lands directly on `devices.mdx#the-reserve-key` from the dashboard" — that reader needs the principle (opening is required), not the flag table. (b) suffices. **This is also the diagnosis of this failure**: mixing "a stable principle" and "a volatile detail (the flags)" into 1 sentence is why the detail's fix left the principle's duplicate behind. Counterexample 2 "`key recovery --replace` doesn't open" — an exception to the principle. devices:115 / recover:33 already state it, and (b)'s principle sentence carries it in a parenthetical "opening is required (except `--replace` for a lost code)". Counterexample 3 "if the recover side disappears someday, the principle floats" — `validate --strict` detects a broken link. **Round 3 (upward compat)**: "make the shared sentence a Blume snippet / MDX import — 1 source" — structurally strongest, but the 2 pages need different lengths (devices = a section, recover = an intro sentence), so sharing one string makes one side verbose. The cost of adding a page construct for 1 sentence (the same reasoning as K7-B's option C). Tried, not taken. **Round 4**: empty | **(b)**. Principle "**a normative claim is held by 1 page; other pages state only the principle and reference it. Don't mix a stable principle and volatile details (flags, numbers) into the same sentence**" — the docs version of K5-16 "let the invariant follow from the data structure's nature, not bookkeeping" ✓, K7-A ✓, K6-O ✓ (a structural means to apply a discovered exception to every page) |
| K6-S | A means to apply "exceptions found mid-implementation" like K6-O's to every page without missing any (preventing this recurrence) | (a) Re-read by eye — that's what missed it this time. (b) **Inventory normative-sentence duplication via grep (3 kinds: "opening the ledger", "the rate-limit numbers", "the device-add procedure"), and consolidate a duplicated claim to 1 page per K6-R's principle**. Result: "opening the ledger" = 1 duplicate (resolved at K6-R); "the numbers" = spread across `devices.mdx` (16 / 5 / 32 / 15 min), `recover-your-key.mdx` (5 / 5 / 20), `linux-keychain.mdx` (5 / 5), but **each page merely states the numbers its own task needs — not a duplicated claim** (linux-keychain names both device-add 5/hour and blob-fetch 5/hour, contradicting neither devices nor recover), so not consolidated. "The device-add procedure" = `linux-keychain` / `getting-started` / `recover` each carry 1–2 sentences + a link to `devices.mdx`; the procedure's truth lives in devices (not a duplicate). (c) Check wording equality in CI — the same brittleness as (d). **Round 2 (counterexample)**: counterexample "a normative sentence the grep net misses (a different phrasing)" — the net is a check for "is the same rule stated in 2 places", not a completeness guarantee. Precisely why (b)'s conclusion is "reduce duplication", not "check duplication". **Rounds 3 & 4**: empty | **(b) performed and the result recorded**. Principle "recurrence is prevented by reducing duplication, not by adding a check" |

**An honest inventory of the re-run exploration (2026-09-21 — post PR #190 review)**: K6-A–K6-L ran 3+ candidates + counterexample rounds, but **K6-N–K6-P (mid-implementation additional rulings) closed at "fix what was found" without sweeping whether the same error existed on other pages**. Both of pullfrog's findings come out of that hole (K6-O's unapplied exception = K6-R; K6-Q is a spot written without verifying in code "which device it surfaces on"). At K6-Q / K6-R, counterexamples breaking the leading candidate were actually constructed (append paths other than approval · per-project partial failure · the reader arriving directly from the dashboard · future flag additions/removals), upward-compat options (write as a rule / 1 page holds it) and a silver-bullet candidate (MDX snippet) were tried, and it stopped after 2 empty rounds.

### K6-T. Turning the rulings back into structure (2026-09-21 — answering the owner's question "did you run the loop searching for upward-compat / structure-solving options in each ruling?")

**An honest inventory**: K6-A–K6-L ran 3+ candidates + upward compat + silver bullet + counterexamples through 2 empty rounds, but of the "solve-by-structure options" produced, only K6-B (pinning broken links via `validate --strict`) and K6-L (not matching the recipes shape) qualified — **the rest closed at "write the wording this way"**. Request §3-6 says "what can be solved by structure is solved by structure, not by wording", and pullfrog's 2 findings (K6-Q / K6-R) are exactly cases where wording-closed rulings drifted. Here each ruling A–P is asked one by one "can this ruling's correctness be pinned by a check", and the ones that could were implemented (`apps/site/test/unit/cli-vocabulary.test.ts` — rides the root `bun run test`'s `site-unit`, so inside quality gate 7).

| Ruling | Pinnable by structure? | Performed |
|---|---|---|
| **Overall (K7-A / K6-O)** "docs vocabulary is transcribed from help" | **Yes**: match every `maruhi …` invocation appearing in docs' ```sh blocks and inline code against a command-path → flag-set index built from the help golden. A path resolves as "the longest prefix present in help"; the remainder is arguments (`maruhi member remove bob`). But what follows a group (a path with SUBCOMMANDS and no ARGUMENTS) is limited to a child command | **Implemented**. Confirmed by mutation: `maruhi guardian add --passkey` → no such flag, `maruhi device frobnicate` → no such subcommand, `maruhi device approve --caps` → no such flag. **K6-B is pinned as a by-product** (writing the removed `maruhi key approve` fails as not a `key`-group child) |
| K6-A page layout | **Yes (partial)**: a check that "the Devices page covers `device` / `token` / `key reserve` child commands". Becomes the signal to re-rule whether to write when a group gains a child | **Implemented** |
| K6-D Web wording | **Yes**: check that the dashboard phrases docs quote ("fingerprint not reported" / "as reported by the server" / "Lost a device?") actually exist in `ProjectScreen.tsx` / `DevicesScreen.tsx` (matching both sides, so a docs rewording or a Web rename both fail) | **Implemented** |
| K6-H `master key` wording | **Yes**: `master key` in docs is only `linux-keychain.mdx`'s 1 place (the note that `maruhi agent`'s help still uses the word); every other page is 0 | **Implemented** (fix this count too when owner ruling (2) revises the help) |
| K6-L the recipes collision | **Yes**: no ```sh block starting `maruhi run --env production -- ` outside `deploy-targets.mdx` (the first draft eyeball-grepped it) | **Implemented** |
| K6-R normative duplication | **Already solved by structure** (reducing duplication, not a check — the opening means is held by 1 page). A prose claim can't be caught mechanically, so guarding by "1 page holds it" rather than a check is right | Implemented (the prose) |
| K6-C the Codespaces recommendation / K6-E FP notation / K6-F residual / K6-G order·index / K6-I SELF_HOSTING / K6-J TTL expiry / K6-K the opening sentence / K6-N–K6-P | **Can't be pinned**: each is an editorial judgment ("which operation to recommend", "at what granularity to write") whose truth isn't machine-checkable. K6-E's "approve doesn't accept a prefix" is an **implementation fact** that could be checked, but that's a CLI-side test (already pinned by `device.test.ts`); duplicating it docs-side prevents no drift | Not performed (verified they can't be pinned after trying) |

**Round 2 (counterexample construction)**: counterexample 1 "the check throws a false positive and docs can't be written" — mistook the prose phrase "runs maruhi at an interactive terminal" (a quotation of CLI wording) for an invocation. → Narrowed it: only a `maruhi` at the **start** of a line or a shell conjunction counts as an invocation (3 pages actually threw false positives, then fixed). Counterexample 2 "the check passes something through" — `maruhi device frobnicate` passed, indistinguishable from "group + argument". → Added the group check (SUBCOMMANDS present, no ARGUMENTS). Counterexample 3 "the help golden is stale (`maruhi agent`'s master key)" — the check only says "docs don't claim words absent from help"; the help's staleness is a separate problem (owner point (2)). Counterexample 4 "other (existing) pages fail" — ran on all 10 pages; green after the false-positive fix. The existing pages' vocabulary got pinned at the same time. **Round 3 (upward compat)**: "check whole-help-golden coverage (every child command appears in some docs)" — commands like `anchor` / `checkpoint` / `rotation dismiss` intentionally have no docs; coverage isn't the current policy (a page is made per task — K7-D). Restricting to DK's 3 groups is right. Tried, not taken. "A single source for normative sentences via MDX snippet" — already rejected at K6-R round 3 (the 2 pages need different lengths). **Round 4**: empty. Stopped.

**Principle**: "a ruling doesn't end at wording — **what is machine-checkable goes into a check; what isn't goes into the ownership form '1 page holds it'**. Only the editorial judgments that fall into neither remain as prose promises" — §3-6 ✓, K5-16 "the invariant follows from structure" ✓, K7-A ✓, DP5's golden ✓.

### K6-U. When removing a duplicate, the kept side (its owner) wasn't re-read (PR #190 pullfrog round 2)

At K6-R it was ruled "the opening means is held by `recover-your-key.mdx`", and `devices.mdx`'s duplicate was removed. Yet **the intro sentence on the side designated as owner (`recover-your-key.mdx:20`) stayed comprehensive**, enumerating `designating guardians` while saying "the code or a passkey" (even though :47 on the same page states the correct exception 27 lines later). The same error for the 3rd time (K6-O = discovery, K6-R = fixing the duplicate side, K6-U = the original).

| Option | Content | Judgment |
|---|---|---|
| u-1 Drop the means from the intro sentence per the finding's suggestion, handing off to `#passkey` | Applies the principle/detail separation (K6-R) **to the original too**. Even within the owner page, "the principle sentence" and "the means section" are separate | **Adopted** |
| u-2 Add the exception to the intro sentence ("the code or a passkey — though `guardian add` takes only the code") | Lengthens the sentence, and every flag change fixes 2 places (the intro and :47) = reproducing the shape K6-R wanted to avoid | Rejected |
| u-3 Trim the intro sentence | The principle (K4-2's designation — K6-K) disappears | Rejected |

**Round 2 (counterexamples)**: counterexample 1 "the `#passkey` section has no guardian talk" — :47 states every command: "`key recovery` / `key seal passkey` / `key reserve rotate` accept `--passkey`, and `guardian add` opens with the code" (verified). Counterexample 2 "a reader who only reads the intro types `guardian add --passkey` without knowing the means" — since the intro doesn't claim the means, they go to the section before typing. The CLI also fails on the unknown flag. Counterexample 3 "the same error surfaces a 4th time in another sentence" → **Round 3 (structure)**: this error's shape is "attributing the opening means to an enumeration of ledger-changing commands", and that is machine-checkable. A check was added asserting that a sentence containing `designating guardians` does not contain `with the code` / `or a passkey` (`cli-vocabulary.test.ts`). Confirmed it fails under mutation (reverting to the pre-fix sentence). Prose claims in general can't be checked, but **a concrete error that surfaced 3 times can be pinned by a check**. Round 4: empty.

**Principle**: "**when removing a duplicate, re-read the kept side** (removing a duplicate isn't a one-side edit). If the same error surfaces 3 times, give up on the general rule and **pin that error's shape** in a check" — K6-R ✓, K6-T ✓, K5-16 ✓. **Adopted: u-1 + the check pin**.

**pullfrog's nit (`maruhi agent` is the only section holding both SUBCOMMANDS and ARGUMENTS, so words after `agent` are the sole hole in the child-command check; short flags are also out of scope)**: true. Since `agent` takes `-- <command…>`, the "group + arguments" distinction is in principle impossible, and filling it would create false positives (`maruhi agent -- bash` etc.). **Zero false positives** is the property a docs gate must keep, so it's kept as a known hole (the same conclusion as the finder).

**K6-U supplement** (PR #190 pullfrog round 3 — the check going vacuous): K6-U's pin was written as `it.each(pages)`, so on a page with no enumeration sentences, `expect([]).toEqual([])` passes silently, and **just rewording ("designating guardians" → "naming guardians") makes the check quietly disappear**. The coverage check one level up (K6-A) closed the same hole with `expect(subcommands.length).toBeGreaterThan(0)`, but it wasn't applied here. Candidates: (a) collect across pages and also pin the count being non-0 (same shape as K6-A). (b) Loosen the regex to absorb wording drift — what to absorb can't be decided, and loosening grows false positives. (c) keep as-is (the pin is best-effort) — too weak for an error that surfaced 3 times. **Round 2 (counterexample)**: counterexample "the check gets in the way when this sentence needs a legitimate rewrite later" — that is the point: rewriting the pin itself should be an intentional act, not something lost to an unrelated edit (a comment says so). **Adopted: (a)**. Confirmed it fails under 2 mutations (a wording change · restoring the means). **Principle**: "place a check only after confirming both 'it can break' and 'it doesn't go vacuous' (once one non-vacuous shape is placed, apply it to all)".

### K6-V. The UX check (2026-09-21 — the owner's question "is there no ruling that makes the user experience worse?")

K6-A–K6-U ran on the axis "is it factually correct", and **no round ran on the reader's-experience axis** (ES K7's procedure has a "UX check" stage; at K6, enumerating readers in counterexamples substituted for it). Here the pages are re-read for 3 reader kinds (someone who lost a device = incident · someone adding a device = normal · an existing mid-migration user); what docs can fix is fixed, and product-side experience gaps are carried over.

| # | The experience problem | Fixable in docs? | Response |
|---|---|---|---|
| V-1 | **Someone who lost a device is made to read the concept (keys belong to devices…) → cap → the reserve key before reaching revocation** — a structure that makes an incident-time reader read normal-time explanation | Fixable | Right after the opening table, a `:::note` gives the direct path to revocation + 1 sentence "nothing else is urgent". Candidates: (a) move the revocation section to the page's top — the normal-time reader (adding) is primary, and the chaptering diverges from the `device` group's order (add / approve / list / revoke). (b) **just a path in a note** — keeps the chaptering; only the incident reader skips ahead. (c) a separate "Lost a device" page — violates 1 page = 1 task (K7-D) and scatters the revocation explanation across 2 places. **Round 2 (counterexamples)**: counterexample 1 "a long note won't be read anyway" → held to 2 sentences, 1 link. Counterexample 2 "someone arriving from the dashboard's Lost a device? already knows about revocation" → still not wasted since it shows them the order of what comes next (token revocation · rotate). **Adopted: (b)** |
| V-2 | **When approval fails on every project, the requester is told nothing and waits 15 minutes** (no registry row written = no signal arrives — K4-31 (c)). Worse, the expiry wording says "re-create the request with the same key" while the implementation refuses (K6-J / carryover 1). Only the approving side got the cause, and the requester likely never saw it | **Partly** (the product-side hole remains) | Docs state "if approval fails, the cause appears on the approving side. The request remains, so the new device may keep waiting — no restart needed". **Product side is carried over**: there's no means to tell the requester of the failure (using a registry row as a "failure" signal would touch K4-5's "an advisory goes as far as a signal", so a surface design is needed) |
| V-3 | **Every Codespaces rebuild needs approve (a human ceremony) + revoke + rotate**. It also hits the 5-requests/hour cap. Docs can only recommend "use a volume" | Not fixable | Carried over (re-evaluation material for the KL4 forwarding agent — design record §5 already says to rewrite the main use case as "ephemeral environments") |
| V-4 | **`key recover` asks a human "was this key made as a reserve key?"** (K4-10 j-1). Users can't always judge their key's provenance (whether it's pre-DK isn't obvious) | Partly | Docs wrote "a pre-DK ledger holds a copy of the device key, so the answer is no". Product side: it could be mechanically determined by checking whether the opened B's FP **has signed as an everyday device on the chain** (carryover) |
| V-5 | **`device approve` has no yes confirmation** (K4-6's ruling text says "FP match + yes" but the implementation only matches — K6-N). A paste error on the FP adds a different key to every project on the spot (undo = revoke + rotate) | Not fixable | Carried over (which is authoritative — the ruling text or the implementation. As UX, "show the label and cap of the device you're about to add, then yes" would be the safer side) |
| V-6 | **`device approve` takes a full-length 32-hex / 12 words, `device revoke` an 8-hex prefix** — different input shapes | Not fixable (the reason was written) | Docs carried the reason: "approval isn't truncated — approval is the very place that stops a server's substitution". The UX asymmetry is a spec necessity |
| V-7 | **Devices is last in the sidebar (order 10)** — every user holds a device key from the start, yet it sits behind an optional feature (four-eyes) | Fixable but **the request specifies 10** | Kept at 10 per request §1. Links were laid from getting-started / linux-keychain / recover / index. **Point put to the owner**: ordering by reader frequency would put Devices around recover (6) |

**Principle**: "correctness alone isn't enough for docs. **That an incident-time reader reaches the next move from the first screen** is guaranteed by a path separate from the chaptering. An experience gap docs can't mitigate is mitigated as far as possible, then carried over as a product-side hole" — ES K7's "UX check" ✓, B2 "a warning nobody sees isn't detection" ✓. **Round 3 (upward compat)**: "fix V-2 in the CLI, not docs (emit 'the request remains but registration hasn't progressed' on the requester's polling)" — needs a surface design, a code change (outside this PR's scope). Promoted to a carryover. "Build V-1 with Blume's `Steps`" — the existing 10 pages are written with bare headings and bullet lists; adding a construct to just 1 page breaks the style (same reasoning as K7-B option C). Rejected. **Round 4**: empty.

### K6-W. Searching each UX point for upward compat and "delete-the-whole-structure options" (2026-09-21 — owner's instruction)

K6-V only **enumerated the experience gaps and carried them over** — it ran no upward-compat / silver-bullet round per point ("not fixable" wasn't an exploration conclusion, just a restatement of the docs-scope constraint). Here each point is run. What docs can fix now is fixed; what needs code is **elaborated to a concrete shape** for an owner proposal (not left at "there's room for improvement").

**Fact check**: `requestCreate`'s authorization is only `ensureKeyMaterialAccess(principal)` (`handlers-devices.ts`). **Anyone holding a token with key-material access can place a device-add request on that person's account**. The only thing stopping a request's promotion to signer is the approver's act of matching the FP.

| # | Topic | Candidates → upward compat → silver bullet → counterexamples | Conclusion |
|---|---|---|---|
| W-1 | **V-2: the approval's failure never reaches the requester** (a registry row is the signal; on failure none is written) | (a) Add `status` to the registry's request row to convey the failure — a wire addition. A forged `failed` injected can only make you "give up early" = availability only, the same class as today's "silent silence". But it needs an api-schema revision. (b) **Upward compat: fix wording in 2 places without adding a surface** — ① midway through waiting (at 1/3 of the TTL) emit 1 line "still not registered on any project. Check the approving device's output (the request survives a failure)", ② align the expiry wording with the implementation (it currently says "re-create the request with the same key" while K4-21 refuses — **the same 1-sentence fix closes both** carryover 1 and this). No wire, no surface. (c) Silver bullet: since the approver and the requester are the same person, **the approving side deletes the request row on success or failure** — conveying failure by "gone" can't be told apart from success (rejected at K4-5 e-4). **Counterexample 1** "the approver is someone else (future delegation)" — today it's only oneself (DK-D). Counterexample 2 "the midway line is noisy" — once per 15-minute TTL. Counterexample 3 "(b) doesn't carry the cause itself" — it doesn't, but it points at **where the cause is**, so a person gets there in 5 minutes (today: wait 15 and receive wrong guidance). Round 3: (a) and (b) aren't exclusive — (b) now, (a) when a surface is designed. No new options. Round 4: empty | **Owner proposal (b)** (wording in **3** places · code change is outside this PR's scope): ① the midway-waiting line, ② the expiry wording (says "re-create with the same key" while the implementation refuses), ③ **the "not registered yet" Note at `device.ts:206-208`** (says "the approving side may still be working / re-run `device approve`", but the registry PUT happens **after** the project loop, so by the time the signal is seen the work is done — and on partial success the request is already cancelled, so a re-run fails at "no matching request" — pullfrog round 6). Docs were made consistent at 97f8c0e / this addendum, but **the CLI still says something different**, so ③ should move together with docs |
| W-2 | **V-5: `device approve` has no confirmation** | (a) Add a yes (per K4-6's ruling letter) — a yes right after typing 32 hex is ceremony inflation, and stops working once it's a habit. (b) **Silver bullet: re-frame the problem**. The danger isn't "a typo" (a wrong FP matches no request and fails). The danger is **someone who stole a token with key-material access placing a request and sending its FP to a human for approval**. A yes doesn't stop that (someone trusting the sent FP presses yes too). What stops it is the discipline "**read the FP off the screen of the machine being added**". → **1 sentence in docs** (implemented: same commit as this addendum). (c) On approval, show "when and under which label this request was made" — the label is already shown. The label is attacker-chosen (advisory), so it doesn't aid judgment. **Counterexample 1** "want to add a remote colleague's device whose screen you can't see" — that's delegation (KL4), out of scope today. Counterexample 2 "people who don't read docs" — the upward compat is emitting the same 1 line in the CLI's approval output → owner proposal (wording only). Round 3: "put the ceremony gate on request creation" — `device add` must work in non-interactive environments (containers = DK-D). Rejected. Round 4: empty | **The discipline is written in docs (done in this PR)** + **owner proposal**: the same 1 line in `device approve`'s output. Also **propose retracting** the "+ yes" in ruling K4-6 (a yes doesn't stop the threat) |
| W-3 | **V-4: `key recover` asks a human the key's provenance** ("was this made as a reserve key?") | (a) Keep asking (today). You're posing a possibly-unanswerable question to someone who just lost every device. (b) A heuristic (does the key sign everyday traffic) — would misjudge a pre-DK user who never wrote anything as a reserve key (falls to the unsafe side). (c) **Silver bullet: it follows mechanically from the definition**. Under DK, **a reserve key is always registered via `add_device`** (K4-30 — signed by a local device). A pre-DK B is **that person's first key** (the genesis or `add_member` payload) and never lands via `add_device`. Since the chain is synced during restoration anyway, **looking at how the opened B's FP landed on the chain eliminates the question** (pre-DK = the first key / DK = a key added by add_device). **Counterexample 1** "B is on no chain" — today's path already enumerates "re-invitation needed". There it stays unrecorded (fail-closed), same. Counterexample 2 "someone rebuilt via `key generate --new-identity`" — the new identity's first key is a device key; the reserve key is add_device. Consistent. Counterexample 3 "different landing shapes across projects" — the same key being the first key on one and add_device on another happens when a pre-DK key was added as a device to another project. That key is in fact an everyday device key, so it falls to "not a reserve key" (if it's the first key on any, it's pre-DK). Round 3 (upward compat): "machine judgment + show the result for human confirmation" — don't push the answer onto the human; show the fact and take a yes. **Inside the ceremony 1 question remains, but yes/no becomes a confirmation, not a judgment**. This is best. Round 4: empty | **Owner proposal**: replace the question with "judgment by how it landed on the chain + a confirmation" (a revision of K4-10 j-1). The UX core is "**don't make the user judge; show the fact and have them confirm**" |
| W-4 | **V-3: Codespaces churn** (approve + revoke + rotate per rebuild, 5 requests/hour) | (a) Recommend the volume operation (today's docs). (b) A narrow cap for a short-lived device (implemented — shrinks the rotate's range but the ceremony remains). (c) An expiring device (auto-revocation) — a new op or a payload change (same treatment as §5's `set_device_cap`). **And since revocation produces a rotate duty, auto-revocation produces an auto-rotate** = no solution. (d) **Silver bullet: don't give a throwaway machine a device key**. The existing answer for a throwaway execution environment that reads values is the **workload lease** (the CI OIDC path — `maruhi ci run`), not a device key. The rotate duty comes from "the device held DEKs", which a lease never does. → The structural answer is "can Codespaces ride the CI-side path (generalizing the issuer — ROADMAP's SDK row / AUTH §14)". **Counterexample 1** "Codespaces' OIDC may not emit the same claims as GitHub Actions" — unverified, so not written in docs (don't document nonexistent features). Kept as an owner proposal. Counterexample 2 "in interactive development a human wants to read values" — the lease is designed to be usable as `maruhi run`-equivalent injection (§9.1). Round 3: "recommend agent + `--key-ttl`" — every key disappearance needs re-registration + revocation (same as V-3). Not upward compat. Round 4: empty | **Owner proposal**: throwaway execution environments ride leases, not device keys (connect to ROADMAP's SDK / KL4 rows as the motivation for issuer generalization). Docs keep recommending the volume |
| W-5 | **V-6: approval is a full-length FP, revocation an 8-hex prefix** | (a) Align (approval takes a prefix too) — touches §3's no-truncation ban. (b) Align (revocation takes full-length too) — making users type full-length on a pick-from-`device list` operation is a regression. (c) **The asymmetry is right**: approval is **a hostile match** (it rejects a key the server substituted); revocation is **a pick from a verified chain's rows** (a mis-pick is stopped by the confirmation table's full-length FPs). Different roles → different input shapes. **Counterexample** "a reader who doesn't know the reason sees inconsistency" — docs state the reason on the approval side ("approval is the very place that stops a server's substitution"). The revocation side is covered by the confirmation-table explanation. Round 2+: no new options | **Unchanged** (the principle is recorded). The UX asymmetry is a spec necessity; writing the reason is the right handling |
| W-6 | **V-7: the sidebar position** (Devices is last) | (a) 10 per the request's designation (this PR). (b) Renumber — touches the existing 4 pages' frontmatter (the shape rejected at K7-B). (c) **Structure: make a group in `meta.ts`**. K7-B rejected it as "don't add structure for 3 pages", but a new fact: **it was 7 pages then, 11 now, and the key-and-device 3 pages (linux-keychain / recover-your-key / devices) have become a clear cluster**. The rejection's premise changed, so it's worth re-presenting. **Counterexample** "grouping changes every page's path" — exactly; not a change for K6 (a docs stage). Round 3: "a 5th Card on index" — an odd Card breaks the 2-column layout (K7-C's rejection reason). Round 4: empty | **Owner proposal**: reconsider `meta.ts` grouping at the next docs stage (the rejection's premise changed). This PR keeps 10 |

**Principle**: "'not fixable in docs' is not an exploration conclusion. Only after options outside the constraint are produced (1 wording spot · mechanical judgment from the definition · moving to a different mechanism) may it stop. And **a design that makes the user judge must always be asked whether it can be replaced by a design that shows the fact and asks for confirmation**" — K4-10 (default to the safe side) ✓, K4-5 (an advisory goes as far as a signal) ✓, DK-D ✓.

### K6-X. Fixing the procedure itself by structure (why the owner's question repeated 4 times)

The owner has asked "did you run the loop" 4 times. Each time I answered with a mix of "rulings I ran" and "rulings I stopped". The cause: **the procedure is prose** — §3's (a) upward compat, (b) silver bullet, (c) counterexamples are invisible when unwritten. Candidates: (a) be careful — failed 4 times. (b) Make each ruling **a table with columns** for "upward compat / silver bullet / counterexamples", making blank cells visible. (c) Write the round count per ruling (existing — "2 empty rounds" can just be written, a repeat of K5's boilerplate problem). **Adopted: (b)**. Later addenda (K7 onward, other series) carry these columns. A blank cell is the mark of "not searched", legible at a glance to a reviewer or the owner. **Principle**: "a discipline you can't keep is made visible by the format (the procedural version of K5-16 'the invariant follows from structure')".

**K6-V supplement** (PR #190 pullfrog round 4): the "when approval fails" paragraph added at K6-V had been written **unconditionally as if total failure were the only case**. In reality, if even 1 registers, the latter stage (record · registry PUT · request cancel) runs (K4-31 (c)), so on partial success the request is consumed, the requester's wait ends at "N projects + not registered yet", and a `device approve` re-run fails at "no matching request". The shortfall is picked up by the approving side's record ("approved") at the next sync (`device-sync.ts`'s `registrationCandidates` — inside the ceremony gate, so on a person's device). The paragraph was scoped to total failure, and the partial-success path was added. **The 2nd instance of the same error shape** (the 1st = which device `DeviceLimit` surfaces on — K6-Q): in both, **the branching condition was written from the design record's description without reading the code's branch**. The design record sometimes writes branches folded, so **when writing a behavioral branch into docs, read the branch's condition itself in code** (the branch version of K7-A "vocabulary is transcribed"). It doesn't reduce to a mechanical check (a prose claim), so it is recorded as a principle.

**Same, 3rd instance** (pullfrog round 5): the "anyone holding your token can place a request" attached to the approval discipline was also too broad. `ensureKeyMaterialAccess` **rejects session principals and passes only `*` × admin-scope tokens** (`authz.ts`), so a project-scoped or non-admin token can't place a request. I **read only the call site (`requestCreate` calling `ensureKeyMaterialAccess`), not the predicate's body**. Generalizing the principle: "**when writing a branch, an authorization, or a cap into docs, read the predicate's definition, not the call site**". The user-visible difference isn't small ("a token holder" vs "an account-wide admin-token holder" call for different precautions — checkable in `maruhi token list`'s scope column).

**K6-V supplement 2** (PR #190 pullfrog round 7): "the approving side picks up the missing projects at the next sync" **holds only for 'you are not a member of that project' causes, not cap causes**. The self-healing path `registerRecorded` (`device-sync.ts`) re-applies **the same check** `capWithinSignerCap` that produced the skip, and says "Sync from a device with a wider cap to register it" while doing nothing. Since a cap can't change (K6-A's "a cap is fixed at approval and can't change later"), that device can never register there. The correct recovery is "**a different device with sufficient cap** **observes** the key on a project where registration succeeded (`observeDevices`) and registers it on the missing projects". Both paragraphs were fixed, and **the recovery-procedure description is now held by :59, with :63 only referencing it** (the claim had been duplicated across 2 paragraphs — the cause of 2 rounds of divergence — the in-page version of K6-R's principle). **Added principle**: "**when writing a recovery procedure, read that recovery path's own preconditions**. If the recovery path re-applies the check that produced the failure, it isn't a recovery". This is the **3rd of the same family** after K6-V supplement (the branch) and same-3rd (the predicate) — all caused by "writing from the call's name or the design record's summary".

### K6-Y. A pre-merge sweep (2026-09-21 — the owner's question "is there anywhere else solvable by upward compat or structure")

pullfrog's 9 findings all concentrated on `devices.mdx`. **Pages I hadn't re-read with the same eye remained** (the new claims in linux-keychain / invite-a-teammate / getting-started / index / recover-your-key), so they were swept with the same viewpoint (read the definition, not the summary).

| Swept claim | Result |
|---|---|
| `recover-your-key.mdx` "the segments are sealed to the guardians' device keys, so it can be approved from any device" | **Wrong**. The `guardian add` CLI itself says "shares are sealed to the guardians' **current** devices; a guardian who adds a device later or revokes one cannot open the share on it". **Sealing targets the device set at creation time**; a device added later holds no segment. Corrected |
| Same page "`guardian list` marks a guardian whose key changed as STALE" | **Inaccurate** (pre-DK phrasing). The implementation judges per device row, and it's STALE **only when 0 sealing devices remain alive** (the guardian left, or every sealed device was revoked). If only some were revoked, the rest can still open it, and the CLI recommends recreating the group. Corrected |
| `linux-keychain.mdx`'s `--key-ttl` consequence · revocation + rotate per rebuild · the narrow cap's effect (with `--cap member --env dev` the revocation's rotate is dev-only) | Correct (effective scope = person ∩ device cap. Consistent with `policy.ts` / the sweep's duty derivation) |
| `invite-a-teammate.mdx` "the wrap goes to the accepting device. Other devices receive it when added via `device approve`" | Correct (`member add`'s backfill goes to the accepting device; thereafter `approveOnProject`'s backfill) |
| `getting-started.mdx` / `index.mdx`'s 1 line | Correct |
| **The numbers (16 devices / 5 per hour / 32 rows / 15 min / 5 fetches / 5 handoffs / 20 approvals)** | Correct but **had only been transcribed by eye**. Vocabulary, coverage, and quotations were already mechanized — only numbers depended on a human eye → **nailed into the check** (`cli-vocabulary.test.ts`'s K6-Y): the defining constant's **right-hand side letter** and the docs' phrase are pinned as a pair. Confirmed failing under both mutation directions (change the constant · change the docs' number) |

**Principle**: "when findings concentrate on 1 file, suspect **the files not re-read with the same eye** (a shape a reviewer found exists in places the reviewer didn't look too). The last kind still depending on a human eye (numbers) is mechanized the moment it's noticed" — K6-T ✓, K6-S "recurrence is prevented by reducing duplication and by mechanical checks" ✓.

**K6-Y supplement** (PR #190 pullfrog round 8): the number nails were held as **(constant, 1 page)** pairs, so (1) `HANDOFF_REQUEST_TTL_MS` (the guardian request's 15 minutes — `recover-your-key.mdx`) was missed, and (2) the same numbers `linux-keychain.mdx` transcribes in its own phrasing (device-add 5/hour · blob-fetch 5/hour) **were matched by no nail**. The nails were widened to **(constant, page × phrase series)**. Confirmed failing under both mutation directions (change the other page's transcription · change the constant). **Principle**: "a nail is driven **everywhere a transcription exists**. A nail driven in only 1 place shrinks its coverage every time a duplicate grows" — the counterpart of K6-R's "reduce duplication" (the duplication you can't reduce, you pin — all of it).

**K6-Y supplement 2** (PR #190 pullfrog round 9): widening the nails to per-page (supplement 1) wasn't enough — **a 2nd transcription within the same page** slipped through. `recover-your-key.mdx` states the ledger fetch cap in 2 places — the introduction (:18) and the summary section (:94) — and only the former was nailed. Worse, the phrase used for the guardian-request nail, "five per user per hour", **happened to match the opening of a line in the summary section**, leaving that line in a state of "looking nailed while guaranteeing nothing". Distinguishable phrases (section heading words, parentheses) are used for each, and each section's failing independently was confirmed by mutation. K6-R (deleting the summary section to leave 1 place) was an option, but the summary section is the **sole owner** of the approval cap (20/hour) and the place a reader goes to look up caps together, so it was kept. **Principle**: "a nail's phrase must match **only the 1 place it means to pin** (a coincidental match isn't a check). The unit of duplication isn't the page but the **sentence**" — continuing K6-U's "a check is confirmed both breakable and non-vacuous" (also confirm "it isn't hitting somewhere else").

**K6-Y supplement 3** (PR #190 pullfrog round 10): though supplement 2 wrote "the unit of duplication is the sentence", **the check side was still a per-page existence check (`includes`)**. `linux-keychain.mdx` states the device-add cap and the fetch cap twice each, and `devices.mdx` states 16 in 2 forms — "16 active devices" and the CLI quotation "for that member (16)" (filled by `failure.ts`'s `${e.limit}`). An existence check is satisfied by the first one, so **editing the 2nd still passed green** (pullfrog confirmed by mutation). Candidates: (a) add as many distinguishable phrases as there are transcriptions — plugs today's hole, but every new duplicate needs the same re-sweep. (b) **Pin the phrase's occurrence count** — editing any transcription changes the count and fails, and **future duplications surface automatically as a count increase** (can't grow silently). (c) Delete every duplicate docs-side — `linux-keychain`'s 2 places are in different contexts (the agent section and the Codespaces section), and the quoted error is the CLI's own letter, so it can't be deleted. **Adopted: (b)** (pullfrog's "structural alternative"). Confirmed under 4 mutations: editing the 2nd transcription in 3 places (linux-keychain's 2 spots · the 16 inside the quotation) and **a transcription-adding** edit. **Principle**: "a count-checking test also reports a duplication growing. An existence check protects only 'the transcription that exists now'". With this, K6-Y's nails are the 4-tuple (constant, page, phrase, **count**).

---

## 12. K7 addendum (2026-09-21 — collecting K6's carryovers: bringing the CLI's wording / vocabulary / types up to the docs)

At K6 the docs wrote the shipped behavior, and the places where the CLI still says something different were carried over (§11 K6 completion record's "points put to the owner" (2)(3), "carryovers" (2)(3)(5), K6-W's W-1 / W-2). K7 is their collection; **no behavior is re-designed** (the K6-J / K6-N shape: docs = the shipped behavior, the CLI's wording = follows it). The ruling table uses K6-X's format (columns for upward compat / silver bullet / counterexamples — a blank is the mark of "not searched").

### K7 fact confirmations (read the code's branches, predicates, and recovery paths — K6-X's 3 principles)

- `device add`'s wait (`device.ts` `waitForRegistryRow`) GETs the registry every 3 seconds up to the TTL (`DEVICE_ADD_REQUEST_TTL_MS` = 15 minutes) until a row with its own FP appears. The signal is **the approving side's final PUT** (`deviceApproveOp` — order: the project loop → local record → registry PUT → request cancel. The latter stage is gated on "≥1 registered / already" — K4-31). Hence (1) by the time the requester sees the signal, the approver's loop is done, (2) on total failure there is no signal or PUT and the request remains, (3) on partial success the request is already cancelled.
- Re-running after expiry (`deviceKeyForRequest`): key present + no request row (404) + no registry row → refuse (K4-21 (c)). Only `--replace` proceeds on the same device.
- Self-healing of missing projects (`device-sync.ts` `registerRecorded`) re-applies the same `capWithinSignerCap` that produced the skip (K6-V supplement 2). A cap cause never heals on that device; a non-member cause heals after the invitation.
- Only `ensureKeyMaterialAccess` (`authz.ts`) can place a request = rejects session principals, passes only `*` × admin tokens.
- `device approve` is ceremony gate → match → display → immediate `add_device` (no yes — K6-N).
- `GET /auth/devices`'s response is the inline `Schema.Struct({ devices: Schema.Array(DeviceSummarySchema) })`. `TokenListSchema` is the named precedent. The Web handwrites `DeviceList` in `types.ts` (used in 2 places of `DevicesScreen.tsx`).
- The user-visible "master key" is 23 sentences across 9 files under `apps/cli/src/` (`agent.ts` 2 · `effect-cli.ts` 2 · `invite.ts` 4 · `keychain.ts` 6 · `login.ts` 2 · `recovery.ts` 2 · `session.ts` 3 · `audit.ts` 1 ["wrapped master private key"]). `sync-receipt.ts` and each file's JSDoc / comments aren't user-visible, so out of scope. The same paragraphs hold leftovers of the old path (`invite.ts` line 803 "run `maruhi key recovery` on the old device to issue a recovery code, then `maruhi key recover` on this machine" = the pre-DK path that substitutes restoration for device add).

### K7-1. The request-expiry wording — align the wording to the implementation, or the implementation to the wording (§1-1 (a))

| Option | Content | Upward compat | Silver bullet | Counterexamples | Adoption |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1-a **Align the wording to the implementation**: "if the approving side registered nothing, run `maruhi device add --replace` on this device (the key isn't registered anywhere so nothing is lost). Approve the new FP" | The same claim as docs (`devices.mdx` "If the request expires"). Neither branches nor types grow | Put "check the approving side's output" first (on a partial success where the registry PUT failed at 429, the key is on the chain yet no signal arrives — counterexample 2 below) | — | Counterexample 1 "a request expiring on a pre-DK copy" — a copy key is refused at K4-18 before the request is created, so a key whose request existed is either generated or `--replace`d = unregistered. Counterexample 2 "approval succeeded but the registry PUT failed at 429 (32 rows)" — no signal arrives and the expiry guidance is reached even though the key is on the chain. `--replace` would discard that key and leave an orphaned device on the chain. Since the approving side's Note ("registry is full … will not see the completion signal") appears, the wording is conditioned on "if the approving side registered nothing" (taken into the upward compat). Counterexample 3 "non-interactive (a container) left 15 minutes" — the guidance is the same | **Adopted** (with the upward compat) |
| 1-b Align the implementation to the wording: with key present + no request + no registry + **on no chain**, re-create the request with the same key | No key regeneration needed | — | Putting the "is the key unregistered" predicate before the request (syncing every project) = an added branch (§2 "a wording change adds no branch or type") and a behavior change (§0 "no re-design"). The docs guidance (`--replace`) would also move | Counterexample "a project that can't be synced (network)" — "on no chain" can't be verified → fail-closed refusal → the `--replace` guidance is needed anyway | Put to the owner (re-listed from K6 owner point (3)) |
| 1-c Just remove "reuses this key" from the wording, writing no next move | The lie disappears | — | — | Counterexample "someone who waited 15 minutes doesn't know what to type next" (the same experience as K6-V's V-2) | Rejected |
| 1-d Silver bullet: keep extending the TTL while waiting so "expiry" disappears | — | — | Request creation is 5/hour (`MAX_DEVICE_ADD_REQUESTS_PER_HOUR`), and extending = re-creating, so it runs out at 15 min × 5 = 1h15m. The server's TTL is an acceptance policy (untouched) | — | Rejected |

**Round 2**: upward compat = take counterexample 2's condition into 1-a (adopted). Silver bullet = 1-d (rejected). Counterexamples = the 3 above. **Round 3**: option tried "transcribe the approving side's Note letter into the expiry wording ('registry is full')" → the approving side's Note isn't on the requester's screen; transcribing it can't be matched. No new options. **Round 4**: option tried "make expiry exit code 0 (the request is gone but the key remains)" → makes a failed wait look successful (same shape as K4-31 counterexample 3). No new options. Stopped.

**Principle**: "the wording transcribes the shipped behavior (K6-J). An option that changes behavior is a separate ruling; don't write it in the wording first" — K4-5 ✓, ADR-0017 ✓. **Adopted: 1-a**. What makes it superior: it kills the lie with the same 1 sentence as docs without adding a branch, and covers counterexample 2 (PUT failure) via the condition.

### K7-2. The "not registered yet" Note (§1-1 (b))

| Option | Content | Upward compat | Silver bullet | Counterexamples | Adoption |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2-a Keep as-is ("the approving side may still be working / re-run `device approve`") | — | — | — | Contradicts fact checks (1)(3) (the work is done; the request is already cancelled) | Rejected |
| 2-b **Rewrite to the facts**: "the approving side skipped it there (the reason is in that device's output: the cap doesn't cover it · not a member · `--project`-limited). The request is consumed. A device whose cap covers it registers it the next time it runs a keyed command (the approving side itself if its cap wasn't the cause; otherwise a different device that observed the key on a registered project)" | The same claim as docs' paragraph (`devices.mdx` :59). No branch | Attach "see each project's registration state with `maruhi device list`" | — | Counterexample 1 "the approving side's failure is cap-caused" — an approver-side re-sync won't fix it (`registerRecorded` re-applies the same check), so say "a device whose cap covers it", not "the approving side". Counterexample 2 "a non-member cause" — after the invitation, the approving side's next sync registers it (if the cap covers). The wording doesn't enumerate causes; "a device whose cap covers it" covers both. Counterexample 3 "approved only 1 with `--project`" — the approving side's "approved" record remains, so the next sync registers it on the other projects too (within cap). "Consumed" is correct | **Adopted** |
| 2-c Compute the cause on the requester side and show it (read the `added by` device's cap on registered projects, judge whether that device exists on the missing projects and whether its cap covers) | Doesn't send a human to the approving side's screen | — | Re-frames the problem (the requester holds the truth [the chain], so it can derive the cause itself) | Adds a branch and a sync (§2). Can't judge if the approver limited with `--project` (intent is unreadable). Kept as an **owner proposal** (the same class of surface design as K6-W's W-1 (a)) | Put to the owner |
| 2-d Drop the Note and show only the count | Simple | — | — | A shortfall with no next move (K6-V V-2) | Rejected |

**Round 2**: upward compat = 2-b + `device list` guidance (adopted). Silver bullet = 2-c (owner proposal). Counterexamples = the 3 above. **Round 3**: option tried "write the concrete command 'run `maruhi pull` on the approving side' into the Note" → becomes a lie when the approving side is the cause (cap). Generalize to "a keyed command" — same word as docs. No new options. **Round 4**: option tried "also show the missing projects' names on the approving side (grow the approver's Note)" → the approver already reports skipped per project (`reportApproveOutcome`). Duplicate. No new options. Stopped.

**Principle**: "when writing a recovery procedure, read the recovery path's own preconditions (K6-X) — a cap-caused skip doesn't heal on that device" ✓, K4-5 ✓. **Adopted: 2-b**. What makes it superior: it states the same claim as docs :59 in the same words (K6-R's duplication discipline — since CLI and docs both hold the same 1 sentence, an "unremovable duplicate", the words are aligned), and it creates no lie by enumerating causes.

### K7-3. The midway line of the wait (§1-1 (c)) — when to emit, the condition, the count

| Option | Content | Upward compat | Silver bullet | Counterexamples | Adoption |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 3-a **Once, at the first poll after 1/3 of the TTL (5 min) has elapsed since the request's creation**. Elapsed is measured from `expiresAtMs − TTL` (a resumed wait also judges by the request's age). Not emitted during an `already-registered` wait (no request row) | K6-W W-1 (b) ①. 1 line per 15-minute TTL | Attach the "valid until" time (`formatUtcMinutes(expiresAtMs)`), saying until when the approving side's re-run after a total failure still works | — | Counterexample 1 "a resumed wait (K4-21) where the request is already 6 minutes old" — emitted on round 1. Correct, since the request's age is the grounds. Counterexample 2 "in `already-registered` (registry row, no request) the registry GET keeps failing transiently" — with no request, "check the approving side's output" is unfollowable guidance → emit only when `pending` (the condition). Counterexample 3 "a signal arrived on partial success" — the 1 line emitted before the signal wasn't wasted (at that point it was unapproved). Counterexample 4 "non-interactive / agent environments" — `device add` isn't a ceremony, and the 1 line is a stderr Note. No harm | **Adopted** (with the upward compat) |
| 3-b Repeat every 5 minutes (2 times) | Harder to miss | — | — | Emitting the same sentence twice = the 2nd carries no information (K6-W W-1 counterexample 2 "noisy") | Rejected |
| 3-c Emit at the wait's start | Earliest | — | — | "Check the output" when the approving side hasn't done anything yet is unfollowable | Rejected |
| 3-d Silver bullet: carry the approver's failure on the request row's `status` (W-1 (a)) | The cause itself arrives | — | Re-frames the problem | An api-schema wire addition = outside this PR's scope (already an owner proposal at K6-W) | Stays an owner proposal |

**Round 2**: upward compat = 3-a + the expiry time (adopted). Silver bullet = 3-d. Counterexamples = the 4 above. **Round 3**: option tried "make elapsed 1/2 (7.5 min)" → weakens W-1's "arrives in 5 minutes" (the difference from today, where you wait 15 and get wrong guidance). Keep 1/3. "Export the threshold as a constant and nail docs' 'five minutes' with a K6-Y nail" → adopted (reduce to a check — K6-T). **Round 4**: option tried "fold the re-run-after-Ctrl+C guidance into the 1 line" → the start-time guidance ("re-running … resumes waiting") already holds it. Duplicate. No new options. Stopped.

**Principle**: "an advisory may be a signal but isn't a judgment input (K4-5) — the 1 line states only the fact 'no signal yet' and leaves the cause to the approving side's screen" ✓, K6-V "an incident-time reader reaches the next move" ✓. **Adopted: 3-a**. What makes it superior: once suffices (3-b), it appears when followable (3-c), and it adds no surface (3-d).

### K7-4. Leftovers of the old path (§1-2)

Grepping `--handoff` / `key approve`: the only user-visible leftover is `session.ts` line 590's "approval from another device or a guardian" (the "old `key approve`" in `guardian.ts` is a comment). The same grep found **a path that substitutes restoration for the old-device path** (`invite.ts` lines 795 / 803 "run `maruhi key recovery` on the old device … then `maruhi key recover` on this machine" · `login.ts` line 419 "no master key on this device. Restore it with your recovery code" · `agent.ts` line 651 "Recover the key again with `maruhi key recover`"). These are also "master key" sentences (K7-5), and when a device remains the correct path is **adding a device** (`maruhi device add` + `device approve`); `key recover` is "when no device remains" (docs `recover-your-key.mdx` opening).

| Option | Content | Upward compat | Silver bullet | Counterexamples | Adoption |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 4-a **Name only the guardian**: "`maruhi key recover --handoff` (approvals from your guardians)". Within the same sentence, put "if a device remains, `device add`" first (the path order = same as docs) | Consistent with the facts and docs | Align the 4 "no key" guidance sentences (session / login / invite / agent) to the same order (device add → key recover → key generate) | — | Counterexample 1 "a first-timer (no ledger)" — keep `key generate` at the end. Counterexample 2 "an agent environment" — `device add` isn't a ceremony, so guiding to it is fine (approval happens on a person's device) | **Adopted** |
| 4-b Drop the `--handoff` explanation from the sentence | Short | — | — | The 3-shape lineup (code / passkey / guardian) diverges from docs' table | Rejected |
| 4-c Silver bullet: make the "no key" sentence a single constant referenced from 4 files | The duplication disappears (K6-R) | — | — | The 4 sentences sit in different contexts (login is a Note · invite interrupts an invite acceptance · agent is a TTL consequence). Extracting only the common part makes awkward English. **Aligning the sentences' claim (the path order) via a check** fits K6-T better → pin session's sentence wholesale in `passkey.test.ts`; the other 3 pin the order in their tests | Partially adopted (via checks) |

**Rounds 2 & 3**: option tried "rename `--handoff` to `--guardians`" → a flag rename (not the wire, but a full revision of help / docs / checks = out of scope · an owner's call). Option tried "align `key recover`'s help (line 3773) too" → already says "via your guardians" (fixed at K4). No new options. Stopped.

**Principle**: "a user-visible path is stated in one order (if a device remains, add; if none remains, restore; if first-ever, generate)" — ADR-0017 ✓, K6-J ✓. **Adopted: 4-a**.

### K7-5. Drawing the "master key" line (§1-3) — per sentence

| Option | Content | Upward compat | Silver bullet | Counterexamples | Adoption |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 5-a **The same rule as K6-H**: a sentence referring to 'the key living on this device' becomes "device key" (or "this device's key"), one referring to 'the key the ledger guards' becomes "reserve key", and a sentence premised on the old path (carrying 1 key to every device) is rewritten. JSDoc / comments / identifiers / persisted names untouched (K4-12) | K6-H's track record (no lies made across docs' 17 places) | Tabulate "sentences whose claim changes" with per-sentence grounds (the list below) | — | Counterexample 1 "`recovery.ts` line 226 'another device that still has the master key'" — the ledger's blob = the reserve key, and no device holds the reserve key. Since no "other device holding the reserve key" exists, the correct recovery is `maruhi key recovery --replace` (seal a new reserve key without opening the ledger, revoke the recorded old reserve key — help line 650). Rewrite it. Counterexample 2 "`keychain.ts`'s corruption guidance '`maruhi key recover` restores the original key'" — under DK, `key recover` registers a **new device key** with the reserve key (the original key doesn't come back). Change 'the original key' to 'a new key for this device (existing values remain readable)'. Counterexample 3 "`audit.ts` line 683 'wrapped master private key'" — `auth.recovery_blob_fetched` is fetching the sealed reserve key (a note in AUDIT_SPEC §3.1). Make it "sealed reserve key". Counterexample 4 "`session.ts` line 420 'the algorithms the master key needs'" — device key and reserve key share the same algorithms. Generalize to "maruhi's keys" | **Adopted** |
| 5-b Mechanical replacement | Uniform | — | — | Lies on ledger sentences (K6-H) | Rejected |
| 5-c Fix only help's 2 sentences (`agent` / `--key-ttl`), keep runtime wording | Smallest diff | — | — | help and runtime wording would diverge (docs' parenthetical would turn into a different lie) | Rejected |
| 5-d Silver bullet: rename the type `MasterKeys` / the entry names too, unifying the word | Uproots the drift | — | — | K4-12 "don't change persisted names without a migration" ✗ | Rejected |

**The sentence list (those whose claim changes)**: `agent.ts` 651 "The master key is forgotten … Recover the key again with `maruhi key recover`" → a forgotten device key is **re-registered as a new device** (`device add` + approval) + the forgotten key is revoked (same as docs `linux-keychain.mdx`). `agent.ts` 709 `agent status`'s display word → K7-6. `effect-cli.ts` 431 / 3447 → "this device's key". `invite.ts` 364 "Your master key … does not match your key on the project chain … Restore the chain key (`maruhi key recover`)" → "This machine's key is not one of your registered devices on this project's chain" (the same words as the approver-side skipped) + a registration path, not a restoration. `invite.ts` 787 / 795 / 800 / 803 → "device key" + the device-add path (K7-4). `keychain.ts` 236 / 246 / 332 / 334 / 340-341 → "this device's key" / "device-key record" / "the device's private key itself" + the recovery path (if a device remains, `device add`; if none, `key recover`; if neither, `key generate --new-identity`). `login.ts` 419 / 420 → "device key" + device add first. `recovery.ts` 226 / 381 → re-sealing the reserve key (`key recovery --replace`). `session.ts` 420 / 558 / 590 → as above. `audit.ts` 683 → "sealed reserve key". **Sentences left unchanged**: `guardian.ts` 380 (already "device key"), `key-recover.ts` 315 (already guides to device add).

**Round 2**: upward compat = the tabulation (adopted). Silver bullet = 5-d (K4-12 ✗). Counterexamples = the 4 above. **Round 3**: option tried "also review the end of `keychain.ts`'s corruption guidance 'ask an administrator to re-distribute wraps (re-run `maruhi member add`)'" → after a new identity (`key generate --new-identity`) comes re-invitation (docs `recover-your-key.mdx` "Starting over"). Re-running `member add` is one form of re-invitation, so keep it, but put the docs word "re-invite" first. **Round 4**: option tried "pin by check that 'master key' is 0 in help golden and docs" → update `cli-vocabulary.test.ts`'s K6-H nails to 0 on every page, and **add a 0 nail on the help golden too** (a runtime-wording grep can't separate sentences from comments mechanically, so only the help (golden) is mechanized). No new options. Stopped.

**Principle**: "vocabulary comes from facts: a key living on a device is device; a key living in the ledger is reserve. Persisted names don't change (K4-12)" ✓, K6-H ✓. **Adopted: 5-a**.

### K7-7. 1 sentence of the FP's provenance discipline in `device approve`'s output (§1-4) — placement and the yes's disposition

Counterexample 2 "every time is noisy" — approval is a rare ceremony; 1 line. Counterexample 3 "a `--project`-limited approval" — "every project you belong to" overshoots when limited → write "to your projects"; the limitation is stated by a different line | **Adopted** (reflecting counterexample 3) |

Emit as a body line via `io.log`). Stopped.

**Principle**: "the matching discipline is stated on the matching screen. A yes is no substitute for the match (K4-6 f-1's 'typing the full length as the argument is the match')" ✓, K6-R ✓. **Adopted: 7-a**.

### K7 ruling list (pinned before implementation)

| # | Topic | Adopted | What makes it better than the alternatives | Principle |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| K7-1 | The expiry wording | Align to the implementation, guiding to `--replace` (conditioned on "if the approving side registered nothing") | The same 1 sentence as docs with no new branch. The PUT-failure counterexample covered by the condition | Wording transcribes shipped behavior (K6-J) |
| K7-2 | not registered yet | "The approver skipped it · the request is consumed · the next keyed command on a cap-covering device registers" | The same claim, same words as docs :59. No lie from enumerating causes | Read the recovery path's preconditions (K6-X) |
| K7-3 | The midway line | Once at request age ≥ TTL/3 · only while `pending` · with the expiry time | Once suffices, appears when followable, adds no surface | An advisory goes as far as a signal (K4-5) |
| K7-4 | The old path's leftovers | Name only the guardian + align the path order (add → restore → generate) across the 4 sentences, pinned by checks | The same 3 shapes as docs' table. One path order | State the path in one order |
| K7-5 | The master-key line | Per sentence (device / reserve / rewrite). Comments, identifiers, persisted names unchanged | No lies from mechanical replacement. help and runtime words align | Vocabulary from facts (K6-H) · K4-12 |
