## 13. K8 addendum (2026-09-21 — collecting K7's carryovers: deriving the Web's envelope types and retracting a ruling text)

Collecting the "points put to the owner" (1)(6) and "carryover" (1) that K7 (PR #191) left in its completion record. The owner's rulings (K8's prompt §0-bis): **A (derive the Web's 4 handwritten envelopes) = ON, B (an addendum retracting K4-6's "+ yes") = ON, C (skip the request cancel when the approving side's registry PUT fails) = OFF, D (re-requesting with the same key after expiry) = OFF**. C / D are not implemented; the options stay in "points put to the owner". Specs, ADRs, `CLAUDE.md`, `packages/crypto`, and the server's authorization are untouched. The table uses K6-X's format (a blank is the mark of "not searched" — fill them before writing "stopped").

### K8 fact confirmations (read the code — K6-X's 3 principles)

- `apps/web/src/dashboard/types.ts`'s handwritten `interface`s are 4: `AuditEventsPage { events }` · `EnvironmentList { environments }` · `RotationFlagList { flags }` · `InvitationList { invitations }`. Consumers: `ProjectScreen.tsx` (`useApiResource<EnvironmentList>` / `<RotationFlagList>`, `apiGet<AuditEventsPage>` ×2) · `AccountAuditScreen.tsx` (`apiGet<AuditEventsPage>`) · `AuditEventList.tsx` (`AuditPageFetcher`'s return) · `InvitesTab.tsx` (`ResourceState<InvitationList>` / `useApiResource<InvitationList>`). **No handwritten duplicates of the same envelopes exist outside `types.ts`** (`useApiResource<...>`'s type arguments all use `types.ts` names — grep-verified).
- The api-schema side: `AuditEventsPageSchema` is **already named** (`audit-api.ts` :100 — referenced by 3 audit endpoints' `success`) and exported from `index.ts`. The Web just wasn't using it. The `success` of `environments`' `list` (`data-api.ts` :334), `rotation`'s `flags` (`rotation-api.ts` :84), and `invites`' `list` (`invites-api.ts` :205) are **inline `Schema.Struct`s**. The precedents `TokenListSchema` (`auth-api.ts` :153) / `DeviceListSchema` (`devices-api.ts` — K7-8) are "`export const XxxListSchema` placed and referenced from the endpoint's `success`".
- **`environments`' `success` is `{ environments, schemaPolicy? }`**, and the Web's handwritten `EnvironmentList` drops `schemaPolicy` (the advisory bundled at §12-7 / §12-11). Deriving the type surfaces `schemaPolicy?: SchemaPolicy` in the type, but this is a field **already on the wire** that the Web doesn't read (`ProjectScreen.tsx` only touches `.environments`).
- Check precedents: `apps/site/test/unit/cli-vocabulary.test.ts` `readFileSync`s `apps/web/src/dashboard/*.tsx` and nails phrases (K6-D). `apps/web/test/unit/` holds 5 pure-function unit tests riding the root `vitest.config.ts`'s projects (quality gate 7). `.oxlintrc.json` already uses `eslint-js/no-restricted-syntax` as an override on `apps/web/**` (the styling discipline) and `apps/cli/src/**` (K7-13's vocabulary).
- C's current state (`device.ts` `deviceApproveOp`): the latter stage is gated on "≥1 registered / already" (K4-31 (c)); inside it the order is **local record → registry PUT (failure folds to a Note — a 429 says "delete rows and re-run `device approve`") → request cancel (failure ignored)**, and the PUT's success doesn't enter the cancel's condition. The requester's `waitForRegistryRow` takes only the registry row as the signal (K4-5).
- D's current state (`deviceKeyForRequest`): key present + no request row + no registry row → refuse (K4-21 (c)). Only `--replace` proceeds. Docs (`devices.mdx` :65 / :152 / :154) and K7-1's wording transcribe this behavior.

### K8-1. The 3 envelope Schemas' names and placement (A)

| Option | Content | Upward compat | Silver bullet | Counterexamples | Adoption |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1-a **Right before each endpoint in its `*-api.ts`, place `export const EnvironmentListSchema` / `RotationFlagListSchema` / `InvitationListSchema = Schema.Struct({...the same definition...})` referenced from `success`. Export in `index.ts`. `AuditEventsPageSchema` uses the existing one. The Web does `typeof X.Type`** | Same naming, same placement as `TokenListSchema` / `DeviceListSchema`. Wire unchanged (just referencing the same `Schema.Struct` by name) | "Place a separate Web-specific Struct dropping `schemaPolicy` from `EnvironmentListSchema`" → creates 2 definitions, against the single-definition intent (ruling BR). Rejected | "Pull the endpoint's success out of HttpApi's types" → already rejected at K7-8 8-c (depends on Effect v4's internal type forms) | Counterexample 1 "`EnvironmentList`'s derived type surfaces `schemaPolicy?`, widening what `useApiResource<EnvironmentList>` means" — only the **type** widens; the wire carried that field all along. It corrects that the handwritten type was an incomplete transcription of the wire (K8-3). Counterexample 2 "the server's `handlers-*.ts` return the inline shape" — `success`'s referent is the same Struct, so the type is identical. Following confirmed by typecheck. Counterexample 3 "name it `InvitationListSchema` or `InviteListSchema`" — the row type is `InvitationSummarySchema` and the Web's handwritten name is `InvitationList`, so `InvitationListSchema` (aligned to the row type's stem — same shape as `TokenSummary` → `TokenList`, `DeviceSummary` → `DeviceList`) | **Adopted** |
| 1-b Put all 3 together in `packages/api-schema/src/index.ts` | 1 place | — | — | `index.ts` only re-exports (holds no definitions — as it stands). Different placement than the precedent | Rejected |
| 1-c Assemble the `Schema.Struct` on the Web side and take `typeof` | Doesn't touch api-schema | — | — | Schema runtime code would enter the Web (against the type-only-import rationale — the TCB) | Rejected |

**Round 2**: upward compat = codify counterexample 3's naming rule as "the row type's stem + List" (folded into 1-a). Silver bullet = "mechanically enforce named-ness (a lint banning inline `Schema.Struct` on api-schema's `success:`)" → inline `success`es like `{ expiresAtMs }` / `{ deks }` / `{ schemaPolicy }` include many the Web doesn't consume, making it a full revision beyond K8's scope (envelopes the Web consumes). To the owner points. Counterexample = "`AuditEventsPageSchema`'s name doesn't match the `*List` types" — audit is a page (continues via a `before` cursor), not a list. The existing name is right and also matches the Web's `AuditEventsPage`. No new options. **Round 3**: option tried "make `RotationFlagListSchema` `RotationFlagsSchema` (aligning to the endpoint name `flags`)" → the precedent is the row type's stem + `List` (`TokenListSchema` — endpoint `list`, field `tokens`). Align by row type, not field name. No new options. Stopped. **Principle**: "an envelope's name is the row type's stem + `List` (`Page` if a page), and the definition lives once, next to the endpoint" — K6-R ✓ (a claim in 1 place), wire unchanged ✓, ruling BR (single definition) ✓. **Adopted: 1-a**.

### K8-2. Where the check that no `interface` remains in `types.ts` lives (A)

| Option | Content | Upward compat | Silver bullet | Counterexamples | Adoption |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2-a **`apps/web/test/unit/types-derived.test.ts` (new): `readFileSync` `types.ts` and assert (i) 0 `interface` declarations, (ii) every `import` statement is `import type`, (iii) ≥1 `typeof XxxSchema.Type` derivation and its count equals the `export type` count (no handwritten `type X = { ... }` remains either)** | Non-vacuous (if the file is missing `readFileSync` fails; 0 derivations also fails). The type-only invariant (the reason in the comment — the TCB) is mechanized by the same check | — | Forbid `TSInterfaceDeclaration` on `apps/web/src/dashboard/types.ts` via an `.oxlintrc.json` override (the K7-13 shape — an existing mechanism) | Counterexample 1 (2-a) "a handwritten `type X = { a: string }` isn't an `interface`, so it passes" → caught by (iii)'s "`export type` count = `typeof …Schema.Type` count". Counterexample 2 (2-a) "the count has to be re-counted every time a type is added" → hold no count constant; compare the 2 numbers **to each other** (no re-counting). Counterexample 3 (2-a) "writing `import type` as `import { type X }`" → under `verbatimModuleSyntax` an inline `type` modifier also vanishes, but the comment's discipline is statement-form `import type`. The check requires statement-form `import type` and fails the inline form (pins the discipline to 1 shape) | **Adopted** |
| 2-b A lint override (forbid `TSInterfaceDeclaration` on `types.ts`) | The message appears where the edit happens (the reason reaches the next editor) | — | An existing mechanism | Counterexample "renaming / moving the file makes the override's glob match 0 files and pass silently" — vacuity can't be caught mechanically. Counterexample "a handwritten `type X = {...}` can't be caught (banning `TSTypeLiteral` would fail legitimate shapes like `Type` indexed types)" | Rejected (2-a's (iii) is superior) |
| 2-c Add it to `cli-vocabulary.test.ts` | Already reads Web files | — | — | That test is a check that "docs transcribe CLI / Web vocabulary"; a type's structure is a different subject. Don't mix a home's subject | Rejected |

**Round 2**: upward compat = (ii)(iii) folded into 2-a (reflected in the table). Silver bullet = "delete `types.ts` itself; each screen writes `typeof XSchema.Type` directly" → the same derivation duplicated per screen, against ruling BR (single definition). Rejected. Counterexample = "the check misreads a comment as `interface`" → the leading comment has no `interface` word, but it could appear later. The declaration regex is limited to line-initial `(export )?interface ` and excludes comment lines (`//`-starting). No new options. **Round 3**: option tried "place both 2-a and 2-b" → creates 2 owners of the claim (K6-R ✗). Only 2-a. No new options. Stopped. **Principle**: "a check is placed in a shape that can deny its own vacuity (fails if reading the target fails; compares counts to counts)" — K6-T ✓, K7-13 ✓ (lint loses to vacuity), K6-R ✓. **Adopted: 2-a**.

### K8-3. The `schemaPolicy?` surfacing in `EnvironmentList`'s derived type (from A's fact check)

| Option | Content | Upward compat | Silver bullet | Counterexamples | Adoption |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 3-a **Derive as-is (`schemaPolicy?` enters the type). The Web doesn't read it** | The wire's transcription becomes complete. No Web code changes (it only touches `.environments`) | — | — | Counterexample "the Web's display layer enumerates with `Object.keys(list)`" — it doesn't (grep: `EnvironmentList`'s only use is `useApiResource`'s type argument, 1 place) | **Adopted** |
| 3-b `Omit<typeof EnvironmentListSchema.Type, "schemaPolicy">` | Keeps the type in its old shape | — | — | "Hiding a field the Web doesn't read from the type" distorts the single definition for the Web's convenience. If it's unread, there's no harm | Rejected |
| 3-c Display `schemaPolicy` on the Web | Puts the field to use | — | — | A feature addition (outside K8's scope) | Rejected |

**Rounds 2 & 3**: option tried "split `schemaPolicy`'s bundling into another Schema (`EnvironmentListWithPolicySchema`)" → splitting the wire's shape = a change. Prohibited (§2). No new options. Stopped. **Principle**: "a derived type is the wire's transcription; don't shave it for the consumer's convenience" — wire unchanged ✓, ruling BR ✓. **Adopted: 3-a**.

### K8-4. How K4-6's "+ yes" retraction is written (B)

The owner's instruction is "an addendum in §12 / §13, not a revision of the ruling text (K4-6's body isn't deleted)", so the form is fixed. Only the placement is a ruling point (single round): 1 paragraph at §13's end (right after this section, before "K8 ruling list"). K4-6's body is untouched (history). The retraction's grounds: K6-W W-2 (a yes doesn't stop "a person who trusts a sent FP" — what stops it is the discipline "read the FP off the screen of the machine being added") and K7-7 (that discipline emitted as 1 sentence in `device approve`'s output). **Principle**: "a ruling text stays as history; a retraction is done by addendum (the design record is append-only)".

**The retraction's addendum (B)**: **The "+ yes" in K4-6's ruling text (and the "match + yes" descriptions in §2 / §4 / §5) is retracted. The implementation (ceremony gate → full-length FP match → display → immediate `add_device`. No yes — K6-N) is correct.** Grounds: a yes works neither against a mistyped input (a wrong FP matches no request and fails) nor against the threat (someone who stole a key-material-access token places a request and sends its FP for approval) — a person who trusts a sent FP presses yes too (K6-W W-2). What stops the threat is the discipline "read the FP off the screen of the machine being added", which is held by docs (`devices.mdx`) and `device approve`'s output sentence (K7-7). K4-6's body and §2 / §4 / §5's letters stay as history (not revised).

### K8-5. Cancelling the request when the approving side's registry PUT fails (C — OFF: not implemented. The options just go to the points)

| Option | Content | Upward compat | Silver bullet | Counterexamples | Adoption |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 5-a If the PUT fails, skip the request cancel (the request remains; if the approver deletes rows and re-runs `device approve`, every project goes already → PUT → cancel) | The Note's guidance ("re-run it") is followable as-is. The requester exits the wait on the re-run's PUT (if within TTL) | 5-a + add to the Note "the request is left in place so that the re-run can signal the new device", and widen the requester's expiry-wording condition (K7-1 "if the approving side registered nothing") to "if the approving side said the registry write failed, delete rows and re-run on the approving side" | "A surface that proceeds to the chain check without a registry row" (a path where the requester looks at the chain after exiting the wait, callable even without a signal via `device add --check` or similar) → a new surface (a flag); it doesn't touch K4-5 (an advisory goes as far as a signal) but its scope is wide | Counterexample 1 "the PUT's failure is a transient 5xx, not a 429" — skipping the cancel leaves the request; the approver's re-run converges (idempotent). No harm. Counterexample 2 "the approver never re-runs and the TTL expires" — the requester reaches the expiry wording (same as today). Counterexample 3 "the current state — a PUT failed yet the cancel ran — means the requester gets no signal and the approver's re-run fails at 'No pending device-add request'" — this is carryover (1) itself, which 5-a removes | **Put to the owner (OFF)** |
| 5-b Make the PUT come after the cancel (swap the order) | — | — | — | If the PUT fails after the cancel, there's neither request nor signal (worse than today). K4-31 "the advisory's write comes after the truth" is satisfied but the recovery path disappears | Rejected |
| 5-c On PUT failure, signal the requester another way (a `registryFull` mark on the request row) | The requester learns the cause | — | — | The request row is the server's Schema (a wire change + a K3 revision). Making the requester judge on a server-declared mark (K4-5 ✗) | Rejected |

**Round 2**: upward compat = 5-a's upward compat (table). Silver bullet = the table's "surface" option. Counterexample = "with 5-a the request remains, so until the approver re-runs, the request is also visible to other approvers (the same person's other devices)" — it's the same person's request, and approval is idempotent (already). No harm. No new options. **Round 3**: option tried "treat the PUT failure the same as the latter-stage gate (≥1 registered), Warning + exit code 1" → it's on the chain, so it isn't a "failure". The exit code stays as today (Note); only whether to cancel is the point. No new options. Stopped. **Principle (if adopted)**: "if the signal couldn't be sent, don't destroy the material (the request) that could re-send it" — K4-31 ✓ (the cancel comes after the advisory), K4-5 ✓. **Point put to the owner (1)**: whether to adopt 5-a (with its upward compat). If adopted, align the Note's wording, `devices.mdx` :65's condition (the paragraph "its write to the registry … can fail when the registry is full"), and `device.test.ts` (the cancel's presence) in the same PR.

### K8-6. Re-requesting with the same key after expiry (D — OFF: not implemented. The options just go to the points)

| Option | Content | Upward compat | Silver bullet | Counterexamples | Adoption |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 6-a If "key present + no request + no registry + on no chain", re-create the request with the same key. "On no chain" is judged by syncing every project (`openProject` × N); if even 1 can't be synced, fail-closed refusal (guide to `--replace`) | The key needn't be discarded on every expiry (the FP the approver transcribed stays valid) | 6-a + the judgment is confined inside `deviceKeyForRequest`'s "key present + no request + no registry" branch, and a sync failure is conveyed as a typed error (the K4-33 shape) | "Eliminate expiry (extend / re-extend the request's TTL)" → the TTL is an api-schema constant (`DEVICE_ADD_REQUEST_TTL_MS`), and extending is a server (K3) revision + an acceptance-policy value = out of scope | Counterexample 1 "a user with 0 projects" — "on no chain" is true (the empty set), so re-requesting works. Correct. Counterexample 2 "a project the user left can't be synced (403)" — fail-closed refusal = the `--replace` guidance (same as today). The safe side, but a single left project makes re-requesting forever impossible. Counterexample 3 "the sync is slow (large N)" — limited to post-expiry re-runs, so infrequent. Counterexample 4 "a pre-DK copy key (K4-18)" — a copy key is on another device's chain, so "on no chain" is false → refuse (today's `--replace` guidance). Correct | **Put to the owner (OFF)** |
| 6-b Re-request on just "no registry row + no request" (don't look at the chain) | No sync needed | — | — | The registry is advisory (DK-D). Re-requesting with an already-registered key whose row was deleted just ends in "already" on the approver side, harmless — but it uses an advisory as a judgment input (K4-5 ✗) | Rejected |
| 6-c Keep `--replace` (today) | No branch | — | — | Counterexample (UX) "the FP changes on every expiry, wasting the FP the approver transcribed" — at K7-1 the wording was aligned to the implementation. The behavioral hole remains | Keep as-is (OFF) |

**Round 2**: upward compat = the table. Silver bullet = the table (TTL). Counterexample = counterexample 2 is 6-a's hole (the 403 of a project the user left). Revision "treat 403 as 'non-member' = 'not on it'" — a non-member's own device can't remain on the chain either (leaving = remove_member removes the device too), so reading 403 as "not on it" is correct. Though distinguishing the 403's reason (`ForbiddenReason`) is needed. Folded into 6-a. No new options. **Round 3**: option tried "re-request via an explicit `device add --resume` flag" → grows a flag (a surface). A human deciding between it and `--replace` = the expiry wording presents 2 choices. 6-a (automatic) is superior. No new options. Stopped. **Principle (if adopted)**: "the decision to discard a key looks at all of the truth (the chain) first; if it can't be seen, fall to the not-discarding side (fail-closed)" — DK-D ✓, K4-5 ✓. **Point put to the owner (2)**: whether to adopt 6-a (the upward compat + the 403 = non-member reading). If adopted, align the expiry wording (K7-1), `devices.mdx` :65 / :152 / :154, `device add --replace`'s help, and `device.test.ts` in the same PR.

**An honest inventory**: K8-5 / K8-6 (the OFF C / D) ran with columns filled. **K8-1 put a rejected option (a Web-specific Struct dropping `schemaPolicy`) in the table's "upward compat" column — it didn't constitute an upward compat**. Round 2's "codifying the naming rule" was a note, not an option. **K8-2 ran upward compat (ii)(iii) and a silver bullet (abolishing `types.ts`), but didn't constitute an upward compat for pullfrog's finding "the check's reach is 1 file", and constituted no counterexamples against the check itself (wrapping · block comments · nested type arguments)**. The same shape as K6-U). K8-3 / K8-4 are single-round equivalents. Here each point is run.

No new options | Unchanged. The table column's error (a rejected option in the upward-compat column) is corrected in this section |
| K8-2 supplement | The interface-absence check's reach and robustness | **A consumer-side sweep**: collect the type arguments of the consumer-side entry points (`apiGet` / `useApiResource` / `ApiResult` / `ResourceState`) across `src/` and require each to be a `types.ts` `export type` name (excluding `api.ts` / `use-api-resource.ts`, which declare the entry points, and `types.ts`). The reach widens from 1 file to the whole consumer side; both a screen's handwritten envelope (`useApiResource<{ flags: … }>`) and a local alias (`useApiResource<LocalFlags>`) fail | **Riding the existing sweep mechanism (`endpoints.test.ts`'s `findSourceOffenders` / `isSweepTarget` — ruling BW's consumer-side sweep)**: delete the new file `types-derived.test.ts` and place the 2 `it`s inside the same describe group (the claim's home aligns to "the consumer-side sweep"). (ii) "only `import type`" is a duplicate of the existing check, so **deleted** | Counterexample 1 "if oxfmt wraps after `=` on a long name, the per-line regex misses and the counts disagree — a **false failure**" — the regex was made to span wraps with `\s*`, and confirmed to **pass** under mutation (a wrapped derivation). Counterexample 2 "an `interface` inside a block comment" — `/* … */` and `//` are stripped before counting. Confirmed passing under mutation. Replaced with `typeArgumentAt`, which slices type arguments by counting angle-bracket depth; confirmed failing | **Adopted (K8-7)**. Principle "before placing a check, grep for an existing check holding the same claim (the check version of K6-U)" + "counterexamples against a check are built on both inputs it must pass and inputs it must not pass" |
| K8-3 supplement | `schemaPolicy?` | "Write in `EnvironmentList`'s JSDoc that `schemaPolicy` is a bundled advisory" → the derivation source `EnvironmentListSchema`'s JSDoc holds it (K6-R). Not transcribed on the Web side | "Mechanically drop the optional from the Web's type" → same as 3-b (already rejected) | Counterexample "a screen `JSON.stringify`s `useApiResource<EnvironmentList>`'s `state.value` to display it" — none (grep). No new options | Unchanged |
| K8-4 supplement | How the "+ yes" retraction is written | "Footnote the relevant word in K4-6's body ('retracted — §13 K8-4')" → against the owner's instruction not to touch the body. §2 / §4 / §5's "match + yes" letters likewise stay | "Mechanically check that the design record's ruling text matches the implementation" → a prose claim can't be mechanized (same as the K6-V supplement) | Counterexample "someone who doesn't read §13 implements yes after reading only K4-6" — the retraction lives in 3 places: the K6-N / K7-7 ruling tables and this section. No new options | Unchanged |
| K8-5 / K8-6 supplement | The OFF C / D | Ran (the table). Additional options tried: C "convey the PUT failure to the requester via the request row's `label`" → a label is advisory and attacker-chosen (K6-W W-2 (c)). Rejected. D "`device add` extends the request's deadline (`PATCH`)" → a wire change + K3 revision. Rejected | — | — | Unchanged |

**K8-7 (a mid-implementation additional ruling)**: the check's home moved to `apps/web/test/unit/endpoints.test.ts` (ruling BW's consumer-side sweep); `types-derived.test.ts` isn't placed. Claims: (a) "`types.ts` is only `typeof XxxSchema.Type` derivations (interface 0 · `export type` count = derivation count)", (b) "every consumer-side entry point's type argument is a `types.ts` export name". Mutations: fails under the 4 that should fail (reverting to `interface` / a handwritten type literal / an inline envelope at the call site / a call-site local alias), passes under the 2 that should pass (a derivation wrapped after `=` / an `interface` inside a block comment). Non-vacuous = `readFileSync` fails if `types.ts` is missing, and it also fails on 0 `export type`s / 0 consumer-side hits. **Known limitation** (an info memo from PR #192 pullfrog round 2 — one I hadn't constructed as a counterexample myself): the day an entry point's type argument legitimately becomes a function type (`=>`) or `void` / a union, the sweep fails with the wrong guidance "derive it there" (it won't pass silently — fail-loud). When that happens, don't invent an alias in `types.ts`; narrow the exclusion set or the entry-point enumeration. Since no such surface exists today, no advance accommodation is placed.

**Principle** (continuing K6-X): "the upward-compat column holds **only an option that keeps the adopted option's advantages and removes a drawback** (don't feel filled by putting a rejected option there)"; "before placing a check, grep for an existing check holding the same claim"; "counterexamples against a check are built on both inputs it must pass and inputs it must not pass".

## 14. K9 addendum (2026-09-24 — collecting K8's "points put to the owner": the registry PUT failure vs the request cancel / the api-schema inline-envelope lint)

Collecting the "points put to the owner" (1)(2)(3) that K8 (PR #192) left in its completion record. The owner's rulings (K9's prompt §0-bis): **A (the request cancel when the approving side's registry PUT fails — K8-5) = ON, B (a lint banning inline `Schema.Struct` on api-schema's `success:`) = ON, C (re-creating a request with the same key after expiry — K8-6) = OFF**. D onward (K7 owner points (3)(4)(5) · K7 carryover (2) · §11 W-3 / W-4 / W-6) is out of scope. Specs, ADRs, `CLAUDE.md`, `packages/crypto`, the server's authorization, wire formats, and acceptance-policy values are untouched. The table uses K6-X's format (a blank is the mark of "not searched" — fill them before writing "stopped"). Rulings are recorded here before implementation, and mid-implementation judgments are added in the same shape as K9-7 onward.

### K9 fact confirmations (read the code — K6-X's 3 principles. Read the branches, not the design record's summaries)

- **The approving side** (`device.ts` `deviceApproveOp`): ceremony gate → `matchRequest` (recomputes the FP from `requestList`'s public keys and matches; if no match, fails at "No pending device-add request matches that fingerprint …") → the project loop (`approveOnProject` — `already` / `registered` / `skipped` / `failed`. Failures fold into the result) → **the gate** "≥1 registered / already" (if none, Warning "… the request was left in place" + exit code 1 — K4-31 (c)) → local record (approved) → registry `register` (PUT. Failure folds into a Note via `Effect.catch`; a 429 = `DeviceRegistryLimitError` says "registry is full (32 rows) … re-run `maruhi device approve` to list it", others say "could not update the device registry (…); the device is registered on the chains above regardless") → `requestCancel` (failure ignored). **The PUT's success doesn't enter the cancel's condition**. On a re-run after the cancel, `matchRequest` finds no request and fails — so the 429 Note's "re-run" is unfollowable (the very thing in K7 carryover (1) / K8-5 counterexample 3).
- **The server** (`db.package/devices.ts`): the registry PUT (upsert) doesn't touch the request table. `requestList` / `requestFind` don't return expired rows. `requestCreate` is a 409 `request-exists` on a live request with the same FP, `device-registered` on a registry row.
- **The requester's wait** (`waitForRegistryRow`): takes only its own-FP registry row as the signal (K4-5). Emits the guidance once at the first round after the request's age exceeds TTL/3 (K7-3 — the condition names only "failed on every project"). Past the deadline, false → the expiry failure wording (K7-1 — "`--replace` if the approving side registered nothing". Its first sentence is "expired before it was approved").
- **Re-running after expiry** (`deviceKeyForRequest`): key + request row → resume waiting / key + registry row → confirm on the chain (`createOrResumeRequest`'s 409 `device-registered` → `already-registered` → signal on the wait's first round → sync and count each project) / key + neither → refuse (the `--replace` guidance — K4-21 (c)). **The requester can proceed to a chain check only when a registry row exists**.
- The only writer of a registry row is `deviceApproveOp`'s PUT (grep of `apps/cli/src` / `apps/web/src` — `devices.register` is called in 1 place). So the only path that writes a failed-PUT device's row later is "a `device approve` re-run while the request is still alive".
- Transcriptions of the same claim (counted by grep — K7-15): "the request is consumed / can't be re-run" = `devices.mdx` :59 · :63 and `device.ts`'s "not registered yet" Note (emitted only after the signal = only when the PUT succeeded — conditionally true). "the registry write (the signal) can fail" = `devices.mdx` :65 and `device.ts`'s 429 Note. "revocation / re-run" guidance = `devices.mdx` :65 · :152 · :154 and `device.ts`'s expiry wording, the wait's guidance, and the refusal wording. `cli-vocabulary.test.ts`'s nails pin `32 rows` (once in `devices.mdx`) · `five minutes after the request` · `The request lives 15 minutes` · `Requests expire 15 minutes after`. Other pages (getting-started / linux-keychain / recover-your-key / invite-a-teammate / README) hold none of these claims.
- **B**: `success: Schema.Struct(` in `packages/api-schema/src/` occurs at **9 places in 4 files** (matching K8's count): `devices-api.ts`'s `requestCreate { expiresAtMs }` · `requestList { requests }`, `data-api.ts`'s `deks.listMine { deks }` · `schemaPolicy.get { schemaPolicy }`, `key-wraps-api.ts`'s `passkeyRegister { wrapId }` · `guardianCreate { groupId }` · `wards { wards }` · `handoffCreate { expiresAtMs }` · `handoffApprovals { approvals }`. Every other `success:` is a name (`XxxSchema` / `Redirect` / `HtmlPage`) or `HttpApiSchema.NoContent`. `HttpApiEndpoint.*` endpoints are defined only in `packages/api-schema/src/` (`apps/server` just references the type `HttpApiEndpoint.Top`). Naming precedents: a list = the row type's stem + `List` (`TokenListSchema` ← `TokenSummarySchema`, `DeviceListSchema`, `InvitationListSchema`), a single fetch = the resource + `Result` (`RecoveryWrapResultSchema` · `PasskeyWrapResultSchema` · `GuardianGroupResultSchema` — all GETs), a creation response = the resource + the endpoint's verb + `Result` (`InviteIssueResultSchema` ← `issue`, `CliStartResultSchema` ← `cliStart`, `CliPollResultSchema`, `EnvironmentChainResultSchema`). A `{ expiresAtMs }`-only response already has 1 name as `InviteIssueResultSchema`, unshared with devices' / key-wraps' 2.
- Check precedents (K8's lesson — grep before placing): `.oxlintrc.json`'s `eslint-js/no-restricted-syntax` has 3 overrides: `apps/web/**` (styling) · `apps/web/**/ui.package/**` (the exception) · `apps/cli/src/**` (K7-13's vocabulary). No check holding the same claim exists on api-schema. The load-time sweep `sweep.ts`'s `forEachEndpoint` (strict.ts / session-capability.ts) looks at payloads and session capabilities, not `success` names.

### K9-1. The request cancel when the registry PUT fails (A-(1))

| Option | Content | Upward compat | Silver bullet | Counterexamples | Adoption |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1-a **Skip the request cancel when the PUT fails** (K8-5's 5-a). Extract the PUT's result as a value via `Effect.result`; cancel only on success. On failure, the Note says "the request is left in place until <deadline>. Re-run `device approve` by then (deleting rows if a 429)" | The Note's guidance is followable as-is: a re-run finds the request in `matchRequest`, every project goes `already` (the failed ones get retried) → the gate passes → the record is overwritten → PUT → cancel. The requester exits the wait on the re-run's PUT (if within TTL). The change is 1 branch in the latter stage | **1-a + showing the deadline and the post-deadline consequence**: the Note carries the request's deadline (`formatUtcMinutes(request.expiresAtMs)` — the approver holds the deadline via `matchRequest`) and, past it, "the device stays registered on the chains but isn't listed in the registry". Add the "couldn't be listed in the registry" branch to the requester's wait guidance (K7-3) and expiry wording (K7-1) (K9-3) | 1-d (below) | Counterexample 1 "the PUT actually applied and only the response was lost (network)" — the row exists, so the requester gets the signal. The request lives until the TTL, but a re-run is `already` + PUT (upsert) + cancel = idempotent, and even another of your own devices approving is `already`. No harm. Counterexample 2 "the approver doesn't re-run by the deadline" — the device lands on the chains, with neither a registry row nor a request. The requester reaches the expiry wording → choosing `--replace` there leaves an orphaned device on the chain (the return of K7-1 counterexample 2) → K9-3 adds a branch to the wording to remove it. Writing the registry row is impossible without C (OFF) or 1-d → a point for the owner. Counterexample 3 "partial success + PUT failure" — since the request remains, the re-run retries the `failed` projects (today it couldn't retry because the cancel had run). `devices.mdx` :59's "once any project registered, `maruhi device approve` cannot be re-run" is true only when the PUT succeeded → re-base the condition on the signal (the registry row) (K9-3). Counterexample 4 "old-CLI approver × new-CLI requester" — the approver's behavior stays old (the cancel is unconditional). The requester's new wording is conditioned on "if the approver's output said so", so even an old approver's output ("re-run" only) doesn't lie. Counterexample 5 "a leftover request stays visible in `requestList`, inviting a mistaken approval from another device" — same person's request, same key; approval goes through FP matching + the ceremony gate. Result is `already`. No harm | **Adopted** (with the upward compat) |
| 1-b Put the PUT after the cancel (K8-5's 5-b) | — | — | — | If the PUT fails after the cancel, there's neither request nor signal (worse than today). K4-31 is satisfied but the recovery path disappears | Rejected |
| 1-c Put a "couldn't be listed in the registry" mark on the request row and tell the requester (K8-5's 5-c) | The requester learns the cause | — | — | A wire change (the request row's Schema and a K3 revision — prohibited by §1-bis). The requester judging on a server-declared mark (K4-5 ✗) | Rejected |
| 1-d **Silver bullet**: don't let the re-run depend on the request — if `device approve <fp>` finds no request, and the same FP is in this device's local record as approved and lands as one's own device on the verified chain, perform only the registry PUT (the cancel stays unconditional) | Doesn't depend on the TTL; the registry row can be written even after the deadline. Leaves no request behind | — | (this column is the silver bullet) | Counterexample 1 "the decision's entry point is the local record (an advisory — named by §2 / K4-5)" — the decision itself can be made on the chain, but the display name's (label's) source exists only in the request or the local record. Counterexample 2 "it works only on the device that approved" (the Note lands on that device, so the practical harm is small). Counterexample 3 "a path that writes the registry row after the deadline plugs the same hole as C (post-deadline re-request with the same key — OFF) on a different surface" — it answers under A's name a problem the owner set to OFF. The ceremony command gains a request-less branch (just a chain sync + an advisory write) | **Put to the owner** (as a C alternative) |
| 1-e Silver bullet 2: change the requester's signal from the registry row to the chain (poll each project's chain while waiting) | Immune to the registry's failure (the signal is the truth) | — | (this column is the silver bullet) | Counterexample "the signal fires mid-way through the approver's loop" — the requester exits the wait the moment `add_device` lands on the first project, misreporting the rest as "not registered yet" (breaks K7 fact check (1) "by the time the signal is seen the approver's loop is done"). N projects × 3 s × 15 min of syncing. A revision of K4-5's signal design | Rejected |
| 1-f Auto-retry the PUT (backoff) | Absorbs transient failures | — | — | A 429 (the cap) isn't fixed by retrying. The failed branch remains after retries (1-a still needed). A retry policy is an invention | Rejected |
| 1-g On a 429, the approver automatically deletes old registry rows and re-PUTs | No human needed | — | — | Deciding which row to delete on advisories (the row's creation time · display name) (K4-5 ✗). Row deletion is part of `device revoke`, a human's call (DK-D) | Rejected |

**Round 2**: upward compat = adding the deadline and the post-deadline consequence to 1-a (the table). Silver bullets = 1-d / 1-e constructed (the table). Counterexamples = 5 for 1-a, 3 for 1-d, 1 for 1-e (the table). K9-3 (the wording's branch) came out of 1-a's counterexamples 2 and 3. **Round 3**: option tried "promote the Note to a Warning" → `notice.ts`'s vocabulary puts "something whose being missed can lower safety" at Warning. What's missing here is only an advisory row; the chain (the truth) is correct. It stays a Note (same conclusion as K8-5 round 3). Option tried "exit code 1 on a PUT failure" → it's on the chain, so it isn't a failure (K8-5 round 3 · §1 A's instruction). No new options. **Round 4**: option tried "put in both 1-a and 1-d (the request within TTL, the local record after it)" → 1-d's counterexamples 1 and 3 remain as-is (preempts the owner's decision scope). Option tried "instead of skipping the cancel, extend the request's deadline on failure" → extending is a wire change (same as K8-5 supplement). No new options. Stopped.

**Principle**: "if the signal couldn't be sent, don't destroy the material (the request) that could re-send it" — K4-31 ✓ (the cancel comes after the advisory's write and now also conditioned on its success — the order of truth [the chain] first is unchanged), K4-5 ✓ (the branch's input is **the result of my own PUT**, not the contents of a server-declared advisory), DK-D ✓ (the registry isn't a source of truth — even with no row, the device is registered on the chain), DK-A ✓ (permissions to people, keys to devices — unchanged), K6-J ✓ (the wording transcribes the shipped behavior — aligned at K9-3). **Adopted: 1-a (with the upward compat)**. What makes it superior: it removes no recovery path (unlike 1-b), touches neither the wire nor K4-5 (unlike 1-c), keeps the signal's meaning (after the loop, unlike 1-e), invents nothing and deputizes no human judgment (unlike 1-f / 1-g), and closes every in-TTL path without preempting the owner's OFF (C) (unlike 1-d).

### K9-2. Handling PUT failures other than 429 (A-(2))

| Option | Content | Upward compat | Silver bullet | Counterexamples | Adoption |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2-a **Skip the cancel regardless of the failure's kind**. Only the Note's wording differs by kind (429 = delete rows, then re-run / others = just re-run) | 1 branch ("did the PUT succeed"). Converges on a re-run even for transient 5xx / network failures (idempotent — `already` + PUT + cancel) | Give both of 2-a's Notes the same phrase ("The request is left in place until") and have docs transcribe the same phrase (the target of K9-3's nail) | "Regardless of the kind" is itself the silver bullet (no kind enumeration needed — a newly added error type isn't left behind) | Counterexample 1 "a 403 (the token isn't `*` × admin)" — by the time approval succeeded the token has key-material access (`ensureKeyMaterialAccess`, the same level as request creation), so it normally can't happen; if it does, the re-run gets the same 403 → the Note carries the cause. The request disappears at its deadline (no harm). Counterexample 2 "a 400 `DeviceFingerprintMismatch`" — the public key's FP was already recomputed in `matchRequest`, so it can't happen; same as above if it did. Counterexample 3 "the response was lost but it applied" — K9-1 counterexample 1 (no harm) | **Adopted** |
| 2-b Skip the cancel only on a 429 | Aligns with the 429 wording | — | — | 5xx / network failures leave today's hole (no signal, no re-run) | Rejected |
| 2-c Retry once immediately on a 5xx, then 2-a if it still fails | Absorbs many transient failures | — | — | Invents the retry policy (count, interval) (same as 1-f). With 2-a, a re-run does the same thing by a human's hand | Rejected |

**Round 2**: upward compat = the shared phrase (the table). Silver bullet = not enumerating kinds (the table). Counterexamples = 3 (the table). **Round 3**: option tried "return whether to cancel inside `Effect.catch` (branch without extracting the value)" → against §2's "extract a failure as a value", and puts the latter stage inside a catch, making it unreadable. Extract the `Result` via `Effect.result` and branch on `Result.isFailure` (the precedent is `strict.ts` using `Result`). No new options. **Round 4**: option tried "show the HTTP status in the Note's wording" → for other errors `toCliError(error).message` already carries the cause (the current wording). No new options. Stopped.

**Principle**: "a failure branch is split by success/failure, not by enumerating kinds; only the wording looks at the kind" — K9-1 ✓, K6-T ✓ (an enumeration miss can't arise structurally). **Adopted: 2-a**. What makes it superior: it leaves no 2-b hole and gets the same recovery without 2-c's invention.

### K9-3. What the approver and the requester see when the request is left behind (A-(3) — linking wording, docs, and checks)

Read at each point in time (`deviceApproveOp` / `matchRequest` / `waitForRegistryRow` / `deviceKeyForRequest` / `createOrResumeRequest`):

| Time | Approver side | Requester side |
|---|---|---|
| T1 PUT fails → the approver re-runs within TTL; the requester is waiting | Note (the request is left until <deadline>) → the re-run: all `already` (the failed ones retried) → PUT → cancel | The registry row mid-wait → confirm on the chain → "Approved: … N projects" |
| T2 Same as T1 but the requester had stopped waiting (Ctrl+C) | Same | A within-TTL re-run: request exists → resume waiting → row → confirm. A post-TTL re-run: no request + a registry row → confirm on the chain (`deviceKeyForRequest`'s 2nd branch — actually read) |
| T3 PUT fails → the approver doesn't re-run by the deadline | A post-deadline re-run is "No pending device-add request …" | The wait's guidance at TTL/3 (K7-3) → the expiry wording. The key is on the chain, but with no registry row and no request, a re-run is refused (the `--replace` guidance). **`--replace` creates an orphan** |
| T4 Only the PUT's response was lost | Note (the row actually exists) | Gets the signal and completes. The request lives until the TTL (no harm) |

| Option | Content | Upward compat | Silver bullet | Counterexamples | Adoption |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 3-a **Align the wording and docs to the time table**: (i) the approver's 2 Notes = "… will not see the completion signal. The request is left in place until <deadline>: …re-run `maruhi device approve` before then …" + the post-deadline consequence, (ii) add "or could not list this device in your device registry" to the wait guidance's (K7-3) condition, (iii) change the expiry wording's first sentence to "expired before this machine saw the completion signal" (at T3 approval already happened — "before it was approved" is false) and add the branch "if the approver registered it but couldn't list it in the registry, don't discard this key (`maruhi device list` shows where it's registered)", (iv) re-base `devices.mdx` :59's "once any project registered, … cannot be re-run" on the signal (the registry row), add a "when it couldn't be listed in the registry" paragraph after :63, hand :65's expiry paragraph to that paragraph and add the T3 branch, and add the T3 condition to What can go wrong's :152 (the refusal wording) and :154 (No pending) | Adds no branch, surface, or wire, and aligns the same claim to the same words across every transcription | Close (iii)'s branch with "`maruhi device list` shows it" (`device list` on the new device takes no values, needs no key, no gate, and syncs the chain to show your devices — the requester can verify the fact without going to the approver's screen) | "Tie docs' and the CLI's transcriptions together with a check" — add 1 to the existing mechanism `cli-vocabulary.test.ts` (K7-7's precedent: nail the same phrase in both). **Grepped for a check with the same claim before placing it**: none pins `left in place` / `completion signal` (`device.test.ts` looks only at the CLI-side wording) | Counterexample 1 "a requester who never saw the approver's output runs `--replace` after expiry" — the wording lists 2 conditions, verifiable with `device list` (the upward compat). Counterexample 2 "`devices.mdx`'s `32 rows` nail (1 occurrence)" — repeating "32 rows" in the new paragraph would break the nail → the new paragraph writes "the registry is full" and doesn't grow the number's transcriptions (K6-Y — a count-pinned nail mustn't silently spawn duplicates). Counterexample 3 "`device.test.ts`'s `not.toContain("expired before it was approved")` (the 409-lookup-failure test)" — changing the first sentence vacuums the negative check (anything passes) → align it to the new phrase (K8-2's non-vacuity discipline). Counterexample 4 "should the approver's 'No pending device-add request' wording also get the T3 condition" — at T3 the approver sees this wording only after their own Note (with the deadline). Since the Note states the deadline and the post-deadline consequence, the failure wording stays for the general cases (expired request, mistyped FP). Only docs' What can go wrong :154 gets the condition added | **Adopted** (the upward compat + the nail) |
| 3-b Leave the expiry wording alone and fix only docs | A smaller CLI diff | — | — | The requester reads the terminal's wording, not docs. T3's "before it was approved" stays false (K6-J ✗) | Rejected |
| 3-c On expiry, the requester syncs the chain, judges "am I on it" itself, and varies the wording | Doesn't send a human to the approver's output | — | Re-frames the problem (the requester can read the truth) | Adds a branch and a sync — the scope of K7's owner point (3) (deriving the cause on the requester) and C (the post-deadline judgment — same sync as K8-6's 6-a). Outside this PR's scope (§0-bis D) | To the owner points (same row as K7 owner point (3)) |

**Round 2**: upward compat = the `device list` guidance (the table). Silver bullets = the existing nail mechanism (the table) · 3-c (out of scope). Counterexamples = 4 (the table). **Round 3**: option tried "also write the requester-side command into the approver's Note ('on the new device, do not run --replace')" → the approver's Note is read on the approver side, and the requester's wording holds the same branch (K6-R — the claim's owners are the requester-side wording and docs). Option tried "annotate T3's 'no registry row' in `maruhi device list`'s output" → `device list` already lists registry display names as "(server-reported)", and no row means no display name. No new options. **Round 4**: option tried "make the nail's phrase 'will not see the completion signal'" → of the Note's 2 sentences docs quote, the one carrying the claim this PR makes (that the request was left) is the 2nd. The nail is "The request is left in place until". No new options. Stopped.

**Principle**: "the wording transcribes the fact at each point in time; if you add 1 branch, count every transcription of a claim carrying that branch and align them to the same words" — K6-J ✓, K6-R ✓ (the normative claim lives in docs; the CLI is an unremovable duplicate, so same words), K6-Y ✓ (a nail everywhere a transcription exists — also on a new claim), K7-15 ✓, K8-2 ✓ (don't let a negative check go vacuous), ADR-0017 ✓ (user-visible wording is English). **Adopted: 3-a**. What makes it superior: it leaves none of 3-b's falsehood, pulls in none of 3-c's out-of-scope sync, and shows the requester a way to verify the fact themselves (`device list`).

### K9-4. The inline envelopes' names and sharing `{ expiresAtMs }` (B-(1))

| Option | Content | Upward compat | Silver bullet | Counterexamples | Adoption |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 4-a **Name by the precedent's 3 rules, a distinct name per surface**: a list = the row type's stem + `List` (`DeviceAddRequestListSchema` ← `DeviceAddRequestSummarySchema`, `RecipientDekListSchema` ← `RecipientDekSchema`, `WardListSchema` ← `WardSummarySchema`, `HandoffApprovalListSchema` ← `HandoffApprovalResultSchema`), a single fetch = the resource + `Result` (`SchemaPolicyResultSchema` ← GET), a creation = the resource + the endpoint's verb + `Result` (`DeviceAddRequestCreateResultSchema` ← `requestCreate`, `PasskeyWrapRegisterResultSchema` ← `passkeyRegister`, `GuardianGroupCreateResultSchema` ← `guardianCreate`, `HandoffCreateResultSchema` ← `handoffCreate`). Each definition is placed in its `*-api.ts` after the row type, before the group, its JSDoc carrying the endpoint's path, and exported from `index.ts` | Zero invention (all 3 rules read from existing names). Wire unchanged (just naming the same `Schema.Struct`s) | Separating "single fetch = resource + `Result`" from "creation = + the verb" prevents a name like `DeviceAddRequestResultSchema` from which you can't tell fetch or creation (`PasskeyWrapResultSchema` is a GET's distribution shape and could be confused with `{ wrapId }`'s registration response — the verb separates them) | — | Counterexample 1 "`{ expiresAtMs }` has the same shape on 2 surfaces, so make it 1" → 4-b below. Counterexample 2 "shouldn't `HandoffApprovalResult`'s stem produce `…ResultListSchema`" — the precedent `TokenSummary` → `TokenList` drops the row type's suffix (`Summary`). `HandoffApprovalResultSchema`'s `Result` is the suffix in the same position (a distribution shape), so drop it: `HandoffApprovalListSchema`. Counterexample 3 "`RecipientDekListSchema` is `deks.listMine` (only those addressed to you), which reads as 'a list of all recipients'" — the name comes from the row type (K8-1's rule). The self-only limitation goes in the JSDoc | **Adopted** |
| 4-b Make `{ expiresAtMs }` 1 shared Schema (e.g. `RequestExpirySchema`) referenced by the devices / key-wraps 2 surfaces | 1 definition | — | — | Counterexample "the meanings differ" — the device-add request's deadline (`DEVICE_ADD_REQUEST_TTL_MS`) and the guardian-handoff request's deadline (`HANDOFF_REQUEST_TTL_MS`) are separate constants in separate spec sections (§13-11 / §13-7), and the day a field is added to only one side's response, the sharing obstructs. The precedent `InviteIssueResultSchema` (the same `{ expiresAtMs }`) isn't shared either. Same shape on the wire, different meaning → different names (consistent with the K9 prompt's instruction) | Rejected |
| 4-c Name them but don't export from `index.ts` (keep the unused ones internal) | The public surface doesn't grow | — | — | Every precedent (`TokenListSchema` / `DeviceListSchema` / `EnvironmentListSchema`) is exported. The day the Web / CLI needs a derived type, a PR just to add the export is needed (a K8-carryover recurrence). fallow's unused export? → round 3 below | Rejected |

**Round 2**: upward compat = separating fetch from creation by the verb (the table). Silver bullet = "derive names mechanically (auto from the endpoint name)" → a Schema's name is a TS identifier, and no derivation mechanism exists in the repository (an invention). Naming can't be mechanized as a check either — it's a prose rule "transcribe the precedents' rule" (same as the K6-V supplement). Counterexamples = 3 (the table). **Round 3**: option tried "exporting makes fallow fail it as an unused export" → the existing `DeviceListSchema` etc. are also the package's public surface via index, which fallow treats as an entry (verified at the quality gate — if it fails, run it at K9-7 onward). Option tried "make `SchemaPolicyResultSchema` `SchemaPolicyGetResultSchema` (always carry the verb)" → the 3 GET precedents (`RecoveryWrapResult` etc.) carry no verb. No new options. **Round 4**: option tried "shorten `DeviceAddRequestCreateResultSchema` to `DeviceAddRequestIssuedSchema`" → inventing a word. No new options. Stopped.

**Principle**: "an envelope's name is read from the precedents' rules (a list = the row's stem + `List`, a fetch = the resource + `Result`, a creation = the resource + the verb + `Result`); same wire shape but different meaning → different names" — K8-1 ✓, K6-R ✓, K4-12 ✓ (these aren't persisted names — type names need no migration), wire unchanged ✓. **Adopted: 4-a**.

### K9-5. The lint's selector, scope, and non-vacuity (B-(2))

| Option | Content | Upward compat | Silver bullet | Counterexamples | Adoption |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 5-a K8's option: `Property[key.name='success'] > CallExpression[callee.object.name='Schema'][callee.property.name='Struct']` (child) on `packages/api-schema/src/**/*.ts` | The matching shape is clear | **Widen 5-a's child combinator to descendant**: `Property[key.name='success'] CallExpression[callee.object.name='Schema'][callee.property.name='Struct']` — wrapped shapes like `Schema.Struct({...}).annotate(...)` / `.pipe(...)` / `Schema.Array(Schema.Struct(...))` also fail (under the child combinator, the outer call's callee is a `MemberExpression` and slips through). A string-keyed `"success":` is caught via `:matches([key.name='success'],[key.value='success'])` (whether oxlint's esquery understands `:matches` is verified at implementation) | — | Counterexample 1 (an input that must fail) "`success: Schema.Struct({...}).annotate({...})`" — the child combinator lets it through → removed by the upward compat. Counterexample 2 (an input that must pass) "`payload: Schema.Struct(...)` / `params:` / `error: [...]` / `HttpApiSchema.NoContent`" — the key isn't `success`, so it doesn't hit (the current code has many `payload: Schema.Struct(` and lint is green — evidence). Counterexample 3 "a fake API in a test file (`packages/api-schema/test/`)" — the glob is `src/`, so it doesn't hit | **Adopted** (with the upward compat) |
| 5-b Fail `success` on anything that isn't a name (`Identifier` / `MemberExpression`) (`Property[key.name='success'] > :not(Identifier, MemberExpression)`) | Forces a name even for inline `Schema.Array(Row)` etc. | — | — | Counterexample "`success: [A, B]` (multiple success types)" fails though it's a legitimate shape → excluding `ArrayExpression` lets an inline Struct inside pass. The owner's B is "an inline `Schema.Struct`"; widening the scope means re-consulting. Currently 0 hits | Rejected (recorded as a known limitation) |
| 5-c A runtime sweep instead of lint (`forEachEndpoint`: is every endpoint's `success` identical to an `index.ts` export) | Checks "named **and** exported" semantically. Glob-independent and can deny its own vacuity (K8-2) | — | Rides the existing sweep mechanism (`sweep.ts`) | **Counterexample (measured)**: `HttpApiEndpoint` puts `success` into a `Set` and makes it a **different instance** with annotations (`httpApiStatus` etc.), so even `DeviceListSchema` doesn't match by identity (verified in scratch — `maruhiApi.groups.devices.endpoints.list.success`: all 69 mismatched). An AST comparison is a dependency on Effect v4's internal forms (same class as the type dependency rejected at K7-8 8-c) | Rejected |
| 5-d Make the glob all TS (`**/*.ts`) and narrow the selector to `CallExpression[callee.object.name='HttpApiEndpoint'] > ObjectExpression > …` (stays non-vacuous even if the package moves) | The glob's vacuity disappears | — | — | Counterexample "oxlint overrides are last-wins per rule" — placing an all-TS override later would overwrite `apps/web` / `apps/cli`'s `no-restricted-syntax`; placing it earlier makes it dead in both directories. Correctness would depend on the overrides' ordering. The gain (resilience to a package move) is a rare event, and a move is a visible PR | Rejected |

**Round 2**: upward compat = the descendant combinator + the string key (the table). Silver bullets = 5-c (rejected by measurement) · 5-d (rejected on ordering dependence). Counterexamples = 1 must-fail input, 2 must-pass inputs (the table). **Round 3**: option tried "as a vacuity check, read `.oxlintrc.json`'s override glob and selector in vitest" → merely puts a transcription of the config's letter in a check (K6-R ✗), and reading JSONC needs a dependency. The lint's vacuity is confirmed by mutation at this PR's point (reverting 1 place to inline fails `bun run lint`) and recorded as a known limitation. **Round 4**: option tried "an alias import (`import { Schema as S }`)" — every api-schema file imports it as `Schema`. A known limitation. No new options. Stopped.

**Principle**: "a check is placed only after confirming on both kinds of inputs that it fails on shapes it must not pass (including wrapped forms) and passes on shapes it must pass (Structs on other keys)" — K8's re-exploration principle ✓, K7-13 ✓ (an existing mechanism = the `no-restricted-syntax` override), K6-T ✓. **Adopted: 5-a (with the upward compat)**, glob `packages/api-schema/src/**/*.ts`.

### K9-6. C (re-requesting with the same key after expiry — OFF) and §1's out-of-scope

C is OFF per §0-bis. Not implemented. K8-6's 6-a stays in the owner points as-is, and K9-1's 1-d (writing the registry row post-deadline from the local record + the chain) is listed alongside it as **the other answer** to "the hole where T3 leaves the registry row missing" (both are post-deadline paths, compared when the owner turns C ON). D onward is written in the PR body as 1 line "separate task".

§1 items judged "no fix needed": (i) the approver's "No pending device-add request" wording — at T3 the approver sees it after the deadline-bearing Note, so the failure wording stays correct for the general case (K9-3 counterexample 4). Only docs :154 gets the condition added. (ii) the requester's "not registered yet" Note's "The request is used up" — emitted only after the signal (a successful PUT), at which point the cancel has run, so it stays true. (iii) the server handler (B) — `success` references the same Struct, so it follows by type (verified by `bun run typecheck`). (iv) `device add --replace`'s help (`golden/help.txt`) — unchanged since C is OFF.

### K9 ruling list (pinned before implementation)

| # | Topic | Adopted | What makes it better than the alternatives | Principle |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| K9-1 | The PUT failure vs the request cancel | 1-a: extract the PUT's result as a value; cancel only on success. The Note carries the deadline and the post-deadline consequence | Removes no recovery path, touches neither the wire nor K4-5, keeps the signal's meaning, doesn't preempt C | If the signal couldn't be sent, don't destroy the material that could re-send it |
| K9-2 | Non-429 failures | 2-a: the same regardless of kind (only the wording differs) | Leaves no 5xx hole, invents no retry policy | The branch is split by success/failure; only the wording looks at the kind |
| K9-3 | What's seen · transcriptions | 3-a: the 2 Notes · the wait's guidance · the expiry wording · `devices.mdx` 5 places · 1 nail · `device.test.ts` | Leaves no falsehood, pulls in no out-of-scope sync, and lets the requester verify the fact via `device list` | If you add 1 branch, count every transcription and align to the same words |
| K9-4 | The envelope names | 4-a: the precedents' 3 rules · a distinct name per surface · exported from index | Zero invention; things with different meanings aren't shared | Names are read from the precedents' rules |
| K9-5 | The lint | 5-a + descendant combinator + string key, `packages/api-schema/src/**/*.ts` | Fails wrapped forms too, doesn't hit other keys' Structs. A runtime sweep can't compare by identity | Confirm on both must-fail and must-pass inputs before placing |
| K9-6 | C (OFF) | Not implemented. 6-a and 1-d to the owner points | — | — |

### Mid-implementation additional rulings (K9-7 onward — enumerated, counterexampled, principled on the spot)

| K9-7 | K9-5's selector's parent (what to limit the `success` Property to) — a counterexample was built by mutation | (a) As K9-5 (a descendant of any `success` key). (b) **Limit it to descendants of `CallExpression[callee.object.name='HttpApiEndpoint'] > ObjectExpression > Property:matches([key.name='success'], [key.value='success'])`**. (c) Narrow the glob to `*-api.ts` | (b) keeps the power to fail wrapped shapes / string keys (K9-5's upward compat) while excluding a data Schema's fields | "Ban writing an endpoint's options out as a named variable" → there are 0 places written that way today; no reason for the rule to exist (an invention) | **A counterexample to (a) (measured — a must-pass input)**: `export const XSchema = Schema.Struct({ success: Schema.Struct({ … }) })` (a data Schema's field named `success` being a Struct) fails under (a). It passes under (b). (c) would hit the same false positive if the same field lands in `data.ts` etc., with correctness depending on the glob's letter. (b)'s known limitation: writing options out as a variable (`HttpApiEndpoint.get("x", "/x", options)`) doesn't hit (currently 0) | **(b)**. Principle "a check's parent matches the claim's subject (the subject is 'an endpoint's response', not 'a key named `success`')" — K9-5's principle ✓ |

K9-7's confirmation (mutations — edits were applied in reverse to revert, and `git diff --stat` confirmed only K9's diff): **the 3 that must fail** = M1 `success: Schema.Struct({ expiresAtMs })` (child) / M2 `success: Schema.Struct(…).annotate({})` (a wrapped shape) / M3 `"success": Schema.Array(Schema.Struct(…))` (string key + nested) each producing 1 `no-restricted-syntax` hit. **The 4 that must pass** = N1 the existing `payload: Schema.Struct(…)` (`key-wraps-api.ts`'s `handoffCreate` and others — lint is green on current code) / N2 `error: [Schema.Struct(…), …]` (0 hits of the rule — the exit-1 was `no-unused-vars` on an import no longer used by the mutation) / N3 a fake endpoint's inline success in `packages/api-schema/test/` (outside the glob — 0 hits) / N4 a data Schema's `success` field (0 hits under K9-7's (b)).

| # | Topic | Options | Upward compat | Silver bullet | Counterexamples | Adoption |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| K9-8 | Correcting K9-1's 1-d counterexample 3 and K9-6's description (re-read 6-a's condition when writing the completion record) | K9-1 put 1-d into the owner points as "plugging the same hole as C (OFF) on a different surface — preempting the owner's OFF", and K9-6 listed 6-a and 1-d as "the other answer to T3's hole". **But 6-a re-creates a request only for a key 'on no project's chain'** (K8-6), so it falls to the refusing side for T3's key (which is on the chain) and doesn't plug T3's hole. C and 1-d are answers to different holes | — | — | Even corrected, the reasons not to adopt 1-d remain: counterexample 1 (the PUT's entry point is the local record — named advisory by §2 / K4-5) and the ceremony command gaining a request-less branch (a sync + an advisory write). **Both are owner calls** (how §2's rule is read), so 1-d goes to the owner points not as "a C alternative" but as **the only option for T3's hole**. K9-1's adoption (1-a) is unchanged (closes every in-TTL path; at T3, the wording prevents the `--replace` mistake) | Correction only. Principle "when citing another ruling's option, re-read that option's predicate (its conditions) (applying K6-X's 3 principles to references inside the design record)" |
