### Additional rulings during review handling (K9-9 onward — responses to PR #196 pullfrog round 1)

- **K9-9 (ℹ️ nit — the docs quotation's letter)**: `devices.mdx` quotes the Note as "… will not see the completion signal. The request is left in place until …", but the 2 sentences are adjacent only in the 429 branch; the generic branch had "; the device is registered on the chains above regardless." in between. Options: (a) make docs' quotation 2 per branch — grows transcriptions (K6-Y). (b) **Swap the generic branch's sentence order so the 2 sentences are adjacent in both branches** ("…; the device is registered on the chains above regardless, but `maruhi device add` on it will not see the completion signal. The request is left in place until …") — the transcription stays 1 and the quotation is verbatim. (c) Drop the first sentence from the quotation — the reader loses the reason "the signal won't come". **Adopted (b)**. Check: `device.test.ts`'s 429 / 500 cases now expect the 2 sentences adjacent (previously they were separate `toContain`s that didn't check adjacency — the check didn't hold the quotation's claim). Principle "a quotation being verbatim across **every** branch of the quoting side is pinned by a check on the quoted side" — K6-Y ✓.
- **Known limitation (pullfrog's info memo — a counterexample I hadn't constructed)**: `deviceApproveOp`, after the project loop and before the PUT, overwrites the local record's (approved) `roleCap` / `scope` with **this invocation's `--cap`**. Since K9-1 made the request remain, a re-run with **a different `--cap`** after a PUT failure widens a window where the chain's cap (immutable — `approveOnProject` returns `already` and doesn't overwrite) and the local record's cap diverge. The local record is the input for display (`maruhi device list`'s "recorded here as approved") and for the first sync's registration candidates (`registerRecorded` — uses that cap when registering to projects not on the chain); authorization reads the chain's state. On already-registered projects the divergence is display-only, but **if a re-run with a different `--cap` also leaves some projects failed**, a later `registerRecorded` may register them under the new cap (a device whose cap differs per project). The overwriting itself predates K9 (it's not this PR's diff), and whether to refuse a `--cap` divergence on re-runs or stop overwriting the record is a separate ruling (the record-writer discipline — K4-3), so this PR doesn't change it and adds it to carryovers.

## 15. K10 addendum (2026-09-24 — collecting DK carryovers: the discipline of the approval record's [approved] cap / the registration path for a device on only some projects)

Collecting what K9 (PR #196) left in §14's K9-9 as a "known limitation" (the divergence between a re-run's `--cap` and the record's cap) and K7's carryover (2) (the direct registration path for a device registered on only some projects). The owner's rulings (K10's prompt §0-bis): **A (the divergence between the approval record's cap and the chain's cap) = ON, B (the registration path for a device on only some projects) = ON, C (K9's T3 hole — K9-1's 1-d) = OFF**. D onward (K5 owner points (1)(2), K2 carryover 4's reinforcement, K7 owner points (3)(4)(5), K8 owner point (2), K9 owner points (2)(3), §11 W-3 / W-4 / W-6) is out of scope. Specs, ADRs, `CLAUDE.md`, `packages/crypto`, the server, wire formats, acceptance-policy values, and `own-devices.json`'s fields are untouched. The table uses K6-X's format (a blank is the mark of "not searched" — fill them before writing "stopped"). Rulings are recorded here before implementation, and mid-implementation judgments are added in the same shape as K10-8 onward.

### K10 fact confirmations A (read the code's branches — K6-X's 3 principles. Read the branches, not the design record's summaries)

1. **The record-writing condition and the fields' sources** (`device.ts` `deviceApproveOp`): ceremony gate → `matchRequest` → the project loop (`approveOnProject`) → **inside** the "≥1 registered / already" gate (K4-31 (c)) runs `store.record`. Field sources: `roleCap` / `scope` = **this invocation's `--cap` / `--env` / `--all-envs` / `--no-envs`** (`effect-cli.ts` builds `{ roleCap, scope }`. Defaults owner / all), `source` = "approved", `label` = the request row's display name, `addedByFingerprintHex` = this device's FP, `observedProjectId` = null, `recordedAtMs` = now, `revokedAtMs` = null. **It does not read the on-chain cap**.
2. **The overwrite's extent in `OwnDeviceStore.record`** (`own-devices.ts`): `merge`'s `{ ...devices, [keyFingerprintHex]: record }` **replaces the row wholesale**. Every field — `source` (observed → approved) · `revokedAtMs` (→ null) · `label` · `addedByFingerprintHex` · `observedProjectId` (→ null) · `recordedAtMs` — becomes this invocation's value ("A re-approval overwrites a revoked row" is an instance of this property).
3. **What the record's cap is used for** (`device-sync.ts` `registerRecorded`): `capOfRecord(candidate)` → `capWithinSignerCap(cap, own)` (if it exceeds, a Note and nothing is done) → `requireScopeEnvironmentsExist` → `appendAddDevice({ candidate: { …, cap } })`. The record's cap **becomes the `add_device` payload's cap as-is**.
4. **An observation record's cap** (`observeDevices`): only when there's no record (`known === undefined`) is "observed" written with the observed chain's `device.roleCap` / `device.scope`. **An existing row isn't overwritten** (an approved row is never corrected by an observation).
5. **`device list`'s display** (`describeListRow`): from the record it shows only the provenance ("recorded here as approved" / "recorded here as revoked") and **doesn't show the cap**. A cap is shown only by the chain's rows (`collectChainRows`' `cap=`). A grep (`capOfRecord` / `.roleCap` / `.scope` in `apps/cli/src`) shows **the only reader of the record's cap is `registerRecorded`** (excluding `own-devices.ts`'s encode / decode and the writers in `device.ts` / `reserve.ts` / `device-sync.ts`).
6. **Correction of K9-9's description (an addendum — the K9-8 precedent)**: §14's K9-9 known limitation wrote "on already-registered projects the divergence is display-only" and "the local record is the input for display (`maruhi device list`'s 'recorded here as approved')…", but per 5 above `device list` doesn't display the record's cap. On already-registered projects **there's no visible divergence** (the divergence works only when `registerRecorded` adds to an unregistered project).
7. **Where the divergence arises (every branch that builds the set — K7-15)**: the record overwrite runs when "≥1 registered / already". An overwritten cap can differ from the chain's only on a re-run when "**the request is alive and the key is already on ≥1 project**". The branches that create that state are these 4:
   - (i) **A re-run after the registry PUT failed** (since K9-1 the request remains). And since K9's Note guides with **no flags** — "re-run `maruhi device approve` before then" — someone who approved with `--cap member --env dev` and types what the Note says overwrites with the default owner / all (**the widening direction**).
   - (ii) **An interrupted approval** (Ctrl+C mid-loop — no record, no PUT, no cancel runs). docs :67 guides "re-run `maruhi device approve` … it skips the projects that already have the device".
   - (iii) **A race between 2 approving devices** (approve on another device before the request's cancel). Records are per-device, so each device's record holds its own `--cap`.
   - (iv) Approve with `--project P1` → while the request remains via (i) or (ii), `--project P2` with a different `--cap`.
   - "Re-approving a revoked device" isn't a divergence case: the revoked key is on no chain (`duplicate-member-key` looks only at the current member set, so re-adding is possible per consensus rules) and doesn't go `already`. And a revoked device loses its registry row while the key stays in the keychain, so `device add` is refused (K4-21 (c)) and no request can be made — a re-approval is actually a `--replace` new key.
8. **A path where a re-run directly creates divergent caps (one K9-9 didn't write)**: on a re-run after (ii)'s interruption there's no record, so `approveOnProject` **appends directly with this invocation's `--cap`** to unregistered projects — bypassing `registerRecorded`, a single command produces a device whose cap differs per project. On a (i) re-run, `approveOnProject` opens each project via `openProject` (the keyed prelude), so `syncOwnDevices` → `registerRecorded` **first** adds them **with the previous record's cap**, and the loop sees `already`. Then the record is overwritten with this invocation's cap — later new projects (e.g. after an invitation) get this invocation's cap.

### K10 fact confirmations B (every branch that builds the "on only some projects" state, and the path that recovers each)

**The paths' preconditions (the predicates were read)**: 
- (R1) The approver's "approved" record is picked up by `registerRecorded`. Preconditions = the record exists and isn't revoked / the device is **that project's** on-chain own device (`own !== undefined`) / the ceremony gate (`registrationAllowed` — TTY + non-agent) / `capWithinSignerCap` / the environments exist / the server's acceptance. **It launches from a keyed prelude (`openProjectWith`), and a prelude is 1 command 1 project** (`resolveProjectId(flags.project, config)`) — registration on missing project Q happens only on a keyed command **targeting Q** (`maruhi pull` opens only the default project). The exceptions are `device approve` and `key reserve rotate`, which open every project via `openProject`.
- (R2) Another device's observation → registration. Since `observeDevices` runs **before** the `own` check, even a device not on that project's chain records the key as "observed" (with the chain's cap) once a keyed command syncs it. Thereafter a keyed command targeting Q registers it under the same preconditions as R1.
- (R3) `key recover` (`registerDeviceWithReserve`): adds **this device** as owner / all on projects where the reserve key sits. Doesn't add other devices. `--resume` backfills even registered ones.
- (R4) Re-running `device approve`: `matchRequest` requires a live request. The request is cancelled once the PUT succeeds, and the requester's `device add` is either key + registry row → `already-registered` (no request made) or key + neither → refused (K4-21), so **a request for a key whose signal has fired can't be re-created**. R4 is a path that exists only while the request is alive (total failure · interruption · PUT failure).

| The "on only some" branch | The recovering path (preconditions) | Unrecovered? |
|---|---|---|
| skipped: the approver isn't a member | After accepting the invitation, the approver's R1 (a keyed command targeting Q). If the accepting device is a different one, that device's R2 | Recovered |
| skipped: the approver's device isn't on that chain | The R2 of a different device M that's on Q (after M syncs P1 and observes the key, a keyed command targeting Q) | Not recovered if M's cap doesn't cover (U1 below) |
| skipped: cap exceeded | The R2 of another device whose cap covers | If no covering device is on Q → U1 |
| failed: the append failed (`DeviceLimit` · network · a missing environment) | The approver's R1 after the cause is removed | Recovered |
| `--project` | The approver's R1 (a keyed command targeting other projects adds it automatically) | Recovered (whether adding it against intent is acceptable is K10-7's known limitation) |
| A project joined later (after accepting an invitation) | The accepting device's R1 / R2 | Recovered |
| An interrupted approval (no record) | R4 while the request is alive. After the deadline, each device's — including the approver's own — R2 (syncing a project the key is on observes it) | Recovered |
| `key recover`'s reserve-missing / failed | For this device, `--resume` (R3). For projects without the reserve key, R2 | Same condition as U1 |
| Revoking just one via `device revoke --project` | An intended state. The revocation's observation makes the record revoked and no device adds it back (K4-3 counterexample 1) | By design |

- **U1 (the only branch that isn't recovered)**: of your own devices on Q, none covers that key's cap. Then **no command** can add it to Q (`add_device`'s signing device must be at or above the new device's cap — consensus rule `device-cap-exceeded`, principle D2). Adding a direct command doesn't close it (closing it needs either adding at a lower cap — against A's "a device has 1 cap" — or changing a consensus rule — both out of scope).
- **Confirming K7 carryover (2)'s claim**: "a device with no record has no direct command" — it's not that the path doesn't exist; the path (R2) is invisible. A recordless device also gets a record by observation once it syncs a project the key is on. "A person with only non-covering devices" is U1, not a command problem.
- **Among the guidance wordings, there are unfollowable procedures** (counted transcriptions of the same claim by grep — the premise "you can `device approve` without a request"): (1) `approveOnProject`'s skipped "approve this machine first from a device that is" (`device.ts`), (2) `deviceNotOnChainMessage` "From a device that is registered, run `maruhi device approve` for this machine" (`device-key.ts` — followable only while a waiting `device add` exists), (3) `observeDevices`'s "if it should be active again, approve it explicitly with `maruhi device approve`" (`device-sync.ts` — a device marked revoked can't make a request), (4) `devices.mdx` :158's quotation and explanation of (1). **Transcriptions of a different premise** ("a `device approve` re-run completes the backfill" — `approveOnProject`'s `already` calls no backfill): `reportApproveOutcome`'s "Re-run `maruhi device approve` for this device to complete it", `key-recover.ts`'s "or have a registered device run `maruhi device approve` for this machine", `pull.ts`'s "re-run `maruhi device approve` for it … that backfills the missing epochs". The latter 3 are a behavior hole (outside `key recover --resume`, there's no surface that retries a registered device's backfill) — outside B's scope (the registration path), so to a separate task (K10-6).
- **Transcriptions of "the next keyed command"** (the premise "any command picks it up" — in reality it's **a command targeting that project**): `device.ts`'s "not registered yet" Note "on its next keyed command run at a terminal", `registrationAllowed`'s Note "Run any keyed maruhi command (for example `maruhi pull`)", `devices.mdx` :59 "picked up by the next keyed command run at a terminal" · :63 "`maruhi pull` on a device whose cap covers them" · :158 "on its next keyed command at a terminal" · :163 "Run any keyed command in a terminal".

### K10-1. When a re-run's `--cap` differs from the chain's cap (A-(1))

| Option | Content | Upward compat | Silver bullet | Counterexamples | Adoption |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1-a **Refuse**: if the key is already on any target project's chain and its cap differs from this invocation's, **append nothing**, record nothing, and fail leaving the request in place (showing the on-chain cap and the command to re-type with it) | Changes nothing silently (fail-closed). 1 predicate ("this cap = the on-chain cap") closes all of branches (i)–(iv). The record's cap matches the chain's (K10-3). Also closes branch 8's "divergent caps within 1 command" | **1-a + emit the command to re-type**: the refusal prints `maruhi device approve <fp> --cap … --env …` (with `--project` if given) verbatim, and K9's PUT-failure Note's "re-run" becomes the same command — typing what the Note says can't be refused (removes branch (i)'s "widening with no flags" at the entrance) | 1-f / 1-g (below) | Counterexample 1 "someone who follows the Note and types with no flags" — the upward compat makes the Note emit the flags. Even someone who saw an old CLI's Note (no flags) converges in 1 round-trip since the refusal prints the right command. Counterexample 2 "a device whose on-chain cap already differs per project (made by a pre-K10 re-run)" — every `--cap` diverges from some, refused forever → make the refusal 2 forms; when they differ, print "one by one with `--project`, at that project's cap". Counterexample 3 "after an interruption, a user re-types wanting to change the cap" — the cap is immutable (docs :50). The refusal says "revoke and re-add instead" (same words as docs). Counterexample 4 "someone with only narrow-cap devices re-runs at the owner default" — refused (the chain's cap is shown). Even unrefused, it'd land at `capWithinSignerCap` skipped — the conclusion points the same way | **Adopted** (with the upward compat) |
| 1-b Don't overwrite the record (if an approved row exists, keep its cap) | The record's cap stays at the first approval's | — | — | Counterexample 1 "a re-run after branch 8's interruption" — unpreventable since there's no record; `approveOnProject` appends directly with this cap (half the divergence remains). Counterexample 2 "the don't-overwrite decision's input is the record's (advisory's) existing value" — touches K4-5. Counterexample 3 "whether to overwrite an observation row (observed)" adds a branch | Rejected |
| 1-c Take the chain's cap (ignore this `--cap`; append and record at the chain's cap; a Note if they differ) | A re-run always passes | — | — | Counterexample "someone who typed `--cap reader` wanting it narrower" — if the Note is missed, they don't notice it landed as owner on the remaining projects. Silently replacing a value a human typed (the fail-open direction). Diverges from both docs' "the cap is fixed at approval" and "the flags fix the cap" | Rejected |
| 1-d Allow per-project divergent caps and show it (show the record's cap in `device list`) | Behavior unchanged | — | — | Counterexample "typing with no flags per K9's Note" — the display doesn't stop it from adding owner / all to unregistered projects (the widening direction). The display is after the fact; registration is before | Rejected |
| 1-e Only when the cap flags are **omitted** on a re-run, take the chain's cap; if explicit, 1-a | Zero friction for someone following the Note | — | Read "omitted = continue" (re-frames the problem) | Counterexample 1 "help says `--cap`'s default is owner" (golden) — omitting means different things by state and help becomes false. Counterexample 2 "someone who typed `--cap owner` explicitly and someone who omitted it get different results" — 2 meanings for the same value. 1-a's upward compat (the Note emits the flags) gets the same friction reduction without adding a meaning | Rejected |
| 1-f **Silver bullet**: stop writing the approval record (approved), leaving only observation (the chain's cap) — the record's cap matches the chain's by structure | The divergence can't arise in principle | — | (this column is the silver bullet) | Counterexample 1 "right after approving, the device gets the Note 'observed your device … It was not approved from this machine; … revoke it'" — K4-4's visibility signal fires on one's own approval (crying wolf). Counterexample 2 "the approver holds no record until it syncs a project the key is on" — the `--project` / skipped recovery (R1) falls 1 step behind. Counterexample 3 retracting K4-3's writer (2) = changing an existing ruling (an owner's call) | Rejected |
| 1-g Silver bullet 2: remove the re-run window itself (if the key is on anything, `device approve` always refuses) | The divergence can't arise | — | (this column is the silver bullet) | Counterexample "K9-1's recovery (the re-run after a PUT failure) and docs :67's post-interruption re-run become unusable" — breaks K9's adoption | Rejected |

**Round 2**: upward compat = emitting the command to re-type (the table). Silver bullets = 1-f (retracting the writer) · 1-g (removing the window), both constructed and rejected on counterexamples. Counterexamples = 4 for 1-a (counterexample 2 produced the "2 forms of refusal"), 3 for 1-b, 1–2 each for 1-c / 1-d / 1-e. **Round 3**: option tried "instead of refusing, skip the diverging projects and continue the rest" → appends this cap to unregistered projects (branch 8 remains). Option tried "make the refusal a yes confirmation ('the chain's cap is X. Add the rest at this run's Y?')" — a yes would proceed to make a device whose caps differ per project in a single command. There's no reason to allow that (to change the cap — revoke and re-add, docs :50). No new options. **Round 4**: option tried "put the refusal's check inside `approveOnProject` (per project)" → the ordering counterexample: visit unregistered P2 first, append at this cap, then notice the divergence at registered P1. **Open every project, judge, then append** (K10-4). This is 1-a's implementation shape, not a new option. No new options. Stopped.

**Principle**: "**a re-run is a continuation of the first approval, and doesn't change a cap already written on a chain. Given an input for something unchangeable, stop having written nothing**" — CRYPTO_SPEC §1 ✓ (consensus rules are immutable — only a pre-send check on the CLI is added), DK-A ✓ (a cap is a device's nature, not re-decided per approval), DK-D ✓ (the comparison's counterpart is the chain — the truth), K4-3 ✓ (the writers stay at 3 paths. The cap the approved writer writes now matches the cap of the chains it touched), K4-5 ✓ (the judgment's input is the chain. The record's existing value isn't read), K4-12 ✓ (the fields are unchanged), K4-31 ✓ (the refusal precedes appending — the latter stage's order unchanged), K6-R ✓ / K6-Y ✓ (the refusal and its docs transcriptions are pinned by K10-5's check), K9-1 ✓ (the refusal leaves the request — doesn't destroy the material that could re-send the signal), ADR-0017 ✓. **Adopted: 1-a (with the upward compat)**. What makes it superior: it also closes the recordless branch (an interruption) unlike 1-b and keeps advisories out of judgment inputs; unlike 1-c it doesn't silently replace a human's input; unlike 1-d it stops a widening-direction registration beforehand; unlike 1-e it keeps help's default meaning single; unlike 1-f / 1-g it doesn't break existing rulings (K4-3's writer, K9-1's recovery). **Exit code**: existing branches' exit codes don't change. The new refusal takes the same shape as the existing pre-append refusals (`That request carries this machine's own key` etc. — `cliError` = 1).

### K10-2. Which projects' chain caps are authoritative (A-(2))

| Option | Content | Upward compat | Silver bullet | Counterexamples | Adoption |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2-a **The chains of the projects this approval visits (those in `--project` if given, else all of membership)**. Requires every on-chain cap of the key to equal this invocation's cap. If even 1 differs, refuse and list every project carrying it with its cap | No extra syncs (approval opens these anyway). The refusal shows "where, at which cap" — all of them | If the carried caps are 1 kind, "re-type at that cap"; if 2+ kinds (a state made by a pre-K10 re-run), "one by one with `--project`, at that project's cap" — either way there's a next move | — | Counterexample 1 (branch (iv)) "with `--project P2`, the unvisited P1 carries it at a different cap" — undetected. But `--project` is a user's explicit project choice, and giving P1 and P2 different caps can be intended (each chain is per-project independent and the consensus rules allow it). The record ends up with the last-approved cap, and `registerRecorded` registers at that cap thereafter → via K10-3's upward compat (the registration Note shows the cap), it's visible at the moment it works. Recorded in K10-7 as a known limitation. Counterexample 2 "a visited project's sync fails" — same as `approveOnProject`, it goes `failed` (unusable for judgment). Nothing is appended to that project, so no divergence is created | **Adopted** (with the upward compat) |
| 2-b All membership projects' chains (even with `--project`, read the unvisited projects via a keyless prelude) | Detects branch (iv) too | — | — | Counterexample 1 "a `--project` approval costs N syncs" — an operation the user narrowed to 1 costs N×. Counterexample 2 "stop the approval on an unrelated project's sync failure (fail-closed), or proceed without reading it (a judgment hole)" — whichever way it falls, worse than 2-a. Counterexample 3 "refuses a `--project` approval intended for a different cap" | Rejected |
| 2-c Take the cap in the existing record (approved / observed) as authoritative | No sync needed | — | — | The record is advisory (K4-5 — not a judgment input). On the recordless branch (an interruption) there's nothing to compare against. The record's cap itself may already diverge from a pre-K9 overwrite (K10's very problem) | Rejected |
| 2-d Take the cap at the key's "first registration" (the earliest `add_device` by seq / time across all projects' chains) as authoritative | Closest to the meaning of "the first approval" | — | — | Times can't be compared across projects (each chain's timestamps are self-declared). Same N syncs as 2-b. The gain (which cap to recommend) is chosen by the human via 2-a's upward compat (list them all) | Rejected |

**Round 2**: upward compat = splitting the refusal by 1 kind vs 2+ kinds (the table). Silver bullet = "remove what to compare against" = K10-1's 1-f / 1-g (already rejected). Counterexamples = 2 for 2-a · 3 for 2-b · 2-c / 2-d (the table). **Round 3**: option tried "also compare projects where the key is carried but this device (the signer) isn't on that chain (skipped)" → compare them. The carried cap is a chain fact regardless of the signer's presence; nothing is appended to that project, but it isn't excluded from the divergence report (excluding it would make the 2-kind judgment wrong). No new options. **Round 4**: option tried "detect a race (branch (iii) — another device appends at a different cap between the judgment and the append) via a post-append re-sync and stop it" → by the time it's detected, the appends to the other projects are done and can't be stopped. In the rare case where 2 people (2 devices) approve the same request simultaneously, each device's record holds its own cap. A known limitation (K10-7). No new options. Stopped.

**Principle**: "**the comparison's counterpart is only the chains this operation touches** (don't stop on a chain you don't touch)" — DK-D ✓, K4-5 ✓, K10-1's principle ✓. **Adopted: 2-a (with the upward compat)**. What makes it superior: it carries neither 2-b / 2-d's N× syncs nor dependence on unrelated failures, and unlike 2-c it doesn't take an advisory as input.

### K10-3. The meaning of the record's cap (A-(3)) and making its moment of effect visible

| Option | Content | Upward compat | Silver bullet | Counterexamples | Adoption |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 3-a **The record's cap = "the cap this key holds on the chains" (the input for registration)**. With K10-1's refusal, the value an approved writer writes matches the cap of the chains that approval touched | Consistent with K4-3's "the writers only transcribe cryptographic facts" (a transcription of a fact — approved, or observed on a chain). Also consistent with how the reader (`registerRecorded`) uses it (the payload's cap) | **Show the cap in the Note at the record's cap's only moment of effect (`registerRecorded`'s registration)** ("registered your device … with cap member/dev on project …") — even on a device whose record's cap already diverges (a pre-K10 state · K10-7's known limitation), the cap it registered with is visible at that moment | — | Counterexample 1 "a record overwritten by a pre-K10 CLI (a divergence already exists)" — K10-1 stops new overwrites but doesn't repair existing records. With the upward compat's Note, it's visible at its moment of effect (to fix it, `device revoke` — no record-editing surface is created). Counterexample 2 "an old CLI and a new CLI share the same `own-devices.json`" — the fields are unchanged and only the meaning is re-read, so both directions can read it. An old CLI's re-run can still overwrite (an old CLI's behavior can't be fixed — a known limitation) | **Adopted** (with the upward compat) |
| 3-b The record's cap = "the intent at approval time" | Honest as an approval history | — | — | The reader uses it not as intent but as the input for registration (fact check 3). If the intent differs from the chain, the intent propagates to later projects — K10's very problem | Rejected |
| 3-c Show the record's cap in `device list` (noted only when it differs from the chain) | Visible beforehand | — | — | The display's fields and vocabulary grow, and post-K10-1 they normally match and show nothing. The moment-of-effect Note (3-a's upward compat) suffices. It would change `device list` row vocabulary (the 3 provenances — docs :78–82) | Rejected |

**Round 2**: upward compat = the cap in the registration Note (the table). Silver bullet = "remove the cap from the record and read it from the chain at registration time" → `registerRecorded` holds only the target chain (no key), and reading another chain the key is on costs N syncs (the same counterexample as K10-2's 2-b). The record's cap exists to "carry a fact seen on another chain" (the same as K4-3 c-4's rejection reason). Counterexamples = 2 for 3-a (the table). **Round 3**: option tried "the observation writer (observed) also corrects an existing approved row's cap against the chain" → overwriting an existing record row on observation splits the handling of the revocation mark, the provenance, and the display name (K4-3's revoked discipline), and the question "which chain's cap to correct against" (K10-2) lands on the observation side too. Since K10-1 makes no new divergence arise, no surface for existing divergences is created. No new options. **Round 4**: option tried "add a cap-provenance field (`capSource`) to the record" → adding a field needs confirming the old CLI's strict decode (does `decodeEntry` ignore unknown fields), and the gain is already met by 3-a's upward compat. No new options. Stopped.

**Principle**: "**an advisory field is written with the meaning the judgment that reads it requires** (since the record's cap's only reader is registration, its meaning is 'the on-chain cap')" — K4-3 ✓, K4-5 ✓ (the record becomes no new judgment input — only the existing use [K4-3's writer (2) → `registerRecorded`]), K4-12 ✓ (neither the field's name nor its form changes). **Adopted: 3-a (with the upward compat)**.

### K10-4. Where the refusal's judgment lives (the implementation shape)

| Option | Content | Upward compat | Silver bullet | Counterexamples | Adoption |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 4-a **2 phases**: phase 1 opens every project to be visited (`openProject` — the same 1 pass as today) and collects each project's resolution (skipped / already / failed), "to be appended", and — if the key is carried — its cap. Before phase 2, judge per K10-1 / K10-2; on divergence, append nothing and fail. If it passes, append + backfill only the "to be appended" projects in the contexts phase 1 opened | The sync count is unchanged (the contexts are reused). The judgment precedes all appends | The resolution branches' order (non-member → no signing device → already → cap exceeded → missing environment) moves to phase 1 in `approveOnProject`'s current order (the output's wording and order unchanged) | — | Counterexample 1 "phase 1's `openProject` runs `syncOwnDevices` and may add the same key from the previous record" — the same side effect as today (it adds at the previous record's cap). After it's added, phase 1's judgment sees "carried" (judged after the sync, so correct). Counterexample 2 "another device appends between phase 1 and phase 2 (a race)" — `appendAddDevice` doesn't append an existing one and returns `appended: false` (the same already as today). No cap detection (K10-2 round 4's known limitation). Counterexample 3 "a context opened in phase 1 is stale in phase 2" — `appendAddDevice` re-syncs on CAS (same as today) | **Adopted** (with the upward compat) |
| 4-b Read every project beforehand via a keyless prelude (`openMetadataProject`), judge, then the current loop | Doesn't change today's `approveOnProject` | — | — | 2× the syncs (N + N). The time between judgment and append grows | Rejected |
| 4-c Judge inside the loop (per project) | Minimal change | — | — | The ordering counterexample (K10-1 round 4) — visit an unregistered project first and append before noticing | Rejected |

**Round 2**: upward compat = preserving the resolution branches' order (the table). Silver bullet = "reorder the projects to 'registered first'" — whether it's registered isn't known until it's opened (there's no sort key). Counterexamples = 3 for 4-a (the table). **Round 3**: option tried "split phase 1's results by type into 'resolved' and 'awaiting append'" → that is 4-a's way of writing. No new options. **Round 4**: option tried "include phase-1 failures (can't open) in the judgment (stop everything fail-closed)" → today's discipline doesn't stop on 1 failure (folds to `failed`). Nothing is appended to a project that can't be opened, so no divergence is created. No new options. Stopped.

**Principle**: "**a stop judgment is completed entirely before the writes**" — K4-31 ✓ (a generalization of the ordering discipline), K10-1 ✓. **Adopted: 4-a (with the upward compat)**.

### K10-5. B — add a direct registration path, or align the guidance to the existing paths

| Option | Content | Upward compat | Silver bullet | Counterexamples | Adoption |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 5-a **Add no surface; align the unfollowable guidance to the real paths**: per fact check B, the only unrecovered branch is U1 (no command can add it); every other is closed by R1 / R2. What's missing isn't a path but wording that names the path. Remove the "run `device approve` with no request" procedure from (1) the skipped wording (`approveOnProject`), (2) `deviceNotOnChainMessage`, (3) `observeDevices`'s revocation-mark Note, and say R1 / R2 instead. (4) Change "the next keyed command" to "a keyed command **targeting that project**" (e.g. `maruhi pull --project <id>`) — `device.ts`'s Note · `registrationAllowed`'s Note · `devices.mdx`'s 4 places | No surface grows. The wording matches the predicates (`openProjectWith` is 1 project / `observeDevices` runs before the `own` check / `matchRequest` requires a live request) | Put R2's precondition "having synced a project the key is on once" into the wording (a recordless device can tell what to do) | "Drop onto an existing mechanism" = the existing sync path (`syncOwnDevices`) itself is the direct path. No new surface is needed | Counterexample 1 "someone with only narrow-cap devices" — U1. The wording states the condition "a device whose cap covers"; that none covers is verifiable in `device list` (the chain's cap). Counterexample 2 "agent environments / non-interactive" — R1 / R2 are inside the ceremony gate (`registrationAllowed`'s Note already says so). Counterexample 3 "someone who meant to narrow to 1 with `--project`" — R1 adds it to the other projects too (existing behavior — docs :59 already says it, in the sentence after "or you approved with `--project`"). Behavior unchanged (K10-7's known limitation) | **Adopted** (with the upward compat) |
| 5-b Pick it up by re-running an existing command: even if `device approve <fp>` finds no request, if it lands as your own device on the verified chain, add it to the missing projects at that cap (K10-2) (inside the ceremony gate. No registry row is written) | All projects in 1 command. Independent of records — the input is the chain (the truth) | — | Widen "the request = the approval's input" to "an on-chain registration = the approval's input" | Counterexample 1 "the same entrance as C (K9-1's 1-d — a request-less approve writing the registry row)" — even in a form that doesn't write the registry row, it grows a request-less branch on the ceremony command and preemptively builds the entrance of the C the owner set to OFF. When C is turned ON it narrows the owner's room to choose the entrance's form (write the registry / don't). Counterexample 2 "R1 / R2 already do the same" — the gain is only the convenience of "all projects in 1 command"; no extra branch is closed (U1 isn't closed). Counterexample 3 "the FP match's meaning changes" — approving a request is the ceremony of 'comparing with the new machine's screen' (K7-7); in the request-less branch there's no screen to compare | Rejected (listed in K10's owner points as material for when C is turned ON) |
| 5-c A new subcommand (e.g. `device sync`) that opens every project and runs R1 / R2 | All projects in 1 command | — | — | A surface grows (§1 B "after showing it can't be closed by an existing command's re-run or a sync path" — it can). Same as running `maruhi pull --project <id>` per project | Rejected |
| 5-d Widen the sync path (on every keyed command, open every project and register) | The user does nothing | — | — | A keyed command costs N× (every `maruhi run` syncs every project). Changes the command's meaning (1 project) | Rejected |
| 5-e Fix only docs | No CLI diff | — | — | The CLI's wording stays an unfollowable procedure (K6-J ✗). People read the screen's wording, not docs | Rejected |

**Round 2**: upward compat = R2's precondition in the wording (the table). Silver bullets = the existing sync path (the table — 5-a's silver-bullet column) · 5-b (widening the request's meaning — rejected). Counterexamples = 3 for 5-a · 3 for 5-b · 5-c / 5-d / 5-e (the table). **Round 3**: option tried "make the skipped wording show the concrete command for registering missing projects (`maruhi pull --project <id>`)" → adopt (goes into 5-a's (1) wording — a way of writing, not a new option). Option tried "make `observeDevices`'s revocation Note 'revoke and re-add'" → for a revocation-marked device, if you want it on other projects too the only path is re-adding with a new key (the record's mark is cleared only by re-approval, and that device can't make a request — fact check A's 7). The same words as docs :50, "revoke and re-add". No new options. **Round 4**: option tried "also fix the 3 backfill re-run guidances in the same PR" → a hole of a different predicate (`already` doesn't call backfill); fixing it needs designing a surface that retries backfill for an already-registered device (e.g. backfilling even on `approveOnProject`'s `already`) — a behavior change outside B's scope. To a separate task (K10-6). No new options. Stopped.

**Principle**: "**guidance names only the paths the predicates permit. If a path already exists, don't add a surface — transcribe the path's preconditions (target, order, cap) into the wording**" — K6-X's 3 principles ✓ (a recovery procedure reads the recovery path's own preconditions), K7-13 ✓ (an existing mechanism = the sync path), K4-5 ✓ (no new judgment input), DK-D ✓, K6-J ✓, K9-1 ✓ (doesn't preempt C's entrance), ADR-0017 ✓. **Adopted: 5-a (with the upward compat)**. What makes it superior: it closes the same branches without growing a surface or an entrance unlike 5-b / 5-c (no option closes U1), changes no cost unlike 5-d, and makes the screen's wording true unlike 5-e. **Relation to C**: since B makes no request-less `device approve`, C's (OFF) entrance stays as it was at K9.

### K10-6. The wording's placement, the transcription count, and the check's placement

- **The transcription count (counted by grep)**: A's claim "a re-run requires the same cap" = the new refusal (`device.ts`) · K9's PUT-failure Note's "re-run" in 2 branches (`device.ts`) · `devices.mdx` :57 (the cap paragraph) · :65 (when it couldn't be listed in the registry) · :67 (the interruption) · What can go wrong (quoting the refusal — new). B's claims = the last 2 items of fact check B at 4 places + 6 places. **The 3 backfill re-run guidances** (`reportApproveOutcome` / `key-recover.ts` / `pull.ts`) are a different predicate's hole, so they're not fixed in this PR and are proposed as a separate task (1 line in the PR body and carryovers).
- **The check's placement**: grepped for a check holding the same claim before placing — `cli-vocabulary.test.ts`'s K9 nail (the Note's 2 sentences) pins **for 1 wording** that "docs' quotation exists in the CLI's wording". No check pins that What-can-go-wrong quotations (the `- **\`…\`**` form) exist in the CLI's wording (it was outside K6-T's scope).

| Option | Content | Upward compat | Silver bullet | Counterexamples | Adoption |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 6-a Add K9-style nails per changed wording (the refusal · the skipped wording) | Per the precedent | — | — | The nails grow by the number of wordings. Untouched quotations (the other 8 What-can-go-wrong items) stay unpinned — the K9-9 shape (a quotation not verbatim) could recur for the next person changing a wording | Rejected |
| 6-b **Silver bullet: collect all of `devices.mdx`'s What-can-go-wrong quotations (`- **\`…\`**`) and pin, in 1 check, that each fragment cut on `…` exists in `apps/cli/src/*.ts` wording (minus backquotes)** | Checks the quotation form itself (K6-T). The wordings K10 changed and the ones it didn't are pinned by the same net | Denying vacuity (K8-2): the same check confirms ≥10 collected quotations and that a fabricated quotation fails | (this column is the silver bullet) | Counterexample 1 (a must-pass input) "a wording where the CLI interpolates a value (`countNoun`'s '1 device key' / '2 device keys')" — today's :163 quotation "device key(s) recorded on this machine (…)" has no fragment in the source → fix the quotation's ellipsis placement to quote only verbatim fragments (a fix in the direction of not falsifying quotations). Counterexample 2 (a must-fail input) "a reworded quotation" — no fragment exists in the source; fails (confirmed by mutation). Counterexample 3 "other pages' (environment-scopes / four-eyes) quotations are value-filled examples (`production` · `(32)`)" — not verbatim quotations, so they don't enter the net (the target is `devices.mdx` only — the page K10 touches, where the K9-9 shape surfaced). Counterexample 4 "a fragment so short it coincidentally matches (`on`)" — set a minimum fragment length (12 chars); shorter fragments aren't counted. Vacuity is denied by the quotation count and the fabricated quotation | **Adopted** (with the upward compat) |
| 6-c Stop quoting and write docs in plain prose | No check needed | — | — | Readers search docs by the screen's wording (the What-can-go-wrong section's purpose). Quotations are a docs feature | Rejected |

**Round 2**: upward compat = denying vacuity (the table). Silver bullet = 6-b. Counterexamples = 1 must-pass · 1 must-fail · 1 scope · 1 coincidental-match (the table). **Round 3**: option tried "put K9's nail (the Note's quotation — `("… will not see the completion signal. The request is left in place until …")` inside prose) into 6-b's net too" → an in-prose quotation has no fixed form (parens + double quotes). K9's nail stays as-is (not replaced — it pins a quotation of a different form). No new options. **Round 4**: option tried "widen the net to every doc in the repository" → counterexample 3 (value-filled examples) would need an exclusion list. Other pages have no instance of that error shape to justify widening. No new options. Stopped.

**Principle**: "**when the same-shaped transcriptions are many, don't nail each — cast a net over the shape itself. The net denies vacuity and coincidental matches itself**" — K6-T ✓, K6-Y ✓ (everywhere a transcription exists), K8-2 ✓, K7-13 ✓ (placed inside the existing check file `cli-vocabulary.test.ts`), K6-R ✓. **Adopted: 6-b (with the upward compat)**.

### K10-7. C (OFF) and the known limitations (pinned before implementation)

- **C is not implemented**. K9-1's 1-d (performing only the registry PUT, request-less, from the approving device's local record + the verified chain) stays in the owner points as-is. K10-5's 5-b (adding to missing projects via a request-less approve) is the same entrance with a different use — added to the owner points as material to compare when C is turned ON.
- **Known limitations (A)**: (1) with `--project`, a device carried on an unvisited project at a different cap (branch (iv)) isn't detected (K10-2 counterexample 1 — possibly intended). The record ends up with the last-approved cap, and `registerRecorded` registers at that cap thereafter (the cap appears in the Note). (2) A race between 2 approvers (branch (iii)) isn't detected (K10-2 round 4). (3) A divergent record overwritten by a pre-K10 CLI isn't repaired (K10-3 counterexample 1 — visible via the Note). (4) An old CLI's re-run can still overwrite.
- **Known limitations (B)**: (1) U1 (a project with no cap-covering device) can't be closed by any command (a consensus rule). (2) Even narrowed to 1 via `--project`, the approver's record adds it the next time it opens other projects (R1 — existing behavior; docs :59 says it after "or you approved with `--project`"). Whether to let the record carry the "narrowing" intent is a behavior change and a separate ruling.
- **§1 items judged "no fix needed"**: (i) overwriting the record's `source` / `label` / `addedByFingerprintHex` / `recordedAtMs` (a re-run turns observed → approved, adder = this device) — `describeListRow` shows only the provenance, and `addedByFingerprintHex` isn't displayed from the record (`device list`'s "added by" is the chain's `deviceProvenanceOf`) and doesn't enter the reader's judgment. K4-3's "re-approval overwrites" stays. (ii) re-approving a revoked device — per fact check A's 7 it doesn't go `already`; not a divergence case. (iii) the total-failure Warning "re-run `maruhi device approve` with the same fingerprint" — the key is on no chain, so no cap can be refused. No flag needs emitting. (iv) help golden — neither a flag nor a subcommand is added (K10-5).

### K10 ruling list (pinned before implementation)

| # | Topic | Adopted | What makes it better than the alternatives | Principle |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| K10-1 | A re-run's `--cap` differs from the chain's | 1-a: refuse before appending (write nothing, leave the request). The refusal and K9's Note emit the command to re-type (`--cap` / `--env` / `--project`) | Closes the recordless interruption too, takes no advisory as input, doesn't silently replace a human's input, keeps help's default meaning, breaks no existing ruling | A re-run continues the first approval and doesn't change the chain's cap |
| K10-2 | Which chain's cap | 2-a: the chains of the projects visited. List all that carry it, splitting the guidance by 1 kind / 2+ kinds | No N× syncs, no dependence on unrelated failures, no advisory as input | The comparison's counterpart is only the chains it touches |
| K10-3 | The record's cap's meaning | 3-a: the on-chain cap (the input for registration). The cap in `registerRecorded`'s Note | Matches the reader's use and is visible at its moment of effect | An advisory field is written with the meaning its reading judgment requires |
| K10-4 | The judgment's placement | 4-a: 2 phases (open and judge → append). The sync count is unchanged | No ordering counterexample, no extra syncs | A stop judgment is completed entirely before the writes |
| K10-5 | B's path | 5-a: add no surface; align the unfollowable guidance to R1 / R2 and transcribe "targeting that project" | Closes the same branches without growing a surface or C's entrance | Guidance names only the paths the predicates permit |
| K10-6 | Transcriptions and the check | 6-b: a net where each fragment of `devices.mdx`'s What-can-go-wrong quotations exists in the CLI's wording (denies vacuity and coincidental matches) | Wider than per-transcription nails; also pins untouched quotations | For same-shaped transcriptions, cast a net over the shape |
| K10-7 | C · known limitations | C not implemented. 1-d and 5-b to the owner points | — | — |

(b) **The re-synced on-chain device's cap (`targetDevice`)**. (c) Both | (b) reports the truth on both "registered" (you added it) and "found" (already there via a race — `appended: false`) | "Emit no Note and leave it to `device list`" → not visible at the moment of effect (rejected at K10-3) | A counterexample to (a): on the "found" branch, the adder is another device possibly at a different cap (the race — K10-2 round 4). Showing the record's cap would call a cap absent from the chain "found". (c) normally shows the same value twice | **(b)**. K5's owner points (1)(2), K2 carryover 4's reinforcement, K7's owner points (3)(4)(5), K8's owner point (2), K9's owner points (2)(3), and §11's W-3 / W-4 / W-6 are kept.
