Split it into "a re-run on this device does nothing for this project" and "while the request is alive, an approval from a device that's on it also works". The CLI's skipped wording doesn't touch approval (doesn't close the path enumeration), so unchanged. The principle is the same as K10-5 (guidance names only paths the predicates permit, and **doesn't eliminate paths they permit**).

### Additional rulings during review handling (K10-11 onward — responses to PR #197 pullfrog round 1)

- **K10-11 (ℹ️ — a docs parenthetical)**: the What-can-go-wrong refusal item attributed the per-project divergent-cap state only to "a re-run before this check existed". But per K10-2 counterexample 1 (K10-7's known limitation A-(1)), a `--project` re-run while the request is alive doesn't compare against unvisited projects, so post-K10 it can still create the same state. The parenthetical was widened to "before this check, or via `--project` to some projects — the check compares only the projects it visits". Same principle as K10-10 (guidance doesn't eliminate paths or states the predicates permit).
- **K10-12 (ℹ️ — a counterexample I hadn't constructed. Recorded as a known limitation)**: the backfill re-run guidances sent to a separate task at K10-6 (`reportApproveOutcome`'s rerun · `pull.ts`'s missing-epochs guidance) were "harmless if followed" before K10, but after K10, **following them with no flags while the request is alive** hits `refuseCapMismatch` with exit code 1 if the device's cap isn't the default owner / all (it converges in 1 round-trip since the refusal prints the right command; if the request is already cancelled it fails at `matchRequest` as before). Adding flags to these guidances wouldn't fix them — the guidance itself stays false as long as `already` calls no backfill (K10-6) — so this PR doesn't change them. The backfill-retry design of owner point (3) will decide whether the guidance carries `approveCommandOf`'s cap or moves to a different path.

## 16. K11 addendum (2026-09-25 — collecting a DK carryover: retrying a backfill for an already-registered device, and the unfollowable "a re-run will fill it" guidance)

Collecting K10's (PR #197) completion-record "points put to the owner" (3): outside `key recover --resume` there's no surface that retries a backfill for an already-registered device, and the guidances "re-running `device approve` will complete it" / "it's retried at the next sync" are unfollowable. The owner's rulings (K11's prompt §0-bis): **A (retrying a backfill for a registered device) = ON, B (making `--project` mean "only this project") = OFF, C (the request-less `device approve` entrance) = OFF**. D onward (K5 owner points (1)(2), K2 carryover 4's reinforcement, K7 owner points (3)(4)(5), K8 owner point (2), K9 owner points (2)(3), K10's carryover [help/docs for "re-adding a revoked device needs `--replace`"], §11 W-3 / W-4 / W-6) is out of scope. Specs, ADRs, `CLAUDE.md`, `packages/crypto`, the server, wire formats, acceptance-policy values, and `own-devices.json`'s fields are untouched. The table uses K6-X's format (a blank is the mark of "not searched" — fill them before writing "stopped"). Rulings are recorded here before implementation, and mid-implementation judgments are added in the same shape as K11-8 onward.

### K11 fact check 1 (every branch that builds the "on the chain but has missing epochs" state — K7-15. The branches were read)

The backfill's shared core is `backfill.ts`'s `backfillEnvironmentFor` (per environment), called for a device target by `device-ops.ts`'s `backfillToDevice` for each environment of the target device's effective scope (`backfillEachEnvironment` — one environment's failure doesn't stop the rest; they're collected as `failed`). `backfillEnvironmentFor` wraps epochs 1 through current in order, and **if even 1 epoch can't be opened by the signing side, that whole environment fails** ("No DEK wrap addressed to you exists for epoch …" — it doesn't register even the epochs it could wrap. Per-environment all-or-nothing). Registration is bulk → on a 409 it's per-epoch (a 409 is treated as already registered). Given that, the branches that create a gap:

| # | Branch (where in the code) | What goes missing | Today's report |
|---|---|---|---|
| G1 | `device approve`'s `appendOnProject`: `backfillToDevice`'s `failed` after `appendAddDevice` (per environment — network · a non-409 refusal · a gap on the signing side) | All epochs of the failed environments | `reportRegisteredDevice`'s Warning + exit code 1. rerun = "Re-run `maruhi device approve` for this device to complete it" (unfollowable — 2 below) |
| G2 | Same `appendOnProject`: after the append, `context.resync` failing or `backfillToDevice`'s leading `verifiedDeletedEnvironmentSet` failing (an exception → folded into `failed` via `Effect.catch`) | All environments of that device on that project | "failed — …" (looks unregistered despite being appended). If everything else is also failed, the "not registered on any project" Warning leaves the request → a re-run resolves at `planApproveOnProject`'s `already` and **doesn't backfill** |
| G3 | Same: an interruption between the append and the backfill (Ctrl+C · the process dying) | The remaining environments | None (the request remains — a re-run resolves at `already` and doesn't backfill) |
| G4 | Same: the signing device itself has missing epochs | All epochs of that environment (including the epochs the signing side holds — all-or-nothing) | The same Warning as G1 (the wording is `backfill.ts`'s "Have another member who holds wraps for every epoch run this operation …" + rerun) |
| G5 | The first sync's `registerRecorded`: `backfillToDevice`'s `failed` | The failed environments | Note "… environment(s) failed and are retried on the next sync" (unfollowable — the candidates are `registrationCandidates` = only records **absent from the chain**) |
| G6 | Same: the post-append re-sync doesn't show the device ("the resync does not show it yet") | All environments (no backfill is called) | Note "… the backfill runs on the next sync" (unfollowable — an on-chain device isn't a candidate at the next sync) |
| G7 | Same: `context.resync` / `verifiedDeletedEnvironmentSet` failing after the append | All environments | Note "… could not be registered … the registration is retried on the next sync" (the registration is done. The claim stays true as "if not on the chain, retried at the next sync" — per the candidates' predicate. Filling the gap is outside this wording) |
| G8 | `key recover`'s `registerDeviceWithReserve`: `backfillToDevice`'s `failed` with the reserve key as recipient (including the reserve key's own gaps — same shape as G4) | This device's failed environments | Warning + rerun "Re-run `maruhi key recover --resume` or have a registered device run `maruhi device approve` for this machine" (the latter half is unfollowable — it can't make a request. K4-21) |
| G9 | `key reserve rotate`'s `rotateReserveOnProject`: `backfillToDevice`'s `failed` for the new reserve key | The new reserve key's failed environments | **Unreported** (`reportReserveRotateOutcome` shows only `describeBackfill`'s count and discards `failed`. Exit code 0). Since the old reserve key is revoked afterward, an environment the reserve key can't open silently remains at restoration time |
| G10 | Scope widening on the same person (`member change-role`) · `member add`'s backfill failing | All of that person's devices on the affected environments | Existing guidance (re-running `member change-role` / `member add` — includes device targets: `backfillMemberEnvironment` iterates the target's every device). **Not caused by adding a device**, so outside K11's scope. A recovery path already exists |

- **G2 / G3 note**: a `device approve` re-run while the request is alive (K10's R4) never enters `appendOnProject` — `planApproveOnProject` resolves `present !== undefined` to `settled("already")` — so it calls no backfill.
- **G4 note**: `backfillToDevice`'s failure wording (`backfill.ts`) is shared with member backfill and says "another member". The device-targeted report appends a rerun after it, so the device case's path is named there (the shared wording stays true in the member case — unchanged).

### K11 fact check 2 (which path fills each branch today — the paths' preconditions were read)

| Path | Preconditions (the predicates were read) | Branches it fills |
|---|---|---|
| P1 Re-running `device approve` | A live request (`matchRequest` — cancelled once the registry PUT succeeds, and a registered key can't re-make a request: K4-21 / K10's R4). **And an already-carried project resolves at `already` without backfilling** | **None** (doesn't fill even while the request is alive) |
| P2 The first sync (`registerRecorded`) | Candidates = records **absent from this chain** (`registrationCandidates`). Inside the ceremony gate (K4-37) | **None** (a carried device isn't a candidate) |
| P3 `key recover --resume` | The ceremony of opening the ledger's reserve key (passkey / handoff). Calls `backfillToDevice` even when `appendAddDevice` is `appended: false`. The target is **this device only**; the recipient (wrapper) is the reserve key | Only G8 (when the reserve key holds that epoch) |
| P4 Re-running `member add` (admin) | A current member holding the acceptance block's key (`prepareMemberAdd`'s `memberHasKeys` — `alreadyAdded` only while the accepting device is still valid). `backfillMemberEnvironment` covers the target's every device | Only another person's device's gap (if the accepting device remains). You can't target your own device |
| P5 Re-running `member change-role` | Only the environments of the historical widening (`scopeChangesOf`) | Only G10 |
| P6 `env rotate` | The new epoch goes to all of R(E)'s devices (exact match) | **Only the new epoch**. Doesn't fill missing past epochs |
| P7 Re-running `key reserve rotate` | Makes a **new** reserve key each time (the previous new key becomes a revocation target) | Doesn't fill G9 (just replaces it with a different key; that key's backfill can fail again) |

- **The server's authorization (just read)**: `registerDekWrapsProgram` lowers the role floor to reader when `allRecipientsAreOwnDevices` (recipient class member · recipients = the calling principal · the enc keys are the calling principal's valid device keys), else member. The scope is re-judged by **the signing device's effective authority** (`ensureDevicePermission`). The recipient side is `checkWrapRecipient` (matches a current member's valid device key ∧ `deviceReceivesEnvironment`). **Cap monotonicity doesn't apply to wrap registration** (it applies to `add_device`'s consensus rule) — a narrow-cap device can fill a wide-cap sibling's gap within its own effective scope's environments. A recipient's slot is (environment, epoch, user_id, enc key); an existing exact match is a 409 = treated as registered.
- **Consequence**: the paths that fill device-add-caused gaps (G1–G9) are **none** except G8's P3. K10's owner point (3)'s claim ("none besides `key recover --resume`") is right when read through the branches, and moreover G9 isn't even reported.

### K11 fact check 3 (who can notice a gap — the server's API was read)

- **The gapped device itself**: a valued pull's (`pullVariables`) §7 difference check ("no DEK wraps for you exist at epochs …"). The material is the bundled wraps (`pulled.deks`) **narrowed to its own device-key rows** (`verifyAndUnwrapDeks`' `mine`).
- **The same person's other devices**: **can notice**. The server's distribution (`listWrapsForRecipient(environmentId, actor.userId)` — both the valued bulk pull's bundle and `deks.listMine` use the same function) returns **rows addressed to all of that person's devices**, each carrying `recipientEncPubHex` (`programs-dek.ts`'s note "the response is the rows addressed to all your devices", the CLI-side `verifyAndUnwrapDeks`' "a row addressed to another device isn't a poison wrap"). So a sibling device can derive, **with no extra communication, for an environment it valued-pulled itself**, which epochs lack a row addressed to the sibling's device key (the material is the server's declaration — an advisory). A row from an old server not carrying `recipientEncPubHex` (pre-K3 = 1 device) can't be attributed.
- **Other people**: can't (distribution is to the person only — AUTH §12-6). `environment-scopes.mdx` :52's "Nobody else can see that a backfill is incomplete: keys are distributed to their recipient only, so the CLI has no view of what other members hold" stays true as a claim about "other members" (your own other devices aren't "somebody else"). **No fix needed** (device-to-device visibility is `devices.mdx`'s claim — K6-R).
- **The chain**: doesn't represent gaps (wrap delivery doesn't land on the chain). What the chain can state is only "which devices are which environment's recipients (R(E))" and "the current epoch".

### K11 fact check 4 (transcriptions of the unfollowable guidance — counted by grep)

`grep -rn` over `apps/cli/src` / `apps/cli/test` / `apps/site/docs` / `apps/site/test` / `docs/SELF_HOSTING.md` / `README.md` for "to complete it" / "backfills the missing epochs" / "backfill runs on the next sync" / "retried on the next sync" / "have a registered device run" / "device approve` for it":

1. `apps/cli/src/device.ts` `reportApproveOutcome`'s rerun "Re-run `maruhi device approve` for this device to complete it" (G1–G4's report)
2. `apps/cli/src/key-recover.ts` `reportRecoveryOutcome`'s rerun's latter half "or have a registered device run `maruhi device approve` for this machine" (G8)
3. `apps/cli/src/pull.ts`'s missing-epochs warning's last sentence "If this machine was added as a device recently, re-run `maruhi device approve` for it from a device that is already registered — that backfills the missing epochs"
4. `apps/cli/src/device-sync.ts` `registerRecorded`'s Note "— the backfill runs on the next sync" (G6)
5. Same "; N environment(s) failed and are retried on the next sync" (G5)
- **Non-transcriptions (no fix needed — reasons)**: `device-sync.ts`'s `describeRegistrationFailure` "the registration is retried on the next sync" (G7 — a registration claim, true per the candidates' predicate). `noteWriteFailure` "the device set is re-observed on the next sync" (an observation claim, true). `docs/SELF_HOSTING.md` :988 "registers it on each project at the next sync" (a reserve-key registration claim, true). `backfill.ts`'s "Have another member … and re-run" (true in the member case — G4's note).
- **docs transcriptions**: `devices.mdx` holds **none** of 1–5 (it doesn't touch a backfill failure — only :48's "distributes the keys of every environment in the device's cap, every epoch"). `environment-scopes.mdx` :79's What-can-go-wrong item quotes the pull warning but explains **only the member case** (doesn't touch the device case — not false, but there's no explanation corresponding to the CLI warning's latter half).
- **Test expectations**: `pull-run.test.ts` :326 expects "re-run `maruhi member add`" (the warning's first half — unchanged). `device.test.ts` :648 expects `deviceNotOnChainMessage` to **not contain** "run `maruhi device approve` for this machine" (K10 — a different wording from this matter. Deleting 2's latter half doesn't contradict it). No test expects the letter of 1–5.
- **K10-12's relation**: 1 and 3 name a flagless `device approve`, so following them while the request is alive can hit K10's `refuseCapMismatch`. If the guidance's path changes (K11-5), the need to carry `approveCommandOf`'s cap disappears.

### K11-1. Where the retry lives (A-(1))

| Option | Content | Upward compat | Silver bullet | Counterexamples | Adoption |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1-a Backfill even on `device approve`'s `already` | Natural as an approval's continuation | — | — | Counterexample 1 "the request is cancelled on PUT success and can't be re-created (R4)" — in G1's main case (some environments failed, the PUT succeeded) a re-run fails at `matchRequest`. What it closes is only the 15-minute live-request window (G2 / G3). Counterexample 2 "G5–G9 don't go through `device approve`" — not closed. Counterexample 3 (relation to C) "widen it to 'fill on `already` even without a request'" — that's C's (OFF) entrance itself | Rejected |
| 1-b On sync (`syncOwnDevices`), check and fill the on-chain own other devices' gaps every time | The user does nothing. Any command picks it up | — | — | Counterexample 1 (cost) "the sync doesn't know environments" — getting the gap's material (the sibling-addressed rows) needs `deks.listMine` per environment: **E GETs plus re-verifying own-addressed wraps on every keyed command** (every `maruhi run`). The same shape as K10-5's 5-d (keyed-command cost × N). Counterexample 2 "placed alongside the sync's registration inside the ceremony gate, the gate's necessity blurs" | Rejected |
| 1-c The gapped device itself puts out a request; other devices answer | The device that first notices the gap is the origin | — | — | A registered key can't make a request with `device add` (K4-21). A new request kind = a wire · server change (untouched per §1-bis). An owner's call | Rejected (not put to the owner points — 1-f below closes the same branches) |
| 1-d A new subcommand (e.g. `maruhi device backfill`) | Explicit · can iterate all projects | — | — | §1 "after showing it can't be closed by an existing command's re-run or a sync path" — 1-f closes it with an existing command. A surface grows (K10-5) | Rejected |
| 1-e Fix only the guidance wording (make no path) | No behavior change | — | — | Per fact check 2 there's no path, so the only guidance that can be made true is "no command can fill it (revoke and re-add)". The re-added device's backfill can also fail (the same hole). The user is left unable to read their history | Rejected |
| 1-f **A registered device doing a valued `maruhi pull` derives the missing epochs of the same person's other valid devices that are that environment's recipients (`deviceReceivesEnvironment`) from the wrap rows the pull already received, and fills them** (wraps only the epochs it could open itself, registering via `backfillEnvironmentFor`'s existing registration path) | No added surface (an existing command). **Zero extra communication when there's no gap** (the material is the pull's bundle — fact check 3). Closes all of branches G1–G9 regardless of which origin caused the gap (including the reserve key — G9). If a sibling device pulls as usual, it closes before the user notices | **(i) Wrap only the epochs it could open itself** (don't drop a whole environment on the signing side's gap — avoids G4's all-or-nothing; unopenable epochs are reported). **(ii) Wrap only the missing epochs** (avoids a bulk falling to a 409 then N per-epoch round-trips — only missing ones, normally 1 round-trip). **(iii) Reuse the opened DEK** (`environmentKeysFor`'s `cached` — no re-fetch, no re-verify) | "Drop onto an existing mechanism" = the sibling-addressed rows the server already distributes (the valued pull's bundle) and the existing registration path (`backfillEnvironmentFor` / AUTH §12-6's sixth). Neither new material nor a new registration path is created | Counterexample 1 "the sibling device never pulls that environment" — the guidance names `maruhi pull --project <id> --env <env>` (K11-5). Counterexample 2 "the sibling's cap doesn't cover that environment" — it holds no DEK for a non-recipient environment, so it can't fill it. The guidance states the condition "a device whose cap covers". Counterexample 3 "no device holds that epoch (e.g. a restore from a reserve-key gap)" — can't be filled. The report (K11-8) says "this device doesn't hold it either" and docs write that `env rotate` is only the new epoch (P6). Counterexample 4 "a read-scoped token" — the registration POST 403s. The Note gives the reason and the pull stays a success (one of the reasons K11-4 doesn't put it on `run`). Counterexample 5 "an old server (rows lack `recipientEncPubHex`)" — can't attribute, so it doesn't derive (no false positives. An old server has 1 device). Counterexample 6 "the server's declaration lies" — an "it's missing" lie lands at 409 in an idempotent round-trip; an "it's there" lie just leaves the gap (the server can always withhold wraps — availability is in the server's hands. K11-3). Counterexample 7 "a revoked device's row" — only valid devices are looked at (the chain). Counterexample 8 "2 siblings fill simultaneously" — the latter gets 409 = registered. Counterexample 9 "an old-CLI sibling" — just doesn't fill (no breakage in a mix). Counterexample 10 "agent environments / non-interactive" — K11-2 | **Adopted** (with upward compats (i)(ii)(iii)) |

**Round 2**: upward compat = (i)(ii)(iii) (the table). Silver bullets tried = "don't create the gap": (α) register the wraps before `add_device` → the server rejects a recipient not on the chain with `recipient-key-mismatch` (the recipient's predicate). (β) Make `add_device` + the wraps a single composite request (the same shape as §12-4's environment create / rotate) → a wire · server change, out of scope (an owner's call — not enough difference to put in the owner points: since 1-f closes regardless of origin, atomization's only benefit is "eliminating the gap's duration"). (γ) 1-f itself (existing material and path). Counterexamples = 3 for 1-a, 2 for 1-b, 1–2 each for 1-c / 1-d / 1-e, 10 for 1-f (the table). **Round 3**: option tried "combining 1-a and 1-f (also fill on an approval re-run)" → 1-f closes the same branches and 1-a works only inside the request's window. With 2 paths the guidance becomes 2 forms, and 1-a also hits K10-12 (the cap refusal). No new options. Option tried "widen 1-f from 'a sibling's gap' to 'all recipients other than self (including other people)'" — no material since the server doesn't distribute other-people-addressed rows (fact check 3), and registering for other people is member-or-above (outside the reader's self-backfill). No new options. **Round 4**: option tried "limit the fill targets to the epochs whose backfill was needed after `add_device` on the chain (≤ the current epoch at add time)" → create / rotate distribute to all of R(E) by exact match, so a gap in a later epoch can't arise. Limiting it doesn't change the targets — it just adds a predicate. No new options. Stopped.

**Principle**: "**filling rides a path that already sees the gap. Don't go fetch material anew — derive it from distributed facts**" — CRYPTO_SPEC §1 ✓ (the crypto operations are the same as the existing backfill — re-wrapping and the registration signature), §7 "a wrap's performer = a DEK holder" ✓ (wraps only epochs it opened itself), DK-A ✓ (recipients are devices — the chain defines them), DK-D ✓ (the target devices and keys are the chain's), DK-E ✓ (the reading of registration path 6 — K11-7, within the predicate of the reader's self-backfill), K4-3 ✓ (doesn't read or write the local record), K4-5 ✓ (K11-3), K4-12 ✓ (touches no persisted name or field), K4-31 ✓ (writes no advisory), K4-37 ✓ (K11-2), K6-T ✓ / K7-13 ✓ (existing mechanisms), K9-1 ✓ (doesn't rely on a request), K10-1 ✓ (doesn't touch `device approve`'s cap discipline), K10-5 ✓ (adds no surface — the path is an existing command), ADR-0017 ✓. **Adopted: 1-f (with the upward compat)**. What makes it superior: unlike 1-a it doesn't rely on the request's window and closes all of G1–G9; unlike 1-b its cost is zero (no extra communication in the normal no-gap case); unlike 1-c it changes neither spec nor wire; unlike 1-d it adds no surface; unlike 1-e it lets the user read their history.

### K11-2. Does the fill go inside the ceremony gate (TTY + non-agent)? (A-(2))

| Option | Content | Upward compat | Silver bullet | Counterexamples | Adoption |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2-a **Outside the gate** (fill wherever a valued pull can run) | Closes even on CI / agent-environment pulls (maximum self-healing) | Failures are Notes only · the pull's success and exit code don't change (the fill is a SHOULD accompaniment — the same discipline as K4-3 counterexample 5) | Re-ask from "what does the gate protect": K4-37's gate stops "**adding a signer on the grounds of an unsigned state**". The fill adds neither a signer nor a recipient (the recipients are the same-person devices the verified chain already defined as R(E)). There is no object to protect | Counterexample 1 (agent environment) "an agent runs a pull to trigger the fill" — the wrap targets are only the on-chain own device keys (the agent can't choose). That device was already a recipient of the new epoch under rotation's distribution, so no capability is gained (same reason as AUTH §12-3's reader self-backfill). Counterexample 2 (a planted record / `own-devices.json` corruption) "getting targets added via the record" — the fill doesn't read the record (targets are the chain). Corruption is irrelevant. Counterexample 3 (non-interactive · pipes) — a valued pull also runs over a pipe (only the value display is the ceremony — `--show`). The fill runs too. Counterexample 4 (a server's lie inducing writes) — 1 idempotent round-trip (409) per environment × 1 sibling. The cap per pull is bounded by the sibling count (DeviceLimit 16) | **Adopted** (with the upward compat) |
| 2-b Inside the gate (the same 2 layers as `registrationAllowed`) | Aligns with the sync's registration | — | — | Counterexample "someone who uses it only from CI / an agent" — never filled, and the gapped device's warning persists. It drops only availability though the threat the gate guards (adding a signer) is absent. The gate's meaning (ADR-0016 decision 7 — the ceremony a human verifies) dilutes into "operations with a signature in general" | Rejected |
| 2-c Ask interactively yes / no | The user's explicitness | — | — | A question on every pull (the same counterexample as K4-37 (d)). Non-interactive = same as 2-b | Rejected |

**Round 2**: upward compat = failures stay Notes (the table). Silver bullet = re-asking from "what the gate protects" (the table). Counterexamples = 4 for 2-a, 1 each for 2-b / 2-c. **Round 3**: option tried "in agent environments, don't fill; emit a Note prompting a pull on a human's device (a compromise)" → absent a protected object, the compromise carries 2-b's counterexample as-is. Existing precedents are on the same line: `maruhi env rotate` (wraps the DEK to all of R(E)'s devices) and `member change-role`'s resume (a backfill) have no ceremony gate. No new options. **Round 4**: option tried "don't fill when the signing device's cap is reader" → the server permits a reader's self-backfill (AUTH §12-3), and a reader legitimately holds DEKs of its own effective scope. No reason to limit it. No new options. Stopped.

**Principle**: "**the ceremony gate goes on operations that grow a signer or a recipient. Completing a distribution to a key the chain already made a recipient is outside the gate**" — K4-37 ✓ (K4-37's principle is "if the underlying state isn't signed, gate on the operation's side" — the fill's grounds [target, key, DEK] are the verified chain and wraps verified via §5.1 / §5.2, i.e. signed. The signal [the server's rows] isn't a ground — K11-3), K4-6 ✓ (the verification ceremony stays on adding an approver), ADR-0016 decision 7 ✓ (the value display's boundary stays `--show`), DK-E ✓. **Adopted: 2-a (with the upward compat)**.

### K11-3. The gap's judgment material and the per-run cost (A-(3))

| Option | Content | Upward compat | Silver bullet | Counterexamples | Adoption |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 3-a **Let the bundled wrap rows the pull received (the server's declaration) be the signal, and build the fill's contents only from the chain and verified wraps** | Zero extra communication. Does nothing when there's no gap | If even 1 row lacks `recipientEncPubHex` (an old server), don't derive | — | Counterexample 1 "a declaration is a judgment input (K4-5)" — the declaration decides only "whether to attempt the fill". What to wrap and to whom (targets = on-chain devices, keys = the chain's enc public keys, DEK = own-addressed via §5.1 / §5.2), and whether it's already registered (the server's acceptance — 409), don't rely on the declaration. A lie's consequence is "one extra idempotent round-trip" or "the gap remains" — both inside a capability the server already has (withholding wraps). Counterexample 2 "a row invisible despite no gap (paging truncation etc.)" — the bundle covers every epoch (§12-7) with no truncation. Even if invisible it converges on 409 | **Adopted** (with the upward compat) |
| 3-b Judge on the chain alone | Only the truth is input | — | — | The chain doesn't represent wrap delivery (fact check 3). It can't judge | Rejected |
| 3-c The backfilling device writes a local "unfinished" mark, picked up by the next command | No declaration used | — | — | An interruption (G3) can't write the mark (writing it before the append = K4-31 ✗). Only picked up on the device that wrote the mark (can't pick up G9's reserve key or another device's origin). Needs an `own-devices.json` field addition (K4-12's compat check) | Rejected |
| 3-d No judgment — on every pull, wrap all the sibling's epochs and register (converging on 409) | No judgment material needed | — | — | Per pull: HPKE × epochs × sibling count, plus a 409 per-epoch fallback (N round-trips). The normal no-gap cost is maximal | Rejected |

**Round 2**: upward compat = not deriving on an old server (the table). Silver bullet = "make judgment unnecessary" = 3-d (rejected on cost). Counterexamples = 2 for 3-a, 1–3 each for 3-b–3-d. **Round 3**: option tried "verify the sibling-addressed rows' registration signatures before counting" → even verified, the judgment stays a declaration (the server can hide verified rows), and nothing is gained. Only the verification cost grows. No new options. **Round 4**: option tried "send a signal to the sibling from the gapped device's side (its own gap warning)" → there's no wire that carries the signal (K11-1's 1-c). No new options. Stopped.

**Cost estimate**: when there's no gap = 0 extra communication · 0 extra crypto operations (just count the rows' epochs — O(siblings × epochs)). When a gap exists = per sibling, HPKE wraps for the missing-epoch count + the registration signature, and 1 `deks.register` round-trip (`cached` — no re-fetch of own-addressed).

**Principle**: "**an advisory decides only 'whether to attempt'; 'what to do' and 'whether it's done' are decided by the truth and acceptance**" — K4-5 ✓ (may be a signal, but isn't a judgment input — the declaration enters no judgment [targets · keys · DEK · registered]), DK-D ✓, K4-31 ✓ (writes no advisory). **Adopted: 3-a (with the upward compat)**.

### K11-4. Which command carries it (the implementation's placement)

| Option | Content | Upward compat | Silver bullet | Counterexamples | Adoption |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 4-a **`maruhi pull` only** (enabled by an explicit input on `pullVariables`, passed only by the pull handler) | The command a person runs for verification and confirmation — docs and guidance can name a single command | DEKs don't leave `pullVariables` (the fill happens inside `pullVariables`; the result is returned as facts [epochs filled · epochs not held · failures] — K4-35 "facts travel by type") | — | Counterexample 1 "someone who uses it only in CI (never runs pull)" — the guidance names `maruhi pull --project <id> --env <env>` (the gapped device's warning also prints that one line). Counterexample 2 "`maruhi pull`'s meaning changes (it writes)" — help's description is "Verify … decrypt … list". The fill is an accompaniment only when a gap exists and doesn't change pull's result (output · exit code). Written in docs' devices.mdx in 1 paragraph | **Adopted** (with the upward compat) |
| 4-b `pull` and `run` | More self-healing occasions | — | — | Counterexample 1 "`run` is an execution path that spawns a child process, run under CI's read-scoped token" — while a gap exists, every execution emits a 403 Note. Counterexample 2 "a write mixed into `run`'s meaning (inject and execute)" — the execution path gains a latency · failure surface | Rejected |
| 4-c Every `pullVariables` caller (pull / run / sync / the sync receipt) | Maximum self-healing | — | — | In addition to 4-b's counterexample, `maruhi sync` is an integration path that decrypts only the chosen variables (sending them outside) — mixing an unrelated write | Rejected |
| 4-d `syncOwnDevices` (the keyed commands' prelude) | On every command | — | — | It doesn't know environments (K11-1's 1-b counterexample 1 — cost) | Rejected |

**Round 2**: upward compat = DEKs don't leave (the table). Silver bullets = the shapes that don't ask "which command" = 4-c / 4-d (rejected on cost / meaning). Counterexamples = 2 for 4-a, 1–2 each for 4-b–4-d. **Round 3**: option tried "summarize what the pull filled on the output's last line" → a question of where Notes live; `logNote` (1 ledger entry per command) suffices. No new options. **Round 4**: option tried "make it stoppable via a flag like `--no-fill`" → the fill is idempotent, and the reason to stop it (an environment that dislikes writes) is already stopped by a read-scoped token (403 → Note). No surface added. No new options. Stopped.

**Principle**: "**an accompanying write rides exactly 1 command the user runs to verify that environment, and guidance names that command**" — K10-5 ✓ (adds no surface), K6-R ✓ (the normative claim lives in devices.mdx), K4-3 counterexample 5 ✓ (an accompaniment doesn't change the command's success). **Adopted: 4-a (with the upward compat)**.

### K11-5. The guidance's wording and placement (A-(4))

| Option | Content | Upward compat | Silver bullet | Counterexamples | Adoption |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 5-a **Replace transcriptions 1–5 with the fill's path (`maruhi pull --project <id> --env <env>` on a registered, cap-covering device). The command's letter is built from 1 function** | Followable. A single phrase | **Add G9's report** (surface the new reserve key's backfill failure as a Warning naming the same path. The exit code is unchanged — §1). **Close K10-12** (since `device approve` leaves the guidance, there's no need to carry `approveCommandOf`'s cap). `key recover` keeps `--resume` (once more with the reserve key) (the only path in a restoration where no other device exists) | Pin the transcriptions' agreement by **structure**, not a check (the same function builds the letter — K6-T's "mechanically decidable" satisfied by a type and a function) | Counterexample 1 "the guided device (this device) is itself the gap's cause (G4)" — the wording states the conditions "a device whose cap covers and that holds that epoch". Counterexample 2 "the guidance diverges from docs' quotation" — `devices.mdx`'s What-can-go-wrong quotations are matched by K10-6's net. `environment-scopes.mdx` is outside the net, so count to confirm (K11-6). Counterexample 3 "`--env`'s default (`defaultEnvironment`)" — the guidance always makes `--env` and `--project` explicit (relying on the default pulls a different environment). Counterexample 4 "in G6 (invisible in the re-sync) the environment isn't known" — don't enumerate environments; say "for each environment that device reads" | **Adopted** (with the upward compat) |
| 5-b Cut the guidance (say nothing) | The falsehood disappears | — | — | Counterexample "a failing user doesn't know the next move" — only the gapped device's warning remains, with no idea where to fix it | Rejected |
| 5-c Make the guidance a docs link | The CLI's wording is short | — | — | People read the screen's wording (same as K10-5's 5-e). No reason not to name a command that can be named | Rejected |

**Round 2**: upward compat = G9's report · K10-12's resolution · keeping `--resume` (the table). Silver bullet = building the letter from 1 function (the table). Counterexamples = 4 for 5-a, 1 each for 5-b / 5-c. **Round 3**: option tried "vary the device sentence of `pull.ts`'s warning by whether this device was added as a device (its on-chain provenance)" → the provenance (`deviceProvenanceOf`) distinguishes "the first key" from "an added device", but even for an added device the gap's cause can be a member backfill (G10). Keep today's form listing both paths (the member case → the device case), changing only the device sentence. No new options. **Round 4**: option tried "show the failed environments' names in `registerRecorded`'s Note" → adopt (5-a's way of writing — needed to name `--env`). No new options. Stopped.

**Principle**: "**guidance names only the paths the predicates permit, and its letter is built in 1 place**" — K10-5 ✓, K6-Y ✓ (everywhere a transcription exists), K10-6 ✓ (docs' quotations go through the net), K10-10 ✓ (doesn't eliminate a permitted path — keeps `--resume`), ADR-0017 ✓. **Adopted: 5-a (with the upward compat)**.

### K11-6. The check's placement

- **Existing checks grepped before placing**: `cli-vocabulary.test.ts`'s K10-6 net (each fragment of `devices.mdx`'s What-can-go-wrong quotations exists in the CLI's wording) · the help golden (flags' · subcommands' letters) · `pull-run.test.ts`'s missing-epochs warning (the member case's sentence) · `device.test.ts`'s approval and first-sync describes. **No check holds the fill's (deriving siblings' gaps and registering) property**.

| Option | Content | Upward compat | Silver bullet | Counterexamples | Adoption |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 6-a **Add a fill describe to `pull-run.test.ts`** (must-pass inputs: only the sibling's missing epochs are registered addressed to the sibling's key, with this device's signature / an epoch this device also doesn't hold is unwrapped and reported / on a failure the pull is still 0. Must-fail inputs: with no gap, 0 registration POSTs / on old-server rows [no `recipientEncPubHex`], 0 / for a non-recipient device [outside the cap's scope], 0) + a `device.test.ts` check that the approval's backfill-failure Warning names the pull command | Pins the property (K8-2 — non-vacuity is denied on the "if a gap exists a POST is issued" side) | Open the registered rows **with real crypto** — they decrypt under the sibling's key and their §5.1 registration signatures pass (fails a recipient mix-up) | Transcription agreement is structural (K11-5 — 1 function). The quotation added to `devices.mdx` is picked up automatically by K10-6's net (no new net needed) | Counterexample 1 (coincidental match) "watching only the POST count, a re-registration to self also passes" — watch the destination enc key and the epoch. Counterexample 2 "the existing pull test's rows lack `recipientEncPubHex`" — treated as an old server, so no fill = the existing tests' expectations stay unchanged (that's itself one of 6-a's must-fail inputs) | **Adopted** (with the upward compat) |
| 6-b Per-wording nails ("device approve … to complete it" must not exist in the CLI) | Stops regression | — | — | A negation nail doesn't hit a rewording (vacuous). Structure (1 function) suffices | Rejected |

**Round 2**: upward compat = opening with real crypto (the table). Silver bullets = pinning by structure · the existing net (the table). Counterexamples = 2 for 6-a, 1 for 6-b. **Round 3**: option tried "put `environment-scopes.mdx`'s quotation into K10-6's net too" → that page's other quotations are value-filled examples (`production` · `(your scope: dev, staging)`) and the net fails (K10-6 counterexample 3). Since this change is just 1 item, count it by hand and write it in the completion record. No new options. **Round 4**: option tried "update the help golden" → no flag, subcommand, or description changes (`maruhi pull`'s description stays "Verify … decrypt … list" — the fill is an accompaniment). No new options. Stopped.

**Principle**: "**a check pins properties (to whom, what, how many); transcription agreement is pinned by structure**" — K6-T ✓, K8-2 ✓, K7-13 ✓ (inside the existing test file and the net). **Adopted: 6-a (with the upward compat)**.

### K11-7. Reading AUTH §12-6's registration path · B / C · known limitations (pinned before implementation)

- **The reading of registration path 6**: AUTH §12-6 enumerates the independent registration API's path as "the backfill of every epoch addressed to the new device by a same-person device, after `add_device` (the environments of the new device's effective scope. Includes the reserve key)", with acceptance rules shared across all paths. The fill is the same tuple as #6 — (signer = a same-person device, recipient = the device added by `add_device`, contents = the DEKs of that device's effective scope's environments) — and the enumeration **doesn't fix an activation point (when)**. "New device" means new with respect to `add_device`, not a time condition. Precedents: `key recover --resume` (re-activating the same #6 later), `registerRecorded`'s "found" branch (a same-person device that isn't `add_device`'s actor filling it), `member add`'s re-run (re-activating path 1 later). CRYPTO_SPEC §7 puts the backfill **obligation** on `add_device`'s actor but doesn't forbid another same-person device from doing it ("a wrap's performer = a DEK holder"), and the reader's self-backfill predicate (AUTH §12-3) requires only that every recipient be the calling principal's device key. Judged **no enumeration revision needed** (if the reading needed a revision, don't adopt — send it to the owner points instead — §3's 5). Listed among the human-review-required spots.
- **B / C stay OFF, unimplemented**. The only line where B / C appeared in A's ruling's counterexamples: K11-1's 1-a counterexample 3 (a fill-on-`already`-without-request form is C's entrance) — the adopted 1-f doesn't touch `device approve`, so it creates no C entrance. B (`--project`) is independent of the fill (the fill covers only the pulled project's environments).
- **Known limitations**: (1) the fill runs only when a sibling device valued-pulls that environment (someone who only `run`s in CI follows the guidance's command — K11-4). (2) A gap no own device holds the epoch for (e.g. a restore from the reserve key's own gap) can't be filled. `env rotate` distributes only the new epoch (P6). (3) On a read-scoped token's pull, registration 403s and the Note keeps appearing while the gap lasts. (4) On an old server (rows lack `recipientEncPubHex`) it doesn't derive. (5) G9's exit code stays 0 (only the report is added — changing exit codes is prohibited by §1. To the owner points). (6) G2's "reported `failed` despite being appended" keeps its wording (since the fill closes the gap, the remaining divergence is display-only).
- **§1 items judged "no fix needed"**: (i) `describeRegistrationFailure`'s "the registration is retried on the next sync" (G7 — a registration claim, true per the candidates' predicate). (ii) `backfill.ts`'s failure wording "another member" (true in the member case; in the device case the rerun names the path). (iii) `environment-scopes.mdx` :52 (true as a claim about "other members" — fact check 3). (iv) `device approve`'s `already` resolution (doesn't backfill) — the fill closes the same branch (K11-1 round 3). (v) the help golden (K11-6 round 4). (vi) exit codes · the ceremony gate · the FP match · K10's cap refusal (untouched).

### K11 ruling list (pinned before implementation)

| # | Topic | Adopted | What makes it better than the alternatives | Principle |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| K11-1 | Where the retry lives | 1-f: a registered device doing a valued pull derives the same person's other devices' gaps from the pull's bundled wrap rows and fills the epochs it could open | Closes every branch without relying on the request's window, zero cost in the normal case, changes neither spec · wire · surface | The fill rides a path that already sees the gap |
| K11-2 | The ceremony gate | 2-a: outside the gate (failures are Notes · pull's success unchanged) | Doesn't drop availability where there's no protected object (adding a signer) | The gate goes on operations that grow a signer or a recipient |
| K11-3 | The judgment material and cost | 3-a: the bundled rows are the signal; the contents come from the chain and verified wraps | Zero extra communication; the declaration decides only "whether to attempt" | An advisory decides only whether to attempt |
| K11-4 | The carrying command | 4-a: `maruhi pull` only (DEKs don't leave `pullVariables`) | No write mixed into the execution path or CI's read token | An accompanying write rides 1 verification command only |
| K11-5 | The guidance wording | 5-a: transcriptions 1–5 to the fill's path, the letter built from 1 function. G9's report added. `--resume` kept | Followable guidance in a single phrase, eliminating no permitted path | Guidance names only paths the predicates permit; the letter is built in 1 place |
| K11-6 | The check | 6-a: the pull fill's property (down to the recipient, with real crypto) + the approval Warning | Pins properties; transcriptions pinned by structure and the existing net | Checks pin properties; transcriptions, structure |
| K11-7 | The path's reading · B / C · limitations | Within registration path #6 (no revision needed). B / C unimplemented | — | — |

### Mid-implementation additional rulings (K11-8 onward — enumerated, counterexampled, principled on the spot)

| # | Topic | Options | Upward compat | Silver bullet | Counterexamples | Adoption |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| K11-8 | Where in `pullVariables` to enable the fill (K11-4's implementation shape) | (a) Add an optional input `fillOwnDeviceGaps` (the signing key), fill inside `pullVariables`, and return the result `ownDeviceGapFills` as facts. (b) `pullVariables` returns the opened DEKs and the raw rows; the pull handler fills. (c) In the pull handler, re-fetch `deks.listMine` and fill | On top of (a), make `fillOwnDeviceGaps`'s signing key `undefined`-tolerant and pull the branch into the function's side (fallow complexity — no ternary added to `pullVariables`' body) | — | Counterexample to (b): DEKs leave `pullVariables` (the DEKs' lifetime extends outside the value-decryption path). Counterexample to (c): each pull adds 1 GET + re-verifying own-addressed wraps (breaks K11-3's zero cost) | **(a)** (with the upward compat). Principle "DEKs don't leave the function that opened them; results return as facts" — K4-35 ✓, K11-3 ✓ |
| K11-9 | The shape for adding "the epochs to wrap" and "the opened DEKs" to the shared core `backfillEnvironmentFor` | (a) Optional `epochs` / `cached` (omitted = today's 1–current epochs · fetching `listMine`). (b) Write a separate wrap-and-register function just for the device fill | Move the default's computation into a small function (`epochsToWrap`), adding no branch to the shared core (the new complexity findings were fixed without adding to the baseline) | — | Counterexample to (b): a duplicate of the same procedure — wrap, sign, bulk → per-epoch on 409 (a fallow duplication, and a fix left behind later). Counterexample to (a) "the member / server backfills' behavior changes" — omitted is identical to today (existing tests pass unchanged) | **(a)** (with the upward compat) |
| K11-10 (a procedure record) | How to revert a mutation | For reverting a temporary edit I used a helper "apply the substitution in reverse" (`s.replace(new, old)`); on **a mutation where `new` is the empty string** the revert inserted `old` between every character and broke `device-sync.ts` (visible in `git diff --stat` as a 673-line diff). Not using `git checkout` (it would erase my uncommitted edits), I recovered the original content from the corrupted content being "the original with `old` interleaved between every character" (verified by reproducing the same corruption via the inverse operation), and restored `old` at the mutation's location. Thereafter the helper was fixed to the form that keeps the pre-edit content and writes it back | — | — | Counterexample "a mutation where `new` appears elsewhere too" — a reverse substitution rewrites unrelated spots (the other 4 mutations this time were confirmed to have unique `new`s) | Principle "revert a temporary edit by the pre-edit content, not by a reverse substitution" — an **implementation caution** on §3's 7 "apply in reverse to revert" (a reverse substitution isn't an inverse when `new` is empty or non-unique) |

### K11 completion record (2026-09-25)

- **Deliverables (A — behavior)**: `apps/cli/src/device-gaps.ts` (new) — derives, from a valued pull's bundled wrap rows, the missing epochs of the same person's other valid devices that are that environment's recipients (doesn't derive on old-server rows), wraps only the epochs it could open itself addressed to the siblings' keys, and registers them with this device's registration signature (`backfillEnvironmentFor` — AUTH §12-6's path #6). ### Additional rulings during review handling (K11-11 onward — responses to PR #198)

Options: (a) list per-environment commands with real ids, (b) the real id only when there's 1 environment, (c) as-is. A counterexample to (b): "on a 2-environment failure it's a placeholder again". **Adopted (a)**. Re-counted the nearby transcriptions: the `<environment>` placeholder remains only in G6 ("the resync does not show it yet" — the case where the device is invisible in the re-sync and its recipient environments can't be derived from the chain), and since the environment isn't known there it stays a placeholder (K11-5 counterexample 4). `devices.mdx`'s "the warnings print that command" became verbatim-true in the G5 case too with this fix. |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

The test confirms 1 on a scaffolding where neither revocation nor sweep occurs (the old reserve key isn't on the chain — a sweep failure can't mix into the exit code), and confirmed it fails under a mutation removing the check. The reasons: (i) wraps for every environment × every epoch of the target scope can exceed §12-8's request cap (DEK wrap count / request 10,000), so a single request's atomicity can't cover it; (ii) a shortfall can converge via §12-6's append path. The device-add backfill is "the new device's effective scope's every environment × every epoch" — the same shape, and the same reasons apply as-is. Atomizing it would make **adding a device itself impossible** on a project with many environments and epochs (an over-cap composite isn't accepted). (ii)'s convergence path is exactly K11's fill | — | — | When I raised owner point (3) (the completion record) I wrote "a §12-4-shaped composite request" without having read this §12-4 ruling (a violation of K6-X's 3 principles' "read the predicates and the canonical text before writing a norm" — recorded as a correction). Implementing it would require revising the spec to overturn §12-4's "immutable" ruling (CLAUDE.md: a spec change is implemented only after updating the spec and getting human approval) and a design for the over-cap case (splitting loses atomicity and returns to the original problem) | **Not implementing; re-confirming with the owner** (because the existing canonical ruling conflicts with this request's premise [that it can be atomized]). Recommendation: defer — the gap's duration remains, but K11's fill closes it as §12-4's assumed convergence path (§12-6's append) |
