G9 (a backfill failure for the new reserve key) is a Warning + exit code 1, same as approval / restore. Limitation (5) is resolved. For anyone reading only the list, read this line as the correction to the limitations list.
- **K11-17 (owner ruling — owner point (3) = A: deferred. 2026-09-25)**: the `add_device` + new-device-addressed wraps atomization is not done (per AUTH_SPEC §12-4's "the backfill is not composited (immutable)"). The options lined up in the re-examination and their conclusions: (B) single-request atomization — can exceed §12-8's cap (10,000 wraps / request); makes device addition impossible on a large project. (B') bundling only the current epoch — the count fits the cap by environment count, but pre-re-encryption values remain in the old epochs (§12-7), so it doesn't guarantee "readable right away"; needs a spec revision and a server change. (B'') **pre-staging** (deposit the wraps split up, activated at the same time as the chain append) — B's upward compat (keeps atomicity, avoids the cap), but needs new server state (the deposit, a deadline, capacity), wire, and a spec revision, and the gain is only shortening the abnormal-case gap's duration. (B''') **a KEK layer** (reducing the device-addressed wraps to 1 per environment, making atomization possible — the silver bullet) — a crypto-structure change, rejected at DK-E's E-3. **Grounds for deferring (fact check)**: in the normal case the gap is invisible to the new device — `device approve` runs in the order "append → backfill → registry PUT (the completion signal)", and the new device's `device add` waits for the signal (the consequence of K4-31's "the advisory comes after the truth"). A gap remains only in the abnormal cases (G1–G9), and K11's fill (§12-6's append path — §12-4's assumed convergence path) closes it. **The remaining inconvenience and a carryover**: in an abnormal-case gap, the new device notices only at the first valued pull's warning. As an upward-compat small improvement I proposed "a new device (`device add`) that received the completion signal fetches its own-addressed rows once per environment of each project's effective scope, and if a gap exists, emits the fill's path on the spot" (spec · wire · server unchanged. The K4-5 "confirm completion on the chain" extended to keys). Whether to implement it awaits the owner's decision (not implemented in this PR).
- **K11-18 (adding a re-examination candidate — 2026-09-25, a separate session's verification. Not implemented · owner ruling "record only")**: recording as a candidate the **history-key chain** (H below), which isn't among the options lined up in K11-17's re-examination (B / B' / B'' / B''').
  - **Content**: place, per environment × epoch, a 256-bit random history key HK_n **independent of** the DEK (generated at environment creation and `rotate_epoch`). The rotation's performer bundles into the rotation composite a link L_n = AES-256-GCM(HK_n, DEK_{n-1} ‖ HK_{n-1}, AAD = LP(domain, project_id, environment_id, n)) — the old epoch's (DEK, HK) wrapped under the new HK. Recipients receive **only the wrap of the current epoch's (DEK, HK)** and obtain old epochs by walking the links.
  - **Relation to K11-17's rejection reasons**: (B)'s cap excess — a device add is a count by environment number (effective scope ≤ 100 environments → ≤ 100 wraps, ~57 KB; a scope widening is 16 devices × 100 = 1,600) and fits §12-8 (10,000 / request), so §12-4's "not composited (immutable)" reason (i) (every environment × every epoch can exceed the cap — K11-15) disappears. (ii) (a shortfall can converge via §6.3's wrap-destination match check and §12-6's append path) also holds under H, but that's not a reason to not composite — it's a reason compositing isn't needed — and under H it doesn't obstruct compositing. (B')'s "pre-re-encryption values remain in old epochs, so 'readable right away' isn't guaranteed" — the link yields the old epoch's DEK, so it's guaranteed. (B''')'s KEK layer (DK-E's E-3 — a layer is added and a device revocation also rotates the KEK) — H needs no extra rotation on a device revocation (the existing `device-revoked` rotate obligation creates a new HK).
  - **Verification (read the spec, the server, four-eyes, the audit, the code — 4 parallel angles)**: no confidentiality counterexample found — per §7 "secrets don't aim for history confidentiality", recipients already hold every epoch, so HK_cur's reach is the same as today's. Deletion, post-revocation forward secrecy, per-environment scope, and the ghost-member ban are preserved. **Conditions** (holes if unmet): (1) HK isn't derived from the DEK (if HK leaks into a lease [AUTH §14-2 — the response's latest values use all epochs + only the current epoch's DEK], a CI job reads all history), (2) HK is per-environment, (3) HK's commitment goes on the chain (the `create_environment` / `rotate_epoch` payload), binding the link to the performer's signature, with no-overwrite and a repair path, (4) widen §5.2's DEK commitment match to DEKs obtained via links (otherwise rotation re-encryption signs and launders a forged old DEK's plaintext), (5) under four-eyes, attach the wraps to the `approve` that completes the application (attaching to `propose` hands over all history before quorum), (6) don't stop a rotation when the old HK can't be obtained (proceed link-less, with a warning — CRYPTO_SPEC §6.2's four-eyes-policy section "a rotate is incident response and mustn't be delayed". The same line exists in §7's device revocation and K4-7's g-2 [taking ES E]), (7) a granted server receives HK and walks links when issuing leases (the reach is the same as today's all-epoch backfill). **Remaining counterexamples**: a DEK-less voting-only device (DK-C's C-1 — deliberately allowed) completing a four-eyes application can't attach wraps on the spot → this path alone leaves a carryover (K11's fill). **What weakens**: a link is a shared 1-liner, so if it breaks, every later participant loses history (detected and repaired by condition 3). `dek.registered`'s meaning (who received which epoch) changes, and a link audit event is needed.
  - **Cost**: revisions of CRYPTO §2 / §3 / §5 / §5.1 / §5.2 / §6.2 / §6.3 / §6.4 / §7 / §9 / §11, AUTH §11-1 / §12-3 / §12-4 / §12-6 / §12-7 / §12-8 / §12-10 / §14-2, AUDIT §3.3. Vectors (a new `hk-link.json`, regenerating `dek-wrap*` / `chain-entries.json`, keeping `lease-wrap.json` unchanged). Implementation is roughly CLI 20 · server 15 · api-schema 7 · crypto 3–10 files, web 0. The heaviest paths are cross-environment composites (`add_device` / `add_member` / scope widening / `grant_server`) and the four-eyes completion-approval path. With no users at this point, no migration (coexistence with the old format) is needed.
  - **Reason for deferring**: in the normal case the gap is invisible to the new device, and abnormal-case gaps are closed by K11's fill (unchanged from K11-17's grounds). H's remaining advantage is eliminating the class of "partially key-less" states and making backfill cost not proportional to the epoch count — small now against the size of a crypto-spec revision. When the decision is made to simplify the crypto structure before users attach, start a design record from this record as a re-examination candidate of K11-17.

## 17. K12 addendum (2026-09-25 — collecting DK carryovers: confirming the new device's keys' arrival / guidance for re-adding a revoked device)

Collecting the "a new device that received the completion signal confirms the arrival of its own-addressed DEK wraps on the spot" (A below), proposed at K11-17 and awaiting the owner's decision, and K10's carryover "help / docs for 're-adding a revoked device needs `--replace`'" (B below). The owner's rulings (K12's prompt §0): **A = ON, B = ON**. Specs (CRYPTO / AUTH / AUDIT), ADRs, `CLAUDE.md`, `packages/crypto`, the server, wire formats, acceptance-policy values, and `own-devices.json`'s fields are untouched. The table uses K6-X's format (a blank is the mark of "not searched"). Rulings are recorded here before implementation, and mid-implementation judgments are added in the same shape as K12-10 onward.

**Pre-start check**: starting from `origin/main` (7365781 — K11-18's merge). Before this section there's no K12 section or record of the same task, and 0 open PRs on GitHub (no parallel work on the same task).

### K12 fact check A (the new device's keys' arrival — read the branches and the predicates' definitions)

1. **`device add`'s completion confirmation** (`device.ts` `deviceAddOp`): after the signal (`waitForRegistryRow` — the registry row with my FP), it checks `fetchProjectMemberships`'s each project via `deviceOnProjectChain`. That gets the verified chain via **`openMetadataProject` (the keyless prelude — doesn't go through `syncOwnDevices`)**, returns `members.get(userId).devices.has(fp)` as a boolean, and **discards the context**. A sync failure is false (= counted as unregistered). What's at hand: this device's `MasterKeys` (`deviceKeyForRequest`'s return — includes `encKeyPair`), `input.client`, and each project's verified chain (being discarded). **No own-addressed wrap is ever fetched**.
2. **Fetching and verifying own-addressed wraps** (`deks.ts`): the only fetch entry is `environmentKeysFor` — the current epoch (chain-derived) → the opening device = my own valid device matching the enc key at hand (`ownDeviceOrFail`) → fails if outside the effective scope → `cached` / `prefetched` / `client.deks.listMine` → `verifyAndUnwrapDeks`. The latter **narrows to rows where `recipientEncPubHex === my enc key` (or missing = old server)** (`mine`), puts only what passes the §5.1 registration signature, the phantom-epoch, the duplicate, and the §5.2 commitment checks into `deksByEpoch`, and **fails the whole if even 1 doesn't pass**. `listMine`'s response is server `listMyDekWrapsProgram` → `listWrapsForRecipient(environmentId, actor.userId)` (rows addressed to all of that person's devices — per K11 fact check 3), authorized by `requireEnvironmentAccess(…, "reader", …)` (the person's scope) + `requireActiveEnvironment` (a deleted environment is refused).
3. **The gap judgment's existing definition** (`pull.ts` `pullVariables`): counts epochs missing from `environmentKeysFor`'s `deksByEpoch` within 1–`currentEpoch`, producing the "no DEK wraps for you exist at epochs …" warning (`pulled.warnings` → `logWarnings`). The exit code doesn't change (a SHOULD warning). **The judgment's material is what passed verification and unwrap**, not rows' presence.
4. **The fill path's wording** (`device-gaps.ts`): `gapFillCommandOf` / `describeGapFillRoute` are the letter's only placement (K11-5). `fillOwnDeviceGaps` fills **siblings'** gaps — it can't fill if this device lacks the gapped DEK — so the new device itself can't be the fill's performer (same as K11-1's 1-f counterexample 3).
5. **The token's level**: `device add` always goes through `requestCreate` (`createOrResumeRequest` — called every time, including the resume and 409 paths), and the server's `requestCreate` handler (`handlers-devices.ts`) calls `ensureKeyMaterialAccess` (`authz.ts` — rejects session principals and passes only `*` × admin scope tokens) **first**. `deks.listMine`'s required level is read (AUTH §12-3's table "valued bulk pull · own-addressed DEK fetch | read | reader or above | environment ∈ scope", the handler's `permission: "read"`). So **a `device add` token that reached the signal always satisfies `listMine`** (a level-shortage failure can't occur).
6. **The approver's order and the corroboration of "in the normal case the gap is invisible"** (`deviceApproveOp`): phase 1 (every project's `planApproveOnProject`) → `refuseCapMismatch` → phase 2 (`appendOnProject` = `appendAddDevice` → `resync` → `backfillToDevice`, **per project**) → the "≥1 registered / already" gate → `store.record` → the registry PUT (the signal) → the request's cancel. Since the signal comes **after** the project loop fully finished, in the normal case (every environment's backfill succeeding) all wraps are registered at the signal's time (D1 / DO are strongly consistent). A gap is visible at the signal's time in: G1 (a per-environment failure — the PUT still runs) / G2 (a `resync` failure after the append etc. — folded to `failed`; if others are registered the PUT still runs. It's on the chain) / a re-run after G3 (an interruption → the re-run resolves at `already` and doesn't backfill → PUT) / G4 (a signing-side gap — same report as G1). **K11-17's deferral grounds (invisible in the normal case) and the remaining inconvenience (an abnormal gap goes unnoticed until the first pull) are still correct read through the branches**. In addition, on the path that re-runs `device add` after the signal (`already-registered` — the registry row is the signal), a long-ago-registered device's gap (including the member-side G10) lands on the same check.
7. **The backfill's target environments** (`device-ops.ts` `backfillToDevice`): excepting `verifiedDeletedEnvironmentSet` (the environment list GET + chain-verified deletions), the environments in the target device's effective scope (`effectivePermissionOf(member, device).scope`). **The arrival check's targets should be the same set** (a deleted environment is refused by `listMine`; an out-of-scope one is refused by `environmentKeysFor`).

→ A's premise ("today, in an abnormal case, a new device notices a gap only at the first valued `pull`'s warning") hasn't collapsed.

### K12 fact check B (a revoked device's `device add` — read the branches)

1. **The branches when a key exists** (`device.ts` `deviceKeyForRequest`): without `--replace` and with a key, `requestGet(fp)` (a request → resume waiting) → `fetchRegistry` (a registry row → continue via "verifying it on each project's chain") → neither → refuse "This machine already has a device key (…). If it is a copy of another device's key from an install before device keys, re-run with --replace: … Do not pass --replace if this is your only device …". With `--replace`, it deletes the keychain's key, emits "removed the copied key from this machine's keychain (--replace)", and makes a new key.
2. **The revocation's latter stage** (`deviceRevokeOp`): after `executeRevokeAll`, **only when `self` (the target is you)** it runs `finishOwnRevocation` (marks the local record revoked, DELETEs the registry row — a failure is swallowed by `Effect.catch`). Revoking another person's device (`--user`) doesn't delete the registry row (`if (!self) return`). **A revoked device's key stays in that machine's keychain** (revocation is a chain append that doesn't touch the machine. Even on a self-revocation, it only emits `revokeWarnings`' last warning and doesn't delete the key).
3. **The branches a revoked device falls into**:
   - **R1** (revoked from your other device — the usual): the registry row is gone → no request, no row → **refusal**. The refusal says only "if a copy, `--replace`" and doesn't say re-adding a revoked device needs `--replace` (the end of K10 fact check A's 7 — "a re-approval is actually a `--replace` new key" — the path is right but there's no guidance). The unrefused destination of `--replace` is correct: a new key → a request → approval (cap re-choosable).
   - **R2** (an admin / owner revoked with `--user`, or the registry DELETE failed on a self-revocation): the registry row remains → "already in your device registry — verifying" → the signal fires at round 1 → counted on the chain: 0 → "Approved: this device is registered on 0 projects" + "not registered yet on … — **the approving device skipped or failed on them** …". **No approval happened**, so the narrative is false (K6-J — the wording doesn't transcribe the shipped behavior). The chains carry `revoke_device`, and `device add` has already synced each chain at this point (the revocation is derivable with no extra communication — the same material as `device-sync.ts`'s `revokedFingerprintsOf`).
   - **R3** (revoked on only some projects via `--project`): still valid on the other projects. Under R1 it refuses; under R2, "registered on N" + the revoked projects mix into "not registered yet". There's no path to return the revoked key as the same key: `registerRecorded`'s candidates exclude `revokedHere`, and `device approve` needs a request (`device add` can't make one — 1). **Re-adding is a new key** (`--replace` → approval → if needed, revoke the old key on the remaining projects).
4. **`--replace`'s help** (`effect-cli.ts` :717 → `apps/cli/test/golden/help.txt` :775): "removes a copied key from this keychain — only for installs from before device keys". **It denies with "only" a real use — re-adding a revoked device**.
5. **Transcriptions of "revoke and re-add"** (grep — `apps/cli/src` · `apps/cli/test/golden` · `apps/site/docs` for "revoke and re-add" / "re-add it" / "--replace" / "installs from before device keys"): 4 transcriptions don't name the re-add procedure — (i) `device.ts` `refuseCapMismatch` "To give the device another cap, revoke it and re-add it instead", (ii) `device-sync.ts` `observeDevices`'s Note "if that machine should be on more projects, revoke it and re-add it as a new device", (iii) `devices.mdx` :50 "(revoke and re-add instead)", (iv) the same What-can-go-wrong cap item "To give a device another cap, revoke it and re-add it". 3 transcriptions close `--replace`'s use to "a copy" — (v) the refusal (1), (vi) help (4), (vii) `devices.mdx` What-can-go-wrong's refusal quotation and explanation "a copied key, or a key whose request expired", plus (viii) `--replace`'s Note "removed the **copied** key" (also emitted for a revoked key — false). **Non-transcriptions** (stay true): `devices.mdx` :67 (the expired-request `--replace` — an unregistered key), :129–136 (the copy section — true as a copy's procedure), `key-recover.ts` :398 · `docs/SELF_HOSTING.md` :990 (the copy's procedure), `rotation-sweep.ts`'s "has been re-added" (a sweep's counterpart — a different claim), `four-eyes.mdx`'s re-add (a member's re-addition — a different claim), `invite.ts` / `master-ops.ts` / `guardian.ts`'s re-adds (a member's / a guardian's re-addition).
6. **The distinction between a revocation and `key recover` / the reserve key**: the last device can't be revoked (`last-device-protected` — `revokeWarnings`' prelude), so **a revoked device is never "the only device"**. `--replace`'s caveat "don't run it on your only device (use `key recover` from the ledger)" doesn't contradict the revocation case and is kept as-is.

→ B's premise ("`--replace`'s help and refusal explain it as 'only for a copy from a pre-device-key install', and docs' 'revoke and re-add' writes no procedure") hasn't collapsed. Additionally R2 (a revoked device whose registry row remains receives a false narrative) was found.

### K12-1. A — when to check and on what

| Option | Content | Upward compat | Silver bullet | Counterexamples | Adoption |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1-a Do nothing | Status quo (the first pull's warning informs) | — | — | Owner ruling A = ON. Counterexample "someone who set up a new device sees only `device add`'s output and walks away, and that device only runs `run` in CI or scripts" — `run` emits the same warning too (`effect-cli.ts`'s `run` also does `logWarnings(pulled.warnings)`), but it appears inside a later execution's log, after leaving the setup moment (K11-17's remaining inconvenience) | Rejected |
| 1-b **After the chain confirmation, for each project this device is on, check the same environment set as the backfill (excluding deleted ones · this device's effective scope)** | Syncs are shared with the chain confirmation (no extra sync) | Have `deviceOnProjectChain` return the verified context it was discarding and reuse it (decide "is it on" and "which environments" on the same view — extending K4-5's "confirm completion on the chain" to keys on the same view) | **Get the target set from the same function as `backfillToDevice`** (move the enumeration into a function both call — "the set the approver should have distributed" and "the set checked" match structurally) | Counterexample 1 "a deleted environment" — excluded by the same enumeration. A deletion after the enumeration (a race) is refused by `listMine` → reported as a check failure (not called a gap). Counterexample 2 "an environment outside this device's cap" — doesn't enter the enumeration (not a recipient). Counterexample 3 "a project it's not on" — not checked (handled by the existing "not registered yet"). Counterexample 4 "the environment list GET fails" — reported as a project that couldn't be checked (not silent) | **Adopted** (with the upward compat and silver bullet) |
| 1-c Check right after the signal, before the chain confirmation | Earlier | — | — | The targets (the effective scope · the environment set) are decided only by the chain. It would sync twice | Rejected |
| 1-d Check the person's scope's (not the device's cap's) every environment | Simple | — | — | Counterexample "a narrow-cap device" — falsely reports a non-recipient environment as a "gap" (a must-not-pass input passes) | Rejected |

**Round 2**: upward compat = reusing the context (the table). Silver bullet = sharing the enumeration (the table). Counterexamples = 1 for 1-a, 4 for 1-b, 1 each for 1-c / 1-d. **Round 3**: option tried "limit the check to 'projects newly registered by this approval'" → the requester doesn't know the approver's results (registered / already) (the registry is advisory — K7-15). It can't distinguish, and the gap's fact is the same either way. No new options. Option tried "use `openProject` (the keyed prelude) inside `device add`" → `syncOwnDevices`' observation · registration (K4-3's writers) would run on the new device. No reason to open a write path on a check-only occasion (the keyless prelude + the key at hand suffice — fact check A's 1). No new options. **Round 4**: option tried "check every project in parallel" → the existing chain confirmation is sequential, and the cost (K12-4) is a few GETs proportional to the environment count. Parallelization doesn't match the surface change. No new options. Stopped.

**Principle**: "**the completion check happens on the same verified view the confirmation used, under the same predicate as the set the approver should have distributed**" — K4-5 ✓ (the signal is the registry; completion is the chain — the keys' arrival is checked on the set the chain defines), K7-13 ✓ (rides the existing enumeration and prelude), DK-D ✓ (the targets are the chain's), K4-3 ✓ (doesn't read or write the record — the keyless prelude). **Adopted: 1-b**. What makes it superior: unlike 1-a the noticing happens on `device add`'s screen, unlike 1-c no extra sync, unlike 1-d no false report on a narrow-cap device.

### K12-2. A — how to check and the gap's definition

| Option | Content | Upward compat | Silver bullet | Counterexamples | Adoption |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2-a Count `listMine`'s rows (epochs of rows for this device's enc key — unverified) | No crypto operations | — | — | Counterexample 1 "the server distributes a row whose signature fails · a poison wrap" — the row exists so it says "delivered", but pull fails at §5.1 / §5.2 (the judgment diverges from pull). Counterexample 2 (K4-5) — the declaration is used to judge "delivered" | Rejected |
| 2-b **Judge by the epochs that pass `environmentKeysFor` (the §5.1 / §5.2 verification and unwrap)** = the same material as pull's warning | "Delivered" = "this device could open". A poison wrap etc. surfaces as a check failure in the same wording (`verifyAndUnwrapDeks`'s refusal text) | **Move the gap computation (epochs within 1–current missing from `deksByEpoch`) into a function that pull and `device add` both call** (structurally one definition of a gap) | "Anticipating pull's warning" = judging by the same function and same material as pull leaves structurally no input on which `device add`'s report and the first pull's warning disagree | Counterexample 1 (cost) "an HPKE unwrap per epoch" — bounded computation of environments × epochs (K12-4). Counterexample 2 "opening DEKs into memory" — opened DEKs stay `Redacted`, used only for judgment, never leave the function (K11-8's principle) · written nowhere (the diskless invariant). Counterexample 3 "`listMine` 403s on an environment outside this device's scope" — can't happen since the enumeration is limited to effective scope ⊆ the person's scope (if it did, reported as a check failure) | **Adopted** (with the upward compat and silver bullet) |
| 2-c Point the sibling-side derivation (`device-gaps.ts`'s `ownDeviceGapsOf`) at yourself | An existing function | — | — | Its target is "devices other than yourself" and its material is rows (declarations) — carries 2-a's counterexamples as-is | Rejected |

**Round 2**: upward compat · silver bullet = sharing the gap function (the table). Counterexamples = 2 for 2-a, 3 for 2-b, 1 for 2-c. **Round 3**: option tried "count environments whose verification failed as 'gaps'" → a failure's causes (a poison wrap · the network) and a gap (never distributed) are fixed differently (the former by admin repair, the latter by a sibling's pull). Mixing them makes the guidance false. A check failure is reported in a separate sentence with the cause as-is (K12-3). No new options. **Round 4**: option tried "check only the current epoch (a past-epoch gap is only a history-value problem)" → pull's warning counts all epochs (§7's all-epoch distribution). Splitting the definition breaks K12-2's silver bullet. No new options. Stopped.

**Principle**: "**the same fact is judged by the same function on the same material (an anticipatory report structurally agrees with the warning that would come later)**" — K7-15 / K6-R ✓ (unifying transcriptions of the same claim — the judgment itself, not just the wording), K4-5 ✓ (the declaration is only the fetch's entrance; the judgment is what passed verification), CRYPTO §5.1 / §5.2 ✓. **Adopted: 2-b**.

### K12-3. A — the output when a gap exists / couldn't be checked

| Option | Content | Upward compat | Silver bullet | Counterexamples | Adoption |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 3-a **A gap is a Warning (1 line per environment), exit code 0. The wording is built by the same function as pull's warning, with "project / environment" prepended. An environment that couldn't be checked is a Note (the cause as-is). With no gap, nothing is added** | The same weight and same words as pull (the user lands on the same item in docs' What can go wrong) | The gap wording ends with the fill's path (`describeGapFillRoute` — real ids' `maruhi pull --project <id> --env <env>`) (pull's warning already does — sharing it satisfies this) | Make the letter 1 function (move pull's warning into a function that `device add` calls — K11-5's "the letter in 1 place") | Counterexample 1 "should be exit code 1" — `device add`'s job (confirming registration) succeeded, and pull is 0 with the same warning. Making it 1 makes a script read "the device wasn't added". Counterexample 2 "the new device itself can't fill" — the guidance is a pull on a sibling device (the path's sentence states the subject "A registered device of yours whose cap covers …" — it doesn't say run it on this device). Counterexample 3 "the gap's cause is the member side (G10)" — the shared wording lists both the member and device cases (as pull's warning does — K11-5 round 3). Counterexample 4 "many environments → many lines" — only gapped environments are shown (0 lines in the normal case) | **Adopted** (with the upward compat and silver bullet) |
| 3-b Exit code 1 if a gap exists | Automation can notice | — | — | 3-a's counterexample 1 | Rejected |
| 3-c Also emit "keys verified for N environments" when there's no gap | The check having run is visible | — | — | Counterexample "the wording branches when some environments couldn't be checked (all / some / 0)" — grows the normal-case output, and a 2nd completion sentence appears next to "Approved: …"'s completion sentence (which docs quote). The check's existence is described by docs (K12-9) | Rejected |
| 3-d A `device add`-specific wording ("the approver's backfill didn't complete") | Fits the moment | — | — | Counterexample 1 "a gap seen on an `already-registered` re-run can be member-side (G10)" — closing the cause to 1 is false (the same shape as K7-15). Counterexample 2 "different words from pull's warning" (K7-15) | Rejected |

**Ordering**: the "Approved: …" completion sentence → key gaps on carried projects (Warning) and uncheckable environments (Note) → revoked projects (K12-6) → the existing "not registered yet" (Note). The completion sentence is about the carried projects, and the gap is its breakdown, so it's placed right after.

**Round 2**: upward compat · silver bullet = making the wording a function (the table). Counterexamples = 4 for 3-a, 1–2 each for 3-b–3-d. **Round 3**: option tried "collapse the gapped environments into 1 line" → the path's command carries real ids per environment (K11-11 — emits no placeholder). Listing them on 1 line only lengthens the sentence. No new options. **Round 4**: option tried "make the couldn't-check Note a Warning" — being uncheckable isn't a degraded state (the next pull runs the same judgment). A poison wrap fails pull hard (visible at that point). A Note with the cause suffices. No new options. Stopped.

**Principle**: "**an anticipatory report is emitted at the same weight, in the same words, on the same path as the warning that would come later, and doesn't change the command's success**" — K11-5 ✓ (the letter in 1 place), K10-5 ✓ (a path the predicates permit — a sibling's pull), K4-3 counterexample 5 ✓ (an accompaniment doesn't change the command's success), K6-J ✓, ADR-0017 ✓. **Adopted: 3-a**.

### K12-4. A — gate, environments, failures, cost

| Option | Content | Upward compat | Silver bullet | Counterexamples | Adoption |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 4-a **No gate (same as `device add` — DK-D). Every check failure folds into a Note; `device add`'s success is unchanged** | Checked even by `device add` in containers · CI · agents | — | "The check adds nothing and displays nothing (no values or keys — only epoch numbers and environment ids)", so the gate's protected objects (adding a signer · displaying a value) are absent | Counterexample 1 (agent environment) — the output is environment ids and epoch numbers (no values · key material — CLAUDE.md's logging discipline). Counterexample 2 (old server) — `device add` itself requires a device-key server (`DeviceOpsNotAccepted`). An old-format row without `recipientEncPubHex` is opened by `verifyAndUnwrapDeks` treating everything as own-addressed, and if any other-device-addressed row exists the unwrap fails → reported as a check failure (not wrongly called a "gap"). Counterexample 3 (token) — per fact check A's 5 the level is always sufficient. A revoked / expired token (401) fails before the signal's wait | **Adopted** |
| 4-b Check only inside the ceremony gate (TTY + non-agent) | — | — | — | With no protected object, only devices added in a container don't get the check (the same counterexample as K11-2's 2-b) | Rejected |

**Cost**: per carried project, 1 environment-list GET (`verifiedDeletedEnvironmentSet`) + 1 `listMine` per effective-scope environment + per-epoch unwraps and matches. Syncs are shared with the chain confirmation (0 added). Since `device add` is a once-per-device operation, it doesn't affect ordinary keyed-command cost.

**Round 2**: silver bullet = re-asking from what the gate protects (the table). Counterexamples = 3 for 4-a, 1 for 4-b. **Round 3**: option tried "a flag like `--no-check`" → the check is idempotent and writes nothing; there's no reason to stop it. Adds no surface (K10-5). No new options. **Round 4**: option tried "stop the remaining projects' checks on a check failure" → one failure mustn't silence the rest (the same discipline as `backfillEachEnvironment`). No new options. Stopped.

**Principle**: "**the gate goes on operations that have a protected object. A read-only check runs outside the gate, and a failure is reported as a fact**" — K11-2 ✓, DK-D ✓, ADR-0016 decision 7 ✓ (it's not a value display). **Adopted: 4-a**.

### K12-5. B — the refusal wording and `--replace`'s help (2 uses)

| Option | Content | Upward compat | Silver bullet | Counterexamples | Adoption |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 5-a Do nothing | — | — | — | Owner ruling B = ON. Help's "only for installs from before device keys" denies a real use (re-adding a revoked device) — false | Rejected |
| 5-b **Make the refusal, help, and `--replace`'s Note true for both "a revoked device" and "a copy"** (re-adding a revoked device is `--replace` = approving a new key. A copy keeps the key on the original device. Keep the only-device caveat) | Adds no branch or sync | `--replace`'s Note "removed the **copied** key" → "removed the previous key" (also true for a revoked key). The refusal says "approve the new fingerprint on a registered device (cap re-choosable)" (what's needed after `--replace` — K10-5 "transcribe the path's preconditions into the wording") | — | Counterexample 1 "running `--replace` on a device revoked on only some projects (R3)" — a key still valid on the remaining projects disappears from the keychain (it stays on the chains as a key nobody can use). → tie the wording's "a revoked device" condition **to the fact of revocation** ("revoked on your projects"), and docs write the procedure of revoking the old key on the remaining projects after re-adding (K12-9). Counterexample 2 "the only device" — the last device can't be revoked (fact check B's 6), so no contradiction with the revocation case. Counterexample 3 "a key of an expired request" — the existing expiry wording already guides `--replace` (stays true). The refusal just enumerates uses and doesn't conflict with the expiry guidance | **Adopted** (with the upward compat) |
| 5-c In the refusal branch, sync every project and vary the wording by the key's state (revoked / nowhere / carried) | The user needn't distinguish | — | — | Counterexample 1 "adding an N-project sync to the refusal path" — same class of surface design as K7's owner point (2) (re-creating with the same key if on no chain after expiry — adding a branch and a sync); steps into a matter awaiting the owner's decision. Counterexample 2 "a copy (pre-DK) and a partial revocation (R3) are indistinguishable on the chain" — both are "this key is carried somewhere" (a copied key is the original device's key itself). Even distinguished, a human's judgment remains | Rejected (put to the owner — together with K7 (2)) |

**Round 2**: upward compat = the Note's wording · the approval procedure (the table). Silver bullet = "make the distinction unnecessary" — 5-b enumerates the uses and hands the distinction itself to the human, making both sentences true (the only needed distinction is "may I `--replace`", whose predicate is "may I let go of this key" = not the only device + it's a copy or revoked). Counterexamples = 3 for 5-b, 2 for 5-c. **Round 3**: option tried "make `--replace` unnecessary for a revoked device (auto re-create if the key is revoked)" → judging revocation needs a sync (5-c's counterexample 1), and it would discard a key implicitly (K4-18 "a key is discarded only when a human types `--replace`"). No new options. **Round 4**: option tried "a separate flag for a revoked device (e.g. `--readd`)" → the behavior is the same as `--replace` (discard the key and make one). Adds no surface (K10-5). No new options. Stopped.

**Principle**: "**if the same operation serves 2 situations, the guidance names both situations and transcribes the operation's precondition (may the key be let go). If a machine can't distinguish the situations, make both true with a sentence that hands the distinction to the human**" — K10-5 ✓, K6-J ✓, K4-18 ✓ (a key's discard is only the explicit `--replace`), K4-21 ✓ (the refusal's condition is unchanged). **Adopted: 5-b**.

### K12-6. B — distinguishing a revoked device whose registry row remains (R2)

| Option | Content | Upward compat | Silver bullet | Counterexamples | Adoption |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 6-a Do nothing (R2 receives "approving device skipped or failed") | — | — | — | No approval happened. A false narrative (K6-J). The user hunts for approver-side output that doesn't exist | Rejected |
| 6-b **On the chain confirmation's same view, derive whether this key is revoked on that project (the target of an applied `revoke_device`); a revoked project leaves "not registered yet" and is emitted as a separate Note (a revoked key doesn't return; re-adding is `maruhi device add --replace` + approval)** | No extra communication (K12-1's same view) | Make the revocation derivation the same function as `device-sync.ts`'s `revokedFingerprintsOf` (unify the predicate excluding it from record candidates and the predicate calling it revoked here — structurally) | "Return the check's result as 3 values (carried / revoked / absent) instead of a boolean" — 1 function carries both A's context (carried) and B's distinction (revoked) | Counterexample 1 (does it touch K7's owner point (3)) — K7 (3) is "the requester derives the approver's cause (the approving device's cap / absence)", an inference about the approver's state. 6-b only reads **a fact carried on the chain about this key itself** (a `revoke_device` addressed to it) and doesn't infer the approver. Judged outside the scope. Counterexample 2 "the same key re-added after revocation" — if it's actually carried, "carried" wins (the revoked set isn't consulted). Counterexample 3 "a sync failure" — "absent" as before (not called revoked). Counterexample 4 "R1 (the refusal path)" — no sync happens there. The refusal (K12-5) names the revocation case | **Adopted** (with the upward compat and silver bullet) |
| 6-c Even when the registry row signals, if revoked on every project, don't emit "Approved" and end in failure | Removes the completion sentence's falsehood | — | — | Counterexample "revoked on only some projects (R3)" — both the completion sentence and the revocation Note are needed, and the exit code and sentences would branch on all / some. The completion sentence ("registered on 0 projects") is true as a count; the revocation Note suffices to give the reason | Rejected |

**Round 2**: upward compat = sharing the predicate (the table). Silver bullet = the 3-valued result (the table). Counterexamples = 4 for 6-b, 1 each for 6-a / 6-c. **Round 3**: option tried "in `device revoke --user`'s latter stage, also delete the other person's registry row (cut R2's source)" → the registry is the person's own and the server doesn't accept deleting someone else's row (AUTH §13-11 — device-scoped, self only). A server change, out of scope. Since R2 also arises from a self-revocation's DELETE failure, the distinction is needed even if the source is cut. No new options. **Round 4**: option tried "let a revoked key's `device add` put out a request again" → returning a revoked key as the same key contradicts the record's discipline (`own-devices.ts` "Never re-added" · `registrationCandidates`' `revokedHere`) and could undo the revocation's reason (theft · leak). No new options. Stopped.

**Principle**: "**what the requester can state is only facts on the requester's view. It states a chain-carried fact about this key itself, and doesn't infer the approver's state**" — K7-15 ✓ (revocation was added to the branches building the `missing` set and read), K6-J ✓, K4-5 ✓ (confirmed on the chain), K7 owner point (3) ✓ (doesn't infer the approver — outside the scope). **Adopted: 6-b**.

### K12-7. B — how to align the "revoke and re-add" transcriptions

| Option | Content | Upward compat | Silver bullet | Counterexamples | Adoption |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 7-a Fix each transcription's wording | Small | — | — | Next time the procedure changes, some transcriptions go stale (a K6-R recurrence) | Rejected |
| 7-b **Build the CLI's re-add procedure letter from 1 function (`device-key.ts`'s `reAddDeviceRoute`), called by the CLI's transcriptions (K12 fact check B's 5's (i)(ii) and K12-6's Note). In docs, the procedure lives in 1 place (a paragraph in the Revoke section); the rest link to it** | The CLI is structurally 1 phrase; docs follow K6-R (the normative claim in 1 place) | The placement is a lower module both `device.ts` and `device-sync.ts` already read (creates no cycle) | — | Counterexample 1 "docs' transcription drifts from the CLI" — the What-can-go-wrong quotation is matched by K10-6's net. The paragraph's procedure (`--replace` → approval) is caught by the help golden's flag and `cli-vocabulary.test.ts`'s command-path net. Counterexample 2 "in the cap refusal's context, 'choose the cap at the re-approval' is needed" — the caller appends it after (the function builds only the procedure) | **Adopted** (with the upward compat) |

**Round 2**: upward compat = the placement (the table). Silver bullet = making the letter a function (the table — same shape as K11-5). Counterexamples = 2 for 7-b, 1 for 7-a. **Round 3**: option tried "build the refusal (K12-5) from the same function too" → the refusal is a `--replace` typed on this machine ("re-run with --replace"), whose subject differs from the function's procedure ("on that machine"). Align the phrase (`maruhi device add --replace` + approval) but keep the sentences separate. No new options. **Round 4**: option tried "build help's description from the same function too" → help is a static description in `effect-cli.ts` whose letter is pinned by the golden. Building it from the function doesn't change the golden's meaning (pinning the shipped letter), but help's sentence is a use enumeration, not a procedure. No new options. Stopped.

**Principle**: re-applying K11-5's "**guidance names only paths the predicates permit, and the letter is built in 1 place**" ✓, K6-R ✓, K7-15 ✓ (every transcription was counted — fact check B's 5). **Adopted: 7-b**.

### K12-8. The check's placement

- **Existing checks grepped before placing**: `device.test.ts`'s `maruhi device add` (the `--replace` Note · the completion sentence · "not registered yet"), `refuseCapMismatch`'s "revoke it and re-add it instead" (:845), `observeDevices`'s "revoke it and re-add it as a new device" (:1102), `pull-run.test.ts`'s missing-epochs warning, `apps/cli/test/golden/help.txt`, `cli-vocabulary.test.ts` (K10-6's net · the command-path net). **No check holds the property of the new device's key-arrival check or the revoked-project distinction**.

| Option | Content | Upward compat | Silver bullet | Counterexamples | Adoption |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 8-a **Add to `device.test.ts`'s `maruhi device add`**: must-pass inputs — (1) epoch 2 addressed to this device missing → a Warning with pull's same sentence and the real-id path, exit code 0, 0 DEK-wrap POSTs (the new device doesn't fill), (2) a revoked project → the revocation Note carries `maruhi device add --replace`, and it doesn't appear in "not registered yet". Must-fail inputs — (3) every epoch arrived → no Warning (`listMine` is called — non-vacuous), (4) an environment outside the cap → `listMine` not called · no Warning, (5) `listMine` failing → a Note (check failure), not a Warning (gap), exit code 0. The refusal · help · Note's wordings update the existing tests' expectations to the new letters (near-exact `toContain`s) | Pins the properties (whose · which environment's · which epochs) | The rows are wrapped and signed with real crypto (`wrapDekFor` + `recipientEncPubHex`) — the judgment (K12-2) distinguishing a passing row from a failing one actually runs | Wording agreement is pinned by structure (the shared function); the tests check 1 representative letter each | Counterexample 1 (coincidental match) "watching only whether a Warning appears, a Warning for another reason passes" — look at the sentence's body, the epoch number, and the `--env`'s real id. Counterexample 2 "(3)(4) pass even if the check doesn't run" — (3) watches for exactly 1 `listMine` GET, (4) for 0 | **Adopted** (with the upward compat) |
| 8-b A negation nail ("only for installs from before device keys" must be absent) | — | — | — | Doesn't hit a rewording (same as K11-6's 6-b). The help golden pins the letter | Rejected |

**Round 2**: upward compat = real crypto (the table). Counterexamples = 2 for 8-a, 1 for 8-b. **Round 3**: option tried "confirm the function sharing in `pull-run.test.ts`'s warning test" → the existing expectations (the warning's first half and the device sentence) pin the letter, and that it stays unchanged after being moved into a function is shown by that check. No new options. **Round 4**: option tried "a check on docs' new paragraph" → the What-can-go-wrong quotations go through K10-6's net, and commands and flags are caught by the command-path net. No new net needed. No new options. Stopped.

**Principle**: "**a check pins properties (to whom, what, how many); transcription agreement is pinned by structure**" (re-applying K11-6) ✓, K8-2 ✓. **Adopted: 8-a**.

### K12-9. docs (`apps/site/docs/devices.mdx`)

- **A**: Add a device's completion-signal paragraph gains "after completion, for each environment covered by the carried projects' caps, check whether every epoch's key arrived, and if a gap exists emit the same warning as pull plus the filling command (with no gap, nothing is added)". What can go wrong's refusal quotation and explanation move to the new wording, the cap item's "revoke it and re-add it" becomes a link, and an item for R2's Note is added. `--replace`'s copy section (:129–136) is true as the copy procedure, so unchanged.
- **Principle**: K6-R (the procedure lives in 1 place; the rest link) ✓, K10-6 (quotations go through the net) ✓, ADR-0017 ✓.

| K12-2 | A: how to check and the gap's definition | 2-b: judge by the epochs passing `environmentKeysFor`'s verification and unwrap; the gap computation is the same function as pull | Structurally no input where it disagrees with the first pull's warning | The same fact is judged by the same function on the same material |
| K12-3 | A: the output | 3-a: a gap is a Warning (the same wording function as pull + project / environment), a check failure is a Note, exit code 0, nothing added when there's no gap | Same words · same weight · same path, without changing success | An anticipatory report uses the later warning's words |

| K12-6 | B: a revoked device whose registry row remains | 6-b: derive the revocation on the same view (the same function as `revokedFingerprintsOf`); out of "not registered yet", and guide the re-add | Removes the false narrative with no extra communication | The requester states only facts on its own view |
| K12-7 | B: aligning the transcriptions | 7-b: the CLI uses the 1 function `reAddDeviceRoute`; docs use 1 paragraph in the Revoke section + links | A procedure change won't strand transcriptions | The letter in 1 place (K11-5) |
| K12-8 | The check | 8-a: positive and negative cases in `device.test.ts` (real-crypto rows) | Pins properties; vacuity denied by GET counts | Checks pin properties; transcriptions, structure |

(b) Separate the check (`confirmOnChains` — returns the 3-valued standing and the key-arrival facts) from the report (`reportConfirmation` / `reportKeyReachIssue`); `deviceAddOp` emits only the completion sentence | The check returns a facts-only type (`ChainConfirmation` / `KeyReachIssue`) and only the reporting side builds wording (the same shape as K11's `OwnDeviceGapFill` — K4-35 "facts travel by type") | Replace the old `deviceOnProjectChain` (boolean) with the 3-valued `keyStandingOnProject`, returning the context undiscarded (K12-1's upward compat becomes the function's shape as-is) | Counterexample to (a): new fallow complexity / function-length findings, and mixing word-making into the middle of the check (a wording change would touch the judgment's function) | **(b)**. Principle "the judgment returns facts; only the report builds wording" — K4-35 ✓, K11-8 ✓ |
| K12-11 (a procedure record) | The mutation that survived mutation testing | Mutation M2 (ignoring the cap and checking every environment) **survived** the first check: an environment outside the cap fails at `environmentKeysFor`'s scope check before the GET, so both the GET count (0) and "no gap warning" were unchanged, and the only thing emitted was a "could not check" Note. What the check was watching was only the GET count of K12-8's counterexample 2 — **it wasn't watching "doesn't even say a check failed"**. Added `not.toContain("could not check")` to the outside-cap test, and confirmed M2 fails | — | — | Counterexample "I meant to deny vacuity by the GET count, but the failure path came before the GET" — a vacuity denial holds only by watching **the outputs over every path the mutation can take** | Principle "a check's counterexamples watch the output per path a mutation can take (here, the pre-GET failure)" — concretizing K8-2. Found because the mutations were run (a record of K11's §3-7 procedure working) |

Listing it unconditionally in the refusal would break the expiry wording's conditioning. Counterexample to (b) "someone who re-typed after expiry reads the refusal" — the refusal just enumerates uses and doesn't deny the expiry case. docs' What-can-go-wrong explanation lists "a key whose request expired" and carries the T3 caveat ("Do not replace a key the approving device registered …") in the same item | **(b)**. Principle "a situation whose guidance carries conditions isn't listed in a sentence that can't carry the condition" — K10-5 ✓ (transcribing the path's preconditions), K9-3 ✓ |

The `--replace` copy section is unchanged (true as the copy procedure).

Rows are wrapped and signed with real crypto and carry `recipientEncPubHex` (a sibling-owner-addressed row is mixed in — counting only this device's rows is also exercised). 3 existing expectations moved to the new letters (the refusal · the `--replace` Note · the cap refusal · `observeDevices`'s Note). The 1 duplicate group [`device.ts`'s `deviceKeyForRequest`'s key generation and `key-recover.ts`] is inherited from main — the audit also excludes it as "inherited") / `doctor` (exit 0 — no web change) / `test` (143 files, 4990 tests — main's 4984 +6 = `device.test.ts` +6). `apps/site`: `validate --strict` (no broken links) → `build` → `e2e` (11 tests — `/opt/pw-browsers`'s Chromium 1194 aliased in a scratch area to the revision-1243 name Playwright expects, via `PLAYWRIGHT_BROWSERS_PATH`. `playwright install` was not run). The site's unit tests (including K10-6's net) are 113.
- **Points put to the owner**: (1) **Distinguishing revocation on the refusal path** (K12-5's 5-c): since a revoked device whose registry row is gone (R1 — the usual case) refuses without syncing, the CLI doesn't distinguish revocation from a copy (the sentence is true for both). Distinguishing would need an all-projects sync added to the refusal path — the same class of surface design as K7's owner point (2) (re-creating with the same key if on no chain after expiry — adding a branch and a sync), so the decision is asked together. (2) **R2's completion sentence** (K12-6's 6-c): even a revoked device whose registry row remains gets "Approved: this device is registered on 0 projects" (true as a count; the revocation Note gives the reason). Whether to vary the "Approved" word by the signal's source (an approval vs a leftover row) would change the completion sentence docs quotes, so it was left alone.
- **Carryovers (out of scope — separate tasks)**: (1) `device revoke --user` (an admin revoking another's device) can't delete the other person's registry row (the server deletes only one's own row — `handlers-devices.ts`'s `remove`), so R2 structurally remains. Cutting the source needs a server change. (2) K11's completion-record carryovers (K5 owner points (1)(2), K2 carryover 4's reinforcement, K7 owner points (3)(4)(5), K8 owner point (2), K9 owner points (2)(3), §11 W-3 / W-4 / W-6) are kept. K10's carryover "help / docs for 're-adding a revoked device needs `--replace`'" was collected this round.
- **Known limitations**: (1) the key-arrival check runs once at the signal's time; a gap arising later (G10 etc.) isn't reported by `device add` (the valued pull's warning reports it — same as before). (2) The check's cost is GETs and unwraps proportional to the carried projects' environment counts (a once-per-device operation). (3) If an old-format row (no `recipientEncPubHex`) mixes into sibling-addressed ones, `verifyAndUnwrapDeks` tries to open it as own-addressed, fails, and reports "couldn't be checked" rather than a gap (can't happen on a device-key server). (4) R1's refusal doesn't distinguish revocation from a copy (owner point (1)).

### Additional rulings during review handling (K12-14 onward — responses to PR #201)

- **K12-14 (pullfrog — the completion sentence is delayed until after the key-arrival check)**: `confirmOnChains` ran `checkKeyReach` (the environment-list GET + per-environment `listMine` and unwraps) inside the project loop, and only after that emitted "Approved: …". The completion sentence needs only the standing (the 3 values). **Confirmed correct in the source**. Options: (a) split the standing scan from the arrival check and emit the completion sentence between them (`ChainConfirmation.registered` carries the context [the `present` standing] and `reportConfirmation` checks and reports), (b) as-is. A counterexample to (b): "on a project with many environments, a stretch appears where the wait is over but the completion sentence isn't out (damages the signal's experience — K4-5)". **Adopted (a)** (the output order stays K12-3's — completion → gap / check failure → revocation → unregistered). An extension of K12-10's principle (the judgment returns facts; only the report builds wording): "the confirmation's order emits the fact the user is waiting for first".
- **K12-15 (pullfrog — "the projects above" has no referent)**: the revocation Note's "revoke it on the projects above that still list it" has no referent, since what's emitted above is "Approved: … on N projects" (just a count) (the gap Warning appears only when epochs are missing). **Confirmed correct in the source**. Options: (a) list the carried projects' ids, (b) defer to `maruhi device list`. A counterexample to (b): "deferring to a placeholder when the ids are at hand" (the same shape as K11-11 — a known id is emitted as a real id). **Adopted (a)**. The test expectation also uses real ids.
- **K12-16 (pullfrog — docs' `###` lands under "The reserve key")**: "Add a revoked machine again" was placed right before "## Installs from before device keys", but the preceding `##` was "The reserve key", so the navigation nested it under the reserve-key section. The insertion position wasn't checked against the `##` headings' order. **Correct**. Moved to the Revoke section's tail (right before "## The reserve key") (the anchor unchanged — confirmed by `validate --strict`).
- **pullfrog's opinion on owner decision (2)**: in R2, "Approved: … 0 projects" immediately followed by "this key was revoked on …" reads as a contradiction; there's value in rewording the first sentence when `registered.length === 0 && revoked.length > 0`, though since a similar option was rejected at K12-6, it's the owner's wording call. Kept in the completion record's owner point (2) (unchanged in this PR).

## 18. K13 addendum (2026-09-26 — collecting DK carryovers: judging a key's standing by the chain, and the branches of device add · everyday commands · device list · --replace)

Collecting K12's (PR #201 — `80a28ff`) completion-record "points put to the owner" (1) (can't distinguish a revocation from a pre-DK copy on the refusal path) and (2) (a revoked device with a leftover registry row gets "Approved: … 0 projects"). The previous session's exploration found both stem from **`device add` branching on the existing key's standing by advisories (the request row · the registry row) before looking at the chain** (K4-21 (c)). The structural solution is "**judge the key's standing from the chain in a single function, and derive every path's branch holding that same question from it**". The owner's rulings (K13's prompt §0): **the final §2 option = ON; C (re-creating a request with the same key, e.g. after expiry — K8-6 / K9) stays OFF; the option of putting the new key in a separate keychain item and swapping after approval = not adopted**. Specs (CRYPTO / AUTH / AUDIT), ADRs, `CLAUDE.md`, `packages/crypto`, the server, wire formats, acceptance-policy values, `own-devices.json`'s fields, and the floor's persistence format are unchanged. **K4-21 (c) ("with a key present: resume if a request row or registry row exists, else refuse") is revised in this section** (the resume decision is made on the chain, not the registry — K13-9). The table uses K6-X's format (a blank is the mark of "not searched"). Rulings are recorded here before implementation, and mid-implementation judgments are added in the same shape as K13-12 onward.

**Pre-start check**: starting from `origin/main` (80a28ff — K12's merge). Before this section there's no K13 section or record of the same task, and 0 open PRs on GitHub (no parallel work on the same task).

**Principle (this round's axis)**: "**the branch is split not on an undecidable cause (a copy or the original device) but on a decidable safety predicate (may this key be discarded)**".

### Record of the previous session's exploration (provenance: the previous session — fact-checked again in this session)

**Holes that were plugged** (so implementation won't recur them):

| Hole | Content | Upward compat | Silver bullet | Counterexamples | Adoption |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Hole 1 [severe] the provenance rule's direction | "If anywhere it's `add_device`-provenanced, it's an approved device" is wrong. The first sync's registration (`device-sync.ts` `registrationCandidates`) `add_device`s even a key **only recorded by observation** onto other projects, so a pre-DK key K can become `add_device`-provenanced on a post-DK project | Reverse the rule, conservatively: "**if it's a first key anywhere at all → the 2-choice exit 1; exit 0 only when `add_device`-provenanced everywhere**" | The predicate is "may this key be discarded" — a key holding a first key can be the only device (mustn't be discarded), so if even 1 matches, hand it to the human | Counterexample "a device invited post-DK (`add_member`'s first key) runs `device add`" — the 2-choice's first (this device itself · nothing needed) is true | **Adopted** |
| Hole 2 Option B's floor isn't partitioned by server · account | `floorDirOf(configPath)` (`floor.ts`) is 1 per config-file location, and filenames are only project IDs. Projects of a different server · account mix in, and a 404 can't distinguish "hidden" from "absent" | The floor is **only an enumeration of information**, not material that stops a judgment | — | Counterexample "if it stops a judgment, 1 floor of another server perpetuates a false 'couldn't sync'" | **Adopted** |
| Hole 3 The guarded swap | `storeMasterKeyGuarded` (`session.ts`) fails if an existing key is there (detecting concurrent writes). Bypassing it loses the detection | Write a separate guarded swap — "replace only if it matches the old value" | — | Counterexample "`keychain.remove` → save" — wouldn't notice another process writing in between | **Adopted** |
| Hole 4 Option A branches in the body | Fixing the 11 calling files individually (`approval.ts` / `boundary-checkpoint.ts` / `chain-append.ts` / `context.ts` / `dek-wrap.ts` / `deks.ts` / `device-key.ts` / `device-ops.ts` / `env-create.ts` / `env-rotate.ts` / `member.ts`) splits the transcriptions (K7-15) | Pass the verified chain into the body (`ownDeviceOrFail` / `ownDeviceBySigningKey`); the body branches | A device revoked on only some projects sees only that project's chain at the error's point → the wording states the other projects' conditions | — | **Adopted** |
| Hole 5 The existing race | If the request expires between `requestGet` (the live-request check) and `requestCreate`, either a new request can be made with the same key (the same behavior as C) or a 409 `device-registered` enters the wait-on-the-registry-row-signal path | Defense: emit "Approved:" **only when the request it waited on got signaled** | — | Closing it needs a server change (the previous session's judgment) → **premise corrected at this session's fact check 7** (structurally gone on the CLI side) | The defense is adopted. The race's handling is K13-2 |
| Hole 6 A project-list fetch failure | `confirmOnChains` throws a `fetchProjectMemberships` failure outward as-is | Fold it into "couldn't sync" and emit a Note (doesn't fail the command) | — | — | **Adopted** |

**Options considered and not taken**:

| Option | Content | Upward compat | Silver bullet | Counterexamples | Adoption |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Have the server reject revoked FPs / leave a revocation record in the registry | Bounces a revoked key at request-creation time | — | — | A server change, out of scope (owner ruling) | Rejected |
| Estimate copy vs original device from the local record (`own-devices.json`) | Distinguish by the record's provenance | — | — | A pre-DK copy can copy the whole config directory (the record is the same too). The record is advisory (K4-5 — not a judgment input) | Rejected |
| Auto-`--replace` from an everyday command's error / auto-replace a revoked key | Reduces the user's effort | — | — | Contradicts K4-18 "a key's discard is only the explicit `--replace`" | Rejected |
| Give `--replace` a confirmation prompt | Stops a misoperation | — | — | The handling in a non-TTY environment isn't decided. Since "a first key" can't distinguish the only device from a copy, a prompt can only ask about the same facts the display shows → went with just the display | Rejected |
| Put the new key in a separate keychain item and swap it in after approval | The old key keeps working until approval | — | — | Not adopted per the owner's ruling (little gain for the added persistence surface — the same call as K4-18 r-2) | Rejected |
| Decide "copy or original device" mechanically | Doesn't make the user choose | — | — | Essentially a human's choice of "which device keeps holding the key". On the chain it's the same key — a question that can't be eliminated | Rejected |
| (A known limitation — out of scope) | A device that copied the whole keychain post-DK is indistinguishable on the chain (being `add_device`-provenanced, it counts as "registered") | — | — | — | Recorded as a limitation |

### K13 fact check (read the code's branches and the predicates' definitions — K6-X. Line numbers are 80a28ff's)

1. **`device add`'s existing-key branches** (`device.ts` `deviceKeyForRequest` :414–): with a key and no `--replace`, `loadMasterKeys` → `requestGet(fp)` (a 404 = `DeviceNotFound` → false) → `fetchRegistry` (is there a registry row with my FP) → if a request, the Note "resuming the wait"; if a row, the Note "verifying it on each project's chain"; if neither, refuse (K12-5's wording — naming both revocation and copy). **It doesn't look at the chain**. On every branch, after returning the key, `deviceAddOp` **always** calls `createOrResumeRequest` (`requestCreate`). `--replace` runs `keychain.remove` → the Note "removed the previous key" → `generateKeyRecord` → `storeMasterKeyAndReport` (saving and showing the FP) → `requestCreate`, in that order — **if the request creation fails, the old key is already gone**. The no-key case is also "save → request" in that order.
2. **The server's `requestCreate`** (`handlers-devices.ts` :136– — read only): `ensureKeyMaterialAccess` → `requestSweep` → `consumeWindow` (5 / hour — **before the collision check**. The comment "repeated refusals also consume the window") → recomputing the FP → `repo.requestCreate` (`db.package/devices.ts` :166 — `device-registered` if a registry row exists, `request-exists` if a live request exists, replaces a dead request). So **every `device add` re-run on an already-registered device consumed 1 window** (a byproduct of the existing problem). `remove` removes only one's own row (`repo.remove(principal.userId, fp)`).
3. **The request-lookup response**: a `requestGet` success is `toRequestSummary` (`keyFingerprintHex` / `encPubHex` / `sigPubHex` / `label` / **`expiresAtMs`**). **The deadline a resume needs is available from the lookup's response alone** (a `requestCreate` → 409 → `requestGet` re-lookup is redundant).
4. **The completion check** (K12's `confirmOnChains` / `keyStandingOnProject`): a `fetchProjectMemberships` failure propagates outward (the command fails — hole 6). `keyStandingOnProject` folds an `openMetadataProject` failure into **absent** via `Effect.catch` → the false narrative "not registered yet — the approving device skipped or failed on them" (a sync failure isn't an approver-side fact). The revocation judgment is `revokedFingerprintsOf` (the union of FPs of applied `revoke_device`s whose `targetUserId === self`). **The provenance (`deviceProvenanceOf`) isn't looked at**.
5. **Distinguishing a verification failure**: `verifyChainSnapshot` (`sync.ts`) returns contradictions in signed data (chain verification failure · genesis mismatch · a head declaration's self-contradiction) via `evidenceError` (`CliError.evidence === true`). Network · HTTP failures carry no `evidence`. **Tampering signs vs the rest are distinguishable by the existing error type** (no new type needed).
6. **The provenance** (`device-key.ts` `deviceProvenanceOf`): if `device.addedSeq`'s entry is `add_device`, it's the actor's FP; otherwise (genesis / `add_member` / applied via a proposal) `addedByFingerprintHex === null` (a first key). **Confirming hole 1**: `registrationCandidates` is `records.filter(revokedAtMs === null && absent from the chain && not revoked here)`, and `observeDevices` writes a key with no record as `source: "observed"` (regardless of provenance). So a pre-DK key K observed as a first key on another project gets `add_device`d to other projects on this device's — which needn't be K's owner, a sibling's fine — next keyed command. **"`add_device`-provenanced somewhere" is no evidence of approval** (per the previous session's conclusion).
7. **Correcting hole 5's premise**: the race arose from "calling `requestCreate` after seeing a live request via `requestGet`". Per fact check 3 the resume's deadline is in the lookup's response, so **if the existing key doesn't call `requestCreate`, the race's path itself disappears** (no server change needed). `requestCreate` would be called only for a freshly generated key, whose FP has neither a request nor a registry row (a 409 happens only on an FP collision — unreachable in practice). → The previous session's "closing it needs a server change" doesn't hold when combined with **an existing key not going to make a request** (§2-1's `device add` row — adopted this round). The defense (the "Approved:" condition) still goes in because the 409 `device-registered` path remains at the type level (K13-3).
8. **`device list`** (`deviceListOp` :1118–): the displayed FPs = chain rows ∪ registry ∪ valid local records. **This device's key absent from every chain, the registry, and any valid record isn't displayed** (a revoked device running `device list` doesn't see its own key). `collectChainRows` lists only valid devices — no revocation shown. A project-list fetch failure (`resolveProjectIds`) fails the command. With `--project` it still says "(not on any synced project chain)" (the scope differs).
9. **`ownDeviceOrFail` / `ownDeviceBySigningKey`** (`device-key.ts`): their args are `ChainMember` and a key reference (FP / enc / sig) only — **they don't hold the verified chain**. The `deviceNotOnChainMessage` wording says "Either this device has not been registered here yet, or it was revoked" yet doesn't guide the revocation path (re-add with a new key) (a transcription K12 missed counting). The leading comment "if the key at hand isn't on the chain (unregistered · already revoked), guide a `device approve` re-run via a typed failure" is stale (at K10-5 the guidance changed to "approving a waiting request / syncing"). **The FP needed for the revocation judgment**: when the reference is enc / sig, a revoked device isn't in `member.devices`, so its FP can't be resolved → resolvable via `VerifiedProject.keyHistory` (`sync.ts` — the append-only history of all (enc, sig, FP) bound to that person). **Every call site** (grep — 16 places · 11 files): `context.ts` :678 / `deks.ts` :297 / `chain-append.ts` :40 / `dek-wrap.ts` :332 / `device-ops.ts` :134·:263 / `approval.ts` :235·:357 / `member.ts` :205·:345 / `env-create.ts` :86 / `env-rotate.ts` :281·:2079·:2390 / `boundary-checkpoint.ts` :58. Thirteen have a verified chain (`verified`) at hand; 3 don't (`boundary-checkpoint.ts`'s input · `member.ts` `actorAuthority`'s args · `env-create.ts` / `env-rotate.ts`'s signing functions take `verified` as an input) — all can be passed by adding 1 arg.
10. **The floor** (`floor.ts` / `floor-log.ts`): `floorDirOf(configPath) = dirname(configPath)/floor`; the files are `<projectId>.jsonl` (main) · `<projectId>.json` (old format) · `<projectId>.attested.json` · `<projectId>.attestation-evidence.jsonl`. `FloorStoreShape` has no listing function. Project IDs are form-enforced by `isProjectId` (hex 64). A listing is achievable by **just reading the directory** (the format is unchanged). The tests' `FloorStore` is mirrored function-by-function by `test/support/env.ts`.
11. **The keychain** (`keychain.ts`): only `get` / `set` / `remove` — no compare-and-write (CAS). `storeMasterKeyGuarded` is the "get → absent → set → get → match" detection (not atomic, but it catches a concurrent write). The same shape yields "get → matches the old value → set → get → matches the new value".
12. **Transcriptions of the same claim** (grep — `apps/cli/src` · `apps/cli/test` · `apps/cli/test/golden` · `apps/site/docs`. K7-15):
    - "or it was revoked": only `device-key.ts` :52.
    - "Approved:": `device.ts` :217 and `devices.mdx` :59 (quoting the completion sentence).
    - "already in your device registry": `device.ts` :179·:437 (Note) and `device.test.ts`.
    - "not registered yet": `device.ts` :386 and `devices.mdx` :59·:63.
    - "registered on": `device.ts` :205 (the expiry)·:217, `devices.mdx` :59·:65·:67·:125.
    - The refusal "This machine already has a device key": `device.ts` :451, `devices.mdx` :168 (the What-can-go-wrong quotation), `device.test.ts`.
    - `--replace`'s help: `effect-cli.ts` :717 → golden :775 ("removing the old key from this keychain" — reordering makes it false). docs :123 "`--replace` removes the revoked key …, generates a new one and prints its fingerprint", :146 (the copy procedure), :67 (expiry).
    - The revoked-device-with-a-leftover-row paragraph: `devices.mdx` :125's tail "`maruhi device add` without `--replace` ends its wait at once and says which projects revoked the key" — the behavior changes this round.
    - `reAddDeviceRoute`'s call sites: `device.ts` (the revocation Note · `refuseCapMismatch`) and `device-sync.ts` (`observeDevices`).

→ §2's premises are intact except fact check 7 (hole 5 disappears CLI-side). 7 is a correction in the direction of **strengthening** a premise, so the rulings' composition is unchanged.

### K13-1. The standing-judgment function's shape and placement

| Option | Content | Upward compat | Silver bullet | Counterexamples | Adoption |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1-a Do nothing (keep K12's `keyStandingOnProject`'s 3 values; each path adds its own provenance / sync-failure handling) | Small diff | — | — | Owner ruling §2 = ON. The falsehood of a sync failure folding into absent remains (fact check 4), and the provenance judgment gets transcribed per path (K7-15) | Rejected |
| 1-b **Put the pure function `keyStandingIn(verified, userId, fp)` (valid [with provenance: `add_device` / first key] · revoked · absent) plus `keyStandingOnProject` wrapping it (syncs then calls the pure function; a failure becomes "couldn't sync" [carrying `evidence`]) and `keyStandingsOf` (project list → per project; a list failure folds into "couldn't sync") in a new module `device-standing.ts`** | `device add` · the completion check · expiry · the `--replace` display use `keyStandingsOf`; `device list` applies the pure function to already-opened chains (no extra syncs) | Separating the pure function lets `device list` (listing all your devices in one sync) use the same judgment without double-syncing | Carry the "standing" in a type (a discriminated union); only the reporting side builds wording (K12-10 / K4-35) | Counterexample 1 "`device list` lists all your devices — syncing per key is N×" — the pure function just applies to opened chains. Counterexample 2 "if `keyStandingsOf` returns empty on a list-fetch failure, it's indistinguishable from 'on none'" — the failure travels in a type field (`listFailure`). Counterexample 3 "putting a tampering sign and a network failure in the same Note" — carry `CliError.evidence` (fact check 5). Counterexample 4 "placing it in `device.ts` grows it by 1800 lines" — make it a separate module. Below `context.ts` / `device-key.ts`, creating no cycle | **Adopted** (with the upward compat and silver bullet) |
| 1-c Place it in `device-key.ts` | Next to the existing predicates (`revokedFingerprintsOf` / `deviceProvenanceOf`) | — | — | A function calling a sync (`openMetadataProject` — `context.ts`) placed there creates a cycle, since `context.ts` reads `device-key.ts` | Rejected (the pure function's material stays in `device-key.ts`) |

**Round 2**: upward compat = separating the pure function (the table). Silver bullet = carrying it in a type (the table). Counterexamples = 4 for 1-b, 1 each for 1-a / 1-c. **Round 3**: option tried "sync the projects in parallel" → the existing scan (K12) is sequential, and `device add` is a few-per-device operation. Parallelization would split the order of failures from the order of output. No new options. Option tried "make the provenance 3 values (`add_device` / first key / via a proposal)" → an application via a proposal (inside `approve`) also falls to first-key since its `addedSeq` entry isn't `add_device` (the conservative direction — hole 1's rule). Distinguishing wouldn't change the branch. No new options. **Round 4**: option tried "throw the list failure as-is and fold per caller" → the folding would split per path (K7-15). No new options. Stopped.

**Principle**: "**the same question (what is this key on this project) is answered by 1 pure function, and the folding of syncs and failures lives in the 1 place wrapping it**" — K12-2 ✓ (the same fact is judged by the same function on the same material), K4-5 ✓ (the judgment is the chain; advisories aren't inputs), K7-15 ✓, K12-10 ✓. **Adopted: 1-b**.

Before the swap, only display the standing (`keyStandingsOf`) of the key being discarded (doesn't block)** | A request failure changes nothing | **The no-key case follows the same order (generate → request → guarded save)** (a failed request leaves no key behind — a re-run doesn't enter the existing-key path [K13-2]; it plainly re-creates) | "The FP is displayed only after the save" — an unsaved key's FP never reaches the screen, so it can't be approved (approval is the full-length FP / 12-word match — K4-6). A request whose save fails expires in 15 minutes (costing just 1 window) | Counterexample 1 "another process wrote during the swap" — the guard catches the mismatch and fails (the new key isn't saved. No FP is emitted). Counterexample 2 "the old key is unreadable (a corrupted record)" — the standing display is skipped (a Note) and the guard compares against the read string itself. Counterexample 3 "the key being discarded is a first key" — it's displayed ("registered on P as your first key there"). Not blocked (`--replace` is explicit consent — K4-18). Counterexample 4 "the standing display's sync fails" — folds into the display (doesn't block) | **Adopted** (with the upward compat and silver bullet) |

Since the FP never reached the screen, it isn't approved and disappears in 15 minutes. No new options. **Round 4**: option tried "in the standing display, require `--yes` if it's a first key" → the same as the confirmation prompt (a rejected option). No new options. Stopped.

**Principle**: "**an operation discarding a key replaces only the value it saw, and only after the new key is in a usable state (a request exists)**" — K4-18 ✓, hole 3 ✓, `storeMasterKeyGuarded`'s concurrent-write detection discipline ✓. **Adopted: 8-b**.

### K13-9. K4-21 (c)'s revision

K4-21 (c) "with a key present: resume if a request row or registry row exists for that key's FP, else K4-18's refusal" is revised to **"with a key present: if a live request exists, resume the wait (`requestCreate` isn't called). If none, split by the on-chain standing (K13-1) into §2-2's 6 rows. The registry row isn't used for branching"**. The revision's reason: (c)'s principle "advisories only up to the signal (K4-5) — the resume judgment also uses the registry · request rows only as 'existence signals'" was actually using the registry row as **a judgment input** (resume or refuse), contradicting K4-5's own principle (a revoked device with a leftover registry row gets a false completion — owner point (2); an approved key with no registry row gets refused — K9-3's T3). The request row is still used as the signal (a live request is "a reason to wait"; the judgment happens on the chain after the signal). K4-18 (the explicit `--replace`) is kept.

### K13-10. The check's placement

- **Existing checks grepped before placing**: `device.test.ts`'s `maruhi device add` (the refusal · `--replace` · the 2 409 paths · the wait's guidance · expiry) and K12's describe (the key arrival · the revocation Note). **No check** holds the 4-valued standing, the provenance rule, the couldn't-sync distinction, the list failure, `--replace`'s order and guard, `ownDeviceOrFail`'s revocation branch, or `device list`'s own-key display.

Existing expectations move to the new behavior's letters (not loosened) | Pins the properties (which key · where · how many) | "Doesn't make a request" pinned by POST count 0; "doesn't call couldn't-sync absent" pinned by both a negating `toContain` and an affirming sentence (vacuity denial — K12-11) | Wording agreement pinned by the shared functions; the tests check whole main sentences | Counterexample 1 (coincidental match) — whole main sentences in `toContain`. Counterexample 2 "a mutation takes the pre-GET path" (K12-11) — each negative check also watches the output (Note / Warning / exit code) over every path the mutation can take | **Adopted** |

The `--replace` paragraph gains the order (replaced only after the new key's request is created · on failure the old key remains · the discarded key's standing is displayed).
