The scaffolding gained `pendingRequests` · `projectsStatus` · `brokenProjects`. **Mutations** (22 mutations with the keep-pre-edit-content helper — K11-10): M1 reversing the first-key rule → 1 fails (hole 1), M2 a sync failure to absent → 2, M3 not watching the list failure → 1, M4 an existing key making a request → 9, M5 saving before the request → 6, M6 removing the guard's read-compare → 1, M7 removing the revocation branch → 1, M8 removing `keyHistory`'s resolution → 1, M9 list not adding this device's key → 1, M10 list not emitting the revoked row → 1, M11 a 409 entering the Approved path → 1, M12 also counting the floor's companion files → 1, M13 counting only `.jsonl` → 1, M14 not appending the expiry facts → 2, M15 not distinguishing evidence → 1, M16 not splitting the sentence on whether an approval happened → 1, M17 mixing couldn't-sync into unregistered → 2, M18 counting 0 on a list failure → 1, M19 list failing on a list failure → **survived** at first (an equivalent mutation — K13-13), rewritten → 1, M20 not showing the discarded key's standing → 1, M21 not emitting the T3 supplement → 1, M22 not stating `--project`'s scope → 1. Also confirmed K10-6's net fails under a mutation changing docs' quotation by 1 word. Green after reverting; `git diff --stat` identical to pre-mutation.
- **Verification**: `bun run check` (`fmt:check` / `lint` / `typecheck` / `importlint` / `fallow:audit` / `doctor` / `test` — all exit 0). `fallow:audit` reported "No issues in 22 changed files" — the 4 new findings mid-way (unused export `keyStandingOnProject`, `settleExistingKey`'s cognitive 15, the env-create / env-rotate duplication) were fixed (not added to the baseline). `floor-log.ts`'s 1 duplicate group (the append-write loop in 2 places) is inherited from main (the audit also excludes it as inherited). `test` is 143 files, 5006 tests (main's 4990 +16 = `device.test.ts`). `apps/site`: `validate --strict` (no broken links) → `build` (13 pages) → `e2e` (11 tests — `/opt/pw-browsers`'s Chromium 1194 aliased in a scratch area to the revision-1243 name Playwright 1.63.0 expects, via `PLAYWRIGHT_BROWSERS_PATH`. `playwright install` was not run). The site's unit tests (including K10-6's net) are 113.
- **Points put to the owner**: (1) **Dropped the "or it was revoked" clause from the unregistered sentence** (K13-5): prompt §2-1 says "if not revoked, today's sentence as-is", but once the body holds the revocation branch, that clause is an always-false option on this chain (K6-J). Only the clause was dropped; the rest stays today's sentence. Reverting it is a 1-sentence change. (2) **Exit 1 on the first key's 2 choices** (per §2-2): on a legitimate device invited post-DK holding a first key, `maruhi device add` also ends exit 1 (the sentence says "if it's this device itself, nothing is needed"). If there's a situation where a script reads exit 1 as "failure", there's an option to make just that row 0 (hole 1's conservatism is preserved by "doesn't say safe to discard", independent of the exit code).
- **Carryovers (out of scope — separate tasks)**: (1) **§2-3's hole-5 race has disappeared as a CLI-side path** (fact check 7 — an existing key doesn't call `requestCreate`). What remains is the server's `requestCreate` consuming the window before the collision check (a new key's request re-creation also consumes the window — a design that counts repeated refusals) and a new key's 409 `device-registered` (an FP collision — unreachable in practice. Defensively, "Approved" isn't emitted). Whether to change the server's order is a separate task. (2) K12's carryover (1) (`device revoke --user` can't delete another's registry row) — since `device add` no longer uses the registry for branching, **the user-visible harm is gone** (the row remains only as `device list`'s display name). The source still needs a server change. (3) K9-3's 3-c is partially collected (the requester adds chain facts. The condition-removing distinction isn't taken due to the race — K13-4); K7's owner point (3) isn't collected (an approver-side inference). (4) K11's completion-record carryovers (K5 owner points (1)(2), K2 carryover 4's reinforcement, K7 owner points (4)(5), K8 owner point (2), K9 owner points (2)(3), §11 W-3 / W-4 / W-6) are kept.
- **Known limitations**: (1) a device that copied the whole keychain post-DK is indistinguishable on the chain (being `add_device`-provenanced it's "registered" → exit 0). (2) "On none" is an assertion about the projects the server listed — a server-hidden project is invisible (the floor's enumeration is information only — not partitioned by server · account). (3) The existing key's standing judgment and `--replace`'s display sync once per project (a few-times-per-device operation). (4) The everyday commands' revocation sentence sees only that project's chain, so it states the possibility it remains on other projects with a condition and defers to `maruhi device list`. (5) Since the keychain API has no compare-and-write, the guarded swap detects via read → write → re-read — not atomic (the same level as `storeMasterKeyGuarded`).

### Additional rulings during review handling (K13-14 onward — responses to PR #202)

- **K13-14 (pullfrog — the concurrent-write wording on the no-key path)**: under the new order (generate → request → save), when `storeMasterKeyGuarded` detects a concurrent write on a no-key `device add`, the request is already created (1 window consumed) yet the `key generate` / `key recover`-aimed sentence "nothing was left behind … do not run `maruhi key generate` / `maruhi key recover` concurrently" is emitted. **Confirmed correct in the source** (`session.ts`'s `concurrentMasterKeyWrite`; `device.ts`'s `startWithNewKey` calls `storeMasterKeyGuarded` when `previous === null`). Options: (a) pass a different sentence for only the no-key path, (b) **have `storeMasterKeyAndReport` take `deviceAdd: { previous }`, and `device add`'s save · swap both use the same 1 sentence `concurrentDeviceAddWrite` (the request was created but no FP was shown, so it isn't approved and expires at the deadline)**, (c) as-is. Counterexample to (a): the swap's sentence (`concurrentDeviceKeyReplace`) and the no-key path's sentence would state the same fact (request created · FP not shown) in 2 letters (K11-5 — the letter in 1 place). Counterexample to (c): the false "nothing was left behind" (K6-J). **Adopted (b)**. The `key generate` / `key recover` sentence is unchanged (a default arg). Added 1 test (concurrent write → the sentence contains "while `maruhi device add` was running", doesn't contain "nothing was left behind", shows no FP), and confirmed it fails under a mutation removing `device add`'s wording specifier.
- **K13-15 (pullfrog — docs' "Registered on every project where it appears")**: `reportActiveKey`'s exit-0 condition is "at least 1 valid project, and everywhere valid is `add_device`-provenanced" — revoked · absent · couldn't-sync projects are reported in Notes. docs' wording was stronger than the condition. **Confirmed correct in the source** (a K6-X branch-shape slip — I wrote the condition folded). Fixed to "Registered on at least one project, and added by one of your devices everywhere it is registered", and added that revoked · absent · uncheckable projects each get a Note.
- **K13-16 (Cursor Bugbot, 2 items — the count and the absence assertion about unsyncables)**: (i) if all (or some) of the list can't be synced after the signal, "Approved: this device is registered on 0 projects (verified …)" asserts 0 (or an N that's only a lower bound) as fact (K13-3 had plugged only the list's failure). (ii) on `device list --project <id>` when that project's sync fails, after `collectChainRows`'s Note, `printChainLines` asserts the unchecked absence "(not on the chain of project <id>, the only project shown)". **Both confirmed correct in the source** (`awaitApproval`'s count sentence; `deviceListOp` → `printChainLines`'s empty branch). Options: (a) **append ", and N projects could not be checked" to the count sentence (`describeUnchecked` — on both the completion sentence and the existing key's "This key is registered on"). `device list` passes the unsyncable count — with `--project`, "could not be synced, so whether this key is on its chain is unknown"; without, "(not on any synced project chain; N projects could not be synced)"**, (b) withhold the count sentence only when all can't be synced. Counterexample to (b): when some can't be synced, N is still a lower bound and a reader reads it as "the verified-everything count" (the reach of K13-3's principle "what couldn't be checked isn't counted" falls short). **Adopted (a)**. The first half "… (verified on each project's chain)" of the completion sentence docs quote is kept, and the clause appended after (the quotation's letter unchanged). Confirmed via 2 tests (the post-signal count sentence when some can't sync; both `device list` forms) plus 3 mutations removing the appended clause · the 2 branches, each failing 1. An extension of K13-3's principle: "**a count sentence states the checked scope together with the count**".

### Decisions on the owner points (2026-09-26 — the owner delegated the judgment)

The owner delegated the completion-record's "points put to the owner" (1)(2) to the implementer. Both keep the current implementation (no code change).

| # | Topic | Options | Upward compat | Silver bullet | Counterexamples | Adoption |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| K13-17 | Point (1): having dropped the "or it was revoked" clause from the unregistered sentence (prompt §2-1 said "if not revoked, today's sentence as-is") | (a) **Keep it dropped**. (b) Put the clause back | — | Once the body holds the revocation judgment, this sentence's input is only "a key not revoked on this chain" (`ownDeviceOrFail`'s branch). The clause's truth is decided by structure | Counterexample to (b): a key entering this sentence isn't revoked on this chain, so the clause is always a false option (K6-J), and it leaves room for a revoked user to read the unregistered procedure (`device approve` / sync). Counterexample to (a) "a key used by a past membership (re-invited after `remove_member`)" — it isn't a revocation (no `revoke_device`), so it's not false even without the clause | **(a)**. Principle "when you add a branch, drop that branch's case from the other side's sentence (the wording states only facts after the branch — K6-J)" |
| K13-18 | Point (2): the first key's 2 choices' exit code | (a) **Keep exit 1**. (b) Make it exit 0 | — | Decide by the asymmetry of harm when the judgment is wrong: when (b) is wrong it's on a copied device — a script reads "this device was registered under its own key". The copied key keeps working since it's on the chain, leaving the state DK wants to surface (1 key held by 2 devices) **silently** in place. When (a) is wrong it's on the original device — no real harm, just looks like a failure. The sentence says "if it's this device itself, nothing is needed", so a human reading it can resolve it (an **out-loud** error) | Counterexample to (a) "a script running `device add` idempotently in CI" — a first-key device isn't `device add`'s target (it got its key via `key generate` / an invite), and a scripted run overlaps with needing a copy detection. Counterexample to (b): the asymmetry above. Plus it breaks behavior's continuity from K4-18 (an existing key stops by default) | **(a)**. Principle "for a machine-undecidable question's exit code, avoid the side where the error stays silent (choose the error that speaks)" — K4-18 ✓, K13-2's axis (doesn't say safe to discard) ✓ |

→ The completion record's "points put to the owner" (1)(2) are closed by this decision.

## 19. K14 addendum (2026-09-26 — collecting a DK carryover: `key recover`'s reserve-key judgment by how it's carried on the chain)

Collecting §11 W-3 (V-4). After opening the ledger, `maruhi key recover` asked the human "Was this key created as your reserve key …?" (K4-10 j-1 — default no) and split the handling of the opened key B on the answer. Someone who just lost every device may not be able to answer. The owner's ruling (K14's prompt §0): **W-3 = ON** — the question is replaced with "a mechanical judgment by the on-chain carriage + a confirmation that shows the judgment's facts" (**a revision of K4-10 j-1** — revised in this section). **The confirmation's 1 question is kept** (a yes inside the ceremony — made a confirmation, not a judgment — W-3 round 3). Specs (CRYPTO / AUTH / AUDIT), ADRs, `CLAUDE.md`, `packages/crypto`, the server, wire formats, acceptance-policy values, `own-devices.json`'s fields, the floor's format, and the ledger's format are unchanged. W-4 / W-6 · K5 owner points (1)(2) · K2 carryover 4 · K7 owner points (4)(5) · K8 owner point (2) · K9 owner points (2)(3) · K13 carryovers (1)(2) are out of scope. The table uses K6-X's format (a blank is the mark of "not searched"). Rulings are recorded here before implementation, and mid-implementation judgments are added in the same shape as K14-9 onward.

**Pre-start check**: starting from `origin/main` (`31d4353` — #203's merge. #204 just before). Before this section there's no K14 section or record of a W-3 collection (W-3 appears only as "out of scope" in §11's table and K7–K13's carryovers), and 0 open PRs on GitHub.

**Principle (this round's axis)**: "**facts a machine can state are stated by the machine; the human is shown the facts and asked only to confirm. When the facts fall short, don't land on the side where the error stays silent**" (W-3 round 3 + K13-18).

### K14 fact check (read the code's branches and the predicates' definitions — K6-X. Line numbers are 31d4353's)

1. **The question changes only 2 things** (`key-recover.ts` `finishRecovery` :231–): `newOrExistingDeviceKeys` → `askIsReserve` (:210 — the "Opened key …" line, the question, the prompt "Type yes if it is your reserve key (anything else = no): ", `answer === "yes"`) → if yes, `recordReserveLocally` (a "reserve"-provenanced row in `own-devices.json`); otherwise the Warning "the opened key … was not recorded as your reserve key. If it is the key of a lost or retired device, revoke it now … Then create a separate reserve key with `maruhi key recovery`" → `fetchProjectMemberships` → `registerDeviceWithReserve` per project → the report → the discard Note. **The registration (signed by B: `add_device(new device)` + backfill) and the discard are the same on both sides regardless of the answer**. What the answer changes is only the "reserve"-provenanced record and the revocation guidance.
2. **B is observed at the next sync even without a record** (`device-sync.ts` (a) :179 — records as "observed"-provenanced the keys that are your devices on the verified chain but absent from the local record, with a Note; (c) `add_device`s them to other projects). So the error of "not recording the reserve key" doesn't stop the propagation (K4-10 counterexample 1's "observed at the next sync → propagation via (3)" is true today too). Meanwhile, recording as reserve's effects are: `key show`'s reserve-key display, K4-9's reserve-key-absence warning (absent if a record exists), and `key reserve rotate` / `--replace`'s revocation target (`staleReserveFingerprints` — the "reserve"-provenanced rows). **Recording a pre-DK copy K as reserve silences the reserve-key-absence warning, and the user believes an un-separated ledger is separated** (a silently-persisting error). Failing to record the reserve key emits a revocation Warning and an observation Note at the next sync (an out-loud error).
3. **The judgment's parts** (`device-standing.ts`): `keyStandingIn(verified, userId, fp)` is valid (`firstKey` = `deviceProvenanceOf`'s `addedByFingerprintHex === null` — `addedSeq`'s entry isn't `add_device`) / revoked (`revokedFingerprintsOf` — applied `revoke_device`s) / absent. `keyStandingOnProject` (private) syncs via `openMetadataProject` (the keyless prelude — syncable with just the token), calls the pure function, and folds a failure into "couldn't sync" (carrying `evidence`). `keyStandingsOf` folds a list failure into `listFailure`. `groupStandings` splits into 4 groups. **The keyless prelude · the token's level (read suffices — it's just syncs) · the list failure · couldn't-sync are usable as-is in `key recover`'s context too**. What's missing is "the rule saying from the groups whether it's the reserve key" (K13 holds it in `device.ts` as `device add`'s branching — the "may it be discarded" question, not the is-it-the-reserve-key question).
4. **`key recover` already syncs every project** (`registerDeviceWithReserve` :127 — `openMetadataProject` → fails `You are not a chain-derived member` if `members.get(self)` is absent → `reserve-missing` if `member.devices.has(B)` is false). **No extra sync is needed**: applying `keyStandingIn` to the verified chains opened for registration gives the same judgment (same as K13-1's `device list` — no double sync). Though today it reads `members.get` / `devices.has` itself and doesn't distinguish revoked (`revoked`) from absent (`absent`) (both land on `reserve-missing`'s "not registered on this project").
5. **The paths onto the chain** (`packages/crypto` chain-verify.ts — read only): a device lands only via `firstDeviceOf` (:570 — genesis's actor :1154 / `add_member`'s payload :1173) or `applyAddDevice` (:1184 — **into the actor's own device set**). `add_device` is signed by the person's own device (another can't add one). A proposal-routed one (an `add_device` inside `approve`) falls to `firstKey` since its `addedSeq` entry is `approve` (K13-1 round 3 — the conservative direction).
6. **The set of keys entering the ledger** (grep: `issueRecoveryCodeOp`'s 4 call sites + passkey · guardian sealings): (i) `sealNewReserve` (`recovery.ts` :425 — `generateReserveKeys`'s new key. `key generate`'s latter stage / `key recovery`'s first time / the pre-DK separation), (ii) `replaceReserveWithoutOpening` (`--replace` — a new key), (iii) `keyReserveRotateOp` (a new key), (iv) `keyRecoveryOp`'s re-issuance (re-sealing **the opened B itself** under a new code), (v) `key seal passkey` / `guardian add` (sealing the B opened via `openLedgerReserveForChange`). **Every generated key is a reserve key and never enters the keychain** (`ReserveKeys`' brand — `reserve.ts`). (iv)(v), re-sealing an existing B, just carry "what was already in the ledger". So what can enter the ledger is only "a DK reserve key" or "a copy of a pre-DK device key that was in the ledger" — **no path brings a post-DK approved device key into the ledger** (W-3's premise holds).
7. **A reserve key never becomes a first key**: a reserve key's secret isn't in the keychain, so it's never used for `project init`'s (genesis) or `invite accept`'s (`add_member`) signatures · payloads (both use the keychain's device key). A reserve key lands on a chain only via `syncOwnDevices` (c) (`add_device` signed by a syncing device — K4-30) or `key reserve rotate` / `--replace` (`add_device` signed by this device).
8. **W-3's counterexamples' premises**: counterexample 1 (on none) — today enumerates `reserve-missing` and still asks the question. Counterexample 2 (`--new-identity`) — the new identity's first key is a device key; the reserve key is `sealNewReserve` → `add_device`d at a sync. Consistent. Counterexample 3 (the carriage differs per project) — for a pre-DK K, K's device approves a DK device D2, which records K by observation (fact 2) and `add_device`s it onto projects only D2 was invited to — **so first-key and `add_device` projects can coexist**. Additionally (a) if some project can't be synced, whether it's a first key there is unknown. (b) **When the key changed at a re-invite after `remove_member`**: if K is removed from a project where it was the first key and D2 gets re-invited, `add_member`'s first key is D2, and K is re-added as `add_device` by D2's sync (`remove_member` isn't a `revoke_device`, so it doesn't enter `revokedFingerprintsOf`). **If this happens on every project where K was the first key, in the current state K looks `add_device`-provenanced everywhere** (it stays in history as the past membership's first key, but `keyStandingIn` sees only the current device's `addedSeq`). (c) A revocation: after a `revoke_device`, `keyStandingIn` doesn't return a provenance (`revoked`).
9. **`key recovery`'s pre-DK distinction's transcriptions have split** (`keyRecoveryOp` :391 — only "opened B's FP = the keychain's device key's FP". `ledger-open.ts` `openLedgerReserveForChange` :77 is the same comparison). If it doesn't match, B is re-sealed as the reserve key and **`recordReserveLocally`** (:408). **On the device after `key recover`, the device key is new, so it can't match the pre-DK copy K** → `key recovery` calls K "the reserve key", re-issues its code, and records it as reserve (K4-2's distinction assumes "run on a device holding K" and doesn't hold after a restore). Today's `key recover` Warning guides "revoke it → separate the reserve key with `key recovery`", but a `key recovery` after the revocation re-issues the revoked K's code and records it as reserve (`own-devices.ts` `record` :370 overwrites the row back to `revokedAtMs: null`). **Following the guidance leaves the state of believing a dead key is the reserve key silently in place** (an existing hole — a transcription of the same question as W-3's judgment). The same comparison's transcription is also in `openLedgerReserveForChange` (`key seal passkey` / `guardian add` / `key reserve rotate`).
10. **The ceremony gate and the question's position**: `key recover`'s gate is at the opening stage (the code — `recovery.ts` `ensureRecoveryCodeInteractionAllowed`; the passkey — `passkey.ts` `ensurePasskeyCeremonyAllowed`; the guardian — `handoff.ts` `ensureHandoffRequestAllowed`; each is known-agent + all 3 standard I/O being terminals). The question comes after the opening, so **changing the question's position · presence doesn't change the non-interactive refusal** (the refusal precedes the ledger fetch — pinned by `recovery.test.ts`'s existing 2 items). All 3 paths converge into `finishRecovery`.
11. **Transcriptions of the same claim** (grep — K7-15): "Was this key created" / "Type yes if it is your reserve key" are `key-recover.ts` :217 / :220, `recovery.test.ts` :632, `passkey.test.ts` :49, `handoff.test.ts` :307, `agent.test.ts` (a comment and the "yes" response). "not recorded as your reserve key" is `key-recover.ts` :244, `recovery.test.ts` :677, `passkey.test.ts` :646. docs are `recover-your-key.mdx` :31 ("asks one question — whether the opened key was created as your reserve key … the answer is no") and `devices.mdx` :156 ("it asks whether the opened key is your reserve key, and the answer is no"). The `key recovery` help description ("separate it from this device's key on an install from before device keys" — `effect-cli.ts` and the golden) transcribes fact 9's distinction. "copy of a device key" is only `key-recover.ts` :217. **`key recover` has no chain-attached test** (every case has `GET /projects` empty).

### K14-1. The judgment's predicate (what says "is it the reserve key")

| Option | Content | Upward compat | Silver bullet | Counterexamples | Adoption |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1-a Do nothing (keep the question) | No diff | — | — | Owner ruling W-3 = ON | Rejected |
| 1-b Fix only the wording (add "if pre-DK, no" to the question) | Small diff | — | — | Asks the same judgment of someone who can't answer (V-4 unchanged) | Rejected |
| 1-c Call `keyStandingsOf` at `finishRecovery`'s head and judge at the question's position | Keeps the question's position | — | — | Syncs the same projects again for registration (fact 4 — the N× sync runs twice) | Rejected |
| 1-d **Apply `keyStandingIn` to the chains opened for registration (use `keyStandingOnProject` as the registration's prelude — make it public), bundle every project's standing via `groupStandings`, and add to `device-standing.ts` a pure function `reserveVerdictOf(groups, listFailure)` returning the verdict from the groups**. The judgment runs after registration, before recording | No extra syncs; the standing judgment · the sync-failure folding live in K13's 1 place (no transcription made) | Half the syncs of 1-c. The registration's own reads of `members.get` / `devices.has` (fact 4) also move onto the same predicate, letting it distinguish revoked from absent | Returns the verdict as a type (a discriminated union); the reporting side builds the wording (K12-10) | Counterexample 1 "the judgment lands after registration — if it falls mid-registration nothing is recorded" → the not-recording side (fact 2 — speaks out loud). `--resume` judges again. Counterexample 2 "a list-fetch failure never reaches the judgment" → today the command also fails on a list failure (though currently after the question records it). Failing without recording is the safer side | **Adopted** |
| 1-e A new predicate that also looks at history (the past membership's first key — `applied`'s genesis / `add_member`'s payload) | Plugs fact 8 (b)'s re-invite hole | A shape added to 1-d | — | Counterexample "`keyStandingIn` gains a separate 'first key' predicate" — contradicts K13-1's "the same question by 1 pure function", and the judgment splits from `device add`'s 2 choices (one calls the same key a first key, the other `add_device`). Hole (b) is caught by the confirmation's 1 question (K14-3) and the `no` guidance. Adding it aligned with the `device add` side is a separate task | Rejected (known limitation + carryover) |

**Round 2**: upward compat = 1-d's "applied to the registration's chains". Silver bullet = returning it as a type. Counterexamples = 2 for 1-d, 1 for 1-e. **Round 3**: option tried "put the verdict inside `keyStandingsOf`" → `keyStandingsOf` is the sync wrapper, also used by `device add`. Mixing the verdict's rule (is it the reserve key) makes 2 questions. No new options. Option tried "take `KeyStandings` rather than the groups" → just re-calls `groupStandings`. Taking the groups matches `device add`'s entrance (`settleExistingKey` also branches on groups). No new options. **Round 4**: option tried "count a `registerDeviceWithReserve` failure (an append failure) as 'couldn't sync'" → if the sync went through, the standing is known. An append failure doesn't change the standing. No new options. Stopped.

**Principle**: "**the same question is answered by 1 pure function, and the judgment is applied to already-opened chains**" — K13-1 ✓, K4-5 ✓ (the judgment is the chain; the registry · records aren't inputs), K7-15 ✓. **Adopted: 1-d**.

### K14-2. The verdict's rules and the default when undecidable

The verdict's values (`reserveVerdictOf` — the first match from the top):

| Value | Condition | Records | The confirmation question |
|---|---|---|---|
| `first-key` | Valid as `firstKey` on even 1 (even with an unsyncable project — 1 positive fact suffices) | Doesn't | Not emitted |
| `revoked` | No first key, revoked somewhere | Doesn't | Not emitted |
| `unchecked` | Neither of the above, and an unsyncable project exists (a list failure never reaches the verdict — K14-1 counterexample 2) | Doesn't | Not emitted |
| `nowhere` | Valid nowhere (absent only) | Doesn't | Not emitted |
| `added` | Valid on 1 or more, all `add_device`-provenanced. No revoked · unsyncable (absent projects are allowed — the registration's report enumerates "re-invites") | On the confirmation's yes | **Emitted** |

| Option | Content | Upward compat | Silver bullet | Counterexamples | Adoption |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2-a Fall back to today's question (the judgment) when undecidable | Today's escape route | — | — | Precisely where it can't be judged, the human can't answer either (V-4). The question returns to being a "judgment" (contradicts W-3 round 3) | Rejected |
| 2-b Record it as the reserve key when undecidable | Doesn't lose the reserve-key record | — | — | Fact 2: the error of recording a copy as reserve stays silent (K13-18's asymmetry). The opposite direction of hole 1 | Rejected |
| 2-c **When undecidable, don't record (state the facts and the reason in a Note / Warning, and name the next move). The question is emitted only for `added`** | The error is on the side that speaks | By fact 2, the not-recording error is covered by the observation ("observed" provenance) supplementing the propagation, and the reserve-key-absence warning (K4-9) leads to `key recovery`. Since `key recovery` uses the same verdict per K14-4, the next move doesn't contradict the judgment | Replaces the "is it the reserve key" question with the predicate "may it be recorded as the reserve key" (K13's axis: "split not on an undecidable cause but on a decidable safety predicate") | Counterexample 1 "it's a DK reserve key but some projects can't be synced" → `unchecked`, not recorded. The Note says "once syncable, `maruhi key recover --resume` checks again" (`--resume` skips registration on the existing key and re-runs the verdict). Counterexample 2 "someone with no project" → `nowhere`, not recorded. On the next keyed command (no projects) nothing happens; `key show` says no reserve-key record. The Note names `maruhi key recovery` (once judged the reserve key, re-seal and record — K14-4). Counterexample 3 "a revoked B" — `revoked` doesn't work as the ledger's key either way (the restore's registration won't pass on the revoked projects). Recording it skips revocation in propagation, but makes the user believe a reserve key exists. Not recorded | **Adopted** |
| 2-d Swap `revoked`'s and `unchecked`'s precedence | — | — | — | Both don't record. Revocation is a positive fact, so stating it first settles the next move (`key recovery` — separated by K14-4) | Rejected |

**Round 2**: upward compat = 2-c's "the observation and the warning cover the not-recording error". Silver bullet = replacing it with the safety predicate. Counterexamples = 3 for 2-c (each resolvable by the Note's next move). **Round 3**: option tried "emit a confirmation for `first-key` too" → a question whose answer changes no result (the fact takes precedence) is a ceremony-shaped question only, returning the same burden to someone who can't answer. A Warning showing the facts suffices. No new options. Option tried "skip the record even for `added` and leave it to observation (removing the question)" → the owner's ruling "keep the confirmation's 1 question". Plus a "reserve"-provenanced record becomes a rotate / `--replace` revocation target (fact 2), so it's not the same as "observed". No new options. **Round 4**: option tried "estimate first-key-ness from the unsyncable project's floor (`floor.ts`)" → per K13-7 the floor isn't partitioned by server · account and can't witness. No new options. Stopped.

**Principle**: "**when undecidable, don't record — the not-recording error is voiced by the observation and the warning, while the recording error stays silent**" — K13-18 ✓ (avoid the side where the error stays silent), K4-10's default no ✓ (keeps fail-closed), hole 1 ✓ (if a first key anywhere, it isn't called a reserve key), K4-30 ✓ (a reserve key lands only via `add_device`). **Adopted: 2-c**.

### K14-3. The confirmation question's wording and answering against the facts

| Option | Content | Upward compat | Silver bullet | Counterexamples | Adoption |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 3-a Shorten the question to "Is this your reserve key?" | — | — | — | Still the shape of asking for a judgment (W-3) | Rejected |
| 3-b **Emit the fact line (on which projects, signed by whom it was carried — "added by one of your devices (add_device), never as your first key" — and that this is the reserve key's carriage, while a pre-DK copy becomes a first key on the projects it joined), then the prompt "Type yes to record it as your reserve key (anything else = no): "**. yes → record + Note. Otherwise → don't record; emit the current Warning as-is (revocation guidance → `key recovery`) | Shows the facts then takes a confirmation. The default (bare Enter) is the not-recording side (keeps K4-10) | Someone who knows fact 8 (b) (a re-invite makes K look `add_device` everywhere) can choose `no` from the fact line's disagreement with their memory. The guidance after `no` (revoke → `key recovery`) doesn't contradict the verdict per K14-4 (once revoked → `revoked` → separation) | — | Counterexample 1 "a fact-contrary no (it IS the reserve key)" → not recorded (K14-2's safe side). The guidance "if a lost or retired device, revoke" is conditional; if it's the reserve key, nothing happens. Observed at the next sync (fact 2). `key recovery` sees `added` and re-seals · records (K14-4) — self-heals. Counterexample 2 "no fact-contrary yes" → the question isn't emitted outside `added`, so there's no entrance for a fact-contrary yes (don't build a path where yes overrides `first-key`). Counterexample 3 "non-interactive" → the question comes after the opening; the gate is before the opening (fact 10). Unchanged | **Adopted** |
| 3-c Build a new sentence for a `no` ("you answered no although …") | Names the answer's disagreement | — | — | The disagreement can be legitimate under fact 8 (b) (the user is the one who's right). An accusing sentence is a false option (K13-17). The current conditional sentence suffices | Rejected |

**Round 2**: upward compat = 3-b's "can choose no on disagreement with the facts". Counterexamples = 3 for 3-b. **Round 3**: option tried "put the FP in the prompt" → the fact line has the FP and the projects. A long prompt wraps on the terminal. No new options. **Round 4**: option tried "add a yes-overrides path to the `first-key` / `revoked` sentences" → builds counterexample 2's entrance. No new options. Stopped.

**Principle**: "**the confirmation question is emitted only when the machine's verdict says 'may be recorded', and builds no entrance where an answer overrides the facts**" — W-3 round 3 ✓, K13-17 ✓ (don't emit a false option), K4-10's default ✓. **Adopted: 3-b**.

### K14-4. Unifying the transcription with `key recovery`'s pre-DK distinction

| Option | Content | Upward compat | Silver bullet | Counterexamples | Adoption |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 4-a Do nothing (the FP match only) | No diff | — | — | Fact 9: following `key recover`'s guidance re-seals · records the revoked K as the reserve key (silently). The judgment splits in 2 (K7-15) | Rejected |
| 4-b Change only the guidance's order ("`key recovery` first, then revoke") | No code change | — | — | `key recovery` can't distinguish K on the post-restore device (the FPs don't match), so it still records K as the reserve key | Rejected |
| 4-c Change the guidance to `key recovery --replace` | Doesn't depend on the distinction | — | — | `--replace` deletes the passkey · guardian rows that had sealed the old B and emits the "no reserve key in the record, so nothing was revoked" Warning — workable as a procedure, but the hole for someone who ran plain `key recovery` remains | Rejected |
| 4-d **`key recovery`, after the FP match (today — a copy of the device key at hand), runs the same verdict via `keyStandingsOf` + `reserveVerdictOf` when there's no match; `first-key` and `revoked` get separation (`sealNewReserve` — seal a new reserve key), the rest (`added` / `nowhere` / `unchecked`) get today's re-seal + record. `unchecked` names the uncheckable projects in a Note** | The judgment is 1. `key recover`'s guidance (revoke → `key recovery`) reaches separation before or after the revocation | It still separates after a revocation (`revoked`), so the guidance's order (revoke first — stop a lost device's key early) can be kept | Shares the verdict's function (K14-1) | Counterexample 1 "the reserve key was just made and isn't registered anywhere yet (right after `key generate` — under K4-1's order, registration is the next sync)" → `nowhere` gets a re-seal (today). Correct. Counterexample 2 "an unsyncable project where alone K is a first key" → re-seal (today's same error). Though a code re-issuance is an operation for suspected leaks — don't stop it for an unrelated broken project (the same call as K4-38's `--replace`). The Note states the scope (a known limitation). Counterexample 3 "`revoked` but still valid on other projects" → after separation the old B remains where valid (its secret leaves the ledger). The Note says "revoke it where it's still registered" (only when some place is valid — not a false option). Counterexample 4 "the network cost" → a per-project sync only when the FP doesn't match (the same cost as `device add`'s existing-key judgment — K13's known limitation (3)) | **Adopted** |
| 4-e Put `openLedgerReserveForChange` (`key seal passkey` / `guardian add` / `key reserve rotate`) on the same verdict too | Aligns every transcription | — | — | Changes the 3 commands' behavior (the refusal's conditions) and adds an all-project sync to `seal passkey` / `guardian add`. Outside W-3's scope (`key recover`'s question) and the transcriptions the prompt named (`key recovery`) | Rejected (a carryover — an owner point) |

**Round 2**: upward compat = 4-d's "reaches separation before or after the revocation". Silver bullet = sharing the verdict. Counterexamples = 4 for 4-d, 1 each for 4-a–4-c. **Round 3**: option tried "on `revoked`, refuse instead of separating (guide `--replace`)" → re-issuing a revoked key's code is pointless, and separation (sealing a new reserve key) satisfies K4-2's qualification as an operation by the one who opened the ledger. A refusal just adds a step. No new options. Option tried "after separating on `first-key`, auto-revoke the old B" → a device holding the old B (the pre-DK K) may still be alive (K4-2 counterexample 2 — the copy on 2 machines). A key's discard · revocation is explicit (K4-18). No new options. **Round 4**: option tried "drop the FP-match judgment, keeping only the chain verdict" → the match is a fact at hand, independent of syncs (separation works even when it can't sync). Keep it. No new options. Stopped.

**Principle**: "**the same question (does the ledger's key work as the reserve key) uses the same verdict, and the guidance's destination doesn't contradict the verdict**" — K7-15 ✓, K4-2 ✓ (the ledger is changed by the one who opened it — add "the opened B's on-chain standing" to the distinction's material. No local state · declarations used), K11-5 ✓ (guidance names only paths the predicates permit), K4-18 ✓ (revocation is explicit). **Adopted: 4-d**.

### K14-8. What goes to the owner points (not implemented)

- `openLedgerReserveForChange`'s pre-DK distinction (the remaining transcriptions of fact 9 — K14-4's 4-e). If a pre-DK user runs `key seal passkey` / `guardian add` on a DK device D2 approved by K's device, D2's key doesn't match K, so K is sealed as-is and recorded as reserve. Under `key reserve rotate`, K gets revoked on every project as the old reserve key (K's device stops — it does speak). Using the same verdict adds a sync to the 3 commands and changes the refusals' conditions.
- Whether to plug fact 8 (b) (a pre-DK K after a re-invite swapped the first key) via history (K14-1's 1-e). To add it aligned with `device add`'s 2 choices (K13-2), the shape would be adding "was a first key in a past membership" to `keyStandingIn`.

### K14 ruling list (pinned before implementation)

| # | Topic | Adopted | What makes it better than the alternatives | Principle |
|---|---|---|---|---|

### K14 completion record (2026-09-26)

**This section is a revision of K4-10's j-1**: `key recover` no longer asks the human whether the opened key is the reserve key. It judges by the on-chain carriage on the chains opened for registration, and emits the fact-showing confirmation's 1 question only when it may be recorded as the reserve key (everywhere valid is `add_device`-provenanced) (default no — j-1's fail-closed is kept). K4-10's "its provenance can't be judged by cryptographic fact, so it asks the human" is replaced with "it can't be judged by B's contents, but it can by the on-chain carriage (W-3)".

- **Deliverables (the verdict — K14-1 / K14-2)**: `apps/cli/src/device-standing.ts` — `keyStandingOnProject` made public (used as `key recover`'s registration prelude), the verdict type `ReserveVerdict` (`first-key` / `revoked` [with `activeProjectIds`] / `unchecked` [with `listFailure`] / `nowhere` / `added`) and the pure `reserveVerdictOf(groups, listFailure)`. Builds no wording.
- **Deliverables (`key recover` — K14-1–K14-3 / K14-5)**: `apps/cli/src/key-recover.ts` — `askIsReserve` removed. `registerDeviceWithReserve` branches on `keyStandingOnProject`'s standing (couldn't-sync → `failed`; revoked → the new state `reserve-revoked` ["was revoked on this project"]; absent → `reserve-missing`; valid → `addDeviceWithReserve` [`add_device` + backfill — extracted from the old body]), carrying the standing in the result. `finishRecovery` is "Opened key …" → registration → report → `groupStandings` → `reserveVerdictOf` → `settleOpenedKey` (only for `added`: the fact line + the prompt "Type yes to record it as your reserve key (anything else = no): ", recording + a Note on yes, the prior Warning otherwise. `first-key` / `revoked` are Warnings, `unchecked` / `nowhere` are Notes — each naming the next move) → the discard Note. The leading comment was revised.
- **Deliverables (`key recovery` — K14-4)**: `keyRecoveryOp`, after the FP match (as before), runs `separateUnusableLedgerKey` — `keyStandingsOf` + `reserveVerdictOf` separating `first-key` (a pre-DK copy seen from the post-restore device) and `revoked` (`sealNewReserve`); if valid places remain under `revoked` it emits the revocation Note, and `unchecked` emits the scope Note then proceeds to the prior re-seal. The help description became "separate it from a copy of a device key that an install from before device keys left in the ledger" (`effect-cli.ts`, 2 golden spots).
- **Deliverables (docs — K14-6)**: `apps/site/docs/recover-your-key.mdx` ("asks one question …" became the registration paragraph + a "judged on the chain" paragraph + 4 items), `devices.mdx` (the pre-DK procedure 1 gained "on a device with another key, it's told on the chain"; the tail's "it asks … the answer is no" became the verdict's sentence). Nothing added to What can go wrong (the new sentences state their own conditions and next moves — K14-6).
- **Tests**: `apps/cli/test/device.test.ts` 75 → 88 (+13 — the describe "maruhi key recover / key recovery — the ledger key's on-chain verdict (DK K14)": `added`'s yes records / `added`'s default (empty) doesn't record, prior Warning / `first-key` isn't asked, isn't recorded, and registration still happens / mixed + couldn't-sync → `first-key` (doesn't name the `add_device` projects) / couldn't-sync → `unchecked` and `--resume` / on none → `nowhere` and a re-invite / revoked → `revoked` and reports the registration on the revoked projects · registers where valid / non-interactive (piped stdin) stops before the ledger fetch and neither asks nor appends / `key recovery`: the post-restore device's `first-key` separation, the separation of an already-revoked copy (doesn't re-issue · doesn't say "still registered"), revoked on only some → separation + the valid places' revocation Note, `added` gets the prior re-seal, `unchecked` a Note + re-seal). Path agreement: `passkey.test.ts` (`added`'s yes records — replaced the old "is it the reserve key" positive case / `first-key` isn't asked — replaced the old "answers no"), `handoff.test.ts` (the guardian path's `added` yes records), the code path via device.test.ts — pinning that all 3 paths go through the same `finishRecovery` verdict. `recovery.test.ts`'s 2 items moved to the new 0-project (= `nowhere`) behavior (the question isn't emitted · doesn't record · the `nowhere` Note), and the re-issuance test gained the empty list. `agent.test.ts` dropped 1 response. Scaffolding: `test/support/ledger.ts` (new — `storedMasterRecord` / `ledgerHandlerFor` moved from `recovery.test.ts`), `test/support/chain-handler.ts` (`appendableProjectHandlers` / `projectListHandlerOf` / `servedChainResponse` / `acceptAppendedEntry` — `device.test.ts`'s `extraProjectHandlers` moved in. K14-9), `test/support/crypto.ts` (`addOwnerDeviceOp`).
- **Mutations** (12 mutations — the keep-pre-edit-content write-back method [K11-10]. Confirmed after each mutation that `git diff --stat` is unchanged): M1 ignoring `first-key` (4 fail) / M2 ignoring `revoked` (2) / M3 ignoring couldn't-sync (2) / M4 `nowhere` → `added` (3) / M5 ignoring the confirmation's answer, always recording (1) / M6 recording even on `first-key` (2) / M7 skipping `key recovery`'s separation (2) / M8 not separating `key recovery`'s `revoked` (1) / M9 not distinguishing registration's revoked from absent (1) / M10 folding couldn't-sync into "absent" (1) / M11 always emitting "still registered" (1) / M12 not emitting "still registered" (**survived at first** → K14-10 added a check and 1 fails). Green after reverting all mutations.
- **Verification**: `bun run check` (`fmt:check` / `lint` / `typecheck` / `importlint` / `fallow:audit` / `doctor` / `test` — all exit 0). `fallow:audit` reported "No issues in 16 changed files" — the 2 new duplication groups mid-way (K14-9) were fixed (not added to the baseline). The `floor-log.ts` duplication was already resolved at #203, so no inherited findings. `test` is 145 files, 5064 tests (main's 5051 +13 = `device.test.ts`). `apps/site`: `validate --strict` (no broken links) → `build` (13 pages) → `e2e` (12 tests — the session-start hook's `PLAYWRIGHT_CHROMIUM_EXECUTABLE_PATH=/opt/pw-browsers/chromium` worked as-is. K12 / K13's aliasing wasn't needed). `apps/site/test/unit` (including `cli-vocabulary.test.ts`'s K10-6 net and #203's mandatory-positional-arg check for ```sh) is 126.
- **Points put to the owner**: (1) **`openLedgerReserveForChange`'s pre-DK distinction** (K14-8 — fact 9's remaining transcriptions): `key seal passkey` / `guardian add` / `key reserve rotate` distinguish by the FP match only. If a pre-DK user runs them on a DK device approved by K's device, `seal passkey` / `guardian add` seal K and record it as reserve (silently), and `rotate` revokes K on every project as the old reserve key (K's device stops — it does speak). Using the same verdict (`reserveVerdictOf`) would close it, but adds an all-project sync to the 3 commands and changes the refusals' conditions. (2) **The historical first key** (K14-1's 1-e): for a pre-DK user who was re-invited on another device after `remove_member` and got K re-added as `add_device` on every project where K was the first key, K looks `add_device`-provenanced everywhere and is judged `added` (the confirmation's 1 question can answer `no` — the fact line disagrees with their memory). Adding "was a first key in a past membership" to `keyStandingIn` would plug it, but `device add`'s 2 choices (K13-2) also run on the same predicate, so they'd change together.
- **Carryovers (out of scope — separate tasks)**: W-4 (disposable environments into leases) · W-6 (`meta.ts`'s grouping), K5's owner points (1)(2), K2's carryover 4, K7's owner points (4)(5), K8's owner point (2), K9's owner points (2)(3), K13's carryovers (1)(2) are kept.
- **Known limitations**: (1) a reserve key added via a proposal (inside `approve`) falls to `firstKey` (K13-1 round 3) and is judged `first-key` (the not-recording side — speaks out). CLI reserve-key registrations are direct `add_device`s, so it can't occur today. (2) `key recovery`'s `unchecked` proceeds to the re-seal (if K is a first key only on unsyncable projects, today's same error). The Note states the scope, and a re-run after it can be checked can separate it. (3) `key recover`'s verdict runs after registration, so a process drop mid-registration means no recording happens (`--resume` judges again). A project-list fetch failure fails the command before reaching the verdict or the record (it used to fall after the question recorded). (4) A `nowhere` person (no projects) loses the reserve-key record. `key recovery` re-seals + records a `nowhere`, so it comes back there (the code is re-issued). (5) `key recovery` syncs per project when the FP doesn't match (the same cost as `device add`'s existing-key judgment).

### K14's re-loop (2026-09-26 — the owner's question "at each topic, did you search the silver bullet · upward compat · a structural solution until the options ran out")

**Self-inspection**: the pre-implementation pinning (K14-1–K14-8) hadn't run the loop out on 2 points. (i) K14-5–K14-7 collapsed "round 2 · round 3" into 1 line and didn't verify per round that "2 consecutive rounds with no new option = stop". (ii) 3 points that might be structurally solvable were pushed to "owner points" (K14-8) and information on grounds of scope or cost — the historical first key (1-e) · `openLedgerReserveForChange`'s transcriptions (4-e) · the wrong reserve rows left after a separation (pullfrog's info point). Below, each topic's loop is re-run from round 1 (existing tables' options aren't re-listed; only new options and each round's record are written).

**Additional fact check** (31d4353 + this PR):
- (a) `verified.applied` includes genesis too (`chain-verify.ts` `evaluateEntry` :1559 — `{ operation: entry, actorUserId }`). **The first key's carriage (genesis's payload / `add_member`'s payload — `targetUserId` and the public keys) remains in the applied operation sequence even after the membership ends**. So "was this person's first key in any membership on this chain" becomes a pure function decided by just the synced chain (no extra sync · wire). A proposal-routed `add_member` (inside `approve`) also lands on the same sequence.
- (b) `firstKey`'s readers are 3: `device.ts` `reportActiveKey` (`device add`'s 2 choices — :357), `describeReplacedKey` (`--replace`'s display — :572–573), and `reserveVerdictOf`. `device list`'s "first key" display is a different question reading `deviceProvenanceOf` directly (how the current device was added) and doesn't read `firstKey`.
- (c) **`openLedgerReserveForChange` (`ledger-open.ts` :71–) unconditionally `recordReserveLocally`s the opened B if the FP doesn't match** (the prelude of `key seal passkey` / `guardian add` / `key reserve rotate`). If a pre-DK user runs it on another device approved by K's device, K gets recorded as reserve. A recorded K enters `key reserve rotate` / `key recovery --replace`'s revocation targets (`staleReserveFingerprints` — all reserve-provenanced rows), becoming **an input that silently revokes the original device holding K**. One source of pullfrog's info point (an old reserve row remaining after a separation) is here; `key recovery`'s old re-seal (fact 9) is the other.
- (d) `device-sync.ts` (b), on observing an own-addressed `revoke_device` on the verified chain, marks the corresponding row revoked. So **a revoked B's reserve row gets marked at the next keyed sync** (self-heals). **A first-key B's reserve row is never fixed on any path** (observation records only "keys absent from the record" and doesn't rewrite an existing row's provenance — `device-sync.ts` :171).

#### K14-1's re-loop (the judgment's predicate)

| Option | Content | Upward compat | Silver bullet | Counterexamples | Adoption |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1-f **Widen `keyStandingIn`'s `firstKey` to "on this chain, was that person's first key in any membership"** (the current provenance is a first key ∨ an applied genesis / `add_member` payload with this key's public keys for that person). The predicate stays 1; all 3 readers get the same meaning | An upward compat of 1-d (adopted): plugs the re-invite hole (fact 8 (b)) and removes 1-e's rejection reason (the judgment splitting from `device add`) | 1-e split because it "made the predicate 2". **If the predicate itself is widened, no transcription is born**. `device add`'s 2 choices' same hole (a post-re-invite copied device being "registered" → exit 0 — next to K13's known limitation (1)) is plugged at the same time | Fact (a): decided only by facts remaining in the applied operation sequence after the membership ends (no sync · wire added) | Counterexample 1 "a DK device D was a past membership's first key and came back as `add_device` via E's sync after a re-invite" → running `device add` on D gets the 2 choices and exit 1 (previously "registered" → 0). The 2 choices' first ("if it's this device itself, nothing is needed") is true — an error on the speaking side (acceptable per K13-18's asymmetry). Counterexample 2 "a reserve key misjudged" → a reserve key is never a first key in any membership (fact 7 — a key absent from the keychain isn't used for genesis / invite acceptance). Counterexample 3 "someone else `add_member`d with the same public key" → narrow by `targetUserId` (§6.2's key uniqueness is only among current members, so the person narrowing is needed). Counterexample 4 "cost" → 1 scan of the applied operation sequence (`device list` calls it per device, so device count × sequence length. Smaller than the chain's verification itself) | **Adopted** (doesn't replace 1-d; widens 1-d's predicate) |
| 1-g Put the first-key judgment into `device-key.ts`'s `deviceProvenanceOf` | Next to the provenance predicate | — | — | `deviceProvenanceOf` is the "which key added the current device" question, read by `device list`'s display and `device-sync.ts`'s observation Note. Mixing a past membership in would make the "first key" display false about the current provenance | Rejected |

**Round 2**: upward compat = 1-f (widen the predicate). Silver bullet = fact (a). Counterexamples = 4 for 1-f (counterexample 1 is the speaking side), 1 for 1-g. **Round 3**: option tried "also carry 'was a first key before' on revoked (`revoked`) and absent (`absent`)" → `reserveVerdictOf` checks `first-key` before `revoked`, but for a revoked key the guidance (separate via `key recovery`) is the same either way, and `device add`'s revocation branch doesn't read first-key-ness. Carrying it changes no branch. No new options. Option tried "judge by `keyHistory`'s binding provenance" → `KeyBinding` holds just the key and FP, not which op bound it. No new options. **Round 4**: option tried "narrow the `add_member` payload comparison to just sig, not enc / sig" → key uniqueness is per the individual enc / sig keys (§6.2); comparing both is narrower with no error. A wording difference, not a new option. Option tried "return and display the seq where it was the first key" → no branch changes, and the wording is covered by the projects' enumeration. No new options. Rounds 3 · 4 empty — stopped.

**Principle**: "**a hole in the same question is plugged by widening the predicate itself, not by adding predicates (every reader is fixed at once)**" — K13-1 ✓, K7-15 ✓, hole 1 ✓ (if a first key anywhere, hand it to the human), K13-18 ✓ (the `device add` side's error just moves to the speaking side). **Adopted: 1-f** (on top of the existing 1-d).

#### K14-2's re-loop (the verdict's rules and the default)

**Round 1 (enumerating new options)**: 2-e "on `nowhere`, trust a past-recorded reserve row if one exists" → the record is advisory (K4-5), and a row recording a pre-DK K as reserve really exists (additional fact check (c)). Rejected. 2-f "on `unchecked`, retry just the unsyncable projects a few times" → a sync failure isn't necessarily transient (a tampering sign · a deleted project). Retries are better done by `--resume` at the human's moment — the wait is the human's to decide. Rejected. **Round 2**: option tried "fold `revoked` and `first-key` into 1 value 'doesn't work as the reserve key'" → the guidance differs (a first key gets "if a lost device, revoke"; an already-revoked one doesn't need that). Folding just moves the conditional inside the wording. No new options. Option tried "even for `added`, don't ask if absent on some projects" → an absent project is a legitimate reserve-key propagation delay (K4-3). Not asking would always drop a correct reserve key's record. No new options. **Round 3**: option tried "enforce the verdict's order via a type (the discriminated union's exhaustiveness)" → already enforced by the `switch`'s exhaustiveness. No new options. Rounds 2 · 3 empty — stopped. **Conclusion: keep 2-c** (the rules are unchanged. 1-f just grows the keys landing in `first-key`).

#### K14-3's re-loop (the confirmation's 1 question)

**Round 1**: 3-d "have the prompt make you type the FP's last 4 chars (confirming you read it)" → raises the confirmation's strength, but the fact line carries not just the FP but the projects' enumeration, and what the user cross-references is the enumeration. Transcribing an FP shows only "you read it". Just adds ceremony weight. Rejected. 3-e "skippable via a `--yes` flag" → builds a path skipping a confirmation inside a ceremony (K4-30 — opening the ledger is a ceremony). Non-interactive restores are refused at the opening stage, so the flag has no use. Rejected. **Round 2**: option tried "on `no`, record with the 'observed' provenance" → the next sync does the same thing (fact 2). Anticipating it has no benefit. No new options. **Round 3**: an empty round. Rounds 2 · 3 empty — stopped. **Conclusion: keep 3-b**.

#### K14-4's re-loop (the ledger-key verdict's transcriptions — in light of additional fact checks (c)(d))

| Option | Content | Upward compat | Silver bullet | Counterexamples | Adoption |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 4-f **Make the ledger key's verdict a single function `ledgerKeyVerdictOf` (`device-standing.ts` — `keyStandingsOf` + `groupStandings` + `reserveVerdictOf`), called after the FP match by every keyed-device command that opens the ledger — `key recovery` (separation) and `openLedgerReserveForChange` (`key seal passkey` / `guardian add` / `key reserve rotate` — refuse and guide `key recovery`)**. `unchecked` emits a Note and proceeds (the same handling in both) | An upward compat of 4-d (adopted): 4-e's rejection reason (scope) was "the transcriptions the prompt named", but additional fact check (c) showed **`openLedgerReserveForChange` records K as reserve, producing the input on which rotate / `--replace` silently revoke the original device**. Leaving the transcription lets another entrance override K14's verdict (don't record) | "Does the ledger's key work as the reserve key" gets 1 answering entrance (K7-15). The refusal's sentence takes the existing FP-match refusal's (`ledgerHoldsDeviceKeyMessage`) shape — what → why → `key recovery` first → re-run | Sharing the verdict's function | Counterexample 1 "an all-project sync is added to `seal passkey` / `guardian add`" → the same cost as `device add`'s existing-key judgment · `key recovery` (K13's known limitation (3)). A ledger change is a rare ceremony. Counterexample 2 "rotate opens each project keyed afterward anyway, so syncs double" → the verdict is needed before the change (stops it before the revocation). Rotate is rare. Counterexample 3 "proceeding on `unchecked`, if K is a first key only on unsyncable projects, today's same error" → the same known limitation as `key recovery`. The Note states the scope. Counterexample 4 "an import cycle" → `device-standing.ts` only reads `context.ts` / `device-key.ts` / `project-list.ts`, not `ledger-open.ts` | **Adopted** |
| 4-g **When the ledger key is found not to work as the reserve key, fix this device's wrong reserve row in 1 function**: `first-key` → if a reserve-provenanced row exists for that key, replace it with an observed-provenanced row built from chain-observed facts (the first-key projects · cap). `revoked` → mark it revoked (`markRevoked` — the same fact as observing the revocation on the chain). Called by `key recover` (`first-key` / `revoked`), `key recovery`'s separation, and `openLedgerReserveForChange`'s refusal | An upward compat of pullfrog's info point: plugs not just the pointed case (revocation) but the side that never fixes itself (first key — additional fact check (d)) | The row's writer is only a cryptographic fact (K4-3): "observed as a first key on the verified chain" and "observed revoked on the verified chain" are both facts of existing writers. No new provenance is created | — | Counterexample 1 "replacing with observed makes the next sync `add_device` that key to other projects" → the reserve row did the same (propagation doesn't depend on provenance — K4-3 (c)). What the replacement changes is leaving rotate / `--replace`'s revocation targets (doesn't silently revoke the original device) and the reserve-key-absence warning (K4-9) staying loud. Counterexample 2 "the revocation mark lands at the next sync anyway" → a rotate / `--replace` run in between would just re-revoke an already-revoked key (idempotent), but `key show` would display a revoked reserve key. Marking it now leaves no window. Counterexample 3 "no row exists" → do nothing | **Adopted** |
| 4-h Put the verdict inside `recordReserveLocally` itself (bounced at the record's entrance) | The writer's entrance is 1 place | — | — | `recordReserveLocally` is also called right after a sealing (a generated key — no verdict needed) and doesn't hold the sync's context (the token · the project list). If the record's function started a sync, every sealing gains one | Rejected |

**Round 2**: upward compat = 4-f (1 entrance), 4-g (plug the side that never fixes itself). Silver bullet = sharing the verdict's function. Counterexamples = 4 for 4-f, 3 for 4-g, 1 for 4-h. **Round 3**: option tried "on `first-key` at `openLedgerReserveForChange`, auto-separate instead of refusing" → silently inserts reserve-key generation and code issuance (a ceremony of having you store a new code) into someone who ran `seal passkey`. The command's meaning changes. Refusing and naming `key recovery` keeps K4-2's "separation is `key recovery` only". No new options. Option tried "cache the verdict's result to skip the 2nd command's sync" → makes local state a judgment input (K4-2 b-3's rejection reason — it inverts on a wipe). No new options. **Round 4**: option tried "run `key recover`'s verdict via `ledgerKeyVerdictOf` too" → `key recover` has the chains opened for registration; not double-syncing is better (K14-1). The pure `reserveVerdictOf` is shared, so it isn't a transcription. No new options. Rounds 3 · 4 empty — stopped.

**Principle**: "**every entrance answering the same question uses the same verdict, and when the verdict finds an error, the record that made the error is fixed on the spot**" — K7-15 ✓, K4-2 ✓ (separation is `key recovery` only · the ledger is changed by the one who opened it), K4-3 ✓ (the writer is a cryptographic fact only), K4-18 ✓ (revocation · discard are explicit — only the record's provenance is fixed; no on-chain revocation happens), K13-18 ✓. **Adopted: 4-d + 4-f + 4-g**. With this, K14-8's owner point (1) and pullfrog's info point are collected in this PR.

#### K14-5's re-loop (the verdict's position and the output's order)

**Round 1**: 5-c "emit the verdict and confirmation **before** the registration's report; the report after" → the confirmation's material (where it could / couldn't register) would come after the question. 5-a is superior. Rejected. 5-d "mix the verdict's fact lines into the registration report's lines (appending 'first key' / '`add_device`' per project)" → the report is the registration's result; the verdict is the bundle over all projects. Mixed, the confirmation question's bundling becomes unreadable. Rejected. **Round 2**: option tried "run the verdict as a pure function before registration (still 1 sync — collect all standings first, register after)" → the collecting stage and the registration stage can use the same chain, but if the chain advances between collecting and registering, registration re-syncs (`appendAddDevice`'s CAS). Only the verdict's material and the registration's moment drift apart — no benefit (breaks 5-a's axis "the confirmation comes after the material"). No new options. **Round 3**: an empty round. **Round 4**: an empty round. Stopped. **Conclusion: keep 5-a**.

#### K14-6's re-loop (docs)

**Round 1**: 6-c "merge `recover-your-key.mdx`'s 4 verdict items with `devices.mdx`'s `device add` standing's 5 items (K13-11) into 1 place" → the questions differ (`device add` is "may this device's key be discarded"; `key recover` is "may the ledger's key be recorded as the reserve key"). The predicate is shared, but the reader's next move differs. Merging adds conditional sentences. But `devices.mdx`'s pre-DK procedure (when the copy is on another device) needs 1 sentence (why a ledger-changing command stops). **Adopted (1 sentence in the body)**. **Round 2**: option tried "generate docs' 4 items from the CLI's wording" → there's no generation mechanism; `cli-vocabulary.test.ts`'s net verifies quotations' existence. No new options. **Round 3**: an empty round. Stopped. **Conclusion: 6-a + 6-d (1 sentence in `devices.mdx`'s pre-DK paragraph saying a ledger change from another device stops)**.

**Round 1**: 7-b "add a unit test for `device-standing.ts`'s pure function (1-f's membership scan — building a re-invite chain)" → K14-7's rejection reason (the wiring drops) applies to replacing the CLI-result check; **adding** it pins both the wiring and the property. But if the same property (a post-re-invite `key recover` is `first-key`; `device add` gets the 2 choices) can be confirmed via the CLI's result, the unit test is a duplication. Pin it by the CLI's result. Placed inside 7-a. **Round 2**: option tried "put the `ledger-open` refusal in `passkey.test.ts` / the `guardian` tests" → the refusal's verdict lives in `openLedgerReserveForChange`'s 1 place, and the 3 commands go through the same function. Placed on 1 command in `device.test.ts`, which has the chain-attached scaffolding (`key reserve rotate` — the entrance with the largest harm of silently revoking the original device), plus 1 item added in `passkey.test.ts` next to the existing FP-match refusal (path agreement). No new options (an allocation of placements). **Round 3**: an empty round. **Round 4**: an empty round. Stopped. **Conclusion: keep 7-a, adding 1-f / 4-f / 4-g's positive · negative cases**.

### Additional rulings during review handling (K14-13 onward — responses to PR #205)

**K14-13 (Cursor Security Agent — HIGH: `unchecked`'s fail-open leads to a revocation)**: the finding is "`openLedgerReserveForChange` proceeds recording the opened key as reserve under `unchecked`, and `key reserve rotate` `revoke_device`s that key on every project. A pre-DK copy that was a first key only on the unsyncable projects gets revoked while it's a device key still in use. `key recovery`'s `unchecked` also records, creating the revoke targets for a later rotate / `--replace`". **Confirmed correct in the source** (`ledger-open.ts`'s `unchecked` branch → `recordReserveLocally`; `keyReserveRotateOp` → `staleReserveFingerprints` [the opened B + all reserve-provenanced rows] → `registerReserveAndRetire` → `rotateReserveOnProject`'s `appendRevokeDevice`; `keyRecoveryOp`'s `unchecked` → re-seal → `recordReserveLocally`). The mistake in K14-4's 4-d / 4-f judgment — "`unchecked` states the scope in a Note and proceeds (don't stop a code re-issuance for an unrelated outage)" — was **folding into 1 the re-issuance (only changes the ledger's KEK; doesn't change the key's meaning) and the record · revocation (which decide the key's meaning and are unrevocable)**. And a deeper look found **an existing hole of the same root**: rotate / `--replace` revoke **all** of this device's reserve-provenanced rows **without checking the chain** (`staleReserveFingerprints`). If a previous CLI (pre-K14 `seal passkey` / `guardian add` / `key recovery`) had recorded the copy K as reserve, K gets revoked even when the ledger holds a real reserve key.

| Option | Content | Upward compat | Silver bullet | Counterexamples | Adoption |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 13-a Do nothing (keep the Note) | — | — | — | Per the finding, the revocation is unrevocable | Rejected |
| 13-b Refuse every ledger-changing command on `unchecked` (`key recovery`'s re-issuance too) | Simple | — | — | Stops someone wanting to re-issue on a suspected code leak over an unrelated broken project (a tampering sign · a deleted list row). `--replace` (the escape for a lost code) is stopped too | Rejected |
| 13-c **Split the effect in 2 and require each one's needed facts**: (1) **recording** (recording the opened key as reserve) only on a positive verdict (`added` / `nowhere`) — `unchecked` doesn't record (the same rule as `key recover`). (2) **revocation** (rotate / `--replace` revoking the old reserve keys) is judged per key to revoke, and `first-key` (a device key — never revoked. The row is fixed to an observed one — 4-g) and `unchecked` (uncheckable) are **left unrevoked**, stated in a Warning. Only `added` / `nowhere` / `revoked` (the continuation of an interrupted rotate — the existing test "the last one broke mid ledger swap") are revoked. The verdict runs **before** rewriting the ledger (decide whether to keep the key, then seal). The ledger's operation itself (re-issuance · passkey · guardian · sealing a new reserve key) proceeds even under `unchecked` | An upward compat of 13-b: stops only the unrevocable effect, not the re-issuance · the escape. On top of the finding's case, the existing hole (an unchecked revocation of a recorded row) is also plugged in the same 1 place | **A gate just before the revocation**: the 1 place building the set of keys to revoke (`confirmRetiring`) passes through the chain's verdict. Whatever the record's provenance (even a previous CLI's wrong row), a revocation requires the chain's fact | Sharing the verdict's function (`ledgerKeyVerdictOf`) | Counterexample 1 "leaving the old reserve key on `unchecked` leaves a secret-less key (the old B swapped out for the new reserve key) on the chain" → a key nobody holds the secret for — not a confidentiality problem (same as K4-2's counterexample 1). The Warning names `maruhi device revoke <fp>` (a human deletes it after confirming). Counterexample 2 "rotate's cost" → a per-project sync per key being revoked (usually 1–2 keys). Rotate is a rare ceremony. Counterexample 3 "`--replace` doesn't open the ledger, so it doesn't know the old B" → what gets revoked is only the record's rows, each passing the gate. Counterexample 4 "may `revoked` be revoked" → on the already-revoked project `appendRevokeDevice` does nothing (idempotent). If still valid elsewhere, it's an interrupted rotate's old reserve key (the verdict confirmed it isn't a first key) | **Adopted** |
| 13-d Inside `rotateReserveOnProject`, skip the revocation per project if a first key | No extra syncs | — | — | If a first-key project can't be synced it's invisible, and on a project where it was `add_device`d (propagation) it would be revoked — a device in use falls off some projects. The verdict needs the bundle over all projects | Rejected (wrapped by 13-c's gate) |

**Round 2**: upward compat = 13-c (splitting the needed facts per effect). Silver bullet = the gate just before the revocation. Counterexamples = 4 for 13-c, 1 each for 13-b / 13-d. **Round 3**: option tried "add a 'verdicted' mark to the record's rows and revoke only marked rows" → changes `own-devices.json`'s fields (out of scope) and makes local state a judgment input (K4-2 b-3). No new options. Option tried "`seal passkey` / `guardian add` also refuse on `unchecked`" → without recording, a sealing is just adding a new way to open the same B (an operation by the one who opens the ledger — K4-2) and decides no key's meaning. No reason to stop it. No new options. **Round 4**: option tried "put `key recover`'s `unchecked` through the same gate" → `key recover` already doesn't record and doesn't revoke. No change needed. No new options. Stopped.

**Principle**: "**what's stopped when undecidable is only unrevocable effects (a revocation) and the records that would later feed that effect. The ledger's operation itself isn't stopped. Just before an unrevocable effect, place 1 gate requiring the chain's fact regardless of the input's provenance**" — K13-18 ✓ (avoid the side where the error stays silent — a revocation speaks but is unrevocable), K4-18 ✓ (discard · revocation after checking), K4-3 ✓ (the record is a cryptographic fact only), K4-38 ✓ (don't block `--replace`'s escape). **Adopted: 13-c**. K14-4's 4-d / 4-f "`unchecked` states the scope in a Note and proceeds" is amended to "the ledger's operation proceeds, but neither records nor revokes".

**K14-13's implementation**: `ledger-open.ts` — `openLedgerReserveForChange`'s `unchecked` emits a Note and proceeds to the ledger's operation but doesn't `recordReserveLocally` (`describeUncheckedLedgerKey` — the uncheckable scope's clause in 1 place). `key-recover.ts` — `separateUnusableLedgerKey` returns `"separated" | "reissue-only" | "record"`, and `keyRecoveryOp` records only on `"record"`. The just-before-revocation gate `confirmRetiring` (per key to revoke, `ledgerKeyVerdictOf`; `first-key` → `retractReserveRecord` + Warning; `unchecked` → left with a Warning) is placed **before rewriting the ledger** in `keyReserveRotateOp` and `replaceReserveWithoutOpening` (generate the new reserve key → compute the revoke targets → the gate → issue the code → register and revoke). `--replace`'s "no old reserve key in the record" Warning is judged on the pre-gate set (a key the gate left is named by a different Warning). docs: 1 gate sentence in `devices.mdx`'s reserve-key section. Tests: `device.test.ts`'s 2 `unchecked` items moved to the new behavior (rotate: the new reserve key is sealed · registered but the old key is neither revoked nor recorded / `key recovery`: re-issues but doesn't record), plus 1 check on the gate (a copy a previous CLI recorded as reserve is not revoked by rotate, and its row is fixed to an observed one) — 96 → 97. The 5 mutations (P1 the prelude records even on `unchecked` / P2 records even on `reissue-only` / P3 the gate passes a first key / P4 the gate passes an uncheckable key / P5 the gate doesn't fix the row) all failed. `bun run check` all exit 0 (5074 tests); `fallow audit --base origin/main` reported nothing on 19 files.

**K14-14 (Cursor Bugbot — High: the revocation gate lets the "revoked" verdict pass even with uncheckable projects)**: the finding is "`reserveVerdictOf` returns `revoked` if a revocation exists on any synced project, before looking at unsyncable ones. The gate (`confirmRetiring`) puts `revoked` on the may-revoke side, so a device key that is a first key only on the unsyncable projects (a copy already revoked on 1 project per the CLI's guidance) gets revoked on the other valid projects". **Confirmed correct in the source** (the verdict's order `first-key` > `revoked` > `unchecked`; the gate's `else` side). K14-13's gate wrote "the chain's fact is required per key to revoke", yet **didn't require having checked every project** for `revoked` (folding 2 branch conditions — isn't a first key · was checked everywhere — into the verdict's 1 value).

| Option | Content | Upward compat | Silver bullet | Counterexamples | Adoption |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 14-a Change the verdict's order to `unchecked` > `revoked` | 1 line | — | — | Weakens the separation · refusal judgments: `key recovery` would re-issue a revoked (dead) key's code (`reissue-only`), and the ledger change's prelude would seal a revoked key unrefused. The order is right for the revocable side's judgments | Rejected |
| 14-b **`ledgerKeyVerdictOf` returns, beside the verdict, an "uncheckable scope" (unsyncable projects · the list failure — independent of the verdict's value), and the gate revokes only when "not a first key ∧ every project was checked"**. The verdict's order is unchanged | The gate's condition is written as the facts a revocation needs (the 2). `added` / `nowhere` are already checked-everywhere values, unchanged; only `revoked` is affected | Carry the verdict's value (the conclusion told to the user) and the completeness the unrevocable effect needs in separate fields — don't fold a condition into the value | — | Counterexample 1 "an interrupted rotate's continuation (`revoked`) stops on an unrelated sync failure" → left unrevoked and named in a Warning; a re-run after it can be checked continues it (the revocation is idempotent). Making the unrevocable side wait is K14-13's principle. Counterexample 2 "the Warning's sentence" → the scope clause shares `describeUncheckedLedgerKey` (no transcription made) | **Adopted** |

**Round 2**: upward compat = 14-b (separating the value from the completeness). Counterexamples = 1 for 14-a, 2 for 14-b. **Round 3**: option tried "add an 'unsyncable projects' field to the `revoked` value" → the completeness is a fact attaching to every value, not just `revoked`; adding a field per value would transcribe 14-b by the number of values. No new options. **Round 4**: option tried "read `groups` directly in the gate" → a list failure (`listFailure`) doesn't enter the groups (it's `keyStandingsOf`'s field). `ledgerKeyVerdictOf` bundling and returning it keeps it in 1 place. No new options. Stopped.

**Principle**: "**the conclusion told to the user (the verdict's value) and the fact the unrevocable effect needs (the completeness) travel in separate fields, and the gate reads the latter directly**" — K14-13 ✓, K13-18 ✓. Along with it, pullfrog's nitpick (`--replace`'s opening Warning states more strongly than the gate's leave case — "every recorded reserve key is revoked on every project") rides this push (which includes a code change).

**K14-14's implementation**: `device-standing.ts` `ledgerKeyVerdictOf` returns, beside the verdict's value, `unchecked` (the uncheckable scope — null = everything was checked). `key-recover.ts` `confirmRetiring` revokes only when "not a first key ∧ `unchecked === null`" (the Warning's clause shares `describeUncheckedLedgerKey`). `--replace`'s opening Warning and help's `--replace` description moved to the letter "revokes only the keys confirmed not to be device keys on the chain" (pullfrog's nitpick — the golden was updated).
