### Re-inspection at the owner's question (2026-09-26 — "are (a)–(c) really right; isn't there a better solution")

The 3 points the completion report listed as "behaviors decided as the default" — (a) the sync cost on the ledger-changing commands, (b) `device add` on a device holding a past-membership first key exits 1, (c) when uncheckable, the ledger's operation proceeds while only the record and the revocation are withheld — were re-inspected with the same procedure (enumerate options → the upward-compat · silver-bullet · counterexample loop → stopped at 2 empty rounds).

**K14-15 ((c): rotate's half-done completion)**: applying (c) to rotate means: when uncheckable, **the new reserve key is sealed · registered while the old reserve key's revocation alone is deferred**. Rotate's purpose is "invalidating a reserve key suspected of exposure" (K4-11), and the ledger alone changing while the old key stays valid is a state that **looks completed without achieving the purpose** (a user who doesn't read the Warning believes it's replaced). K14-13 put rotate on the same shelf as `seal passkey` / `guardian add` (deciding no key's meaning) · `key recovery`'s re-issuance (only changes the KEK) · `--replace` (the escape for someone who lost the code), but for rotate alone **the operation's purpose itself is the revocation**.

| Option | Content | Upward compat | Silver bullet | Counterexamples | Adoption |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 15-a The status quo (proceed, leave the old key, Warning) | — | — | — | The above: a completion that doesn't achieve the purpose. The new and old reserve keys, both secret-less, sit side by side on the chain | Rejected |
| 15-b **Rotate, if the opened ledger key can't be confirmed on every project, stops without changing anything** (before touching the ledger · record · chain). If only the other record rows (old reserve keys other than the opened key) can't be confirmed, the opened key's replacement achieves the purpose so it proceeds, and those rows are left per K14-13's gate with a Warning | The operation's unit matched to its purpose: all of it, or nothing | An operation whose purpose is a revocation makes the fact the revocation needs (every project's verdict) the operation's **precondition** — it doesn't do "the part it can" midway | — | Counterexample 1 "want to swap a suspected-exposed reserve key fast, but it stops on an unrelated broken project" → even stopped, the old key's validity is the same as 15-a (neither revoked it). What 15-a gains is only "a new reserve key", which doesn't invalidate the old one. A re-run once syncable does all of it. For a hurry, the escape `maruhi device revoke <fp>` already exists (an explicit revocation — a human confirms and runs it). Counterexample 2 "should `--replace` work the same" → `--replace` is the escape for someone who lost the code (K4-38); replacing an unopenable ledger is itself its purpose (the old keys' revocation is an accompaniment limited to the record's rows). Stopping it blocks the escape. Left alone | **Adopted** |
| 15-c Split rotate into 2 stages (do just the replacement first, the revocation later via `key reserve rotate --resume`) | Makes the partial progress an explicit state | — | — | Needs a new flag · state carrying the interrupted state; adds a user procedure. 15-b gets the same result with 1 re-run | Rejected |

**Round 2**: upward compat = 15-b. Silver bullet = the purpose's fact as the precondition. Counterexamples = 2 for 15-b (each resolvable). **Round 3**: option tried "stop `seal passkey` / `guardian add` too" → their purpose (adding a new way to open) is achieved regardless of the verdict, and not recording creates no revocation input (K14-13). No reason to stop them. No new options. **Round 4**: option tried "stop `key recovery`'s re-issuance too" → its purpose (replacing the code) is achieved regardless of the verdict (the same key under a new code). Stopping it would delay the remedy for someone suspecting a leak. No new options. Stopped.

**Principle**: "**if the operation's purpose is an unrevocable effect, make the fact that effect needs the operation's precondition (don't proceed just for the doable part). An operation whose purpose doesn't include the effect proceeds with only the effect deferred**" — a sharpening of K14-13's principle, K4-11 ✓, K4-38 ✓.

**K14-16 ((a): the duplicate syncs)**: on rotate, `openLedgerReserveForChange` and the revocation gate `confirmRetiring` each called `ledgerKeyVerdictOf` for the opened ledger key — **syncing every project twice** (pullfrog's nitpick). With multiple old record rows, 1 more per row.

| Option | Content | Upward compat | Silver bullet | Counterexamples | Adoption |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 16-a The status quo | — | — | — | The same verdict twice. Rotate is rare, but the sync is proportional to the project count | Rejected |
| 16-b Pass the opened key's verdict to the gate; only the other rows sync at the gate | Removes 1 duplication | — | — | With 2+ other rows, a per-row sync remains | Rejected (wrapped by 16-c) |
| 16-c **Make the sync "once per project" and derive multiple keys' standings from it via a pure function (`keyStandingsForKeys` — the same shape as `device list` laying out all devices in 1 sync). The ledger-key verdict also returns multiple keys bundled (`ledgerKeyChecksOf`). Rotate checks the opened key and the record's rows together in 1 pass, and the ledger-opening stage and the gate share its result** | The sync is once regardless of key count | Uses K13-1's separate-sync-and-verdict structure (the pure `keyStandingIn`) as-is — just widening the wrapper from "1 key" to "a set of keys" | K13-1's pure function | Counterexample 1 "a 1-key caller like `device add`" → the 1-key wrappers (`keyStandingsOf` / `ledgerKeyVerdictOf`) remain as the set version's special cases (the same behavior · output). Counterexample 2 "the ledger-opening stage holding the verdict tangles the order with the gate" → split the ledger-opening stage into "the opening (up to the FP-match refusal)" and "the verdict-driven handling (the refusal · the row correction · the record)"; rotate calls them in the order open → bundle-judge → handle → gate. `seal passkey` / `guardian add` stay 1 function as before | **Adopted** |

**Round 2**: upward compat = 16-c. Silver bullet = K13-1's pure function. **Round 3**: option tried "also share rotate's latter stage's sync (`rotateReserveOnProject` — opens a project keyed)" → the latter stage is the keyed prelude (`openProject` — goes through the floor · declarations · the first sync's registration), a different check from the keyless prelude. Sharing would skip the latter's check or bloat the former. No new options. **Round 4**: an empty round. Stopped. **Adopted: 16-c**. With this, (a) is reduced to "the ledger-changing ceremony gains 1 per-project-count sync" (the same as `device add` / `key recovery`).

**K14-17 ((b)'s inspection — no change)**: inspected (b) "`device add` on a device whose key was a first key in a past membership gets the 2 choices (exit 1)". Options: 17-a the status quo (exit 1), 17-b exit 0 (registered) when it's only a past membership, 17-c split only the wording to "was your first key". Counterexample to 17-b: if that key is a pre-DK copy, the 1-key-on-2-devices state stays silently (K13-18's asymmetry — re-opens here the hole 1-f plugged). 17-c: the branch doesn't change, and the 2-choice sentence's "the key you created or joined that project with" is also true of a past membership (a key it joined with on that membership). No reader benefit matching the cost of adding a distinction field. **Rounds 2 · 3**: option tried "distinguish copy from original by another chain fact (whether the key signed after the past membership)" → both the original and the copied device sign with the same key, so a signature isn't distinguishing material (the same as K13's "mechanically decide copy vs original" rejection). No new options. Stopped. **Conclusion: keep 17-a** (avoid the side where the error stays silent — K13-18).

**K14-15 / K14-16's implementation**: `device-standing.ts` — sync and verdict were split, with `keyStandingsForKeys` (syncs the list's projects once each, applies `keyStandingIn` per key — internal), `standingFrom` (1 key's standing from 1 sync's result), and `LedgerKeyCheck` / `ledgerKeyChecksOf` (multiple keys' verdicts in 1 sync). `keyStandingsOf` / `keyStandingOnProject` / `ledgerKeyVerdictOf` go through the same parts as the 1-key special cases (`device add` etc.'s output is unchanged). `ledger-open.ts` — `openLedgerReserveForChange` was split into `openLedgerKeyForChange` (the opening and the FP-match refusal) and `settleLedgerKeyForChange` (the verdict-driven handling — `onUnchecked: "proceed" | "refuse"`) (`seal passkey` / `guardian add` stay 1 function as before with "proceed"). `key-recover.ts` — `keyReserveRotateOp` runs open → compute the revoke targets (the opened key + the record's rows) → **a single bundled verdict** → `settleLedgerKeyForChange` ("refuse" — if the opened key can't be confirmed on every project, it stops without touching the ledger · record · chain) → the gate → generate the new reserve key · issue the code · register and revoke. `--replace`'s gate also uses the single verdict. `confirmRetiring` became a pure gate taking the verdict (doesn't sync. A key with no verdict isn't revoked). docs: 1 sentence in `devices.mdx`'s reserve-key section that rotate stops. Tests: `device.test.ts`'s rotate `unchecked` check replaced with "stops without changing anything"; K14-14's gate check moved to `--replace` (rotate stops before the gate); the gate check gained "the chain fetch before the ledger rewrite is once per project". `passkey.test.ts`'s sealing positive case gained "the list is unreadable = uncheckable: the seal proceeds but doesn't record". The 4 mutations (R1 rotate proceeds unconfirmed / R2 syncing the opened key separately / R3 the prelude not emitting its Note / R4 the prelude recording an unconfirmable key) — R4 survived at first (the sealing's positive case hadn't been watching whether a record existed — the behavior does change), a check was added and confirmed it fails. A mid-way fallow unused-export finding (`keyStandingsForKeys`) was closed inside the module. `bun run check` all exit 0 (5075 tests); `fallow audit --base origin/main` reported nothing on 19 files.

**K14-18 (Cursor Security Agent — HIGH: the first key's history never reaches the verdict on an already-revoked standing)**: the finding is "`reserveVerdictOf` emits `first-key` only from the **currently valid** standing's `firstKey`. Since `keyStandingIn` discards the chain on revoked (and absent) standings, a copy already revoked on its first-key project, if valid as `add_device` on another project, becomes `revoked` (`unchecked === null`) even when every project was checked, passes the gate, and rotate / `--replace` revokes it on the remaining valid projects". **Confirmed correct in the source** (`ChainKeyStanding`'s `revoked` / `absent` carry only `kind`. `reserveVerdictOf`'s `groups.active.filter(firstKey)`). At K14-1's round 3 I'd rejected "also carry 'was a first key' on revoked · absent" with "carrying it changes no branch", but on the later-added revocation gate (K14-13) **the branch does change**. A ruling's premise had collapsed under a later change without re-examining the rejected option (K6-X's "when you add a branch, re-read existing rulings' premises" shape).

| Option | Content | Upward compat | Silver bullet | Counterexamples | Adoption |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 18-a Only in the gate, re-read the revoked projects' chains | Local | — | — | "First key"'s answer would split between the verdict and the gate (K7-15). `key recover` / `key recovery` / the ledger-change prelude carry the same hole (call a revoked first key `revoked` — the separation · refusal are the same but the wording's facts are weak) | Rejected |
| 18-b **Widen 1-f's predicate one more step: `keyStandingIn` carries `firstKey` (was that person's first key in any membership on this chain — a revoked key's public keys remain in `keyHistory`) on revoked · absent standings too; `groupStandings` collects into `firstKeyProjects` the projects where it was a first key on any standing; `reserveVerdictOf` is `first-key` if there's even 1** | The verdict · the gate · the ledger-change prelude · `key recovery` are all fixed at once | Widening the predicate, not adding one (an extension of 1-f's principle) | Fact (a) + `keyHistory` (a revoked key stays in the binding history — `device-key.ts` `historicalFingerprintOf` already uses it) | Counterexample 1 "`device add`'s branch" → `device add` decides the 2 choices on only the valid standings' `firstKey` (K13-2), and says already-revoked projects in the revocation Note. It doesn't read the groups' new field, so the behavior is unchanged. Counterexample 2 "in the wrong-row correction (4-g), there's no valid first-key standing" → if any valid standing exists, replace with an observed row from its facts (cap · provenance); if valid nowhere, mark it revoked (both are verified-chain observation facts). Counterexample 3 "an absent (not re-registered after the past membership) standing" → the fact of having been that membership's first key works the same as evidence of a copy | **Adopted** |

**Round 2**: upward compat = 18-b. Silver bullet = widening the predicate + `keyHistory`. **Round 3**: option tried "add 'on which standing it was a first key' to the verdict's value" → the wording (`first-key`'s Warning / refusal) is covered by the projects' enumeration; no branch changes. No new options. **Round 4**: an empty round. Stopped. **Principle**: "**if a rejected option's rejection reason was 'no branch changes', re-examine it the moment a branch is added**" (re-listing K6-X).

**K14-18's implementation**: `device-standing.ts` — `ChainKeyStanding`'s `revoked` / `absent` carry `firstKey` (the public keys resolved from the binding history `keyHistory`, `wasFirstKeyOf` applied), `StandingGroups.firstKeyProjects` (projects where it was [ever] a first key on any standing), and `reserveVerdictOf`'s `first-key` reads it. `device add`'s 2 choices (`reportActiveKey`) still read only the valid standings' `firstKey` (behavior unchanged). `reserve.ts` `retractReserveRecord` — a `first-key` correction replaces the row with an observed row built from a valid standing's facts (preferring the first-key projects, otherwise another valid standing); if valid nowhere, marks it revoked. Tests: `device.test.ts` +2 (a copy already revoked on its first-key project and valid as `add_device` on another isn't revoked by a fully-checked rotate, and its row is fixed to p3's observed row / a pure revocation [a non-first-key reserve key]'s `key recovery` separation). Re-composed 2 existing checks (one had been using the genesis key as "a revoked key" — since the verdict now correctly returns `first-key`, its expectation moved to the `first-key` sentence; "revoked on only some" now uses a non-first-key reserve key). The 6 mutations (S1 not looking at first keys on revoked · absent standings / S2 collecting first-key projects only from valid standings / S3 the correction not using an alternative valid standing / S4 not marking revoked when valid nowhere / re-confirming M11·M12) all failed — S4 survived at first (no check watched the row's revocation mark), so a check was added. `bun run check` all exit 0 (5077 tests); `fallow audit --base origin/main` reported nothing on 19 files. Known limitation: the `first-key` Warning / refusal sentence says "is your first key on …" in the present tense even when already revoked on that first-key project (the enumerated projects are right. Little benefit in adding a field for the tense distinction — the same call as K14-17's 17-c).

**K14-19 (pullfrog — the wrong-row correction says "on none" and marks the row revoked even with an unsyncable project)**: since `first-key` outranks `unchecked`, even with an unsyncable project it entered `retractReserveRecord`'s "no valid standing" branch, claimed "registered nowhere now" covering a scope it hadn't checked, and marked the row revoked. Confirmed correct in the source (the gate protects the key, so the impact is wording and the record — though the record's writer must be only observed facts [K4-3], which this violates). pullfrog marked it a nitpick, but `pullfrog-approval` colored it red as unaddressed feedback, so it was fixed in this push. Options: (a) **with an unsyncable project, don't touch the row**, (b) mark it revoked but soften only the wording (the record would hold an unobserved fact — contradicts K4-3), (c) do nothing (the record would be untrue). **(a)**. 1 test added (with an unsyncable project, doesn't say "registered nowhere" and doesn't mark the row revoked). Mutation T1 (removing the condition) failed. `bun run check` all exit 0 (5078 tests); `fallow audit --base origin/main` reported nothing on 19 files.

## 20. K15 addendum (2026-09-26 — collecting a DK carryover: the ledger-key verdict's "all projects" not relying on the server's declaration alone)

Collecting the hole remaining after K14 (PR #205 — `b82f79f`). K14 decides the ledger key's treatment (`key recover`'s recording · `key recovery`'s separation · the ledger change's prelude · the rotate / `--replace` revocation gate) by the on-chain carriage **on the projects the server listed via `GET /projects`**. A malicious or compromised server hiding from the list a project where a key was a first key makes the CLI see that key as "`add_device`-provenanced everywhere" (`added` · `unchecked === null`), letting rotate / `--replace` revoke a device key in use (a pre-DK copy) and `key recover`'s yes / `key recovery` / `key seal passkey` / `guardian add` record that copy as the reserve key. The harm is availability and record error (confidentiality isn't lost). It isn't a hole K14 worsened — pre-K14 it revoked unchecked — and it shares K13's known limitation (2)'s root. The owner's ruling (K15's prompt §0): handled this round. Specs (CRYPTO / AUTH / AUDIT), ADRs, `CLAUDE.md`, `packages/crypto`, the server, wire formats, acceptance-policy values, `own-devices.json`'s fields, the floor's format, and the ledger's format are unchanged (if a change is needed → an "owner point". Cryptographically backing the list's completeness is fine to consider and put to an owner point). W-4 / W-6 · K5 owner points (1)(2) · K2 carryover 4 · K7 owner points (4)(5) · K8 owner point (2) · K9 owner points (2)(3) · K13 carryovers (1)(2) are out of scope. The table uses K6-X's format (a blank is the mark of "not searched"). Rulings are recorded here before implementation, and mid-implementation · review-handling judgments are added in the same shape as K15-N.

**Pre-start check**: starting from `origin/main` (`b82f79f` — #205's merge. No commits after). **PR #205's last Cursor Security Agent result**: the run on the last head `87201aa` was `success` (check run 108391214994, completed 2026-09-26T11:05:37Z) with no review · finding posted on `87201aa` (the earlier 2 HIGHs were handled at K14-13 / K14-18 and the threads resolved). So there's nothing to fix as K15's precondition. Before this section there's no K15 section or record of the same task ("the server hides" appears only as §18's hole 2 · K13-7 · K13's known limitation (2), and "out of scope" in K14's completion record onward), and 0 open PRs on GitHub.

**Principle (this round's axis)**: "**the server's list is a discovery declaration and witnesses no completeness. A fact outside the list is used only in the direction that stops an unrevocable effect or a silently-persisting record — only when it's partitioned by server · account, written only by verified-chain observation, and is a fact about the key itself**".

### K15 fact check (read the code's branches and the predicates' definitions — K6-X. Line numbers are b82f79f's)

1. **Judgments premised on "all projects"** (grep: the call sites of `fetchProjectMemberships` / `keyStandingsForKeys` / `ledgerKeyChecksOf` / `keyStandingsOf` / `reserveVerdictOf` / `ledgerKeyVerdictOf` / `keyStandingOnProject` / `resolveProjectIds` / `listProjectIds` — K7-15). Every listing goes through `project-list.ts` `fetchProjectMemberships` (a server declaration — its leading note says "for discovery").
   - **Unrevocable effects (revocation · sweep)**: (a) `key reserve rotate` (`key-recover.ts` `keyReserveRotateOp` :978– — `ledgerKeyChecksOf` :997 → `settleLedgerKeyForChange` ("refuse") → `confirmRetiring` → `registerReserveAndRetire` :662 **re-fetches the list** :683 and runs `rotateReserveOnProject`'s `appendRevokeDevice` + sweep on only the listed projects). (b) `key recovery --replace` (`replaceReserveWithoutOpening` :587– — `ledgerKeyChecksOf` :616 → `confirmRetiring` → the same latter stage). **A revocation doesn't reach a hidden project** (the key stays valid there), but a device key in use on a listed project gets revoked, and the sweep runs.
   - **Records (the reserve-provenanced rows — the later rotate / `--replace` revocation's input)**: (c) `key recover` (`finishRecovery` :322– — the list :332 → each project's registration → `groupStandings` → `reserveVerdictOf(groups, null)` :355 → `settleOpenedKey`'s `added` yes → `recordReserveLocally`). (d) `key recovery` (`separateUnusableLedgerKey` :529– — `ledgerKeyVerdictOf` → `added` / `nowhere` → `recordReserveLocally`). (e) `key seal passkey` / `guardian add` (`ledger-open.ts` `openLedgerReserveForChange` :101– → `settleLedgerKeyForChange` ("proceed") → records unless fully checked to be `first-key` / `revoked`). (f) the wrong reserve row's correction (`reserve.ts` `retractReserveRecord` — acts only on `first-key` / `revoked`. Under hiding it lands on the not-acting side; no wrong correction occurs).
   - **Display · branching only (no unrevocable effect)**: (g) `device add`'s existing key (`device.ts` `settleExistingKey` :334 — `keyStandingsOf`. `add_device`-provenanced everywhere valid → exit 0; zero valid → "on none" → guides `--replace` — the sentence states the scope "the N projects the server listed" via `describeListed`, with the floor's enumeration appended as information [K13-7]. `--replace` discards this device's key, but it's an explicit human-typed operation). (h) expiry · `--replace`'s displays (`describeChainsNow` / `describeReplacedKey`). (i) `device list` (`resolveProjectIds` :144 — display). (j) `device approve` (same — `add_device`s onto the listed projects. Under hiding it just doesn't add). (k) `device revoke` (same — revokes on the listed projects. On a hidden project the key stays valid = the direction of revoking too little — the opposite of this task [revoking too much], out of scope). (l) `project list` (display — already says "as reported by the server").
2. **What the server can drop from the list** (`apps/server/src/handlers-membership.ts` "list" :178– — read only): candidates come from D1's projection (the index), each candidate's DO is asked `memberRoleFor`, and only passers are returned. **There are 2 situations where even an honest server drops a row**: (i) outside the token's scope (`scopedProjectIdsFor` — though ledger-touching commands require a key-material-class `*` × admin token [`authz.ts` `ensureKeyMaterialAccess` :129], so it can't occur on K15's target paths), (ii) a DO-confirmation fault (`catchDefect` drops the row — a broken chain's die is decisive and the comment says it **can be permanent**). A malicious · compromised server can drop any row. The list's response is unsigned, and the CLI holds no material to check its completeness.
3. **List-outside paths by which the CLI learns a project ID**:

   | Path | Partitioned by server · account? | Written by | Contents | When gone |
   |---|---|---|---|---|
   | The floor (`floor.ts` `floorDirOf` / `listProjectIds`) | **No** (1 per config-file location. Filenames are project IDs only — K13's hole 2) | A verified sync | The head's record (holds no key set) | Judges nothing (the enumeration of information just shrinks) |
   | pins (`pins.ts` `invites/<projectId>.json`) | **No** (project ID only) | Invite acceptance · issuance | Anchor · issue pins | Degrades to pin-less |
   | **`own-devices.json`** (`own-devices.ts`) | **Yes** (`known[origin][userId][fp]` — `load(origin, userId)`) | 3 paths only (K4-3): the reserve key's sealing (provenance reserve, `addedByFingerprintHex: null`), `device approve` (provenance approved, `addedByFingerprintHex` = the approving device's FP — `device.ts` :941–), **verified-chain observation** (provenance observed, `addedByFingerprintHex` = `deviceProvenanceOf`'s provenance — null for a first key, `observedProjectId` = the project it was observed on — `device-sync.ts` :171–). Plus K14's correction (`retractReserveRecord` — replaced with an observed row built from a valid standing's provenance) | 1 row per key (upsert by FP). An observed row holds **only the 1 project it was first observed on** | No record (fail-open) |
   | The config's `defaultProject` | 1 per config file | The human | 1 ID | — |
   | The fingerprint book (`known-fingerprints.ts`) | Partitioned (origin → user_id) | Ceremonies | **Other people's** key-FP set. Holds no project ID | — |

   `markRevoked` only marks an existing row; it creates none (`own-devices.ts` :377–). So a row with "provenance observed ∧ `addedByFingerprintHex === null` ∧ `observedProjectId` present" is **a record that, on this server · this account, that key was observed on a verified chain as that person's first key (genesis / `add_member` / a proposal-routed application — `deviceProvenanceOf`'s null)** — no other writer exists.
4. **"Hidden" and "left" can't be distinguished by a chain fact**: after a `remove_member`, the list drops the row on the DO's `not-member` (ghost deletion). A chain GET returns 404 to a non-member (`get` :271–'s note "doesn't distinguish uninitialized from non-member (existence concealment)"). A hiding server can return the same 404. There's no project-deleting API (grep: api-schema's `HttpApiEndpoint.delete` has no project-body deletion). **But "the key was a first key" is a key fact, not a membership fact** — still true after leaving (the applied operation sequence doesn't disappear — K14 fact (a)). If a pre-DK copy K was a first key on a project it left, K is still that person's device key (a machine holding K uses K as `add_device` on other projects). So **the membership's completeness (is the list everything) can't be told apart, but the key's fact (was it a first key) doesn't change on leaving**.
5. **No trace remains on the visible chains**: the only path by which K — a first key on a hidden project — lands on a visible project P3 is `add_device` (the sync of a sibling device that recorded K by observation — K13's hole 1), whose payload is the public keys and cap. **The cap is the same as a reserve key's (owner, all)** (CRYPTO_SPEC §6.2 "`add_member` / `genesis` keys are structurally (owner, all)" — an observed row's cap is the first key's cap, and `registerRecorded` copies it into the payload). The provenance (the `add_device`'s actor) is also the same as a reserve key — a device of the same person. `keyHistory` holds only the binding's (enc, sig, FP). **No fact on P3's chain distinguishes K from a reserve key**. The sole exception is a trace of K's own signature (`entries`' `actor.keyFingerprintHex` — if a machine holding K signed a non-`add_device` op on P3, K is an everyday device key), but "a reserve key signs only `add_device`" is a CLI convention — the spec's reserve key is an ordinary device key (§3). Handled at K15-1.
6. **The counterexamples' premises**:
   - (i) **on an honest server, another server's · account's clues mix in** (K13's hole 2): the floor and pins mix (fact 3). `own-devices.json` doesn't.
   - (ii) **the floor's leftovers after removal from a project**: the floor's file stays forever (no deletion path). Making "a floor project absent from the list" a stopping material permanently stops a post-removal rotate (the same shape as K13-7's counterexample). `own-devices.json`'s `observedProjectId` also stays after removal, so **"the observed project is absent from the list" as a stopping material also persists forever**. Meanwhile "the key was a first key" stays true even after removal (fact 4) and resolves on the path stopped onto (`key recovery`'s separation) — it doesn't persist.
   - (iii) **A new device** (`key recover`'s case): normally neither floor nor record exists. A device that lost only its keychain while the config directory survived has a record.
   - (iv) The typical attack scenario: a pre-DK user approves a DK device D2 on K's machine M1 → D2 syncs p1 and observes K (a first key — the observed row `addedBy null` · `observedProjectId p1`) → D2 joins p3 via an invite, and D2's sync `add_device`s K onto p3 → the server hides p1 → `key reserve rotate` on D2 (the ledger is K). **D2 holds a record of observing K as a first key** (K landed on p3 precisely because that record existed — only a device holding K's record can add K to p3 [`registrationCandidates`]). Though if the server had hidden p1 since before D2 ever synced it, D2 can't observe K and K lands on neither D2's record nor p3 (hole 1's path closes) — in that case K isn't on p3, and the rotate verdict is `nowhere` or, if another device D3 observed K on p1 and added it to p3, D2's observed row is `addedBy = D3` (can't witness).
   - (v) On a device where a previous CLI (pre-K14) recorded K with provenance reserve, K's row is reserve (upsert by FP — 1 row per key), and no observation witness exists. K14's correction (`retractReserveRecord`) replaces that row with an observed row the moment the verdict becomes `first-key` — if valid on the first-key project, it becomes an `addedBy null` witness and survives that project being hidden thereafter.

→ The task statement's premise (the list is a server declaration whose completeness the CLI can't check) hasn't collapsed. **2 corrections**: (1) even an honest server can permanently drop a row on a DO fault (not a malice-only problem — fact 2). (2) "hidden" and "left" can't be told apart, but the fact the verdict needs (was the key a first key) doesn't depend on membership (fact 4). By (2), the line can be drawn: of the list-outside clues, **membership-speakers** (the floor · pins · `observedProjectId`'s "absent from the list") produce a persistent false-stop, while **key-speakers** (the observed row's "was a first key") don't.

### K15-1. Should the unrevocable effect's (the revocation's) precondition take a list-outside clue

| Option | Content | Upward compat | Silver bullet | Counterexamples | Adoption |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1-a Do nothing | As K14 | — | — | In fact 6 (iv)'s typical scenario, D2 revokes K while holding a record at hand that K was a first key | Rejected |
| 1-b Fix only the wording | Say the checked scope is "the N projects the server listed" (K15-3) | — | — | A revocation is unrevocable, and the wording is read after the effect (rotate's revocation happens inside `registerReserveAndRetire` — no moment to stop it) | Rejected (alone. Combined in K15-3) |
| 1-c Count floor-present · pin-present but list-absent projects as "couldn't confirm" and stop the revocation | Conservative | — | — | K13-7's counterexample itself: 1 floor of another server · account perpetuates a false "couldn't confirm" and keeps a correct reserve key's rotate stopped forever (fact 6 (i)(ii)). **K14-18's re-examination shape changes no rejection reason either** (adding a branch doesn't un-mix the floor by server · account) | Rejected |
| 1-d Add the floor's · pins' IDs to the list, attempt syncs, and use only the syncable ones for the verdict (failures ignored) | No persistent false-stop | — | — | A hiding server can also 404 the chain GET (fact 4), so it's powerless against malice. On an honest server's DO fault (fact 2 (ii)) the chain fails on the same DO anyway. Adds a wasted sync per run toward another server's ID | Rejected |
| 1-e Stop if `own-devices.json`'s `observedProjectId` is absent from the list | Partitioned by server · account | — | — | A membership-speaking clue: a persistent false-stop after removal (fact 6 (ii)). The stopped-onto path has no resolving move | Rejected |
| 1-f **If this device's observation record (the rows in this server · this account's `own-devices.json` with provenance observed · `addedByFingerprintHex === null` · `observedProjectId` present — fact 3) says a key to be revoked "was a first key", don't revoke it whatever the chain verdict (both the revocation gate and rotate's prelude). A new verdict value `recorded-first-key` (with the observed projects) is placed after `first-key`, before `revoked`** | An upward compat of 1-c / 1-e: of the list-outside clues, use only **key-facts**, and only **in the stopping direction**. No persistent false-stop (the stopped-onto `key recovery` resolves it by separation — the post-separation ledger key is a new reserve key with no witness). Partitioned by server · account (K13-7's counterexample doesn't hold) | Fact 6 (iv): in the typical scenario, "K could be added to p3 because D2 held K's record" — hole 1's path itself leaves the witness | Fact 3: the observed row's writer is only verified-chain observation (and K14's correction). Neither writers nor fields are added · changed | Counterexample 1 "a device that lost its record · a new device" → no witness, and it returns to K14's behavior (no worse — a known limitation). Counterexample 2 "a false witness stops a correct reserve key's rotate" → a reserve key is never a first key (K14 fact 7 — spec §6.2 "the reserve key is registered by the first device key" = `add_device`), so an honest record holds no reserve-key witness. An attacker who can write the file under the same user is outside K4-37's threat model — and even then it lands on the stopping side (`key recovery` separates — the harm is remaking a reserve key). Counterexample 3 "placed after `revoked`, a K revoked on a visible project and a first key on a hidden one passes the gate" → that's why it's placed before `revoked`. Counterexample 4 "local state as a judgment input (K4-2 b-3's rejection reason)" → b-3's problem was "a flag's **absence** triggers an operation" — it inverts on a wipe. 1-f's **presence** only stops an operation; a wipe returns to K14's behavior (doesn't invert). Counterexample 5 "a witness contradicting the chain's `first-key` verdict" → the witness is used only when the chain doesn't say `first-key`, and the wording says "in this device's record" — stating the provenance — and that the current chain doesn't show it | **Adopted** |
| 1-g Stop on a trace of K's own signature on a visible chain (K having signed a non-`add_device` op) | Works even on a new device | — | — | "A reserve key signs only `add_device`" is a CLI convention — the spec's reserve key is an ordinary device key (CRYPTO_SPEC §3 — the spec is the source of truth). Don't make an un-specced property judgment material. And it only works when a machine holding K ran an administrative op on a visible project | Rejected |
| 1-h Cryptographically back the server list's completeness (deposit an index of participation signed by the person's device; the CLI cross-checks the list, etc.) | Cuts the root | — | — | A wire · server · spec change (owner ruling: not implemented this round) | Rejected (owner point (1)) |

**Round 2**: upward compat = 1-f (key facts, only in the stopping direction). Silver bullet = fact 6 (iv) (hole 1's path leaves the witness). Counterexamples = 5 for 1-f, 1–2 each for 1-c / 1-d / 1-e / 1-g / 1-h. **Round 3**: option tried "sync the witness's project (`observedProjectId`) directly via `--project`, confirm the first key on the chain, then speak" → if syncable, the verdict can share `first-key`'s sentence, but the stop-or-not branch doesn't change (it's stopped on the witness even when unsyncable). Just adds 1 sync (K14-18's shape: a branch-unchanging option — re-examine when a branch is added). No new options. Option tried "extend the witness to `approved` rows too" → an approval row's `addedByFingerprintHex` is the approving device's FP and holds no first-key fact (fact 3). No new options. **Round 4**: option tried "mix the witness into `groupStandings`'s groups (append to `firstKeyProjects`)" → the groups are the synced chains' standings, also read by `device add`'s 2 choices (K14-18 separated "only valid standings" for `device add`). Mixing in erases the boundary between a chain fact and a local record. As a new verdict-value kind, the exhaustive `switch` makes every reader decide its handling. No new options (an allocation of placements). Option tried "rank the witness above `first-key`" → the chain's `first-key` is the present fact, and its wording is stronger (the projects' enumeration is the current chain). The witness is a supplement. No new options. Rounds 3 · 4 empty — stopped.

**Principle**: "**a list-outside clue is used only in the stopping direction, and only when it's a fact about the key rather than the membership, is partitioned by server · account, and was written only by verified-chain observation (if it's gone, it just returns to the status quo)**" — K13-7 ✓ (the floor isn't a witness — what gets used is only the key facts in the record partitioned by (origin, userId)), K4-5 ✓ (an advisory — the server's list — isn't the source of truth. The observation record isn't a server declaration), K4-3 ✓ (the record's writers are cryptographic facts only — only readers were added, no writers), K4-2 b-3 ✓ (absence triggers no operation), K13-18 ✓ (avoid the silently-wrong side), K14-13 / K14-15 ✓ (the unrevocable effect's precondition gains facts), K14-18 ✓ (placed before `revoked` — the verdict isn't blocked by an already-revoked project). **Adopted: 1-f**.

### K15-2. Do the records (`key recover`'s yes · `key recovery` · `key seal passkey` / `guardian add`) need the same remedy

| Option | Content | Upward compat | Silver bullet | Counterexamples | Adoption |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2-a Do nothing (just the revocation gate) | A record is a later revocation's input — the gate stops it | — | — | A record's silent error isn't only revocation: recording a copy as reserve silences the reserve-key-absence warning (K4-9), and the user believes it's separated (K14 fact 2). Plus `recordReserveLocally` upserts by FP, so **writing a reserve row over a witness row erases the witness** (fact 3) — a later rotate's gate loses the witness | Rejected |
| 2-b **Pass the verdict value `recorded-first-key` through every reader with the same treatment as `first-key`**: `key recover` doesn't ask and doesn't record (a Warning), `key recovery` separates, `key seal passkey` / `guardian add` / rotate's prelude stop without recording (naming `key recovery`), and the revocation gate doesn't revoke | 1 verdict value read by every entrance (the same entrances as K14-4's 4-f). The exhaustive `switch` has the type stop a reader's drop | 2-a's counterexample (the witness's overwrite) is removed by stopping before the record | Sharing the verdict's value (`ledgerKeyChecksOf` and `reserveVerdictOf`) | Counterexample 1 "doesn't work on `key recover`'s new device" → without a witness it's the same as K14 (fact 6 (iii)). Works on a device that lost only its keychain. Counterexample 2 "when `key recovery` separates, K leaves the ledger and the reserve key is renewed — if K was actually the reserve key, a wasteful remake" → fact: no witness stands for a reserve key (K15-1 counterexample 2). Counterexample 3 "stopping `seal passkey` is excessive (just don't record — K14-13's "proceed")" → at K14-4's 4-f we decided `first-key` stops without recording, naming `key recovery` (separation is `key recovery` only — K4-2. Recording a copy as reserve feeds rotate / `--replace`'s revocation input). The witness is the same fact as `first-key`, so the same treatment | **Adopted** |
| 2-c Watch the witness and bounce at the record's function's (`recordReserveLocally`) entrance | The writer's entrance is 1 place | — | — | The same rejection reason as K14-4's 4-h (it's also called on a generated key's sealing). Plus the wording (what happened and what's next) can't be said per entrance | Rejected |

**Round 2**: upward compat = 2-b. Silver bullet = sharing the verdict's value. **Round 3**: option tried "append the witness as a fact to `key recover`'s `added` confirmation and leave the answer to the human" → builds an entrance where an answer overrides a fact (K14-3's principle — don't build a path where yes overrides `first-key`). No new options. **Round 4**: an empty round. Stopped.

**Principle**: "**the same fact (the key was a first key) passes to every entrance as the same verdict value, whether its provenance is the chain or the observation record**" — K14-4's 4-f ✓ (1 entrance), K7-15 ✓, K14-3 ✓ (no overriding entrance). **Adopted: 2-b**.

### K15-3. The user-visible wording (the checked scope)

| Option | Content | Upward compat | Silver bullet | Counterexamples | Adoption |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 3-a Do nothing | — | — | — | "revoked on every project" (`--replace`'s opening Warning · help), "registering it and revoking … on every project" (rotate / `--replace`'s latter-stage line), "not registered on any of your projects" (`key recover`'s `nowhere`), "only when every project shows …" (`unchecked`) all assert stronger than the checked scope (it only sees what the server listed) | Rejected |
| 3-b **State the assertion's scope as "the N projects the server listed" (the same shape as K13's `describeListed` — moved into `display.ts` for sharing so no transcription is made)**: `key recover`'s `added` fact line gets "maruhi checked the N projects the server lists for you; a project it does not list is not checked"; `nowhere` becomes `describeListed`; `unchecked` becomes "every project the server lists for you"; rotate / `--replace`'s latter-stage line becomes "on the N projects the server lists for you" (said after fetching the list); `--replace`'s opening Warning and help become "on every project the server lists for you" | Aligns the assertion to the facts' scope. Putting the scope on the confirmation question (`key recover`'s `added`) lets a human choose no using "whether a project I created · joined with this key sits outside the list" as material | Sharing the existing part (`describeListed`) | — | Counterexample 1 "a project ID is a 64-digit hex — a human can't cross-check a list" → the count is checkable. The scope's sentence doesn't force cross-checking; it aligns the assertion to fact. Counterexample 2 "fix every 'every project'" → `device revoke` / `device approve` / `device list`'s help's "default: every project you belong to" is K13's territory, and the direction under hiding is the opposite (revocation · addition come up short). Only the transcriptions of this round's claims (the ledger key's verdict and the revocations) are fixed (a carryover) | **Adopted** |

**Round 2**: option tried "append the scope sentence to every ledger-key Warning" → `first-key` / `revoked` / `recorded-first-key` are positive facts (naming the projects found) — true regardless of scope. Scope is needed only on sentences saying "absent" or "all add_device". No new options. **Round 3**: option tried "transcribe `describeListed` into `key-recover.ts` rather than moving it to `display.ts`" → a transcription (K7-15). No new options. Stopped.

**Principle**: "**a sentence saying something is absent · saying it's true everywhere carries the checked scope (the count the server listed). A positive-fact sentence needs no scope**" — K13-2 ✓ (the scoped assertion), K7-15 ✓. **Adopted: 3-b**.

### K15-4. Should a human confirmation enter (before rotate / `--replace`'s revocation, show the checked projects and take a yes)

| Option | Content | Upward compat | Silver bullet | Counterexamples | Adoption |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 4-a **Don't add one** (handled by K15-1's witness and K15-3's scope sentence) | Doesn't grow the ceremony's steps | — | — | Counterexample "on a witness-less device, a hiding server still revokes" → a known limitation. Whether a human confirmation could plug it is weak, per 4-b's counterexamples | **Adopted** |
| 4-b Just before the revocation, ask "the N projects the server listed: … X is being revoked. If a project you created · joined with this key sits outside the list, answer no" | Obtains the list's completeness — a fact the machine lacks — from the human | — | — | Counterexample 1 "the human can't answer" — IDs are 64-digit hexes; all a human knows is the count. The question asks "is there a project I made with this key outside the list" — the same kind as W-3's is-it-a-copy-or-reserve question (V-4 — unanswerable). Counterexample 2 "the non-interactive refusal's position changes" — `--replace`'s ceremony gate is inside the code's issuance (`issueRecoveryCodeOp`), and the revocation gate precedes it. Putting the question at the gate stops a non-interactive `--replace` at the question before the ceremony gate (changing the refusal's sentence). Moving the gate earlier changes K4-38's escape's order. Counterexample 3 "K13's rejected '--replace confirmation prompt'" — the question can only ask the same facts the display shows; the human holds no verdict. Counterexample 4 "in-ceremony yes fatigue" — rotate already asks for the code input and the save confirmation | Rejected |
| 4-c Emit the scope line before the revocation, using the code-save confirmation (the existing question) as the stopping moment | Adds no new question | — | — | The save confirmation is a question **after** the ledger was replaced; stopping there leaves "the new reserve key was sealed but not registered" (K4-1's order — not a stopping moment). Contradicts K14-15's "don't proceed just for the doable part" | Rejected |

**Round 2**: no upward compat (4-b is broad in obtaining a human fact, but counterexample 1 says the fact can't be obtained). **Round 3**: option tried "put the scope on `key recover`'s `added` confirmation (the existing question)" → just adds material to an existing question — not a new question. Included in K15-3's 3-b. No new options. **Round 4**: an empty round. Stopped.

**Principle**: "**ask the human only for facts the human holds. Don't question facts they don't hold (the completeness of an ID list)**" — W-3 round 3 ✓ (a confirmation, not a judgment), K13-17 ✓ (don't emit a false option), K14-3 ✓, ADR-0016 decision 7 / fact 10 (don't move the non-interactive refusal's position) ✓. **Adopted: 4-a**.

### K15-5. The check's placement

| Option | Content | Upward compat | Silver bullet | Counterexamples | Adoption |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 5-a **Add a describe "projects the server hid from the list (DK K15)" to `apps/cli/test/device.test.ts`. `makeServer` gains `unlistedProjects` (chain GETs / appends are served but the list doesn't show them — the hidden projects), passed through `recoveryFixture` / `recoverFixture`. A witness's row is written through **the actual observation path** (run a keyed command with `--project <p1>` before hiding, letting `syncOwnDevices`'s observation write the row)** | Uses K14's scaffolding | Pins from the witness's writer (observation) to its reader (the gate) in one line — a hand-built row would miss a disagreement between the writer's shape (`observedProjectId`'s presence) and the reader's predicate | — | Counterexample "hand-build an observed row (extend `recordOwnDevice`)" → the existing helper builds a row with `observedProjectId: null` (a shape that doesn't exist — K15-6's predicate bounces it). Running the writer's path pins the property | **Adopted** |

**Rounds 2 · 3**: option tried "a unit test of `device-standing.ts`'s pure function" → same as K14-7 (doesn't pin the wiring). No new options. Option tried "put `seal passkey`'s witness refusal in passkey.test.ts" → the prelude is 1 function (`settleLedgerKeyForChange`) — the same path as rotate. Pinned via rotate (the greatest harm); `key recovery` · `key recover` are placed in device.test.ts. No new options. Stopped.

**Principle**: "**a check using a witness goes through the witness's writer's path**" — K13-10 ✓, K14-7 ✓. **Adopted: 5-a**.

### K15-6. The witness predicate's details

| Option | Content | Upward compat | Silver bullet | Counterexamples | Adoption |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 6-a **Provenance observed ∧ `addedByFingerprintHex === null` ∧ `observedProjectId !== null`. The revocation mark (`revokedAtMs`) isn't asked about**. An unreadable · broken record = no witness (K14's behavior), and an unreadable failure is a Note | Matches the shape the 2 writer paths (observation · K14's correction) always write `observedProjectId` in | — | — | Counterexample 1 "exclude a row bearing a revocation mark" → the mark is "a revocation observed somewhere", and "was a first key" stays true after the revocation (same as K14-18). Excluding it makes a copy revoked on some projects per the guidance lose its witness. Counterexample 2 "don't ask `observedProjectId`" → don't make a hand-made or differently-shaped row (a non-approval provenance-observed row with no ID) a witness. Real writers always write it. Counterexample 3 "go silent on a broken record" → a broken state is `load`'s `corrupt` type, which other commands already warn distinguishably (K4-3's fail-open). A read's I/O failure (`CliError`) becomes a Note (not swallowed) | **Adopted** |

**Rounds 2 · 3**: option tried "when 2+ witness rows exist, enumerate every project" → 1 row per key (upsert by FP — fact 3). No new options. 2 empty rounds — stopped.

### K15-7. What goes to the owner points (not implemented)

- (1) **Backing the server list's completeness** (K15-1's 1-h): e.g. depositing with the server an index of "the projects it participates in" signed by the person's device (updated at each genesis / acceptance), the CLI cross-checking the list and counting an index-present-but-list-absent project as "couldn't confirm" (the server can't sign, so it can't hide — a deletion is detectable). A wire · server · spec change (a new signed object in CRYPTO_SPEC). This round's witness (1-f) amounts to an anticipation, on existing records, of that index's "about-keys" subset.
- (2) **An honest server's list omission** (fact 2 (ii)): a list dropping a row on a DO fault doesn't put "dropped" on the response. If the response carried the dropped count (a number, no IDs), the CLI could count an honest server's fault as "couldn't confirm" (`unchecked` — K14-14's gate stops it). A server · wire change.

### K15 ruling list (pinned before implementation)

| # | Topic | Adopted | What makes it better than the alternatives | Principle |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| K15-1 | Should the revocation's precondition take a list-outside clue | 1-f: a "was a first key" in this device's observation record (the observed rows of this server · account's `own-devices.json` with `addedBy null`) becomes the verdict value `recorded-first-key` (after `first-key`, before `revoked`), used only in the direction that stops a revocation | Unlike the floor · pins · the observed project's membership (persistent false-stops), it's a key fact partitioned by server · account, and the stopped-onto path (`key recovery`'s separation) resolves it | A list-outside clue only stops on key facts |
| K15-2 | Do the records need it too | 2-b: `recorded-first-key` gets `first-key`'s same treatment at every entrance (don't record · separate · stop) | Also closes the path that erases a witness by overwriting it with a reserve row. The exhaustive `switch` aligns the readers | The same fact is the same verdict value regardless of provenance |
| K15-3 | The wording | 3-b: "absent" · "all" sentences carry "the N projects the server listed" (`describeListed` moved to `display.ts` for sharing), `key recover`'s confirmation fact line gets the scope, `--replace`'s Warning and help | Aligns assertions to the checked scope | An absent · all sentence carries a scope |
| K15-4 | A human confirmation | 4-a: none | Doesn't question a fact the human lacks. Doesn't move the non-interactive refusal's position | Ask the human only for facts they hold |
| K15-5 | The check | 5-a: `device.test.ts`'s K15 describe + `makeServer`'s `unlistedProjects`; the witness is written through the observation path | One line from writer to reader | A witness's check goes through the writer's path |
| K15-6 | The witness's predicate | 6-a: observed ∧ `addedBy null` ∧ `observedProjectId` present, the revocation mark isn't asked, an unreadable failure is a Note | Matches the real writers' shape | — |
| K15-7 | Out of scope | Backing the list's completeness · the honest server's omission count go to the owner | — | — |

### Mid-implementation additional rulings (K15-8 onward — enumerated, counterexampled, principled on the spot)

| # | Topic | Options | Upward compat | Silver bullet | Counterexamples | Adoption |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| K15-8 | The revocation gate's (`confirmRetiring`) `recorded-first-key` branch is **unreachable** on the current paths: the gate's inputs are the opened key B and the reserve rows, while a witness is an observed row (1 row per key — fact 3), so no reserve row carries a witness, and if B has a witness, rotate already stops at the prelude (`settleLedgerKeyForChange`) | (a) Remove the branch (the if / else's else falls to revoke). (b) **Keep the branch (defense in depth)**. (c) Rewrite the gate as an exhaustive `switch` | (b) won't drop a witness key into a revocation even if the gate's input construction changes later (the record's shape changes · a caller bypassing the prelude appears) | — | Counterexample to (a): the gate is the 1 place "letting only keys allowed to be revoked pass"; dropping a new verdict-value kind into else (revoke) contradicts K14-13's gate's intent (falls to the silently-revoking side). Counterexample to (c): the gate also reads a field separate from the value (the completeness `unchecked`), so a `switch` yields the same condition set. Rewriting just grows the diff | **(b)**. Mutation V9 (removing the branch) survives — recorded as an unreachable branch's equivalent mutation (K13-13). Principle "an unrevocable effect's gate handles every kind of the verdict's value explicitly, and a stopping-side branch is kept even when unreachable" |
| K15-9 (a procedure record) | The first-written test of "the revocation gate (`--replace`)" overlooked that a witness key **never enters** `--replace`'s revoke targets in the first place (an observed row isn't a reserve row) — an empty check not passing through the gate's branch. Plus, in the re-composed order test, p3 and p4 were built on the same genesis, so **the project IDs (the genesis hash) collided and p3's chain was being served where p4 was meant** (still green) | — | — | — | How it was found: adding a check of the witness row's revocation mark (`revokedAtMs` non-null) made it fail; dumping the sync's observation's contents showed p4's sync reading p3's chain. p4 was rebuilt on another person's genesis, and `p4.projectId !== p3.projectId` was pinned in the check. The `--replace` test was replaced with an order test (`recorded-first-key` before `revoked`) | Principle "chains composed as different projects get different geneses, and the check pins their IDs differ. Once a check is written, confirm via the output it passes through the intended branch" (re-listing K14-12 — compose both sides of a branch) |
| K15-10 (a procedure record) | The mutations that survived mutation testing, V2 / V3 / V9 | V2 (removing "an observed project exists" from the witness predicate) is equivalent at runtime — a Map value's null folds to "no witness" — but **`tsc` bounces it as a type error** (`string \| null` can't go into `Map<string, string>` — quality gate 3). V3 (removing "provenance observed") is equivalent on today's writers: a reserve row has no `observedProjectId` (`reserveEntryOf`), and an approval row has the provenance's device (`device.ts` :941–), so the remaining 2 conditions bounce the same rows. The condition is kept to make the predicate's definition (an observed row) explicit. V9 is K15-8 | — | — | Counterexample "read a surviving mutation straight as equivalent" → V2 was confirmed via `tsc`, V3 via the writers' shapes (grep: the 3 places writing `observedProjectId`), V9 via the reachability path — each verified its own way | Principle "a surviving mutation: confirm check weakness vs equivalent mutation via the output · the type · the writers' shapes, then fix" (re-listing K13-13 / K14-10) |
