- **docs**: `apps/site/docs/devices.mdx` (the reserve-key section's gate sentence gained "confirmed on the chains of the projects the server lists plus this device's records. Since the list isn't a completeness proof, a key observed as a first key on a project that left the list counts as a device key too. A device that never synced has no record"; the pre-DK procedure 1 gained a record-case clause), `recover-your-key.mdx` (only the server-listed projects are checked and the confirmation question states the count; the first-key item gained the "this device's record" case; "none of your projects" → "no project the server lists for you"; `--replace`'s scope). Nothing added to What can go wrong (the new sentences state their own conditions and next moves — same as K14-6).
- **Tests**: `apps/cli/test/device.test.ts` 101 → 108 (+7 — the describe "projects the server hid from the list (DK K15)": rotate is stopped by the observation record of the hidden project's first key and changes nothing [the witness is written via the observation path by a pre-hiding `--project` sync] / the witness is stated before revoked even when revoked on a visible project [a revocation-marked witness — the verdict's order] / `key recovery` separates on the witness and doesn't record / `key recover` (a device that lost only its keychain) doesn't ask and doesn't record on the witness / on an honest server, unrelated clues [a floor · another server's record · a no-observed-project row] don't stop a correct reserve key's rotate from revoking [K13-7] / a reserve key observed on a non-approving device [an observed row with a provenance device] is no witness and rotate revokes it / a non-interactive rotate stops before fetching the ledger and changes nothing even with a witness). Existing letters gained a scope (3 in `device.test.ts` · 1 in `recovery.test.ts`; the `added` fact line down to the scope sentence verbatim). Scaffolding: `makeServer`'s `unlistedProjects` (serves the chains but doesn't list them), `recoveryFixture` / `recoverFixture` plumbing and `extra`, `recordOwnDevice`'s `observedProjectId`.
- **Mutations** (13 mutations — the keep-pre-edit-content write-back method [K11-10]; confirmed `git diff --stat` unchanged after each): V1 removing "no provenance device" from the predicate (1 fails) / V2 removing "an observed project exists" (survived — `tsc` bounces it, K15-10) / V3 removing "provenance observed" (survived — equivalent on the writers, K15-10) / V4 the witness after `revoked` (1) / V5 not passing the witness to the ledger verdict (3) / V6 the prelude not stopping on a witness (2) / V7 `key recovery` not separating on a witness, recording (1) / V8 `key recover` not seeing the witness (1) / V9 removing the gate's witness branch (survived — unreachable, K15-8) / V10 removing the scope from the confirmation's fact line (1) / V11 reverting the latter-stage line to "every project" (1) / V12 removing `nowhere`'s scope (2) / V13 excluding a revocation-marked row from the witnesses (1). The checks failing V1 and V13 (an observed row from a non-approving device · a witness's revocation mark) were added before running the mutations, as both sides of the branch (K14-12). Green after reverting all mutations.
- **Verification**: `bun run check` (`fmt:check` / `lint` / `typecheck` / `importlint` / `fallow:audit` / `doctor` / `test` — all exit 0. The mid-way 1st run's `fmt:check` failed on `device.test.ts`'s formatting; fixed via `oxfmt` and re-ran). `test` is 145 files, 5085 tests (main's 5078 +7). `fallow audit --base origin/main` — "No issues in 12 changed files". `apps/site`: `validate --strict` (no broken links) → `build` (13 pages) → e2e 12 (the session-start hook's `PLAYWRIGHT_CHROMIUM_EXECUTABLE_PATH=/opt/pw-browsers/chromium`), unit 126 (including `cli-vocabulary.test.ts`'s net).
- **Collection's scope**: **K14's "the server hides from the list" hole** was plugged, for both the revocations (rotate / `--replace`) and the records (`key recover` / `key recovery` / `seal passkey` / `guardian add`), **whenever this device has observed that key as a first key on the hidden project's verified chain**. The typical scenario (fact 6 (iv) — the device that added the copy to another project via hole 1's path) falls under it (it could add the copy because it held the record). **K13's known limitation (2)** (`device add`'s "on none" is an assertion about the server-listed projects) is **not collected**: `device add` has no unrevocable effect, and K13-7's floor enumeration (information) and scope sentences stay. The leeway to append the same witness as information to `device add`'s "on none" sentence is carryover (1).
- **Points put to the owner**: (1) **Backing the server list's completeness** (K15-7 (1) — e.g. an index of participation signed by the person's device. A wire · server · spec change). The hole on a witness-less device (a new device · a device that lost its record · a device whose earlier CLI overwrote the observed row with a reserve-provenanced copy record) is closable only this way. (2) **An honest server's list omission** (K15-7 (2) — if the response carried the DO-fault-dropped row count, the CLI could count it as "couldn't confirm").
- **Carryovers (out of scope — separate tasks)**: (1) whether `device add`'s "on none" sentence should append, as information, this device's observation record's (server · account-partitioned) "observed on a list-absent project" (the continuation of K13's known limitation (2) — more precise than the floor's enumeration). (2) `device revoke` / `device approve` / `device list`'s help · output's "every project you belong to" (under hiding, revocation · addition come up **short** — the opposite of this task). W-4 / W-6 · K5 owner points (1)(2) · K2 carryover 4 · K7 owner points (4)(5) · K8 owner point (2) · K9 owner points (2)(3) · K13 carryovers (1)(2) are kept.
- **Known limitations**: (1) a witness-less device keeps K14's behavior (if the server hides, rotate / `--replace` can revoke the copy on the listed projects — and the records alike) — a new device, a device that lost `own-devices.json`, a device that never synced that project before the hiding, a device where a previous CLI recorded the copy as reserve overwriting the observed row (1 row per key — fact 6 (v)). (2) A witness names only 1 project (the first one witnessed — 1 row per key). The witness rewrite (K15-12) reaches only **existing observed rows** and creates none: a key not yet in this device's record that is already revoked on its first-key project isn't observed on that project's sync (observation iterates only valid devices). If a newly observed row was a past membership's first key, it becomes a witness from the next sync. (3) Someone able to write `own-devices.json` under the same user can plant a false witness, but the direction lands only on the stopping side (resolvable by `key recovery`'s separation — outside K4-37's threat model). (4) The revocation gate's `recorded-first-key` branch is unreachable on the current paths (K15-8 — defense in depth).

### Additional rulings during review handling (K15-11 onward — responses to PR #206)

**K15-11 (pullfrog — the witness depends on the observation's order)**: the finding is "`observeDevices` (`device-sync.ts` :171) writes a row only when the FP is unknown — it doesn't update an existing row. If D3, approved by D2, first observes K on p3 as `add_device` (provenance D2), then syncing p1 (K is a first key) later still leaves D3's row with provenance D2 — no witness. If p1 gets hidden, a D3 that has synced p1 returns to K14's behavior. `devices.mdx` only says 'a device that never synced has no record'". **Confirmed correct in the source** (only `observeDevices`' `known === undefined` branch writes. Confirmed that on the pre-fix code, a test syncing p3 → p1 in that order fails [no witness is made]). Fact 6 (iv)'s "the typical scenario leaves a witness" is true for D2 (the device that observed K and added it to p3) but **doesn't hold for a sibling device that joined after K was already on p3** — a miscount in the fact check (K7-15's shape).

| Option | Content | Upward compat | Silver bullet | Counterexamples | Adoption |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 11-a Add it to the docs as a limitation | Small diff | — | — | A synced device lacking the witness contradicts docs' promise that only "a device that never synced has no record". Punting a pluggable hole into a limitation | Rejected |
| 11-b **On the observation path, update an existing row with provenance observed · a provenance device, only when this chain observed it as a first key (`deviceProvenanceOf`'s null), to: no provenance device · observed project = this project. The revocation mark · the record's time · cap are kept** | The witness stops depending on the observation's order | The writer stays observation (K4-3 — writes a verified-chain observation fact); no field changes. The reader is the witness only (grep: `addedByFingerprintHex` / `observedProjectId` are read only by `recordedFirstKeysOf` — `device list`'s display reads only the provenance), and the direction is stopping-only | — | Counterexample 1 "erasing the revocation mark re-registers a revoked key on other projects (K4-3)" → the mark is kept (`...known`). The check got "a revocation mark from p4 survives an update at p1". Counterexample 2 "also update an approval row (provenance approved)" → the approval's fact (the approving device) isn't rewritten by observation. Limited to provenance-observed rows. Counterexample 3 "the cap's disagreement" → the cap stays the first observed row's (a witness doesn't read cap. Registration [`registerRecorded`] uses the record's cap, but the same key's cap is ≤ the first key's cap by `add_device`'s monotonicity — not updating changes no existing behavior) | **Adopted** |

**Round 2**: upward compat = 11-b. **Round 3**: option tried "emit a Note on the update" → a first-observation Note for the key already exists. It's just a provenance fact growing inside a row — no human next move. No new options. Option tried "also make a revoked standing's key a witness via observation (K14-18's first-key history)" → observation iterates only valid devices (`devicesOf(self)`). It'd become a new path writing a revoked key into the record — a wider scope. Left in known limitation (2). No new options. **Round 4**: an empty round. Stopped. **Principle**: "**a witness's record doesn't depend on its writer path's order: devices that saw the same fact end with the same record**" (an extension of K15-1).

**K15-11's implementation** (the predicate was replaced at K15-12 — the re-loop below): `device-sync.ts` `observeDevices` — if a known row is provenance observed with a provenance device and this chain's provenance is a first key, it's rewritten with `addedByFingerprintHex: null` · `observedProjectId: this project` (other fields kept. A write failure is the existing Note). The revocation-mark Note's branch is emitted to the known row independently of the update (same conditions as before). Test `device.test.ts` +1 (sync in p3 → p4 → p1 order: the row goes p3's provenance D2 → marked revoked at p4 → updated to a witness at p1, keeping the mark and the record's time, and a rotate hiding p1 stops) → 108 → 109. The 4 mutations: W1 not updating · W3 erasing the revocation mark on update · W4 not updating the observed project all failed. W2 (removing the provenance-observed condition) survived — no place reads an approval row's provenance field, and the witness requires provenance observed, so it's equivalent. The condition is kept to make "not rewriting an approval's fact" explicit (the same treatment as K15-10).

### K15's re-loop (2026-09-26 — the owner's question "at each topic, did you search the silver bullet · upward compat · a structural solution until the options ran out")

**Self-inspection**: the pre-implementation pinning (K15-1–K15-7) hadn't run the loop out on the following points. (i) K15-3 · K15-5 · K15-6 collapsed "round 2 · round 3" into 1 line and didn't verify per round that 2 consecutive empty rounds had happened (the same weakness K14's re-loop listed for itself). (ii) It didn't ask whether the adopted option's (witness's) **predicate** should be aligned with the verdict's predicate (`wasFirstKeyOf` — on any membership · any standing) rather than riding the writer's existing fields (`deviceProvenanceOf`'s provenance — current membership · valid devices only) as-is. K13-1's "the same question via 1 pure function" was applied only on the verdict side. pullfrog's K15-11 is one face of this (ii) (the observation's order). (iii) It didn't enumerate as a fact the witness's **writer exhaustiveness** (who can create · overwrite a witness row). (iv) It didn't enumerate a structural solution from the "positive proof" side (this key was generated as a reserve key). Below, each topic's loop is re-run from round 1 (existing tables' options aren't re-listed; only new options and each round's record are written).

**Additional fact check**:
- (a) **A witness row's writers** (grep: `OwnDeviceStore`'s `record` call sites — `device.ts` :950 approval [provenance approved · the approving device], `reserve.ts` `recordReserveLocally` [provenance reserve — called only for a generated key's sealing and a ledger key whose verdict is `added` / `nowhere`. It can't reach a `recorded-first-key` key], `reserve.ts` `retractReserveRecord` [rewrites only a provenance-reserve row], `device-sync.ts`'s observation [a new row for an unknown key and, since K15-11, the witness rewrite]). `markRevoked` only marks. **No path exists — including approval (an existing key creates no request — K13 fact check 7 — so it can't reach a witness key) — that deletes or overwrites a witness row**.
- (b) **The ledger seal's record** (`keychain.ts` `StoredMasterKey`) has only `suite` and the key fields — no field distinguishing a DK reserve key from a pre-DK device-key copy. **A positive proof inside the ledger's contents can't be made without changing the ledger's format**.
- (c) Observation (`observeDevices`) iterates only valid devices (`devicesOf(self)`). A key already revoked on its first-key project isn't observed on that project's sync (the cause of the K15 completion record's known limitation (2)). But a revoked key's public keys remain in `keyHistory` and the applied operation sequence, so **for an already-existing row** it can be confirmed via the same predicate as the verdict (the same material as K14-18).

#### K15-1's re-loop (should the revocation's precondition take a list-outside clue)

| Option | Content | Upward compat | Silver bullet | Counterexamples | Adoption |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1-i **Align the witness's predicate with the verdict's predicate**: on the observation path, for **every existing row** with provenance observed · a provenance device, if `wasFirstKeyOf` on this chain (the verdict's predicate — any membership · any standing), rewrite it to no provenance device · observed project = this project (moved to `device-key.ts` for sharing). Replaces K15-11's rewrite by a valid device's provenance | An upward compat of K15-11: beyond the observation's order, it reaches **an already-revoked first key** (additional fact check (c) — the part of known limitation (2) where a row exists) and **a past membership's first key** (re-invite — K14's 1-f) | The verdict and the witness become the same pure function, so "a key the chain would call `first-key` if visible is a key this device has a witness for if it ever saw that chain" holds (K13-1) | `wasFirstKeyOf` and `keyHistory`'s bindings (K14-18's material) | Counterexample 1 "the rewrite erasing the same sync's revocation mark" → the rewrite writes from the pre-sync row, so it's placed before the revocation mark (b) (pinned in a check — the later-placed mutation X2 fails). Counterexample 2 "a newly observed key is a past membership's first key" → a new row is written with the provenance, then rewritten on the next sync (a 1-sync delay — because the sync that created the row has no `records`). Counterexample 3 "create a new row (add a revoked key to the record)" → don't create. Don't widen the writer's path (adding a revoked key to the record would become a new input for the registration-candidate rule [the revocation mark]) | **Adopted** (replaces K15-11) |
| 1-j **Positive proof**: when a reserve key is **generated**, leave a "generated as a reserve key" mark (on the ledger seal's record or an `own-devices.json` field), and limit revocations · records to marked keys | A reverse-direction upward compat of 1-f (the witness — stopping side): doesn't rely on the list's completeness at all and works on a new device (a mark inside the ledger is readable by a decrypting device) | Makes "this key was generated as a reserve key" a cryptographic fact (the decrypted ledger's contents) | — | A change to the ledger's format or `own-devices.json`'s fields (owner ruling: not changed this round — additional fact check (b)). Migration: an existing mark-less reserve key migrates via 1 rotate (the mark lands on the generated new key), but that rotate itself needs today's rules for the mark-less old key's verdict | Rejected (owner point (3)) |
| 1-k Try syncing a list-absent observed project (this server · account's `observedProjectId`) in the keyless prelude (use only the success fact; failures ignored) | Can strengthen the witness's sentence to "on the current chain" | — | — | Same as 1-f's round 3: the stop-or-not branch doesn't change; it just adds a sync. A hiding server can 404 the chain too. K14-18's re-examination shape (was a branch added?) changes nothing | Rejected |
| 1-l Limit revocations not to "not a first key on the chain" but to "a key `add_device`d by this device's signature (a reserve key this device's sync added)" | Uses a positive fact | — | — | The device that added a reserve key isn't necessarily the one that sealed it (a sibling device's sync adds it — K4-3). The ledger's key couldn't be rotated from another device. A pre-DK copy could also have been added by the same device's sync (hole 1), so it distinguishes nothing | Rejected |

**Round 2**: upward compat = 1-i (the witness's predicate → the verdict's predicate), 1-j (positive proof — out of scope). Silver bullet = sharing the same pure function. Counterexamples = 3 for 1-i, 1 each for 1-j / 1-k / 1-l. **Round 3**: option tried "keep the witness with multiple projects per key" → 1 row per key (fields unchanged). 1 suffices to stop, and 1 suffices for the wording's naming. No new options. Option tried "re-ask the server for the key list (the `GET /auth/devices` registry)" → the registry is also a server declaration (K4-5 — an advisory isn't the source of truth). No new options. **Round 4**: option tried "force a `device list`-like all-device sync before rotate" → the synced set is the server's list; a hidden project isn't synced. No new options. 2 empty rounds — stopped. **Conclusion: keep 1-f, and replace the witness's writer with 1-i** (the adoption's content is "the first-key fact, by the verdict's same predicate, in this device's observation record").

#### K15-2's re-loop (the remedy on the records)

**Round 1**: 2-d "for a witnessed key, also stop `key recovery`'s re-issuance (`reissue-only` — the unconfirmable case)" → the witness is judged as `recorded-first-key` before `unchecked`, so it already proceeds to separation (never reaches re-issuance). Not a new option. 2-e "close in the type any of the record's writers (additional fact check (a)) that could overwrite a witness row" → no writer can overwrite one (approval can't reach a witness key; `recordReserveLocally` gets only verdict-passed keys). Nothing to close. Not a new option. **Round 2**: option tried "`seal passkey` / `guardian add` don't stop on a witness — just don't record (the "proceed" treatment)" → at K14-4's 4-f we decided `first-key` stops, and the witness is that fact's transcription. Splitting the treatment makes 2 verdicts of the same fact. No new options. **Round 3**: an empty round. Stopped. **Conclusion: keep 2-b**.

#### K15-3's re-loop (the wording)

**Round 1**: 3-c "append, on rotate / `--replace`'s latter-stage line, the count of keys not revoked due to a witness" → the gate already names each key in a Warning. It just adds a count's transcription. Rejected. 3-d "extend `devices.mdx`'s 'a device that never synced has no record' to 'also a device that lost its record after observing the key as a first key'" → under 1-i, "a synced device holds the record regardless of order · standing", so the remaining limitations are the 2 "never synced · lost the record". The docs' sentence says only the former. The latter is covered by the completion record's known limitation (1), and the docs' reader's next move (cross-check via `device list`) doesn't change. Rejected. **Round 2**: option tried "show the observation's time (`recordedAtMs`) in the witness's sentence" → the rewrite keeps the record's time (the first-observation time, not the becoming-a-witness time). Misleading. No new options. **Round 3**: option tried "use `describeListedScope` in `device add`'s 2 choices too" → the 2 choices' sentence is a positive fact (the first-key projects) — scope-independent (K15-3's principle). No new options. 2 empty rounds — stopped. **Conclusion: keep 3-b**.

#### K15-4's re-loop (a human confirmation)

**Round 1**: 4-d "only on a witness-less rotate (only where the hole remains), show the checked count and take a yes" → narrowing the scope doesn't change what the question asks (is there a project of mine outside the list) — an ID list, where a human can only cross-check a count (4-b counterexample 1). Rotate comes after the opening ceremony so the non-interactive refusal's position doesn't move (4-b counterexample 2 was `--replace`-only), but the question's material stays weak. Rejected. **Round 2**: option tried "compare not the count but this device's record's observed-projects count (this server · account) against the list's count, and ask if they disagree" → a membership-speaking clue — a post-removal persistent disagreement (fact 6 (ii)) becomes a question on every run. No new options. **Round 3**: an empty round. Stopped. **Conclusion: keep 4-a**.

#### K15-5 / K15-6's re-loop (the check's placement · the witness's predicate)

**Round 1**: 5-b "compose the witness writer's paths (order · already-revoked · mark preservation) across varied sync-order scenarios" → 1-i's property itself. Added to K15's describe (adopted — the check's content). 6-b "the reading-side predicate (provenance observed ∧ no provenance device ∧ an observed project) also reads via `wasFirstKeyOf` (applying the record's public keys to the chain)" → at read time there's no chain for a hidden project (that's what the witness means). The reader can only read the record's fact. Rejected. **Round 2**: option tried "put the witness's predicate in `own-devices.ts`" → the predicate is material for "the ledger key's verdict", whose only reader is `device-standing.ts`. Moving the placement births no transcription, but `own-devices.ts` is the record shape's boundary and holds no verdict rules (K4-3's boundary). No new options. **Round 3**: an empty round. Stopped. **Conclusion: 5-a + 5-b, keep 6-a**.

#### The ruling list after the re-loop (amends K15-1–K15-7)

| # | Topic | Adopted | What makes it better than the alternatives | Principle |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| K15-1 | A list-outside clue | 1-f's reader + **1-i's writer**: the observation path rewrites an existing observed row into a witness by the verdict's same predicate (`wasFirstKeyOf`) | The witness doesn't depend on the observation's order · the key's standing (already revoked) · the membership (a past one). The verdict and the witness are 1 pure function | The same question via 1 pure function (K13-1); a list-outside clue only stops on key facts |
| K15-2 | The records | Keep 2-b | — | The same fact is the same verdict value |
| K15-3 | The wording | Keep 3-b | — | An absent · all sentence carries a scope |
| K15-4 | A human confirmation | Keep 4-a | — | Ask the human only for facts they hold |
| K15-5 | The check | 5-a + **5-b** (order · already-revoked · mark preservation) | — | A witness's check goes through the writer's path |
| K15-6 | The witness's predicate (reading side) | Keep 6-a | — | — |
| K15-7 | The owner points | (1)(2) plus **(3) the positive proof** (1-j) | — | — |

**K15-12 (the re-loop's implementation)**: `device-key.ts` — `wasFirstKeyOf` was moved from `device-standing.ts` and made public (shared by the verdict and the witness — `device-standing.ts` reads `device-sync.ts` via `context.ts`, so `device-sync.ts` can't read `device-standing.ts`). `device-sync.ts` — K15-11's rewrite-by-valid-device-provenance was replaced with `recordFirstKeyWitnesses` (applies `wasFirstKeyOf` to every observed row before the sync — placed before the revocation mark (b)). `observeDevices` returned to its pre-K15-11 shape (the known row's revocation Note), and the fallow complexity finding (appeared mid-K15-11 — resolved by splitting the function, not added to the baseline) is gone too. Tests: `device.test.ts` +2 (a key already revoked on its first-key project becomes a witness via that chain's history, keeps the same sync's revocation mark, and stops a hiding rotate / an already-witness row isn't rewritten by another first-key project's sync — keeps the first witness and triggers no per-sync write) → 111. K14-18's gate check's expectation was revised: the wrong reserve row is corrected by K14's correction to p3's observed row, then on the same rotate's p1 sync, rewritten into a witness by p1's history (no provenance device · p1 · a revocation mark) — both are verified-chain facts, the latter a stronger record. The 6 mutations (first confirming the baseline is green — K15-13): X1 not rewriting (3 fail) / X2 the rewrite placed after the revocation mark (1 — K15-12's check) / X3 removing the provenance-observed condition (**survived** — no place reads an approval row's provenance field, and the witness requires provenance observed, so equivalent. The condition is kept to not rewrite the approval's fact — same as W2) / X4 by valid-device provenance instead of the verdict's predicate (2) / X5 removing the provenance-device condition (survived at first — no check of the already-witness rewrite [the named project would move to the last sync's, writing every sync]. Added a check and 1 fails) / X6 erasing the revocation mark on rewrite (1). Green after reverting all mutations.

**Owner point (3) (added in the re-loop)**: **the positive proof** (1-j): when a reserve key is generated, leave a "generated as a reserve key" mark on the ledger seal's record (or an `own-devices.json` field), and limit rotate / `--replace`'s revocations and the ledger key's record to marked keys. Relies on neither the list's completeness nor the observation record, and works on a new device (the ledger is readable by a decrypting device — the mark lives inside the recovery code's AEAD, so the server can't make one). It's a ledger-format (the client contract — CRYPTO_SPEC §8's wrap-target B serialization) or `own-devices.json`-field change, and during the existing mark-less reserve keys' migration (1 rotate) today's rules are needed.

**K15-13 (a procedure record — a mid-re-loop error)**: right after implementing K15-12, I ran only K15's checks (`-t "K15"`), judged green, and counted in the mutation harness that "anything failing even 1 = KILLED". In fact K14-18's gate check **was already failing before any mutation** (its expectation pinned the K14 correction's intermediate state), so X3's / X5's "KILLED"s were false, and I'd once written in the design record an explanation of X3 that wasn't verified via the output (rewritten). How it was found: trying to confirm via the output (the row's contents) why X3 fails K14-18's check showed it fails even on unmutated code. What was fixed: (1) revised K14-18's expectation to the now-correct behavior (the rewrite into a witness), (2) the mutation harness first runs the same check set pre-mutation and stops if not green, (3) the CLI's full test suite runs before mutations. Principle "**a mutation's result counts only after confirming the baseline is green. Don't call it green on a partial check set**" (the flip side of K13-13 / K14-10 — not just survivors; even a killed mutation is read against the baseline).

## 21. K16 addendum (2026-09-26 — collecting K15's owner point (3): the positive proof via a reserve key's "generated" mark)

Collecting K15's (PR #206 — merged at `36bcc01`) owner point (3). K13–K15 had been **retroactively inferring** "is the ledger key a reserve key" **from the on-chain carriage**. The inference's material relies on the projects the server lists and this device's observation record (K15's witness). So on a record-less device (a new device · a device that lost its record), a server hiding from the list defeats it (the K15 completion record's known limitation (1)). In the dialogue with the owner (2026-09-26), we sorted that (1) backing the list's completeness and (2) the omission-count response are not options that close this hole.

- (1): signing prevents forgery but preventing a rollback (an old version of the index) needs a local record (the floor). A record-less device is exactly where the hole lives.
- (2): it's a remedy for an honest server's fault — powerless against malice.

So (3) is effectively the only option. The owner's instruction was "go ahead as it is". Because it touches the spec's wording, per `CLAUDE.md` **the ruling and the spec's amendment are pinned first and implemented after human approval**.

**Pre-start check**: starting from `origin/main` (`36bcc01` — #206's merge). Before this section there's no K16 section or record of the same task ("positive proof" appears only in §20's re-loop 1-j and K15-7 (3)).

**Principle (this round's axis)**: "**a key's provenance is recorded, without inferring, at the place where it was born, when it was born. When there's no record, fall back to today's inference (no worse)**".

### K16 fact check (read the code and the spec — K6-X. Line numbers are 36bcc01's)

1. **The ledger's wrap-target B's shape**: CRYPTO_SPEC §8 :511 "the serialization format is the client's (CLI's) contract; the server is agnostic (currently = the keychain record's JSON. The same shape as a device key's record …)". **Adding a mark makes "the same shape as a device key's record" false, so it's a spec-wording amendment** (the crypto operations · primitives · AAD · wire don't change).
2. **Test vectors**: `master-key-wrap.json` / `recovery-wrap.json` treat B as opaque bytes (`master_secret_blob_hex`) and don't pin the JSON's shape. **`packages/crypto` and the vectors don't change**.
3. **Writing and reading**: `keychain.ts`'s `serializeStoredMasterKey` :474 and `parseStoredMasterKey` :438 both enumerate fields (reading **drops** extra fields).
   - An old CLI can read a marked B (ignores the extra field).
   - But an old CLI's re-seal drops the mark. A mark-less key just returns to "unmarked" — handled by today's rules. No worse.
   - The current CLI too, if it doesn't add the field to the type · write · read, drops the mark on a re-seal (the code re-issuance `keyRecoveryOp` :531, passkey `passkey.ts` :296, guardian `guardian.ts` :260, handoff `handoff.ts` :325 reads).
4. **B's generating paths**: only `reserve.ts` `generateReserveKeys` (`sealNewReserve` — `key generate`'s latter stage · the first `key recovery` · separation, `keyReserveRotateOp`, `replaceReserveWithoutOpening`). Generated keys are closed by the `ReserveKeys` brand against the keychain's save path in the type (K4-1 a-5).
   - **A pre-DK copy is a device-key keychain record sealed as-is by an old CLI — it can't carry the mark**.
   - The only path growing a mark's writer is someone who opened the ledger (whoever holds the code or a passkey — indistinguishable from the person — K4-30) writing via a modified CLI. That's outside the threat model — just the person falsifying their own key.
5. **What the server can do**: B is AEAD-sealed under the recovery-code-derived KEK (and passkey · guardian shares) (§8).
   - The server **can't make · erase · re-attach** a mark (tampering fails the opening).
   - All it can do is **serve an old ledger** (a rollback). An old ledger that is a pre-DK copy has no mark, and "unmarked" falls to today's rules. An old ledger that is a previous reserve key (marked) is actually a reserve key — revoking it stops no device key. **A rollback lands on the safe side in both cases**.
6. **`--replace`'s revoke targets** don't open the ledger; they read only this device's provenance-reserve rows. A row has no mark field (`own-devices.json`'s fields don't change).
   - Provenance-reserve rows written after (3) come only from a generated key or a key opened and mark-confirmed (proof-carrying by writer discipline).
   - But a row written by a previous CLI can't be told apart, so **`--replace`'s rows keep today's rules (the chain + K15's witness's gate)**.

→ (3)'s premises (the server can't make a mark; a rollback lands on the safe side) haven't collapsed. 2 corrections:
- (i) A spec-wording amendment is needed (fact 1 — the owner's approval required).
- (ii) It doesn't reach `--replace`'s record rows (fact 6).

### K16-1. The mark's shape and placement

| Option | Content | Upward compat | Silver bullet | Counterexamples | Adoption |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1-a **Add `"kind": "reserve"` to B's JSON (generated reserve keys only). Added to the `StoredMasterKey` type as an optional field, carried by both read and write** | The mark sits inside the AEAD the server can't touch. Passkey · guardian · handoff wrap the same byte string, so the mark travels along | (3)'s core | Fact 5 | Counterexample 1 "an old CLI's re-seal drops it" → "unmarked" just returns to today's rules (fact 3). Counterexample 2 "the mark lands on a keychain device key" → only `generateReserveKeys` attaches it, and the keychain save accepts only `MasterKeys` (K4-1 a-5). As defense, a path saving a marked record to the keychain is refused (in 1 place) | **Adopted** |
| 1-b Add a "generated" field to `own-devices.json`'s provenance-reserve rows | Reaches `--replace`'s rows too | — | — | A device-local record — absent on a new device (doesn't work where the hole is). A field change (out of scope) | Rejected |
| 1-c Keep the mark as a separate wrap (a ledger row) | B's shape unchanged | — | — | A ledger-format (server · wire) change, and B-and-mark binding would have to be built separately | Rejected |

**Round 2**: option tried "make the mark a signature (the reserve key signs 'I am a reserve key')" → anyone holding the reserve key's secret can make it, and a pre-DK copy's holder can make the same signature on their key. It's no proof of the generation fact (inside the AEAD, it's equally strong as a declaration by the ledger's sealer and needs no new crypto operation — `CLAUDE.md` "no invented protocols"). No new options. **Round 3**: option tried "version the field's value (`reserve-v1`)" → 1 meaning; the version is carried by `suite`. No new options. **Round 4**: an empty round. Stopped. **Adopted: 1-a**.

### K16-2. Reading the mark (the verdict's rules)

| Option | Content | Upward compat | Silver bullet | Counterexamples | Adoption |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2-a A mark decides it's a reserve key; don't look at the chain | No sync needed | — | — | Silently passes a marked key that is a first key on the chain (a contradiction — a modified CLI · an unknown bug). Discards a fact in front of an unrevocable effect | Rejected |
| 2-b **The mark is added as a sufficient positive fact in place of the "may revoke · record" requirement, and the stopping facts (the chain's `first-key` · `revoked` · K15's `recorded-first-key`) still stop. For a marked key, the "couldn't-confirm scope" (`unchecked`) doesn't stop revocation · recording (even if a hidden project has a first key, a marked key can't be a first key — fact 4). A mark-less key keeps today's rules (K14 / K15)** | Even where the hole is (a record-less device · a hiding server), a marked key is handled correctly, and a mark-less key is no worse | Turns "is it a reserve key" from inference into a fact | Fact 4 / 5 | Counterexample 1 "a marked key being a first key on a visible project" → stopped as a contradiction (2-a's counterexample). Counterexample 2 "rotate revoking a marked key unconfirmed" → the revocation reaches only synced projects, and a marked key isn't a device key, so the revocation stops no device. K14-15 (change nothing if unconfirmable) stays only for mark-less keys. Counterexample 3 "during migration" → a mark-less key keeps today's rules (no worse, same as fact 3's counterexample) | **Adopted** |

**Round 2**: option tried "a mark-less key is henceforth neither revoked nor recorded (the mark becomes mandatory)" → every existing reserve key has no mark. Neither rotate nor `key recovery`'s record would work. The migration finishes in 1 rotate, but that rotate needs the mark-less old key's revocation. Leaving a mark-less key's revocation to today's rules doesn't stop existing users. No new options (handled at K16-4). **Round 3**: option tried "stop a marked key too on `unchecked` (keeping K14-15)" → K14-15's reason to stop was "might be a first key on a hidden · unsyncable project", which fact 4 rules out for a marked key. Stopping guards nothing. No new options. **Round 4**: an empty round. Stopped. **Adopted: 2-b**.

### K16-3. `key recover`'s confirmation question (K14-3 — re-examining the owner ruling W-3 "keep the confirmation's 1 question")

| Option | Content | Upward compat | Silver bullet | Counterexamples | Adoption |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 3-a Keep the question (even for a marked key) | The owner ruling as-is | — | — | It becomes a question whose answer changes nothing (answering no to a marked key just drops the reserve key's record). Same as K14-3 round 3's "a fact-overridden question is a formality" | Rejected (as an option) |
| 3-b **For a marked key with no stopping fact, record without asking, plus a Note. A mark-less key keeps today's confirmation question** | When the machine can state the fact, the machine states it (K14's axis) | — | — | Counterexample "changing the owner ruling W-3 (keep the confirmation's 1 question)" → **put to the owner**. W-3 was a ruling to "make a judgment a confirmation", premised on the confirmation's material being an inference | **Adopted (needs approval)** |

**Rounds 2 · 3**: option tried "still ask for a marked key, but default to yes" → a default-yes question isn't a confirmation, and it also creates a gap against non-interactive. No new options (2 empty rounds). Stopped.

### K16-4. Migration and `--replace`

- **Migration**: existing mark-less reserve keys are handled by today's rules (no worse). Keys generated at the next rotate · `key generate` · separation onward carry the mark.
  - No wording urging migration is added. Even urged, rotate is a heavy ceremony (the reserve key's replacement — storing a new code), too weak a reason to recommend just for the mark.
  - Whether `key show` should print "reserve key: marked / not marked" — it can't know without opening the ledger (`key show` doesn't open), so it doesn't.
- **`--replace`**: the record rows' revocation keeps today's rules (fact 6).

### K16-5. The spec's amendment (CRYPTO_SPEC §8 — needs approval)

§8 "wrap-target B"'s parenthetical "currently = the keychain record's JSON. The same shape as a device key's record." is amended to:

> Currently = the keychain record's JSON. A B the client generated as a reserve key additionally carries `"kind": "reserve"` on top of the device-key record's fields (2026-09-26 K16 — a record of the generation fact. Inside the AEAD, so the server can neither make nor erase it. A B lacking it — a copy of a pre-DK device key, or a reserve key an old client sealed — is handled by the client via the on-chain carriage. The crypto operations · AAD · wire format are unchanged).

### K16 ruling list (pinned before implementation — awaiting approval)

| # | Topic | Adopted | What makes it better than the alternatives | Principle |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| K16-1 | The mark's shape and placement | 1-a: `"kind": "reserve"` on B's JSON (generated reserve keys only). Carried by the type · read · write; saving a marked record to the keychain is refused | Placed where the server can't touch it, with no new crypto operation | A provenance is recorded where it's born |
| K16-2 | Reading the mark | 2-b: the mark is a positive fact. Stopping facts still stop. On a marked key, `unchecked` doesn't stop. A mark-less key keeps today's rules | Handled correctly where the hole is; mark-less keys are no worse | With no record, fall back to today's inference |
| K16-3 | `key recover`'s confirmation | 3-b: a marked key is recorded without asking (**a re-examination of the owner ruling W-3 — needs approval**) | Doesn't emit a question whose answer changes nothing | The machine states the facts it can |
| K16-4 | Migration · `--replace` | Mark-less keys and the record's rows keep today's rules | Doesn't stop existing users | — |
| K16-5 | The spec | Amend §8's B-shape sentence (**needs approval**) | — | The spec is the source of truth |

**The owner's approval (2026-09-26)**: approved the 3 points K16-3 · K16-5 · K16-4 (the migration's treatment). Plus, as a new fact: "**there are currently zero users, so compatibility can be ignored**".

### K16-6. Re-examination in light of the zero-users fact (amends K16-2 / K16-4 — before implementation)

K16-2 and K16-4, to not stop users of existing mark-less reserve keys, **handled a mark-less key by today's rules (the chain + K15's witness inference)**. With zero users, no mark-less **legitimate** reserve key exists (every ledger seal will henceforth be by a mark-attaching CLI). No pre-DK ledger exists either. A mark-less ledger key is either a pre-DK copy, a modified CLI's seal, or an unknown bug — **none of which may be treated as a reserve key**.

| Option | Content | Upward compat | Silver bullet | Counterexamples | Adoption |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 6-a Keep K16-2 (mark-less keys under today's rules) | The approved shape | — | — | With zero users there's no existing user to protect. An entrance treating a mark-less key as a reserve key by inference remains, and that entrance relies on the server's list (the hole remains) | Rejected |
| 6-b **Make the mark mandatory**: an opened ledger key is recorded · revoked as a reserve key only when it bears the mark and no stopping fact (a chain first key · K15's witness · a revocation) exists. Without the mark, it's nowhere treated as a reserve key (`key recover` doesn't record it, `key recovery` separates it, `seal passkey` / `guardian add` / rotate stop) | An upward compat of 6-a: **the opened ledger key's verdict loses its dependence on the server's list** (the mark's presence is the ledger's contents; the stopping facts are the visible chains and the record — even under hiding, neither works in the "mistaken for a reserve key" direction). K15's remaining hole closes for every ledger-opening path | "Is it a reserve key"'s positive condition is replaced by the mark (the generation fact) instead of an inference | K16-1's mark | Counterexample 1 "a marked key is a first key on the chain" → stopped as a contradiction (same as K16-2's 2-a counterexample). Counterexample 2 "revoking a marked key while an unsyncable project exists" → a marked key can't be a device key (fact 4), so the revocation stops no device. K14-15's "change nothing if unconfirmable" becomes unnecessary — a mark-less key is no longer a revocation candidate. Counterexample 3 "the confirmation's 1 question" → a marked key is recorded without asking (K16-3 — approved); a mark-less key isn't recorded. **The question disappears** (K16-3's approved "keep today's confirmation for a mark-less key" is replaced by not recording a mark-less key — a revision in the direction of less recording). Counterexample 4 "`--replace`'s record rows" → it doesn't open the ledger, so it can't read the mark. The rows' writers become only generated keys and mark-confirmed keys, but a row has no mark field (out of scope), so the rows' revocation keeps today's gate (chain + witness) | **Adopted** |

**Round 2**: option tried "with zero users, also remove the pre-DK separations (the FP match · `key recovery`'s separation · K13's 2 choices · K15's witness)" → once the mark is mandatory, those become "defense in depth for scenarios that can't occur". Removing them means a large diff and check rewrites, only reducing stopping-side defenses — the gain is just less complexity. Not needed for this round's goal (closing the hole). **Deferred as a separate owner decision**. No new options. **Round 3**: option tried "on opening a mark-less key, auto-replace the ledger" → a ledger replacement (storing a new code) is a ceremony — don't silently sandwich it (same as K14-4's 4-f round 3). `key recovery`'s separation is named. No new options. **Round 4**: an empty round. Stopped.

**Principle**: "**the positive condition is only the generation fact; the stopping facts still stop**" — an upward compat of K16-2, K13-18 ✓ (avoid the silently-wrong side — don't silently record a mark-less key as a reserve key), K14-13 ✓ (an unrevocable effect's precondition is a fact), K4-3 ✓ (the record's writers are only generation and mark confirmation).

#### The verdict's shape (pinned before implementation)

- **`LedgerKeyCheck` gains `marked: boolean | null`** (`true` / `false` for an opened ledger key; `null` for `--replace`'s record rows = unreadable).
- **An opened ledger key may be treated as a reserve key** ⇔ `marked === true` ∧ the verdict's value is none of `first-key` / `recorded-first-key` / `revoked` (the unconfirmable scope isn't asked about).
- **The record rows' (`marked === null`) revocation gate** stays as now (left on a first key · a witness · an unconfirmable scope).
- Readers:
  - `key recover`: a stopping fact → today's Warning. No mark → doesn't record; names `key recovery`. Marked → records without asking, with a Note. **The confirmation's 1 question disappears**.
  - `key recovery`: a stopping fact · no mark → separate (seal a new marked reserve key). Marked → re-seal the same key under a new code and record. The unconfirmable-scope "reissue only" (K14-13) disappears.
  - `seal passkey` / `guardian add` / rotate's prelude: a stopping fact · no mark → stop and name `key recovery`. Marked → record and proceed. `onUnchecked` (K14-15) disappears.
  - The revocation gate: the opened key, if marked, may be revoked regardless of the unconfirmable scope (stopping facts still stop). The record's rows stay as now.
- **The spec's wording (amends K16-5)**: "A B the client generated as a reserve key carries `"kind": "reserve"`. A B lacking it is neither recorded nor revoked as a reserve key by the client (it's separated as a copy of a pre-DK device key)."

### K16 completion record (2026-09-26)

- **Spec**: `docs/CRYPTO_SPEC.md` §8 "wrap-target B"'s parenthetical amended (K16-5 / K16-6). The content is 2 points: a generated reserve key carries `"kind": "reserve"`; a mark-less B is neither recorded nor revoked as a reserve key. `packages/crypto` · test vectors · AAD · wire format are unchanged.
- **Deliverables (the mark — K16-1)**:

- **Deliverables (the verdict — K16-2 / K16-6)**: the stopping facts are `device-standing.ts`'s `stopsLedgerKey` (a first key · a witness · a revocation).
  - `ledger-open.ts` `settleLedgerKeyForChange`: a stopping fact → stop. No mark → stop. Marked → record (`onUnchecked` removed — K14-15's re-examination).
  - `key-recover.ts` `settleOpenedKey`: the confirmation's 1 question removed (K16-3). A stopping fact → Warning; no mark → Warning, no record; marked → recorded without asking.
  - `key-recover.ts` `separateUnusableLedgerKey`: no-mark added to separation. `reissue-only` (K14-13) removed.
  - `key-recover.ts` `confirmRetiring`: `marked` added. Rotate's opened key is revoked even with an unconfirmable scope.

- **docs**:
  - `recover-your-key.mdx`: the confirmation-question item was replaced with a reserve-key-mark paragraph and a "No reserve-key mark" item.
  - `devices.mdx`: wrote that rotate requires the mark, and that a marked key is revoked even when unconfirmable. The pre-DK procedure 1 was rewritten to the mark's distinction.
- **Tests** (CLI 1370 → 1376 — +6 from main. Overall 5088 → 5094):
  - Existing tests rewritten to the new behavior: 6 in `device.test.ts` premised on the confirmation question, 5 in `handoff` / `passkey` / `recovery`.
  - Added mark-less-key negatives: `key recover` doesn't record · rotate stops · `key recovery` separates — each composed with a key `add_device`-provenanced everywhere on the chain (a scenario K14's inference would have called possibly a reserve key).
  - Added that even a marked key isn't recorded if it's a first key (K16-2 counterexample 1).
  - Added the mark's read-write round trip, the unknown value, and the keychain's defense.
  - Added that `key generate`'s ledger contents carry the mark and the keychain's device key doesn't.
  - Scaffolding:
    - `test/support/ledger.ts` gained `storedReserveRecord`.
    - `device.test.ts`'s `ledgerRecordOf` and the `passkey` / `guardian` / `handoff` record compositions make a test's `reserve` marked (the generated reserve key's role) and everything else unmarked.
- **Mutations** (13 mutations — confirmed the baseline is green first. K15-13):
  - The 12 that failed: Y1 not marking on generation / Y2 the mark check always true / Y3 always false / Y4 dropping it on read / Y5 dropping it on write / Y6 accepting an unknown value / Y7 removing the keychain-read defense / Y9 the prelude not stopping a no-mark / Y10 `key recovery` not separating a no-mark / Y11 `key recover` recording a no-mark / Y12 the gate keeping a marked key / Y13 reading the mark before the stopping facts.
  - The 1 survivor: Y8 (removing the keychain-save defense). As expected, there's no path passing a marked record to `storeMasterKeyAndReport` (the save's input is only a generated device key — K4-1 a-5's type). An unreachable defense-in-depth equivalent mutation (same treatment as K15-8).
- **Collection's scope**: **K15's known limitation (1)** (on a witness-less device, a hiding server returns to K14's behavior) is closed on **every ledger-opening path** (`key recover` · `key recovery` · `seal passkey` · `guardian add` · rotate). A mark-less key isn't treated as a reserve key, and a marked key can't be a device key, so the server list's completeness isn't needed on the verdict's positive side. K15's owner point (3) collected. (1)(2) withdrawn (in the dialogue with the owner — §21's opening).
- **Known limitations**:
  - (1) `--replace`'s record rows can't read the mark (it doesn't open the ledger), so the rows' revocation keeps today's gate (chain + witness). With zero users, the rows' writers are only generated keys and mark-confirmed keys — but a row has no field to tell it apart from a previous one (K16-6 counterexample 4).
  - (2) The mark is a record of "this CLI generated it"; it doesn't prevent someone who can open the ledger (indistinguishable from the person) from marking via a modified CLI (outside the threat model — K4-30 / K4-37).
- **Carryover (owner decision)**: with zero users, the pre-DK paths (the FP-match separation · K13's 2 choices · K15's witness · part of K14's chain inference) become, once the mark is mandatory, "defense in depth for scenarios that can't occur". Whether to remove them for simplicity is a separate decision (K16-6's round 2).

## 22. Removing compatibility (2026-09-26 — owner ruling: zero users, so no backward compatibility needed)

### 22-1. The ruling and scope

- Owner ruling (2026-09-26): "there are currently zero users, so compatibility can be ignored"; "every backward-compatibility-conscious piece may be deleted". K16's carryover (the pre-DK paths) and the rest of the compat are removed in 1 PR. Commits are split per group.
- What stays (not compat — current features · defenses):
  - The revoked reserve key's handling: a user revoking a reserve key via `device revoke` still happens today, so the revocation verdict and the record's mark (`markRevokedReserveRecord`) were kept.
  - Strict acceptance · the anti-compat refusal tests · the test vectors (the defense that **refuses** the old shapes).
  - The crypto (group 4 — recovery-wrap's AAD · `master-wrap`'s names) was excluded because `packages/crypto` needs a human review.
  - schemaPolicy's write gate stays as a feature; only its compat wording is removed.

### 22-2. Group 1: the pre-DK paths and the local files' old formats (CLI)

- **The pre-DK paths**: a ledger key is usable as a reserve key only when "it bears the reserve-key mark" and "it's revoked nowhere". The stopping fact is now just revocation.
  - Removed:
    - The chain's first-key inference (`firstKey` / `first-key`).
    - K15's witness (`recorded-first-key` · `recordFirstKeyWitnesses`).
    - The record's rewrite (`retractReserveRecord`'s first-key branch).
    - `key recovery`'s FP-match separation.
    - The revocation gate (`confirmRetiring` — a reserve row's writers are now only generated keys and mark-confirmed keys).
    - `device add`'s 2 choices (K13-2). Even a first key, if valid on any project, reports registered and exits 0.
  - Kept: the defense against a keyless device changing the ledger. `openLedgerReserveForChange` reads the device key first (`seal passkey` / `guardian add`).
  - docs: `devices.mdx`'s "Installs from before device keys" section and related items were deleted, as was `recover-your-key.mdx`'s first-key item. The pre-DK examples were removed from CRYPTO_SPEC §8 and AUTH_SPEC §13-1 (behavior unchanged).
- **Local files' old formats**:
  - The floor's old save shape's (a `<id>.json` single snapshot) compat read and migration.
  - known-fingerprints's v1 (1 fingerprint per person).
  - Tolerating an issue pin's missing scope (pre-K4) and missing `verifiedAtSeq` / `expectedGithubLogin`. Now only null is allowed.
  - The keychain token's missing `expiresAtMs` (pre-W3a).
  - own-devices's and known-fingerprints's outer-envelope decodes were shared into `origin-book.ts` (the version differences are gone, making them duplicates — fallow).
- **Mutations** (9 mutations — confirmed the baseline is green first): 8 failed (the revoked reserve key's record mark × 4 paths · the keyless-device defense · the pin's scope cross-check · the fingerprint book's version · the token's expiry). The 1 survivor (Z9 accepting a pin's missing scope) is an equivalent mutation — a missing `scopeEnvironmentIds` fails the array check the same way.
- **Wording**:
  - CLI: the redacted record's "older maruhi", the checkpoint snapshot-missing's "server predates", the head declaration's "previous release".
  - web: the registry 404's "older servers".

### 22-3. Group 2: wire and server compat

- **Wire fields made mandatory** (the omissibility for old servers / old clients was removed):
  - Responses: `ChainSnapshot.attestations` · `AuthConfig.signupPolicy` · the env list / pull / meta-only pull's `schemaPolicy` · `RotationFlag.trigger` · `RecipientDek.recipientEncPubHex` · `DekWrapExists.storedRecipientEncPubHex` · `LeaseRateLimited.scope` · `GuardianShareResult.deviceShares` (the transcribed top-level `encHex` / `ciphertextHex` were removed).
  - Requests: `DekWrapRef.recipientEncPubHex`. The path that "deleted it if exactly 1 slot" for a device-key-omitted reference (K3-3's 422 `duplicate-recipient`) was removed.
- **Removed types**: `ApprovalNotAccepted` (pre-K5 servers) · `DeviceOpsNotAccepted` (pre-K3 servers).
- **CLI fallbacks**:
  - The key-history inference when the 409 has no stored key (`staleWrapSuspected`). The re-addition-guidance Note was kept.
  - Reading a `recipientEncPubHex`-less row as addressed to self; the device-gap derivation's truncation.
  - A `trigger`-less row's display.
- **The manifest's migration path**: `env rotate --init-manifest` and `allowMissingManifest` were removed. A missing manifest is always refused. The verified pull's `manifest` no longer takes null, and the floor's `manifest-omitted` violation kind is gone too.
  - The server's "if no stored row, latest starts at v1" stays — env creation's compound uses it (only the migration annotation was removed).
- **The audit's old shape `var.read`** (1 variable per line) reading (Q3's column-value branch) was removed. §7's `variable_id` filter's column match stays for other events like `var.created`.
- **Wording**: schemaPolicy's "protecting old verifiers" rationale was removed from CRYPTO_SPEC §4.2 / AUTH_SPEC §12-5 / §12-11. The gate stays as a feature (owner ruling). AUTH_SPEC §12-6's `storedRecipientEncPubHex` became a MUST.

### 22-4. Group 3: deploy · DB

- Done:
  - The rate-limit bindings (5) were made mandatory; the fail-open and warn for an old binding-less `wrangler.jsonc` were removed.
  - The client_id placeholder detection (aimed at forks of the old template) was removed.
  - `docs/SELF_HOSTING.md`'s "Updates" per-release migration steps were removed.
- **DB-state-dependent compat (owner ruling 2026-09-26: rebuild the ops' D1 and DO — the data is thrown away)**:
  - The 18 D1 migrations were folded into 1 current-schema migration (`drizzle/…_init`). Generated via drizzle-kit. Confirmed the only differences from applying the old 18 in order are the intended 4:
    - `recovery_wraps.fetch_window_start` / `fetch_count` removed
    - `api_tokens.expires_at` made NOT NULL
    - `user_audit_events.row_id` / `org_audit_events.row_id` made NOT NULL
  - The 12 DO SQLite migration steps were folded into 1. The only difference from applying the old steps in order is `audit_events.row_id` made NOT NULL (the column order is the same too).
  - Removed readers:
    - The lazy backfill of `row_id` on D1 audit rows
    - Reading a token's `expires_at: null` row as expired (the server · wire · CLI · web's "no expiry recorded")
    - rotation-detect's pre-K3 stored-row `trigger` fill (a `trigger`-less row is now a defect as corruption)
    - The audit's old shape `var.read` (§22-3. Pullfrog's finding once proposed a revert, but the rebuild ruling made it unnecessary)
- **Deploy order (breaks if not followed, but doesn't break silently)**:
  - Rebuild the ops' D1 and DO first, then deploy this code.
  - If deployed without rebuilding:
    - D1: `wrangler d1 migrations apply` fails at `CREATE TABLE` trying to apply the new `…_init` onto existing tables, and `bun run deploy` stops before deploying.
    - DO: an existing DO holds schema version 12, newer than the code's 1, so the constructor refuses to open it (`applyProjectDoMigrations`).
- **Rebuild procedure** (originally meant for ops to run; the owner handed over an API token and Claude ran it on 2026-09-26. Record below):
  1. D1: back up via `wrangler d1 export` just in case (same as ops-backup). `wrangler d1 delete` → `wrangler d1 create`, and swap `env.hosted`'s `database_id` to the new ID.
  2. DO: delete and rebuild `ProjectChainDO`'s namespace. Cloudflare's current procedure is the `exports` `"state": "deleted"` tombstone; a move off the `migrations` array is one-way. Verify against current docs at run time (https://developers.cloudflare.com/durable-objects/reference/durable-objects-migrations/). The deletion is unrevocable (no Trash).
  3. Deploy the new code via `bun run deploy` (migrate → deploy).
- **Execution record (2026-09-26)**:
  1. D1 `maruhi` (old ID `85cb9161-…`) was backed up via `wrangler d1 export` (43 INSERT rows. Not placed in the repo), deleted, and rebuilt. The new ID `c45d4f84-3142-46cf-ab3b-c9571ca7eed0` went into `env.hosted`'s `database_id`.
  2. DO: a dummy Worker that just returns 503 was deployed to `maruhi-server-hosted` with `exports: { ProjectChainDO: { type: "durable-object", state: "deleted" } }`, deleting the namespace (wrangler 4.136.1. Output "Durable Object exports reconciliation: Deleted: ProjectChainDO"). `my.maruhi.app` was 503 meanwhile.
  3. `wrangler.jsonc`'s `migrations` was replaced with `exports` (`storage: "sqlite"`). Having deployed once with `exports`, it can't return to `migrations`. New self-hosted deploys also create the DO via `exports`.
  4. From main, `bun run deploy` (`--env hosted`) ran the D1 migration and deploy, creating the DO in the new namespace.

### 22-5. The unconfirmable-projects Note (2026-09-27 — handling a Cursor security finding. Owner-approved)

- The finding: the ledger key's revocation verdict folds unsyncable projects · list failures into "not revoked" and proceeds (fail-open).
- The ruling: the proceed behavior is unchanged (K16-6: a marked reserve key is used even where unconfirmable — stopping it would block the recovery's only path on a transient fault). Instead, don't stay silent.
- Deliverables:
  - `ReserveVerdict`'s `usable` gained `uncheckedProjectIds` and `listFailure` (`reserveVerdictOf` takes `KeyStandings`).
  - `ledger-open.ts`'s `noteUncheckedLedgerKey` emits a Note: on a list failure, "nowhere was checked"; on unsyncable projects, their names. Called by `key recovery` (the branch using the ledger key) and the ledger-changing commands (`settleLedgerKeyForChange` — rotate / passkey / guardian).
  - Not emitted at `key recover`'s post-registration verdict (the unsyncable projects are already named by each project's report).
